# Architecture contract

**In short:** the rules music2's code must follow, each with an id (`RENDER-8`), why it exists, and how the code matches it today. Tests name the id they check. Where another doc disagrees, this page wins.

**Normative.** Adopted 2026-10-12 as step 2 of the [order of work](#order-of-work), and revised the same day after three reviews (a DAW producer's, a core engineer's and an accuracy check against the code). Every later step is built and reviewed against this page. Where [Song as a program](song-as-program.md) or the [code and graph editing design](code-graph-editing-design.md) disagree with it, this page wins. Those pages keep the product design and the reasoning behind it.

## How to read this

- **MUST**, **MUST NOT**, **SHOULD** and **MAY** mean what they mean in RFC 2119. A SHOULD may be broken only with the reason written next to the code that breaks it.
- Every rule has an id, such as `RENDER-8`. A test that checks a rule names the id in a comment. A change that breaks a rule changes this page first: in the same pull request as the code, in a commit ahead of it. A rule inserted after adoption gets a letter suffix (`RENDER-6a`); one added at the end of its section takes the next number. Ids already cited never move.
- **Why** gives the reason in one line. **Today** says where the code differs, or that it already matches. File and line pointers are to commit `92a19a3`. Step 1's code is named by file only, since it lands after that commit.
- "Step n" means a step in the [order of work](#order-of-work).

### Words used here

| Word | Meaning |
|---|---|
| **key** | A short readable name a person writes in code, such as `"lead-vox"`. Saved data is filed under keys. |
| **label** | Display text, such as `"Lead Vox"`. Never an address. |
| **module** | A graph with lanes and exports: the root module `song`, or a track. |
| **site** | One call expression in the source. |
| **revision** | An accepted state of the project: the pair `(code_rev, data_rev)`. |
| **cell** | Host-owned engine memory that holds one editable constant. |
| **plane** | The path a change takes through the system, chosen by the kind of file it touches. |
| **engine clock** | Samples processed since the live engine started. It only moves forward. |
| **transport clock** | The song position. It jumps on seek and loop. |
| **fixture** | One of the 5 projects in the repo: `examples/first-song`, `examples/with-plugins`, `examples/resample`, `templates/starter`, `templates/empty`. |
| **pure** | Made only of WebAssembly kernels, so it renders the same bits every time. A song with a hosted plugin is impure. |

## Owner decisions

These answer the plan's open questions (2026-10-12).

1. **Live and export.** Live playback may differ from export after a jump or an edit (tails, held notes, replaced nodes that start fresh), as in other DAWs. Export is always exact and deterministic. (Exact means an offline render from sample 0 for every song. Deterministic to the bit holds for pure songs, [RENDER-2](#offline); a song with a hosted plugin reports that it isn't reproducible.)
2. **Track signature.** The key comes first: `track("lead-vox", { name: "Lead Vox" }, fn)`, or `track("kick", fn)` with the label defaulting to the key.
3. **After the core,** synth and plugin work alternates with audio and sample work.
4. **Mute and solo** are listening-only monitoring overlays that never change the export. A separate "mute in song" is saved in code.
5. **Agents** may keep writing files directly. That is the save path, with loud diagnostics. Verified Tx edits are opt-in.
6. **A project whose code doesn't compile** opens silent, with diagnostics, so it can be fixed in the code editor.
7. **Patterns and events generated by code** get reserved room now (the `const-events` op) and are built later.
8. **Realtime target:** 50 tracks now. Partitions and threads come right after the arena.
9. **The data folder** is renamed from `timeline/` to `data/`.
10. **The project lock** is adopted now as a rule, and built in step 6 ([WRITE-17](#the-project-lock), [WRITE-18](#the-project-lock)). Forwarding was deferred then, and is adopted since (2026-10-21) as [AGENT-1](#agents): an `m2` write that finds the app holding the lock hands its Tx to the app; a writer that can't take a lock held by any other program fails with a clear message.

## Principles

- **P1. Identity has three kinds, each with its own rule.** Saved identity is a key written by a person in code. Derived identity lives only in memory. Labels, binding names, file paths, ordinals, array indexes and build-local counters are never identity.
- **P2. Files are the truth, and every writer is first class:** the app, the CLI, agents, text editors and git. Nothing that matters depends on an index only the app maintains.
- **P3. Only the files on disk decide what is accepted.** The session never moves, deletes or repairs files because it noticed something. Relinks, journal recovery and id assignment are offered as fixes, never applied automatically. The build reads nothing but the snapshot.
- **P4. Two write primitives and one music2 writer process:** `save()` for drafts, `Tx` for verified edits, and a project lock. Outside writers (editors, agents, git) can't be locked out; the watcher takes their changes in ([WRITE-19](#outside-writers)).
- **P5. Four planes of change,** chosen by which kind of file changed, not by who wrote it.
- **P6. Offline render is exact from sample 0.** Live playback is a persistent engine with two clocks. Live playback never replays, and export is always offline.
- **P7. Code that builds the graph never reads timeline data.** Notes reach instruments as sample-stamped event buffers.
- **P8. Each value has one home, and the UI shows which.** Monitoring overlays never change the song or the export.
- **P9. Break the format and the SDK once, now,** while the only projects are the 5 fixtures. Version them separately.
- **P10. Core holds no UI vocabulary:** no effect categories, knob ranges, undo labels or unit spellings. Core is what safety, determinism and a single source of truth need.
- **P11. The engine reaches plugins only through `PluginHost`.** Nothing assumes a plugin shares the app's process or threads.

## Planes of change

| Plane | Changed files (`DataPath` class) | Path | Budget |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Control** | none: cells, transport, audition, overlays, leases | an engine message or cell write; never the session | the next audio block |
| **Data** | Data: notes and control lanes | no QuickJS run; checked against the accepted lane declarations; `SwapLane`, sent as a preview once the Tx's candidate passes and its files still read as their bases, while the commit is made durable ([PLANE-5](#planes-of-change)) | under 20 ms from the edit's request to the first block that plays it; the time until the commit is durable, journal sync included, is measured beside it ([PLANE-5](#planes-of-change)) |
| **Prepared data** | Data: audio lanes, assets, plugin state | no QuickJS run; clip renders and state loads prepared off the audio thread; an `Activate` of the same structure that keeps every instance | no glitch; the old data plays until the switch, which waits for the renders ([RENDER-27](#plans-data-and-arrangement-swaps)) and the state load ([RENDER-26b](#plans-data-and-arrangement-swaps)) |
| **Arrangement** | Arrangement: tempo and meter | tables and clip renders prepared off the audio thread; an `Activate` of the same structure | no glitch; the old arrangement plays until the switch |
| **Code** | Code, Manifest | compile, check, verify, prepare against the plan that plays; `Activate` | audible under 200 ms after the compile finishes, not counting plugin loads ([RENDER-25](#plans-data-and-arrangement-swaps)) |

**PLANE-1.** The plane of a change MUST be chosen by `DataPath::class()` of the files it touches, and for Data, by whether it needs a render or a state load; never by who wrote them.
- *Why:* a lane file written by an agent or by git must get the same fast path as the app.
- *Today:* matches since step 6. A Tx or an outside batch that changes no Code, Manifest or Arrangement file takes the data path: its data is read against the accepted code, with no QuickJS run, and only `data_rev` moves (`core/session/src/take.rs`; `kind_of` in `core/session/src/tx/mod.rs`; `an_outside_lane_write_takes_the_data_path`, and through the server's watcher `an_outside_lane_write_is_swapped`). A lane whose file is the text it was read from keeps that read, so only the files a change touches are parsed (`Project::data` in `core/song/src/data.rs`). Since the G11 fix a `Clips` Tx parses nothing it doesn't change either: the editor and the plan take the accepted revision's read of a text that is the file (`DataSet::parsed`), the lane the plan made is the candidate's own data when its canonical text reads back to it bit for bit (`LaneData::reads_back`; else the text is parsed), snapshots share their texts and binaries map (`Arc`), so a clone or a diff costs the files that changed, and check, the planner's assertion and commit are one call with one read of the targets (`Session::commit_with`). `a_lane_write_after_an_outside_edit_is_planned_on_the_file` and `commit_with_is_check_then_commit` test them.

**PLANE-2.** A change that touches files of several classes MUST take the slowest of their planes (Code, then Arrangement, then prepared data, then Data).
- *Why:* one revision must never mix a new graph with stale timing.
- *Today:* matches since step 6: a change is named by the slowest plane of its paths (`take::Plane`), and a batch that changes code is compiled whole with its data (`core/session/src/take.rs`).

**PLANE-3.** Changes to Presentation or Ignored files MUST NOT create a revision.
- *Why:* moving a node on the canvas must not recompile or rebuild anything.
- *Today:* matches since step 4: a snapshot holds only what `DataPath` classes as Manifest, Code, Arrangement and Lane, and state sidecars, so `layout.json`, `.music2/` and other files make no revision (`core/song/src/lib.rs`; tested in `core/session/src/gate_tests.rs`).

**PLANE-4.** The compile itself SHOULD stay under 100 ms at p95 at 50 tracks.
- *Why:* the code-plane budget starts after the compile, so a slow compile is felt directly.
- *Today:* 84 ms measured at 50 tracks. Going over is the trigger for incremental compile ([Deferred](#deferred-with-triggers)). Step 5 made the build and check of p50 about a third slower, 33 → 44 ms at p95 (release, `m2_script::build_full` then `m2_ir::check`), since every control a std call leaves out is now a const ([PKG-3](#component-descriptors)).

**PLANE-5.** A Tx on the Data plane MUST be heard without waiting for the disk, and MUST NOT be accepted before it is durable. Once its candidate passes (its delta, its expectation and its planner's assertion) and every file it touches still reads as its base ([WRITE-15](#commit-journal-and-recovery) step 1), the engine is sent the candidate's lanes as a preview `SwapLane`, and the commit goes on to journal, stage, make durable (one barrier, `m2_os::fs::barrier`: `F_FULLFSYNC` on macOS, or `fsync` on a volume that refuses it; `fsync` on Linux) and rename. A lane that needs more room than its scheduler has, or a change the engine can't reach from what it plays by lane swaps alone, is heard once the commit is accepted, through an activation that keeps every instance ([RENDER-26](#plans-data-and-arrangement-swaps)). The revision, its `data_rev` and its undo step exist only once the renames are done. A commit that fails after its preview swaps the accepted data back, under the sounding-note rules of [RENDER-26a](#plans-data-and-arrangement-swaps), and says so in its error, which the app shows as a message. The sync is never skipped or weakened to meet the budget. The 20 ms is measured on the [reference Mac](#acceptance-gates) from the request to the first block that plays the preview. The time until the commit is durable is measured and reported beside it, on an idle disk and on one another writer keeps busy.
- *Why:* on macOS the durable sync alone took about 5 ms on an idle disk and up to 12 ms on a busy one when step 6 measured it, so a note that waited for it would be late whenever the disk is shared. An edit reported saved must survive a power loss. On the Linux development server a whole commit's syncs took 0.6 ms idle and 1.5 ms at p95 while another program wrote 1 GB (drives with power-loss protection, so a best case); the budget is judged on the reference Mac.
- *Today:* matches since the G11 fix. The session tells its host of a commit between its pre-check and its journal (`Session::before_write`, `Writing` in `core/session/src/tx/mod.rs`; the callback of `stage::commit`), for every judged Tx (a gesture's, an agent's, an undo's), never for a save, a recovery or a refused Tx, and checks its lock again after. The server's actor sends the candidate's lanes there (`crates/server/src/graph/preview.rs`) when the commit is data only, what plays is the accepted revision, and every lane fits its scheduler; what plays is then the candidate, and the settle after the commit adopts the accepted revision, sending nothing, or swaps the accepted data back. A failed commit's error ends "It played for a moment and was taken back." (an agent's refusal says `"taken_back": true`), or, when its rollback failed too, points to Needs attention; with the transport stopped nothing was heard, and the error says only what wasn't saved (`with_the_transport_stopped_no_message_says_the_edit_played`). A commit still not durable after 2 s is said in a message, and so is its save once it is (`a_save_the_disk_is_slow_to_make_is_said`), from a server thread that never waits for the session's actor, so an outside save that settles meanwhile doesn't hold it back (`the_server_says_a_slow_save_while_an_outside_save_waits_behind_it`). Every window is told how a commit ended when the transport played while it was previewed, or the user was told it isn't saved yet, whoever sent it: an agent's commit taken back is said in the app too (`an_agents_write_heard_and_taken_back_is_said_in_the_app`), and the app shows the same words once. Tested in `core/session/src/tx/hook_tests.rs` (the hook's place among the disk calls; failures at the journal, the staging, the barrier, a swap's read-back, the rollback and the lock accept nothing; never told of a refused Tx, a save or a recovery; killed in the hook) and `crates/server/src/graph/preview_tests.rs` (heard before durable; each previewed write made durable by its own one barrier, after the preview and before its renames, with nothing accepted while it runs; a refused write, a rename conflict and an interrupted commit; undo, two writes at once, a code save behind one, a drag, read-only, a replaced player, an export, a lane too big for its scheduler, a panic, a slow disk, a stopped transport, an agent's commit taken back; and `a_failed_build_then_a_lane_edit_rebuilds` for what plays lagging). Measured by `m2-gates --server tx-data` (G11; release, 512-frame buffers, 200 piano-roll writes from 1:00 on p16, each a journaled `Clips` Tx), on an M1 Pro (MacBookPro18,1, not the base M1), in seven runs (two when it was built, three when it was checked, two once its messages were fixed; at load averages 2 to 10):
  - From the request to the first block that plays it: p50 4.8 to 5.8 ms, p95 8.7 to 9.8 ms on an idle disk; p50 4.6 to 6.0 ms, p95 8.5 to 10.7 ms while another writer syncs 4 MB chunks; p50 4.9 to 5.6 ms, p95 8.2 to 10.7 ms with a 5,000-file `assets/`; every write a preview swapped into the renderer that plays, none taken back. Of that, the session's part before the preview (plan, check, pre-check) is p50 1.1 to 2.1 ms, p95 1.3 to 3.5 ms, and the wait for the next callback (one device buffer at most) most of the rest. Step 6 measured p95 28.6 ms idle and 35.7 ms busy, and about 77 ms with the 5,000 files; the same day as these runs, the build with the fix's part A and no preview measured p95 16.2 ms idle and 23.9 ms busy.
  - Until the commit is durable and accepted: p50 8.2 to 9.9 ms, p95 8.8 to 14.5 ms idle; p50 14.6 to 17.0 ms, p95 18.9 to 24.7 ms busy.
  - The barrier alone: p50 4.7 to 4.9 ms, p95 4.9 to 6.4 ms idle; p50 7.7 to 9.5 ms, p95 9.2 to 13.8 ms busy. A G11 run with `fcntl` and the renames interposed (no dtruss) counted an `F_FULLFSYNC` on each of its 809 journals, every one before that commit's first rename into the project.
  - Writes sent back to back wait for the one before to be durable (the step 6 design's C20; WRITE-22a's lane leases are the answer for drags). A held arrow key's nudges, one every 16 to 30 ms and each written as the piano roll writes it once the one before replied, from each nudge to the block that plays it: p50 4.9 to 6.6 ms, p95 8.7 to 13.1 ms idle; p50 4.8 to 6.2 ms, p95 8.6 to 13.2 ms busy.
  - A write the disk refuses is taken back (G11, with `.music2/tx/` read-only, and `a_durable_failure_swaps_the_accepted_data_back`).

## Render

### Offline

**RENDER-1.** Offline renders (Export Audio, `m2 render`, `m2 bounce`) MUST be exact. Engine time equals transport time, both start at sample 0, and every instance starts from init. Rendering from sample `s` is rendering from 0 and dropping what comes before `s`. An offline render MUST fail when any fault changes its sound, naming the part and the code: a node's error, a file that can't be read or decoded, a stretch, a plugin. Never a file with a part silent. What the revision knows before it renders (a plugin that isn't installed, a missing file, a state or lane that doesn't load) isn't a fault: the render goes on, and says it.
- In the app, an offline render of a song with hosted plugins pauses live playback and reuses the live instances through `PluginHost`: each one that holds its node's committed blob and the parameters its code sets. One that holds anything else (a value its code no longer sets, [HOME-5a](#homes-of-values); a blob the plan that plays never loaded), one that failed and one waiting for a restart aren't reused: their nodes play new instances from the committed blob. The engine is held first: it fades out over 5 ms and processes nothing, which it publishes before any host call. Each reused instance is deactivated, loaded with the committed blob (skipped when it holds it, [HOME-6](#homes-of-values)), set to offline mode and activated, and is reset on the render thread before its first `process`. Afterwards each is deactivated, set to realtime and activated, and is left holding the committed blob, with its code-owned parameters sent again at the next block ([HOME-4](#homes-of-values)). The engine resumes where it held, fading in over 5 ms, and each reused instance gets a note-on again for every note the song still holds there, as the export ended them. An export first commits any pending state autosave ([HOME-6](#homes-of-values); since ph-2, `flush_autosaves` in `crates/server/src/graph/plugin_window.rs`, `an_export_commits_a_pending_autosave_first`), and doesn't borrow an instance whose window holds or latched a parameter, or whose state changed and isn't saved (`PluginInstance::lendable`). The cost: playback pauses while such an export runs, and the UI says so, and a note held across it starts again at the resume, so its attack plays twice. A pure song's export runs beside playback. The CLI has no live engine and creates its own instances. Every render that isn't live runs its plugins in offline mode, and the live engine's run in realtime mode.
- *Why:* bounces must reproduce, and caches need a key. A second set of instances would double the memory of sample-based instruments and can hit per-instance licence limits. CLAP's `reset` needs an active instance. A reused instance holds the committed blob, and the export sends the parameters its code sets at its first call, so the file is what new instances make; loading a saved state back into the instance that produced it is what [HOME-6](#homes-of-values) forbids. One that holds a value no file records (a parameter its code released keeps its value, [HOME-5a](#homes-of-values)) would bake that value into the file, and loading the blob into it would change what plays live, so a new instance plays its node: the export then depends on the files alone, not on the edits that led to them. A plugin may render differently offline, so `m2 render` and an export run the same mode.
- *Today:* matches for timing (the render specification at the top of `core/runtime/src/lib.rs`, `Renderer::seek`). Since step 7 an export borrows the live instances as an activation would keep them (`PlanInfo::lend` in `core/runtime/src/plan.rs`), in a renderer that resets each at its first call (`Renderer::lending`). On the export's thread (`Lent::take` in `crates/server/src/export.rs`) it holds the engine (`Msg::Hold`) and waits up to 2 s for the engine to say it has taken that hold (`Player::held`, published after the callback returns), then takes each instance offline. Then one `process` call, before the render, ends every note playback left sounding, a choke and a NoteOff for every key: CLAP's `reset` doesn't end them in every plugin (Surge XT keeps its voices, which rang on through the export), and the export doesn't know their ids (`an_export_ends_the_notes_playback_left_sounding`). The same call lets a plugin apply a state just loaded: Surge XT applies one at its next call, ending its voices and setting its parameters, which would take the export's first notes and the code's values with it (`an_export_that_loads_a_state_lets_the_plugin_apply_it_first`). A guard gives every instance back and resumes the engine on every path: done, failed, stopped, a panic, quitting. It tells the session from the actor's own job, so a job that fails after making the export still frees the session (`an_export_whose_job_fails_still_frees_the_session`). Meanwhile the session's settles prepare nothing for the engine (`Open.exporting`), and the one after the export plays what waited. `PlanInfo::lend` borrows only an instance whose plan says it holds the export's committed blob, and none whose code released a parameter (`PluginInstance::released`, set as the plan is prepared, cleared by a load that lands) or that failed; the actor skips one waiting for a restart (`an_export_after_a_released_parameter_is_what_m2_render_makes`, `an_export_never_changes_what_a_live_instance_holds`, `an_instance_waiting_for_a_restart_isnt_borrowed`). An instance whose host calls fail is given back at once and plays its node anew in the export, which carries `plugin.not-borrowed`; one that won't come back active is marked failed before the resume, so its node plays silence, and the settle after replaces it from its committed blob (`a_plugin_that_doesnt_come_back_after_an_export_is_replaced_as_playback_goes_on`). New instances of a render that isn't live (`Renderer::new`, `Renderer::lending`, `PluginInstance::another`) are set to offline mode before they are activated; the live engine's stay in realtime mode, CLAP's default (`renders_that_arent_live_run_their_plugins_offline`). When playback doesn't pause within 2 s (a stalled device), the export touches no live instance and renders beside playback, saying so. The engine view and `/api/graph/status` say `paused: "exporting"` while held, and the web's status pill says "Paused for export" (taste ST-1). The web says an export's problems in plain words after its done toast. Tested by `an_export_borrows_and_gives_back_the_live_instances` (G7e's probe test, with its negative control), `an_instance_that_cant_be_borrowed_plays_anew_in_the_export`, `an_export_whose_playback_cant_pause_renders_beside_it`, `a_second_export_waits_for_its_own_hold`, `settles_during_an_export_wait_then_apply_once` and `lane_previews_play_during_an_export` (`crates/server/src/graph/tests.rs`), and G7e's server gate (`m2-gates --server export`). Since ph-1 the instances it borrows live in the host process ([HOST-3](#pluginhost)), and the borrow is the same (`an_export_borrows_instances_in_the_host_process`: none made, nothing said, the file the export of new instances). At the resume, each plugin node whose instance the export took offline (which ended its notes) starts again the notes its lane still holds, at the first resumed block and with their own ids, and forgets the ones the lane let go meanwhile, also when the export failed before it rendered a block (`Renderer::resumed` in `core/runtime/src/lib.rs`, `a_note_held_across_an_export_plays_again_at_the_resume` in `core/runtime/src/tests/live/keep.rs`, `a_note_held_across_an_export_that_fails_at_once_plays_again` in `crates/server/src/graph/tests.rs`).
- *Today:* the fault rule matches since track ex's slice 2. Each offline render has its own fault ring (its `audio::Cache`'s), read before each block and after it (`Renderer::faulted` in `core/runtime/src/lib.rs`): the first fault in it stops the render. A node's error stops it as it did, now as the fault it is (`fault::Failed`, naming the node and its code). A plugin's error, or an instance that died (an export's lent one, its host crashed), stops it whenever the render isn't live, strict or not: `plugin.failed`, or `plugin.crashed` when the host process ended. `Render` (`crates/editor/src/render.rs`) names it with the song's `Namer` as one error, `Faulted { code, words }`: the export's `error` event carries the `code`, and `m2 render` and `m2 bounce` exit non-zero, printing the words and the code. The problems an export carries, said with its file, are the revision's errors that leave a part silent: `plugin.missing`, `asset.missing`, `state.*`, `lane.invalid` and `lane.unit-mismatch`. Tested by `an_export_of_a_streamed_file_cut_short_fails_naming_it` (`core/runtime/src/tests/stream.rs`), `an_export_whose_stretch_fails_names_the_clip` (`core/runtime/src/tests.rs`), `a_render_whose_plugin_fails_fails` and `a_sampler_file_that_wont_decode_fails_the_export_naming_it` (`crates/editor/src/render.rs`), `an_export_during_a_host_crash_fails_naming_the_plugin` (`crates/server/src/graph/tests.rs`), `a_missing_clip_file_is_named_in_the_exports_problems` (`crates/server/src/export.rs`) and `m2_render_of_a_host_crash_exits_non_zero_naming_it` (`apps/m2/tests/faults.rs`).

**RENDER-2.** Given the same project files, asset bytes, package contents, sample rate and runtime, a pure offline render MUST give the same bits. Package contents are the content hashes of every package file the build and the runtime read; each render's and bounce's provenance records them, and the package versions. The output MUST NOT depend on thread count or scheduling. A plan's top-level nodes are split into partitions, each run as one task per block, in plan order, on one thread: one per module (a track, or the song). Where modules read each other both ways, their nodes are split further by the number of module crossings that lead to them, so no dependency path leaves a partition and comes back. A fade belongs to the node that reads it, a ghost to its old module, an overlay to the track it carries. A node whose inputs all come from one other module, constants aside, runs with that module: a mixer strip runs with its track. Nothing moves into the song's partition: a bus, which reads only what the song feeds it, runs in its own ([RENDER-34](#buses)). A constant is written only before the graph, so reading it waits for nothing. A node reads only buffers its inputs wrote this block; feedback reads the last block. No two partitions write the same memory. Every sum runs inside one node: buses as the graph's own adds, a `poly`'s voices in voice order into a zeroed buffer. When a node fails, what reads it is skipped and the other partitions still run that block; the error reported is the one first in plan order. Host code that makes audio for a render, and song code in QuickJS, compute every transcendental function with the `libm` crate, never the OS's math library ([PLAT-4](#platforms)). Song code reads no clock and no randomness, and makes no code from text while it runs (`eval`, the `Function` constructors), which would skip the build's lowering of `**`.
- *Why:* determinism is what makes caching, tests and reproducible bounces possible. A version requirement such as `std = "0.2"` admits many builds, so only hashes identify what ran. A sum in completion order would make renders differ from run to run.
- *Today:* matches for pure songs. Since 8a's offline half, offline renders run on any number of threads with the same bits (`every_pure_fixture_renders_the_same_bits_on_1_and_n_threads` in `core/play/tests/bit_identity.rs`, each render checked to run tasks on worker threads; the partitions in `core/runtime/src/parts.rs`, the executor in `render_block_on`). Since 8a's live half a mixer strip runs with its track and constants with no task (`a_mixer_strip_runs_with_its_track`, `constants_run_in_no_partition`, `the_song_tail_is_the_sum_and_the_master`). Since 8a's live half, live playback renders on the player's engine threads with the same bits as one thread ([RENDER-28a](#plans-data-and-arrangement-swaps); `live_on_n_threads_is_the_export_on_1`, `edits_on_engine_threads_match_one_thread` and `a_resampled_song_on_n_threads_matches_one_thread`, each run checked to run tasks on workers). Since step 4 each revision records the hash of every package file its build read, and a bounce's provenance records each package's version and the hash of every package file the build and the runtime read (`apps/m2/src/main.rs`). `m2 render` and Export Audio write no provenance yet. Since the platform step host math on the render path is `libm` (`core/runtime/tests/host_math.rs` bans host math in `core/runtime/src` and `core/play/src`), and so are QuickJS's `Math` and `**`, so `examples/resample` renders the same bits on macOS and Linux. The sandbox removes `Date` and makes `Math.random` throw, saying why; since the platform step it also removes `performance`, and `WeakRef` and `FinalizationRegistry` (when garbage is collected differs between OSes), and `eval` and the `Function` constructors throw (`PRELUDE` in `core/script/src/lib.rs`, `song_code_cannot_make_code_from_text`). A `**=` whose target wears TypeScript's types (`x! **= y`) is lowered too. Song code can't have an `enum` (refused at its place, `an_enum_is_refused_at_its_place`). Each build runs on a thread of its own, with a 32 MB stack and numbers read as C reads them whatever the app's locale (`JSON.parse` once read "0.5" as 0 in a German Linux app; `core/script/tests/locale.rs`). So no real song reaches QuickJS's stack limit (16 MB of C stack, about 15,000 calls, which each compiler spends differently; `deep_recursion_builds_alike_on_every_os`). A song that recurses that deep, or reaches the 256 MB memory limit (counted as each OS's `malloc` counts), or catches those errors, may build differently on another OS. A build is stopped as an endless loop after 10 s of its own thread's CPU time, not of the clock: a build at background priority waits for a CPU far longer than it works (an agent's listen on a Mac's efficiency cores; the Mac's first full check at `a1e3c77` stopped four that way), and its wait is no loop. The clock is a backstop at ten times that, 100 s, so an endless loop that gets almost no CPU (beside other work, at background priority) still ends, and doesn't hold `~/.music2/listen.lock` for good (`Limit` in `core/script/src/lib.rs`; `a_build_that_waits_for_a_cpu_is_no_endless_loop`, `a_starved_endless_loop_is_stopped_by_the_clock`).

**RENDER-2a.** Every thread that renders MUST compute in one floating-point mode: round to nearest, subnormals kept, no default NaN (MXCSR `0x1F80` on x86-64, FPCR 0 on arm64). The engine sets it at the start of each block it renders and before each partition, and again after each plugin call, and puts the calling thread's own mode back at the block's end. The player sets it at the start of each callback, for its own mixing too, and puts the device thread's own mode back at the callback's end. Whatever runs a kernel or a plugin outside a block (a build, a seek, a panic, an activation's resets, the idle check) does the same.
- *Why:* WebAssembly requires subnormals; a plugin may leave flush-to-zero set for the next kernel on its thread; a thread we didn't start keeps its own mode.
- *Today:* since 8a's offline half (`core/os/src/fp.rs` since ph-1, where the plugin host process sets the same mode after each plugin call, [HOST-3](#pluginhost); `m2_runtime::fpenv` re-exports it; `a_flush_to_zero_set_by_the_caller_changes_no_bit`, `a_flush_to_zero_left_by_a_plugin_changes_no_bit` and `between_blocks_kernels_and_plugins_compute_in_the_one_mode`). Since 8a's live half the player's own mixing outside a block (the resampler, the 5 ms fade between engines, the hold's fade, the metronome's click) runs in the one mode too, and the device thread's own mode is back after each callback (`Callback::fill`, `the_callback_mixes_in_the_one_mode` in `core/play/tests/engine_threads.rs`).

**RENDER-3.** Export and bounce MUST use the offline renderer, never the live engine or a range render. Only agent listening ([RENDER-4](#range-render)) may use a range render. Renders read committed files only: no previews, no overlays. An agent's listen may also hear a Tx it checked and never wrote, and the overlays when it asks for them, saying so ([AGENT-7](#agents)).
- *Why:* what is exported must not depend on what someone was listening to, and a bounce must be exact to reproduce.
- *Today:* matches. Export renders the accepted revision offline (`Graph::export` in `crates/server/src/graph.rs`): with instances of its own, or, for a song whose plugins play, in a renderer that borrows the live instances while playback is held ([RENDER-1](#offline)); never through the live engine. A commit's preview ([PLANE-5](#planes-of-change)) reaches only the live engine; an export waits for the commit and renders the accepted revision (`an_export_after_a_taken_back_write_renders_the_accepted_data`). Since `studio` a stem is a track's own output before its mixer channel, rendered as `m2 bounce <song> x --track <key>` renders it, the same bytes; stems and Export Audio share one slot, one at a time. A MIDI export writes no audio: it is the notes, written outside the song only ([FMT-21](#conversion)).

### Range render

**RENDER-4.** A range render takes `(range, preroll)` and is asked for explicitly (an agent's listen, `m2 render --preroll`). The engine starts from init at engine time 0 with the transport at `max(0, range.start − preroll)`. It renders the preroll, then returns `range`. It uses live jump semantics (no note chasing). It is built in step 3a.
- *Why:* an agent must be able to "listen from bar 64" without minutes of replay.
- *Today:* built (`Render::range` in `crates/editor/src/render.rs`, `m2 render --preroll`). `m2 render --from` without `--preroll` replays from 0, which stays the exact way to render a section. Since `ag-listen` an agent's listen renders this way, with 2 bars of preroll ([AGENT-7](#agents)).

**RENDER-5.** A pure range render MUST be deterministic given the project files, `range` and `preroll`; an impure one reports `reproducible: false`, as bounce does. Its result MUST say whether it is exact: it is exact only when `range.start − preroll ≤ 0`, and approximate in its tails otherwise. An approximate result MUST NOT be written to `assets/` or placed on a lane.
- *Why:* agents need to know when they hear the export and when they hear an approximation, and an approximation must never become part of the song.
- *Today:* matches: a range render gives the same bits every time and says whether it is exact (`a_range_render_is_the_same_every_time_and_says_whether_it_is_exact` in `crates/editor/src/render.rs`), and an approximate one is never written into `assets/` (`a_range_render_says_it_is_approximate_and_never_goes_into_assets`, `apps/m2/tests/range.rs`).

### Live engine and its two clocks

Live playback may differ from export after a seek, a loop wrap or an edit (owner decision 1).

**RENDER-6.** Live playback MUST be a persistent engine with two clocks:
- The **engine clock** is monotonic. It drives the 128-sample block grid, feedback rings, voice age and kernel and plugin time such as CLAP `steady_time`.
- The **transport clock** is the song position. It may jump at any frame. It drives lanes, `time.beats`, `time.seconds`, `time.playing`, note scheduling and the transport plugins see.
- *Why:* with one clock, every seek or loop wrap would reset every reverb, delay and plugin.
- *Today:* matches since step 3a (the module doc of `core/runtime/src/lib.rs`). The engine clock (`Engine.clock` in `core/runtime/src/engine.rs`) is each block's `engine` frame: it fixes the block grid, feedback rings read and write at the engine clock modulo a block, a voice's note ends at the engine sample of its Off, and a plugin's `steady_time` is the engine clock at its call. The transport (`Transport` in `core/runtime/src/transport.rs`) is an integer sample position; each block is one or two runs of it, which drive lanes, notes, `time` and what plugins are told ([HOST-6](#pluginhost)). Offline both start at 0 and stay equal. An activation hands both clocks to the new plan ([RENDER-25](#plans-data-and-arrangement-swaps)). Tested by `a_jump_never_resets_a_kernel` and `stopped_lanes_and_time_hold_while_kernels_run` (`core/runtime/src/tests/live.rs`) and `plugins_are_told_the_transport` (`steady_time` against the engine clock). Since the `sounds` merge (std 0.2), std's effects and drums no longer follow the engine clock, as the owner chose (2026-10-15): chorus, phaser and tremolo step their LFOs only where `time.playing` is 1 (`Phase` in `std/kernels-src/src/fx.rs`), the bitcrusher puts its sample clock back at Play to where it was at Stop (`Bitcrush`; it runs on while stopped, so a tail is still crushed), and a drum takes its noise seed from its lane's hit count (`std:hitcount`), never from samples processed. So a song played after the engine sat stopped is the export when its sound comes from std and it has no free-running source at the top level (below; [RENDER-33](#the-bit-identity-test)). Stop, then Play in place, carries the wobble on as if never stopped; the chorus's delay lines and the phaser's filters hold the tail, and the bitcrusher a sample of it. Stop, back to bar 1 (Enter or Home), then Play is a seek: the LFOs and the hit counts carry on from where they stopped, so that play may differ from the export, as this section allows (decided by the lead on 2026-10-16 as the default; the owner may still change it: HANDOFF's open owner decisions). Free-running sources at the top level, std's oscillators and `noise`, still run on the engine clock: one that waited would hold a constant value, a DC offset.

**RENDER-6a.** The engine MUST keep processing while the device is open, playing or not. Stop is a transport event: every sounding note gets an Off, the transport freezes, control lanes hold their value at the stop position, `time.beats` stops and `time.playing` is 0. Kernels and plugins keep running on that input, so tails decay. Audition and live input work while stopped. The engine MAY idle only once every kernel reports `!active` and the output has been silent for 8 blocks; any message wakes it. Except while held for an export with hosted plugins ([RENDER-1](#offline)): the engine then processes nothing, and resumes where it held.
- *Why:* an engine that pauses on stop freezes delay and reverb tails mid-echo and brings them back as a ghost at the next play, from a different position.
- *Today:* matches since step 3a. Stopped, the engine keeps processing: the transport, control lanes and `time` hold at the stop position while kernels run on (`stopped_lanes_and_time_hold_while_kernels_run` in `core/runtime/src/tests/live.rs`), and tails decay (`tails_decay_after_stop` in `core/play/tests/live.rs`, G7). It idles only when stopped, after 8 silent blocks, when nothing can sound (`Renderer::can_idle`, `the_engine_idles_only_when_nothing_can_sound`), and any message wakes it. Since step 3b, a renderer that hosts plugins may idle too, once every top-level plugin's last `process` returned sleep, or "continue if not quiet" with a quiet output (`Renderer::can_idle`); a plugin's `request_process` wakes the idle engine at the next block line (`Renderer::woken`). Surge XT and Airwindows never sleep, so `examples/with-plugins` never idles (measured by the gates' G7). Since step 7 a hold (`Msg::Hold`, `core/runtime/src/engine.rs`) fades the output out over 5 ms, then renders nothing: the engine clock and the transport stand still and its kernels and plugins aren't called; a resume fades back in where it held, a tape pause (`a_hold_freezes_and_resumes_without_a_step`, `core/play/tests/live.rs`). While held, an activation and a panic wait for the resume, a lane swap applies, transport commands apply to the frozen transport, and cell writes wait in their slots.

**RENDER-7.** `time.beats`, `time.seconds` and `time.playing` MUST be transport time, never engine time.
- *Why:* songs describe music in song time.
- *Today:* matches: each frame's beat and second come from the block's transport runs through the tempo map (`Renderer::block` in `core/runtime/src/lib.rs`), never from the engine clock; stopped, they hold (`stopped_lanes_and_time_hold_while_kernels_run`). Since the `sounds` merge `time.playing` (`TimeOf::Playing` in `core/ir/src/lib.rs`, unit `unit01`) is 1 on each frame where the transport moves and 0 where it is stopped, from the same runs (`Run.moving`), as plugins are told ([HOST-6](#pluginhost)): it is the kernels' twin of the playing flag. It stays 1 through a seek and a mid-block loop wrap, and is 0 from the stop's frame (`time_playing_follows_the_transport` in `core/runtime/src/tests/live.rs`). It is filled only for a node that reads it; offline every frame plays, so it is 1 throughout. In code, `time` from `@m2/core` and `ctx.time` are one object; read inside a component call, it makes one node per call, in that call's frame ([WRITE-8](#expectations-and-the-delta)).

**RENDER-8.** Live playback MUST NOT replay from sample 0, for any reason.
- *Why:* replay costs 20.5–24.9 s per seek, note edit, effect add or undo at 1:00 into a 16-track song, and 84.7 s at 50 tracks (measured 2026-10-12). Playback also jumps back by the rebuild time.
- *Today:* matches: live playback never replays. A seek, a loop wrap and a stop are transport events ([RENDER-9](#seek-and-loop)). An edit or an undo reaches the engine as a cell write, a lane swap, or an activation that keeps every instance that continues ([RENDER-25](#plans-data-and-arrangement-swaps)). A project loaded into a new player, a rate change and a refused activation's recovery start a new engine from init at the playhead, by moving its transport there, not by playing up to it (`Open::load` and `Way::Replace` in `crates/server/src/graph/actor.rs`, `Player::replace` in `core/play/src/lib.rs`). Only offline renders replay: `Renderer::seek` (`m2 render --from`).

### Seek and loop

**RENDER-9.** A seek or a loop wrap MUST be a transport event, never a DSP reset. At the jump frame:
1. every sounding note a lane scheduled gets a note-off, and its voice goes into release (a live note keeps sounding, [MON-7](#monitoring-overlays-and-mute-in-song));
2. note cursors are re-sought by binary search;
3. plugins get a transport event at the jump frame, with the new position, the playing and loop flags, and the meter from the arrangement;
4. kernels, feedback rings and plugin instances keep their state, so tails ring through;
5. each audio lane crossfades over 5 ms from its old position to its new one.

Nothing else is declicked at a jump: synth and plugin output ends through note-offs and releases, and control lanes step to their new value, as in other DAWs. This deliberately narrows the plan's 5 ms declick of the whole output, which would also dull every loop's downbeat.
- *Why:* a 4-bar loop must keep its reverb tails for as long as it runs.
- *Today:* matches since step 3a. A transport command lands at the next block line (`Transport::apply`), and a block is split into runs where it jumps (`Runs`, `Jump` in `core/runtime/src/transport.rs`). At a seek, a stop or a wrap, each notes lane's scheduler sends an Off for every note it holds, at the jump's frame, and finds its place in the new position's notes by binary search (`NoteScheduler::run` in `core/runtime/src/sched.rs`; `a_jump_ends_notes_and_never_chases`). A plugin is called once per run, and each call is told its own run's position, playing flag, loop, tempo, meter and bar ([HOST-6](#pluginhost)). Nothing is reset: a delay's echoes ring through a seek as if nothing happened (`a_jump_never_resets_a_kernel` in `core/runtime/src/tests/live.rs`), and a 4-bar loop at 127 bpm keeps its tails for 10 minutes (`a_loop_keeps_its_tails_for_10_minutes` in `core/play/tests/live.rs`, G2). Each audio lane crossfades over 5 ms from its old position to its new one at a seek or a wrap, fades out on stop, and fades in on play from inside a clip (`core/runtime/src/audio.rs`; `an_audio_lane_crossfades_at_a_wrap`, and G6's `audio_lanes_never_click`). While held for an export, a seek moves the frozen transport (`a_seek_while_held_is_where_playback_is`). Offline, `Renderer::seek` still resets and replays from 0, as [RENDER-1](#offline) defines a render from a point.

**RENDER-9a.** The transport MUST be an integer sample position; beats are derived from it through the tempo map. A loop wrap computes the loop end's frame from its absolute beat, `round(samples_at(beat))`, at every wrap, and never by adding up loop lengths. A wrap falls at that frame, mid-block if need be.
- *Why:* at 127 bpm a 4-bar loop is 362,834.6 samples at 48 kHz; rounding and accumulating each wrap drifts notes and audio lanes off the grid within the 10-minute loop gate.
- *Today:* matches since step 3a. The transport's position is a `u64` sample count (`Transport` in `core/runtime/src/transport.rs`), and beats come from it through the tempo map. A loop's start and end frames come from their beats by the notes' own formula (`frame_of`), when the loop is set and again whenever the tempo map or the rate changes (`Transport::retime`), never by adding up laps; a wrap falls at the end frame, mid-block when it must, so a block is at most two runs. A loop shorter than two blocks, or than an audio lane's 5 ms crossfade, is refused (`Transport::loop_refused`). Since 8b.2 a tempo edit from the song bar that makes the loop too short turns it off and says so (`loop_off`, the toast's "Loop turned off"), judged in the tempo map the edit made, also while an export holds playback and the engine still plays the one before (`a_tempo_too_fast_for_the_loop_turns_it_off`, `during_an_export_a_tempo_edit_turns_the_loop_off_and_the_playhead_keeps_the_tempo_heard`). Setting a loop is still judged in the tempo the engine plays, so during a hold a loop the edit made too short can be set, and the engine refuses it at the switch. Tested by `wraps_fall_on_their_frame` and `a_loop_at_127_bpm_runs_10_minutes_without_drift` (`transport.rs`), and recorded on p16 by `p16_loops_ten_minutes_without_drift` (`core/play/tests/live.rs`).

**RENDER-10.** Engine state MUST be reset only: on panic; on stop, when the user chose "reset on stop"; at the start of an offline or range render; and per voice, when the `poly` allocator retriggers a voice ([RENDER-16](#voices)). An instance that a plan activation doesn't match starts from init ([RENDER-25](#plans-data-and-arrangement-swaps)); that is a new instance, not a reset of kept state. A plan activation never resets a kept instance, except one whose output went non-finite or that trapped ([RENDER-25](#plans-data-and-arrangement-swaps)).
- *Why:* any other reset cuts tails the user is listening to.
- *Today:* since step 7 an activation moves every instance it keeps into the new plan untouched (`Renderer::take_over` in `core/runtime/src/plan.rs`); a box marked as gone non-finite or trapped is reset as it moves, and counted in the plan's `Stats`, not in `Renderer::resets`, which counts a panic's resets and an offline replay's. A reused voice is reset (`PolyNode::process` in `core/runtime/src/lib.rs`), unless its poly says `retrigger: false` (track pm), which keeps it, its gate falling for one sample. Since 8b.2 Stop All Sound (⌘., `POST /api/graph/panic`) calls the panic; with no player it does nothing, so it never opens the audio device (`panic_ends_every_note_and_never_touches_the_session`). One sent while an export holds playback applies at the resume, after the plugins the export lent are told to start again the notes the song holds ([RENDER-1](#offline)), so it wins: nothing sounds after the export (`stop_all_sound_during_an_export_stays_silent_after_it`). Since 8a's live half, a sound whose reset fails in a panic stops alone, reported and told, and the panic resets the rest (`Graph::panic`, [RENDER-25](#plans-data-and-arrangement-swaps)): Stop All Sound never stops the song.

**RENDER-11.** Notes already sounding at a seek target are not chased (deferred). After a jump, only notes that start at or after the new position sound.
- *Why:* chasing needs preroll per instrument; it waits for its trigger.
- *Today:* matches since step 3a: after a jump only the notes that start at or after the new position sound (`NoteScheduler::run`; `a_jump_ends_notes_and_never_chases` in `core/runtime/src/sched.rs`). Offline, a render from a point replays from 0 (`Renderer::seek`), so its held notes sound as in the export.

### Events

**RENDER-12.** Notes MUST reach instruments as per-block, sample-stamped event buffers. A notes lane node is a scheduler that fills its buffer each block from the transport. `poly` and plugins consume buffers. `Events<Note>` is the view of a buffer that holds only note events.
- *Why:* live input, audition, code-generated patterns and event-producing kernels all plug into the same buffers.
- *Today:* matches since step 3a. Each notes lane is a scheduler (`NoteScheduler` in `core/runtime/src/sched.rs`) that fills its lane's event buffer (`Events` in `core/runtime/src/event.rs`, sized when the plan is prepared) from the block's transport runs, before the graph runs. `poly` and plugin nodes read that buffer; a plugin node turns it into the host's CLAP-shaped list without allocating (`plugin_events` in `core/runtime/src/lib.rs`). There is no separate `Events<Note>` type: the one buffer holds `Event`s: notes, and since track pm a lane's pitch wheel and mod wheel from live input (`Midi`, [RENDER-14](#events)).

**RENDER-13.** Voices MUST gate on note-on and note-off events, not on comparing the transport with note times.
- *Why:* a jump must end notes through note-offs, and live notes have no end time when they start.
- *Today:* matches since step 3a: a voice's gate opens at its note's On and closes at the engine sample of its Off (`VoiceNote` and `PolyNode::process` in `core/runtime/src/lib.rs`), so a jump ends a voice's note only through the Off its scheduler sends ([RENDER-9](#seek-and-loop)). Plugins get the same Ons and Offs, as CLAP note events.

**RENDER-14.** The event shape is a tagged enum, reserved whole now and built only as far as notes:

```rust
enum Event {
    On  { frame: u32, id: u32, pitch: f32, velocity: f32, from_pitch: Option<f32> },  // from_pitch: glide and legato
    Off { frame: u32, id: u32, velocity: f32 },                                         // release velocity
    NoteExpression { frame: u32, id: u32, kind: Expression, value: f32 },             // MPE, per-note pitch, pressure, timbre (later)
    Param { frame: u32, id: u32, value: f32 },                                         // sample-accurate parameter events (later)
    Midi  { frame: u32, bytes: [u8; 3] },                                              // CC, pitch bend, sustain from MIDI input (later)
}
```

Live input and audition merge into a lane's buffer at the block line: at the block's first frame, before the transport's notes, Offs first ([MON-7](#monitoring-overlays-and-mute-in-song)). The IR reserves a `const-events` op for events generated by code ([ABI-4](#kernel-abi-reservations)).
- *Why:* one shape for every source of notes, and room for expression, controllers and parameter events without bumping the kernel ABI or the IR event type again.
- *Today:* matches since step 3a: `Event` in `core/runtime/src/event.rs` is this enum, whole, with `Expression` naming `Pitch`, `Pressure` and `Timbre`. `On` and `Off` are made, with `from_pitch` always `None`: a poly's allocator slides by its own `glide` ([RENDER-16](#voices)). At one frame Offs come before Ons, so a key can be struck again where it was let go. Since track pm's slice 2 `Midi` is made too, from live input only: a lane's pitch wheel and mod wheel (pitch bend, CC 1; CC 121 puts both back at rest). Each notes lane keeps the latest of each in a fixed slot (`LiveEv::Midi`, `NoteScheduler::live`) and sends it at the next block's first frame, before every note there, on channel 1 whatever channel moved it (`a_live_bend_merges_before_the_notes`). A source's release or a panic puts back at rest what it moved; a new plan's consumers, and plugins an export lent, hear the lane's wheels again. A poly keeps them in its pool and resets them with it: each voice's pitch is its note's (and its slide) plus the bend × 2 semitones, nothing added when centred, so no bit changes; the mod wheel is a note field, `modWheel` (`NoteField::ModWheel`, `Note.modWheel` in `@m2/core` 1.4.0), 0 to 1 (`a_bend_reaches_every_voice_in_its_lane`, `the_mod_wheel_is_a_note_field`, `bend_and_wheel_reset_with_the_pool` in `core/runtime/src/tests/poly.rs`). A plugin gets them as `CLAP_EVENT_MIDI` (`m2_plugins::Event::Midi`, `a_plugin_hears_the_bend_as_midi`). std 0.3.0's polysynth deepens its vibrato with the wheel, by up to half a semitone. The bend steps once a block, with no smoothing. Recording the wheels into clips, and the other kinds (`NoteExpression`, `Param`, other controllers), aren't built ([Deferred](#deferred-with-triggers)). Since 8b.6 audition's live notes merge at frame 0 (`NoteScheduler::run`); a note struck and let go at one block line sends its On then and its Off at the next block, as at one frame its Off would sort first and leave it on (`a_key_struck_and_let_go_at_once_still_ends`). MIDI keyboards are a live source ([MON-7](#monitoring-overlays-and-mute-in-song)).

**RENDER-15.** Code that builds the graph MUST NOT read timeline data.
- *Why:* data edits must never need a compile.
- *Today:* matches. Lanes are graph inputs (`ctxFor` in `core/script/sdk/core.ts`).

### Voices

**RENDER-16.** The `poly` op MUST carry an allocator:

```ts
poly(notes, voice, { voices: 16, mode: "poly", retrigger: true, steal: "oldest", glide: 0 })
//                               "poly" | "mono" | "legato"   "oldest" | "quietest" | "none"   seconds
```

The default MUST reproduce today's output bit for bit. A `poly` is kept or replaced whole; a kept pool's voices keep their slots, which are their RuntimeIds. The op shape is fixed now; its `mode`, `retrigger`, `steal` and `glide` options are built (track pm).
- *Why:* with one fixed reset-on-reuse policy, a song can't choose how a voice is stolen or started again, and mono, legato and glide need a kernel made for them (std's `mono()`, below) instead of working on any voice graph. Voices are most of a song's instances; moving each pool as one box keeps an activation's work in proportion to the top-level graph.
- *Today:* built, the whole op, on any voice graph, since track pm (`@m2/core` 1.4.0, std 0.3.0). The SDK checks the options and writes only those that differ from the default, so a poly without them keeps its IR byte for byte (`default_options_leave_the_ir_as_today`); an unknown value, `voices` with `"mono"` or `"legato"`, or a glide that isn't a time is said in words. In the IR, `Op::Poly` carries `alloc` (`mode`, `retrigger`, `steal`, each left out when default) and `glide`, a ref to a node outside the voice, ordered before the poly (`core/ir/src/lib.rs`, `check.rs`). The allocator is `core/runtime/src/poly.rs`: a free voice, else the oldest, the quietest (the lowest peak over its last block, a tie to the oldest) or none; `retrigger: false` keeps a voice given a new note, its gate falling for one sample if it was open. `"mono"` and `"legato"` have one voice, fed per-sample pitch, gate and velocity from fixed buffers in its pool, which its note fields read; std's mono rules, kept by note id: the latest note wins, a legato note slides over the glide, a note let go brings back the newest one still held (note memory 8 deep), notes that start on one sample count as one, the last; once started, the voice never sleeps. The glide is read where a slide starts, 0 to 10 s; a knob, so turning it is a cell write that keeps the pool. A pool's shape includes `alloc`, not the glide; a pool moves whole, its notes held and its slide with it (`a_mono_pool_moves_whole_across_an_activation`). A voice is free again once its note has ended, its last block peaked below −80 dB and its kernels say they aren't active (`k_active`). A note whose Off lands on a block line is judged at that line, before its voice runs the block, so a note struck on the same line takes that voice instead of stealing a held one (`PolyNode::process` in `core/runtime/src/lib.rs`). So a kernel whose gate's fall starts a sound says it is active while its gate is held and that sound is still to come, as std's `regions` does for a release region ([PKG-16](#component-descriptors), `a_release_region_sounds_when_its_note_ends_on_a_block_line` in `core/play/tests/bit_identity.rs`). Tested on a probe voice that plays its note fields (`core/runtime/src/tests/poly.rs`). Since step 7 a poly's voices are one box (`VoicePool`), which an activation moves whole into a poly of the same shape: its voices, channels, options and voice graph, without its cells' values, origins and names (a plain number written inside the voice is part of it); any other poly is new. std's `mono()` (`std/src/synth.ts`) is `poly(notes, voice, { mode: "legato", retrigger: false, glide })`, and so is any std synth or FM sound given `voices: 1`; `synth.lead`, `synth.sub` and `synth.bass` start that way. `std:mono` and `std:notecv`, which did this at package level since the `sounds` merge, are gone. On velocities a keyboard sends (k/127, which `std:mono` rounded every velocity to) the new path renders the old bits (`mono_sounds_keep_their_bits_on_keyboard_velocities`; `examples/sounds` with its velocities rounded so too); off them a note keeps its own velocity, which changed two lines of `examples/sounds`' renders, recorded under std 0.3.0 (FMT-3). The Device card of an instrument says how it plays, in plain words (`crates/editor/src/voices.rs`), read-only. Since track ab a pool's kernels share one sandbox a package, held in its `VoicePool` ([RENDER-32](#engine-shape-step-7)).

### Latency

**RENDER-17.** The engine MUST own latency, per instance. It measures it after state is loaded, and again when a plugin reports a change.
- Offline measures the total `L` once, after init and state load, and keeps it for the whole render. A change reported mid-render raises the Warning `latency.changed` and takes effect only in the next render.
- Live applies a new latency only at a plan activation, through `deactivate` and `activate` (which CLAP's `request_restart` needs anyway), with the crossfade of [RENDER-25a](#plans-data-and-arrangement-swaps).
- *Why:* a plugin's latency often depends on its loaded preset, and a delay plan that changes mid-render would make the export depend on when the change arrived.
- *Today:* matches since ph-3 (step 9). Each plugin instance keeps its latency as last measured (`PluginInstance::latency` in `core/runtime/src/plugins.rs`): when it is activated, after a state load into it, when it says it changed (`HostEvent::Latency`, kept by the session), and when an export gives it back. A kernel's is its package's declaration (`KernelSig.latency`). A plan compensates what each had when it was prepared (`PluginAt.latency`); a load that changes a kept plugin's latency is listed (`Stats::relatent`), and the session activates again at once to compensate the new one, with RENDER-25a's crossfade (`Open.relatent` in `crates/server/src/graph/actor.rs`). A plugin that asks for a restart, CLAP's way to change its latency while active, is new at that activation ([HOST-5](#pluginhost)), and its activation measures it. An offline render measures `L` once, as it is built. When it is written, each plugin is asked its latency again: one that changed makes `latency.changed` on the render, an export's problem or `m2 render`'s warning (`Renderer::latency_moved`, `Rendered::latency_changed` in `crates/editor/src/render.rs`). Tested by `a_latency_change_applies_at_the_next_activation` (`core/runtime/src/tests/live/keep/latency.rs`) and `a_latency_change_during_an_export_is_said_and_compensated_after_it` (`crates/server/src/graph/tests.rs`), with the probe's `latency-<n>` and `relatency-<m>-at-<k>` tags.

**RENDER-18.** The planner MUST insert compensating delays so every path into a node arrives aligned. Lanes, notes and time read downstream of a latent node MUST be delivered delayed by the same amount.
- *Why:* a lookahead limiter or pitch corrector must not move a track off the beat.
- *Today:* matches since ph-3 (step 9). `core/runtime/src/latency.rs` gives each node its offset, the latest arrival over its inputs, and a delay on every other input for the difference; lanes, notes and time are inputs like any other. With none, the checked graph plays as it is, so a song with no latency keeps its plan and its bits (`no_fixture_gets_a_delay`, `core/play/tests/bit_identity.rs`). Otherwise the graph that plays has each delay as a node after the checked ones (`FOp::Delay` for audio and control, `FOp::NoteDelay` for notes; `PlanInfo.played`), run right before its consumer, which reads it; an overlay on a delayed input reads the delay. A delay's line is a box (`core/runtime/src/delay.rs`): a ring indexed by the engine clock, a control's holding its first value before it starts, a note delay's sized from its lane's room; it allocates nothing on the audio thread. An activation keeps a delay when its consumer continues and the input and length are the same (`continue_delays`), moving the line with what is in flight in it; a new length is a new node, crossfaded as any changed edge is (RENDER-25a), and a kept consumer whose note delay doesn't move lets go of its notes (RENDER-26a). A plugin late by its offset is told the transport that much earlier ([HOST-6](#pluginhost)). Latency inside a `poly` voice stays refused at check time. Tested by the planner's tests (`core/runtime/src/tests/latency.rs`: a chain, two paths, a diamond, a loop, none), `a_latent_plugin_stays_aligned_offline`, `lanes_notes_and_time_arrive_late_by_the_offset`, `a_no_op_activation_keeps_every_delay` and `delays_allocate_nothing_on_the_audio_thread` (`core/runtime/src/tests/live/keep/latency.rs`).

**RENDER-19.** The song's output is late by its total latency `L`. Offline renders MUST drop the first `L` samples and render `L` more at the end, so sample 0 of an export is song time 0 and the song's last `L` samples are kept. Live playback reports the transport position minus `L`.
- *Why:* exports and the playhead must line up with the timeline, and an export must not lose its ending.
- *Today:* matches since ph-3 (step 9). `L` is the output's arrival (`Renderer::latency`). An offline render drops its first `L` frames, a seek's replay and a range render's preroll too (`Renderer::process_on`), and renders on past the end by as much. The engine's status says the position heard: the transport's at the engine sample `L` frames before, from the transport's runs the renderer remembers (`Engine::status`, `Renderer::position_at`). Tested by `a_latent_plugin_stays_aligned_offline` (a sine through the probe 100 frames late against itself inverted sums to exactly 0, and the late path alone is the sine from its first sample to its last) and `a_latent_plugin_stays_aligned_live`.

**RENDER-20.** Latency inside a feedback loop MUST raise the `latency.in-feedback` Warning. The loop plays with the extra delay.
- *Why:* a loop's delay can't be compensated.
- *Today:* matches since ph-3 (step 9). A feedback's input edge is cut, so its output arrives at 0. The nodes between a feedback's output and its input that add latency, or a delay whose source is on the loop too, are listed (`Latency.in_feedback`), and the session says so in plain words, naming each, when the list changes (`latency_on_a_feedback_loop_is_named`).

**RENDER-21.** Until compensation ships, the check-time refusal of nonzero latency stays. It is removed in the same change that ships compensation.
- *Why:* before then, latent plugins would play misaligned.
- *Today:* matches: removed in ph-3 (step 9) with compensation, and with it `plugin.latency-uncompensated`. Latency inside a `poly` voice is still refused at check time, as nothing compensates it there (`core/ir/src/check.rs`; a plugin's from `describe_plugin`, measured at its default state).

### Cells

**RENDER-22.** An editable constant MUST be a cell. A cell is written into kernel input memory (or a constant buffer) once when its plan activates, and again only on `SetCell`. Previews, monitoring overlays and value commits target cells. None of them rebuilds.
- *Why:* thousands of knobs must cost nothing per block, and a knob turn must be heard in the next block.
- *Today:* matches for previews since step 5 (`core/runtime/src/cells.rs`). Every const with an origin that a renderer plays is a cell. Its value is written into its node's buffer, and from there into each kernel input it feeds once, at the kernel's first call, and again only when the cell's slot brings a new value, so a steady cell costs nothing per block. A value written while playing is heard at the renderer's next block line with no rebuild. A lease's update is a cell write to the cells of its value in the renderer that plays (`Player::set_cells`), and a renderer built while a lease lives gets its value in those cells before it is sent (`Renderer::set_cells`, by the renderer's `CellMap`). A committed value reaches the engine as a cell write when only constant values changed (`value_writes` in `crates/server/src/graph/actor.rs`), an inherited control's first edit included; with other changes, in an activation that keeps every instance and applies each changed continued cell as a `SetCell` from its old value would be ([RENDER-22a](#cells), `Cells::carry` in `core/runtime/src/cells.rs`). Monitoring overlays come in step 8.

**RENDER-22a.** A `SetCell` on a control declared `smooth` ([PKG-14](#component-descriptors)) MUST ramp linearly from the old value to the new over one block: the host writes the ramp into the cell's buffer, then the constant. A `SetCell` on an overlay gain cell ([MON-1](#monitoring-overlays-and-mute-in-song)) MUST glide: the overlay op moves its gain toward the new value by `1 / round(0.010 × rate)` a frame, so a full mute or unmute takes 10 ms, and the host writes no ramp. Other cells step, and a kernel that needs smoothing does its own. Offline renders never receive `SetCell`, so they are unaffected. A glide in flight when an activation lands ends there as a step of what is left of it: the new plan starts at the value it was given.
- *Why:* a mute toggle or a gain jump written as a step clicks. A one-block ramp is too short for a mute on a low note by the triangle bound: at 50 Hz a sine moves 0.0065 a sample, gate 8 allows 0.0131, and a 128-frame ramp adds up to 0.0078; a 10 ms glide adds 0.0021.
- *Today:* matches since step 5 for declared controls: a cell whose origin names a control declared smooth (`Origin.control.smooth`) ramps from its old value to the new over the next block, ending exactly on the new value, and the others step (`Cells::apply` in `core/runtime/src/cells.rs`). A ramp is applied at a block line like a step, so it is the same live and offline. Since 8b.4 an overlay cell's value goes to its overlay node's target, and the node glides there (`Exec::Overlay`, `core/runtime/src/overlay.rs`; `an_overlay_glides_over_ten_milliseconds`). Gate 8's click test passes at 440 Hz and 50 Hz with device buffers of 128, 37 and 512 frames (`core/play/tests/mute.rs`). The two terms of the bound above peak at different phases, so a one-block ramp would pass it too, and only a step fails it: the glide's length is pinned by the runtime test.

**RENDER-23.** Cells are addressed by a `CellId` from the active plan, mapped from a ControlId, or from a module key for mute and solo. A CellId MUST NOT outlive its plan.
- *Why:* build-local numbers never outlive their build ([ID-12](#derived-identity)).
- *Today:* since step 5 a cell is its index in its renderer's cell table (`CellId`), written through that renderer's slots (`CellTarget`, `Renderer::cell_slots`), so it can't reach another renderer's cells. Since 8b.4 each overlay site has a cell, numbered after every value cell, so value cells keep their ids; `PlanInfo.overlays` maps each site to its track's key, the module that reads it, and its cell. A plan not yet sent takes its overlays' values with `Renderer::set_overlays`, which writes no slot. A lease aims at a `CellTarget`: the server maps its control's origin to `CellId`s by the `CellMap` of the renderer that plays, and maps again when that renderer takes a value edit or another renderer replaces it (`PlaysView::target_of` in `crates/server/src/graph/lease.rs`). Cells stay per plan. Leases are re-aimed at each activation, under the control plane's lock in the step that sends it; a continued cell whose value changed is applied at activation as a `SetCell` would be ([RENDER-22a](#cells)), after the old plan's ramps end (`Cells::end_ramps`), so a smooth control ramps through a mixed edit and a ramp in flight never moves into the new plan with its voices.

**RENDER-24.** Kernels MUST treat input memory as read-only ([ABI-1](#kernel-abi-reservations)).
- *Why:* a cell is written once; a kernel that wrote its input would corrupt it.
- *Today:* checked since step 5, as [ABI-1](#kernel-abi-reservations) says.

### Plans, data and arrangement swaps

**RENDER-25.** Plans MUST be prepared off the audio thread and activated at a block boundary. Activation keeps every instance whose RuntimeId and shape match. The shape is the kernel id, the content hash of the kernel's wasm module, the channel counts, the state ABI and the settings; for a hosted plugin, its plugin id and plugin version. The others start from init. Replaced objects are retired off the audio thread. Instances are matched when the plan is prepared, off the audio thread, against the plan the engine will be playing; `Activate` carries the moves and names that plan. The audio thread checks that plan's id and each move's shape before moving anything, and refuses the whole activation on a mismatch. A kernel's module hash is the SHA-256 of its package's wasm module, which also fixes its state ABI. Its settings (kernel ABI v1, [ABI-2](#kernel-abi-reservations)) are its channel count per port, the hash of each asset it reads, and its values. A `poly`'s shape is its voices, channels and voice-graph structure without cell values. An instance whose output was non-finite in its last block, or that trapped, is reset as it moves. Live, a node that fails (a kernel's trap, a voice's, a kernel that asks for more than its memory) goes silent alone and stays silent until the next activation or a panic resets it; the rest of the plan plays on, and the app names the sound and its track. Offline the render fails, naming the node ([RENDER-1](#offline)).
- Preparing a plan includes creating, loading and activating every new plugin instance on the plugin main thread. The old plan keeps playing until all of them are ready, and the UI shows "loading <plugin>" meanwhile. The code plane's 200 ms budget doesn't count plugin loads.
- *Why:* an edit must not cut the tails of what it didn't touch. A rebuilt package that keeps a kernel's id but changes its memory layout must never inherit old memory, and a sample-based instrument can take seconds to load.
- *Today:* matches since step 7. The server prepares each plan against the plan it sent last (`Renderer::prepare` with `Basis::Keep`, `core/runtime/src/lib.rs`): the session names which nodes continue (`m2_session::tx::continues`: keyed plugins by module and state path, every other node by its name in step 6's delta, its op and its occurrence counts, [ID-13](#derived-identity), [ID-14](#derived-identity)), and the runtime keeps a continuing node only when its `Shape` matches (`core/runtime/src/keep.rs`), so every instance of an untouched file, and of each call of an edited file whose `(` no change touched, a wrapped instrument included, is kept. New plugin instances are created and activated on the plugin main thread while the old plan plays; a prepare that fails changes nothing that plays. At the block line the audio thread verifies every move (the plan's id, each box filled and of its stored shape hash, each new slot empty), then makes them all by `mem::swap` with no allocation (`Renderer::take_over` in `core/runtime/src/plan.rs`): boxes (a kernel outside an instrument with its sandbox, a voice pool with its voices' sandboxes, a plugin instance, a feedback ring; [RENDER-32](#engine-shape-step-7)), lanes, the transport, the engine clock and note ids, cell values. A refused activation is retired unplayed, counted (`Engine::refused_activations`) and stamped (`Applied::refused`), and the server's next settle replaces the engine at the same beat (`Way::Replace`). Every box nothing moved leaves with the old renderer, which is retired off the audio thread once the stash has room for three retirements, checked before the activation applies; one that can't be retired is forgotten and counted (`Engine::leaked`), never dropped there. Live, each top-level box whose output held a non-finite sample in its last block, or that trapped, is marked (`Graph::poisoned`), and reset as it moves. Tested by `core/runtime/src/tests/live/keep.rs` (G7d's `a_rebuilt_kernel_module_starts_from_init` among them), G7b (`m2-gates tails`) and G7c (`core/play/tests/bit_identity.rs`). Since 8a's live half (its gaps lane), a node that fails live stops alone; before, the whole plan went silent and the transport stopped. Live means played by the engine, which marks each renderer it plays (`Renderer.live`, set by `Engine::with` and at an activation); every other render fails, naming the node, an export (not strict) too. Its box plays silence and is never called again, what reads it reads silence, and the audio thread reports it, `sound.stopped` (`run_part` in `core/runtime/src/lib.rs`). Since track ex's slice 1 every fault found while sound is made takes one path, the fault ring (`core/runtime/src/fault.rs`): a part reports a `Fault` (a code, the plan's id, the part as a number, 24 bytes at most) into a bounded ring of 256 slots that never allocates, locks or waits (Vyukov's design; a full ring counts what it lost, `a_fault_report_never_allocates_locks_or_waits`, `a_full_ring_counts_what_it_lost`). There is one live ring per app, in the server's `Inner`; each live plan reports to it through the live `audio::Cache` it is built with, and an offline render has its own. The next activation resets a stopped node as it moves it, and so does a panic. A node whose reset fails in a panic stops alone too, reported, and the panic resets the rest (`Graph::panic`): Stop All Sound never stops the song. A kernel that asks for more than its sandbox's birth cap (256 MiB, [RENDER-32](#engine-shape-step-7)), or for any memory once its sandbox is sealed, traps at once and says it ran out of memory, naming the node and the cap (`Cap`, `OutOfMemory`; the refusal is taken as it is told, so a later crash of the same sound is told as a crash): live, in a build and in an export; the server's words for the computer running out of memory don't take it for theirs. The session reads the ring every 300 ms (`Graph::hear_faults`, `crates/server/src/graph/faults.rs`) and names each fault with the `Namer` of the plan it came from (`crates/editor/src/faults.rs`): what the sound is and its track, and that Transport ▸ Stop All Sound starts it again. A fault of the plan sent last is a problem (a `Diagnostic` with its track's key, DIAG-4), listed in the news and the state until the part plays again: a new plan, or Stop All Sound. A fault of the plan before is told, not listed, as its part plays again in the new plan; one of an older plan is dropped (`a_fault_from_a_plan_gone_is_never_named_by_the_new_one`). Each part is toasted once per code in the open song, by its words; if it stops again it is listed again, with no toast. Unlike a plugin that fails ([HOST-5](#pluginhost)), a kernel isn't started again on its own: its trap comes from its input, so the same input would trap again. Tested by `a_trapped_kernel_goes_silent_alone`, `a_stopped_kernel_starts_again_at_the_next_activation`, `stop_all_sound_starts_a_stopped_kernel_again`, `a_reset_that_traps_stops_its_sound_alone_and_the_panic_goes_on`, `a_trap_on_any_thread_count_gives_the_same_bits`, `a_trap_still_fails_an_export_and_names_the_node`, `a_kernel_over_its_memory_says_it_ran_out` and `a_crash_after_running_out_of_memory_is_told_as_a_crash` (`keep.rs`), `a_stopped_sound_is_a_problem_naming_it_and_its_track` and `a_problem_goes_when_its_part_plays_again` (`crates/server/src/graph/faults_tests.rs`), and the browser suite `sound-stops`, which plays a real crash: a copy of std whose noise kernel traps. Since track ab's slice 2 a kernel's shape holds its settings: the hash of each asset it reads and its values; a voice pool's holds the hashes its voices' kernels read. So a new file, or a changed file under the same name, makes the box new, and the same file keeps it (`a_new_sample_makes_the_box_new` in `core/runtime/src/tests.rs`). The revision records the hash of each asset a kernel reads (`DataSet::kernel_assets`); one that isn't in `assets/` is `asset.missing`, naming the file and the track, and its kernel reads silence (`a_missing_asset_is_named_and_silent`). Since track ex's slice 2 every part that finds a fault while sound is made reports it through the ring: a plan's build a file a kernel reads, or a clip's, that is there but can't be read or decoded (`asset.unreadable`, `audio::Cache`), a lane a chunk whose file can't be read (`Lane::fill_chunk`), and a stretch that failed (`stretch.failed`). A lane reports each once per plan, through the plan that plays it (`audio::Tell`), and tells a new plan that keeps it what it met, as its chunks stay silent. A plan's id is taken before its build, so its build reports as that plan; the session keeps a fault of a plan not sent yet until it is sent (`a_fault_from_a_plan_not_sent_yet_waits_for_it`). Tested by `a_streamed_file_that_cant_be_read_is_told_once`, `a_sampler_file_that_wont_decode_is_told_naming_it_and_its_track` and `a_stretch_that_fails_live_is_told_naming_the_clip` (`crates/server/src/graph/faults_tests.rs`). Since slice 3 the sound input reports there too ([RENDER-30a](#audio-lanes)), and the browser suite `ex-faults` sees a stopped sound in the status pill's popover, naming its track, toasted once.

**RENDER-25a.** An activation crossfades, linearly over 5 ms, every input of a kept node whose source changed, and the song output when its source changed.
