A new node's audio input from a node that plays on fades in from silence over 5 ms first, and the activation's crossfades wait for it, so that window is 10 ms. A replaced or removed node runs on in the new plan for that window as a ghost, its notes ended, reading what it read, then is retired. Nothing else fades; equal signals stay bit-identical. An edit that would make that window's graph cyclic (a reorder of kept nodes) replaces the nodes on the cycle.
- *Why:* switching from old to new output in one sample clicks on every structural edit made while playing; fading at module outputs would need kept instances processed twice, and fading only changed edges leaves an untouched track's tail bit for bit. A delay or reverb inserted on a sounding track would take a step into its memory and play it back as a click (its first echo, G6); its input fades in before anything crossfades to it, so a wrap that changes no sample (`vca(x, 1)`) still changes no bit.
- *Today:* matches since step 7 (`core/runtime/src/keep.rs`, `Exec::Fade` and `fade.rs`): a fade node reads the old source (a continuing node, a ghost or a constant; silence for a fade-in) and the new one and gives `xfade` of them, after `wait` frames for a crossfade that waits for fade-ins (`Plan.wait`), then copies the new side exactly, taking its version, so a steady cell behind it costs nothing per block. Ghosts are the old sources of the changed edges that don't continue, and what they read that doesn't; a ghost reads no notes, and a control or audio lane only ghosts read moves in for the window as a ghost lane. The window's order is Kahn's over the new nodes, ghosts and fades (feedback inputs aren't counted); a cycle found there demotes the continuing nodes on it and the plan is wired again. At the window's end (`Renderer::end_fade`) the ghosts, ghost lanes and replaced renders leave in `Retired::Nodes`, and the order without ghosts plays on. `LiveFade` in `core/play` crossfades a whole engine only where one replaces another (an open, a rate change, a refused activation's recovery).

**RENDER-26.** `SwapLane` MUST replace one lane's data in the running nodes at a block boundary, without touching any kernel. Step 3a uses it when the old and new `Flat` graphs are equal. From step 6 the Tx delta decides.
- *Why:* a note edit must be audible within 20 ms with no tails cut.
- *Today:* matches since step 6: the server takes the delta from the revision the playing renderer was built from (or took since) to the accepted one (or, while the Compare pill plays Before, to that revision, built in memory: [AGENT-3](#agents)), and swaps the lanes it lists when nothing structural, no state, asset or arrangement changed, and each notes lane fits its scheduler (`reach` in `crates/server/src/graph/actor.rs`); a change that never reached the engine (a build that failed) is never skipped by a later lane edit (`a_failed_build_then_a_lane_edit_rebuilds`). When a change's renderer doesn't build, the version before it plays on, and a notice says so in plain words, once for each revision, though the catch-up tries again every 300 ms (`a_change_that_cant_get_ready_to_play_says_so_once`); running out of memory is named as such there, and in a failed open or export, with wasmtime's words as the detail (`running_out_of_memory_is_said_plainly`). Since the G11 fix, a data Tx's swaps are sent as a preview between its pre-check and its journal ([PLANE-5](#planes-of-change)), and what plays is its candidate until the settle after the commit adopts the accepted revision, sending nothing, or swaps the accepted data back by the same delta from what plays (`reach_to`). Audio lanes and assets take [RENDER-27](#plans-data-and-arrangement-swaps)'s path, as does notes data that outgrows its scheduler (an activation grows it). Since 8b.4 a module muted or unmuted in the song is no structure there: what plays takes it as overlay cell writes ([MON-5](#monitoring-overlays-and-mute-in-song)).

**RENDER-26a.** Each notes-lane scheduler MUST own the set of notes it has turned on and not yet off: each with its runtime note id and its `(clip, pitch, start beat)`, where an anonymous clip is named by its start. Then:
- On `SwapLane` it compares that set with the new data. A sounding note that is gone, or whose new end is at or before the transport, gets an Off at the swap frame. One whose end moved is rescheduled. A new note that started before the transport is not chased.
- Each notes lane's scheduler persists across plans with its sounding notes, keyed by `LaneAddr`, and note ids are unique across the engine. An activation that replaces a note consumer, or keeps one whose notes input now reads another lane, first ends every note sounding into it.
- A stop, a seek or a wrap on an activation's block line is handled as on any block. An arrangement switch reconciles every scheduler with its new data, which recomputes each pending Off from its beat.
- A stop, a seek or a loop wrap sends an Off for every sounding note ([RENDER-9](#seek-and-loop), [RENDER-6a](#live-engine-and-its-two-clocks)).
- A live note isn't in the data: no swap, jump or reconcile touches it. It ends only by its Off, its source's release, a panic, or an activation that replaces its consumer ([MON-7](#monitoring-overlays-and-mute-in-song)).
- *Why:* a scheduler that takes its Offs only from the new data leaves a deleted or shortened note hanging forever.
- *Today:* matches since step 7. An activation moves each lane's scheduler into the new plan by `LaneAddr`, with new data when the data or the tempo map changed (a reconcile at its first block, as a swap), or into a bigger scheduler that takes over its sounding notes when the new data needs more room (`NoteScheduler::take_state`, `core/runtime/src/sched.rs`). A ghost and a kept poly or plugin whose notes lane changed get their notes ended at entry (`Entry.release`: a voice's Off at the activation's first frame, a plugin's NoteOff at its next call); a new consumer of a kept scheduler gets Offs for notes it never had, which it ignores. A one-note pool (`"mono"`, `"legato"`, since track pm) lets go of the notes it holds too (`Mono::release` in `core/runtime/src/poly.rs`), so its gate falls and no older note comes back; a kept one also puts its pitch wheel and mod wheel at rest, as the new lane sends its own (a ghost keeps them through its tail). Its one voice keeps the note it sounds as a poly voice does, so `held_notes`, `active_voices` and `gates_are_owned` see it (the legato halves of `a_replaced_instrument_releases_its_notes` and `a_rewired_poly_is_kept_and_releases_its_notes`). A plan's first block (`Jump::Enter`) ends nothing, but a start, stop, seek or wrap on that block line is that jump (`Transport::advance`). Note ids are the engine's (`Renderer::next_note`, taken over at entry), so no id repeats into a kept instance. Adoption and the step 3b design's clarification C7 are withdrawn: nothing adopts.

**RENDER-26b.** A change to a plugin's state from outside the instance (an undo of a state write, `m2 plugin-state`, a git checkout, an agent writing the blob) MUST reach the running instance as a state load on the plugin main thread into the instance that plays, made while the plan for that revision is prepared. It is skipped when the blob's hash equals the state the instance holds ([HOME-6](#homes-of-values)). The parameters code owns are sent again at the instance's next block ([HOME-4](#homes-of-values)). A removed blob makes the instance new. Until compensation ships, a latency that becomes nonzero raises `plugin.latency-uncompensated` and plays uncompensated. A changed asset takes the path of [RENDER-27](#plans-data-and-arrangement-swaps): the affected clip renders are prepared off the audio thread and switched at one block boundary.
- *Why:* a state or asset change must have a route to the engine that doesn't rebuild the song. CLAP's state load is a main-thread call, allowed while the plugin is active; there is nothing to re-plan while [RENDER-21](#latency) refuses latency.
- *Today:* matches since step 7. A state-only revision is an activation of the same code (`Continues::identity`). Its prepare, as its last step once every new instance exists, compares each kept plugin's committed blob with the one its instance was last given (`PluginAt.blob` in `core/runtime/src/plan.rs`), node by node, so a key paired across paths loads the right blob though no file changed, and loads one that differs (`Renderer::prepare` in `core/runtime/src/lib.rs`). The node's next two calls, in the old plan or the new, send every parameter code owns again (`PluginInstance::take_reloaded`): Surge XT applies a loaded state at its next `process` call, over the values that call brings, and ends every voice there, so only the call after keeps them. A note that starts in that first call doesn't sound in such a plugin. A blob taken away makes the instance new (`keep::wire`): CLAP has no "load defaults". A load that fails never fails the prepare, as the loads before it have landed: that plugin plays on as it left itself, and a notice names it by its plugin and its track and says why. The plan records the refused blob as given, so no later plan tries it again or says so again until another blob is committed; the host then holds nothing known, so the blob it held before loads if it is written back (`a_refused_state_is_said_once_by_the_plugins_name` in `crates/server/src/graph/tests.rs`). A load that changes a plugin's latency has the next activation compensate it ([RENDER-17](#latency)). Tested by `a_changed_state_loads_into_the_same_instance`, `a_state_load_resends_code_owned_parameters` (with the probe applying a loaded state as Surge XT does, too) and `a_state_that_fails_to_load_never_fails_the_prepare` (`core/runtime/src/tests/live/keep.rs`), `a_state_change_loads_into_the_running_instance` and `swapping_two_plugin_keys_moves_the_instances` (`crates/server/src/graph/tests.rs`), and with-plugins' changed state gate (`m2-gates plugins`: one load into the same Surge, the new patch's volume, the code's filter type kept).

**RENDER-27.** An arrangement change (tempo, meter) MUST prepare the new timing tables and every affected clip render off the audio thread, then switch them together at one block boundary. The old arrangement plays until then. The switch keeps the transport's beat position, and its sample position is recomputed under the new tempo map. An audio-lane change that needs a render (a new clip, moved warp markers, a warped clip moved across a tempo change) takes the same path, with the old clip playing until its render is ready. The switch is an `Activate` of the same structure, which keeps every instance. Each audio lane with new renders crossfades from its old renders linearly over 5 ms, exactly where the two are equal. A tempo change re-renders every clip, warped or not, since the tempo map places and sizes each one.
- *Why:* re-rendering warped clips can't fit in 20 ms, and half-switched timing would desync audio. One path for code and data keeps a tempo change from cutting a tail or leaving audio off the grid.
- *Today:* matches since step 7. The server prepares an arrangement, audio, asset or state change as an activation of the same code (`Continues::identity`), with every lane's renders built through the session's cache while the old plan plays (`LanePlan::new` in `core/runtime/src/plan.rs`): an audio lane whose renders changed swaps them in its persistent player (`AudioPlayer::swap` in `core/runtime/src/audio.rs`), which plays both for one window, the old ones at their own position, and crossfades them sample by sample with `xfade`, so a clip the edit didn't change is the same bits on both sides; the transport keeps its beat (`Transport::retime`). Tested by `a_tempo_change_keeps_every_kernel_and_the_beat`, `an_audio_swap_of_unchanged_clips_changes_no_bit` and `a_clip_gain_edit_stays_between_its_two_levels` (`core/runtime/src/tests/live/keep.rs`).

**RENDER-27a.** A tempo drag is a lease ([WRITE-22](#gesture-leases)). Each update reaches notes, control lanes, time and plugins at the next block. Warped audio switches when its render is ready, so it may lag behind during the drag; this is the one place timing may be half-switched, and only in a preview. Release commits one Tx that writes `data/arrangement.json`.
- *Why:* re-rendering every warped clip before each drag step is heard would make the tempo bar unusable.
- *Today:* since `next` (8b.7), with the plan's Q22 and Q23 (`crates/server/src/graph/tempo_lease.rs`; routes `POST /api/graph/tempo/lease/{begin,update,keepalive,commit,cancel}`). An update keeps the newest value and queues one actor job, if none is queued: the job settles with the drag's arrangement (`DataSet::with_arrangement`), an activation of the same code prepared against the plan sent last, so the value is heard at the first block after its plan is prepared. One prepare is in flight; values in between are skipped, never queued; a drag held during a long compile is heard after it (`one_tempo_prepare_in_flight`). While the lease lives, every settle prepares with its arrangement (`Plays.tempo`), and `Timing` (seek, the loop, the status's beat and `bpm`) follows the map that plays; lane previews sent meanwhile are timed in it (`PlaysView.tempo`). The commit is 8b.2's tempo edit, one Tx, refused (409) when the arrangement changed since the drag began; until it lands, settles play the value let go, and a commit that fails plays the accepted tempo again (`a_settle_before_the_commit_plays_the_value_let_go`). Cancel, an idle TTL (10 s) and the window closing play the accepted arrangement again. A song whose plan plays an audio lane previews nothing and hears the drag on release (Q23's fallback): the lag of warped audio isn't built. Exports render the accepted revision.

**RENDER-28.** Engine threads (the device's callback thread and the player's workers) MUST NOT allocate, lock, do I/O or wait on a compile while they render. They wait only for work a running thread took: they spin while it moves, and sleep when it doesn't (the device's thread on its `Wake`, workers parked), so a stuck task costs a dropout and no CPU. Set-up system calls happen once per thread, at its start or on the fill that hands it its engine threads. A panic in a task makes the player crash, as a panic in the engine always did; a panic outside a task aborts.
- Cell values travel in per-cell slots: the latest value wins, and the audio thread reads the slots marked changed at each block. They never use the queue.
- Structural messages (`Activate`, `SwapLane`, `Transport`, `Panic`, `Hold`, `Live`) go over a bounded queue and are never dropped. Activations are never coalesced: each applies after the previous one's fade window, against the plan it names. A sender waits up to 500 ms, then reports the player stalled; the next change opens a new player. `Hold` pauses the engine for an export that borrows the live plugin instances, and resumes it ([RENDER-1](#offline)).
- A player is stalled when a sender waited 500 ms, when its device hasn't called back for a second, or when the device reports it is gone (its stream invalidated, or the device unavailable). Nothing else the device reports stalls it. An xrun (a dropout) is counted, and play goes on. A change of output device makes a new player, which opens at the new device's rate and period. The device's error callback only adds to a count per kind: on some OSes it runs on the audio thread.
- Debug builds and CI run every engine thread under an allocation and lock trap (in the style of `assert_no_alloc`).
- *Why:* any of these can glitch the output, and a dropped `Activate` would leave the engine on an old plan for good.
- *Today:* the queue is bounded (`core/play/src/lib.rs`); a sender retries for 500 ms, then reports `Stalled` and the message is not sent, never silently. Structural messages apply in order, one at a time (`Engine::try_apply`): an activation waits at the head for the window before it to end and for stash room, and none is coalesced. A resume (`Hold(false)`) applies as soon as it is read, unless its hold still waits: an activation sent while held waits for it, and before, the resume queued behind that activation and held the engine for good (`a_resume_never_waits_behind_an_activation_waiting_for_it` in `core/play/tests/live.rs`). The audio thread allocates, frees and locks nothing: buffers, event lists and an activation's moves are sized when a plan is prepared, and every move is a swap. Since step 3b the host's side of a plugin call allocates and locks nothing either: event lists are sized when a node is built, the host's audio side (`core/plugins/src/audio.rs`) is kept free of locks, allocation and thread-locals by a grep test, and `core/play/tests/no_alloc.rs` runs two probe plugins under the trap through an activation that moves both (a real plugin's own allocations are its own), and, from step 7, an activation that keeps kernels, a voice pool, a ring and an audio player, grows a lane, swaps the audio lane's renders, plays out a ghost reading a ghost lane, panics in its window, ends it, and refuses a stale activation. Since step 5 cell values travel in per-cell slots and never use the queue (`CellSlots` in `core/runtime/src/cells.rs`, `Player::set_cells` in `core/play`): the latest value wins, writers are serialised by a lock only control threads take, and the audio thread reads the slots marked changed at each block line with atomics alone. `core/play/tests/no_alloc.rs` writes cells from another thread while the callback runs under the trap. Since step 7's finish the player sorts cpal's errors by kind, the same on every OS (`DeviceEvent::of` in `core/play/src/lib.rs`): the error callback only adds to an atomic count per kind, and the player's thread logs the counts once a second while they grow. Only an invalidated stream or an unavailable device stalls the player (`Player::stalled`). A change of output device (`Player::changed`) makes the server let the player go, as after a stall: the action that meets it fails and says why, and the next settle loads the project into a new player where it was, stopped (`Inner::device` in `crates/server/src/graph.rs`). When the watcher's catch-up lets a playing player go first, a notice says "Playback stopped:", why, and "Press Play to go on." (`playback_the_device_stopped_is_said`). The engine view and `/api/graph/status` carry the counts since the player started (`xruns`, `device_errors`). Tested with injected errors, no device: `the_devices_errors_are_sorted_by_kind`, `an_xrun_is_counted_and_play_goes_on`, `a_device_change_asks_for_a_new_player_and_plays_on_meanwhile` and `a_device_that_is_gone_stalls_the_player` (`core/play/src/tests.rs`), `the_device_error_callback_never_allocates` (`no_alloc.rs`), `an_xrun_is_counted_and_the_player_plays_on` and `a_device_change_or_loss_makes_a_new_player_where_it_was` (`crates/server/src/graph/tests.rs`). Before, any error marked the player stalled, so one xrun (a CoreAudio overload) let it go at the next action and cut every tail, and the callback printed on ALSA's audio thread. Since step 7 debug builds of the app install a counting allocator (`m2_play::alloc` in `core/play/src/alloc.rs`): the callback marks its thread (in debug builds only, so a release callback touches no thread-local, whose first touch can allocate on macOS), every allocation and free there is counted and never panics, and a thread of its own logs the count once a second while it grows (`an_allocation_in_the_callback_is_counted`). `no_alloc.rs` also holds and resumes the engine, with a panic deferred to the resume. Since 8b.6 a live note ([MON-7](#monitoring-overlays-and-mute-in-song)) retires nothing, so it never waits for stash room, and its table is sized when its scheduler is made: `live_notes_never_allocate` strikes and lets go of keys into voices and into a plugin, through a wrap, a stop, the idle engine woken, a full table, a release and a panic. Since 8b.8 the callback times itself with the clock alone, read at its start and its end: its longest share of the device period since the server's meters last read it, and the callbacks that took longer than their period (`Player::load`, two atomics); and the player publishes whether the engine idles (`Player::idle`). A live plan's meter taps are atomics too ([Engine shape](#engine-shape-step-7)). Tested by `the_load_is_the_longest_callback` and `the_engine_says_when_it_idles` (`core/play/src/load_tests.rs`), and under the trap by `taps_and_the_load_never_allocate`, which reads and resets the taps from another thread while the callback runs. Since 8a's offline half the live engine renders each block through the partition executor on one thread (`Runner::one`: no thread, no heap); `no_alloc.rs` runs it under the trap. Since 8a's live half it renders on the player's engine threads ([RENDER-28a](#plans-data-and-arrangement-swaps)): the device's thread dozes on `m2_os::rt::Wake` (a futex, or a Mach semaphore: any thread may wait, one std didn't start included) when a task it waits for is stuck, and the task's end rings it (`a_stuck_task_puts_the_device_thread_to_sleep_on_its_wake`, driven from a thread made with `pthread_create`; the pool's model `the_caller_is_woken`); a task's panic on a worker reaches `crash()` (`a_panic_on_a_worker_crashes_the_player`); a worker that never comes costs only speed (`a_worker_that_never_comes_costs_only_speed`); every worker is under the trap from its start to its stop (`no_alloc_workers.rs`); and the wake's own code is checked by a grep test (`core/os/tests/no_blocking.rs`). On the owner's Mac at `e7518f4` (2026-10-18) the Mach-semaphore wake passed its test, with the rest of `m2-os`'s real-time tests (10 of 10, in `ci/sweep.sh`). Since `next` the live engine makes the metronome's click itself (`core/runtime/src/click.rs`): plain fields, no buffer, no allocation, on when the player's `click` flag is (`no_alloc_click.rs`); an export never makes an `Engine`, so it never has the click ([RENDER-1](#offline)). Since `studio` the count-in before a take is plain fields in `Click` too, with no allocation, and never reaches an export either ([RENDER-3](#offline)). A player let go says when its stream has stopped (`Player::freed`), so a change of output opens the new stream once the old one is gone: an output only one stream may hold at once refuses it otherwise. A start opens the output chosen by its id (`Output::id`, cpal's), and plays on the default when it is gone or won't open (`Player::fell_back`); with no output at all, the server's silent player stands in (gaps #13, `crates/server/src/graph/output.rs`). Since track ab (kernel ABI v1) a kernel memory grows only while its sandbox is born, and never once it is sealed ([RENDER-32](#engine-shape-step-7)). A kernel reads an audio file through `m2.asset_read`, which finds the asset and the memory by index in its sandbox's own data, so a read allocates nothing (`an_asset_read_allocates_nothing`, under the counting trap). The one exception stays: a trap's error allocates, on the thread that trapped.

**RENDER-28a.** Live workers MUST be real-time threads at the device's period, and on a Mac they join the output device's audio workgroup. How many, the device's thread included: `M2_THREADS` if set; else the P-cores that share one L2 cache, no more than the workgroup advises (macOS); one cache's cores when real time is granted (Linux); else 1. A device's pool whose workers aren't all real time plays on one thread. The silent player uses 1. After each callback the workers sleep. A player let go joins its workers. A song with plugins renders on the device's thread alone. Offline renders never use real-time threads.
- *Why:* a worker that isn't real time can be preempted inside a block while the device's thread waits for it, which is worse than one thread.
- *Today:* since 8a's live half (`core/play/src/engines.rs`; `m2_os::rt` in `core/os/src/rt.rs`, `mac/rt.rs`, `linux/rt.rs`). The pool is made on the player's thread once the device calls back (the first callback's frames give the period; the workgroup is read by the device's id, after IO started) and handed to the callback as a message. On this server real time is refused (`RLIMIT_RTPRIO` 0), so a device would play on one thread: `the_live_count_follows_the_rule`, `a_device_pool_is_kept_only_when_every_worker_is_real_time`, `a_device_without_real_time_plays_on_one_thread`, `an_engine_thread_gets_fifo_or_says_why`, `a_player_let_go_joins_its_workers`. Each worker holds its group until it has left the workgroup: leaving takes the group (`m2_os::rt::end_engine_thread`; `live_workers_hold_their_group_until_they_leave` in `core/runtime/src/threads.rs`). The Mac's code (the time-constraint policy, the workgroup join, the Mach semaphore) is type-checked on every commit; on the owner's Mac (`ci/sweep.sh` at `e7518f4`, 2026-10-18) its tests passed (`an_engine_thread_has_the_time_constraint_policy`, `a_worker_joins_and_leaves_the_default_outputs_workgroup`, the Mach wake: `m2-os`'s real-time tests, 10 of 10). The sweep confirmed the rule and `BETWEEN = Park` (`core/play/src/engines.rs`), so no code changed. That Mac (an M1 Pro) has 8 P-cores, 4 to an L2, and 2 E-cores, so the rule gives `live_count` 4. The live sweep, p50 for one minute at each setting, 128 frames at 44.1 kHz (a 2,902 µs period):

  | Threads | Park: p99, dropouts | Spin: p99, dropouts |
  |---|---|---|
  | 1 | 4,332 µs, 2,634 | — |
  | 2 | 2,312 µs, 0 | 4,700 µs, 2,472 |
  | 3 | 1,640 µs, 0 | 6,196 µs, 2,293 |
  | 4 | **1,324 µs, 0** | over 8,192 µs, 6,618 |

  4 parked is the best, and spinning is far worse at every count. Exports use the 8 fast cores: a p50 block took 1,604 µs on 1 thread and 249 µs on 8 (6.4×; 845 on 2, 577 on 3, 451 on 4, 301 on 6; the mean block), with the same bits at every count.

**RENDER-29.** Previews and transport MUST NOT take the session lock. See [gesture leases](#gesture-leases).
- *Why:* a knob preview waited 20 s behind a rebuild (measured).
- *Today:* matches since step 3b. The session runs as an actor (`crates/server/src/graph/actor.rs`), and a knob drag's updates are lease updates that take only the control plane's short lock, which the actor never holds over a compile or a build, and go straight to the engine ([WRITE-22](#gesture-leases)). Seek, the loop and status never touched the session since step 3a. Since 8b.2 the status says a seek, a play or a stop sent as done until the audio thread takes it (`Player::status`, `a_play_or_stop_reports_its_state_until_taken`), so the song bar's Space reads stopped at once, and a playhead never runs on. Since the GarageBand change a stop leaves the playhead where it stopped and the next play carries on from there; nothing remembers where play started. The engine takes a stop up to a block after the status says it, so `Graph::transport` sends a seek back to where the status was with it, and the playhead never moves after the stop (`a_stop_stays_where_it_stopped_and_play_carries_on_from_there`; `e2e/s8-songbar.mjs`). It carries `bpm`, the tempo the engine plays at its beat, which a tempo edit reaches at its switch ([RENDER-27](#plans-data-and-arrangement-swaps)), not when it is accepted: the web's playhead moves at that tempo (`during_an_export_a_tempo_edit_turns_the_loop_off_and_the_playhead_keeps_the_tempo_heard`). Measured: during a 1.5 s compile, updates are heard as fast as the others (`m2-gates --server previews`, G8), and `crates/server/src/graph/tests.rs` (`previews_never_wait_for_a_compile`) checks that the engine hears them before the compile ends. Since 8b.6 audition takes only that lock too: a note struck during a slow compile is heard before it ends (`audition_never_waits_for_a_compile`). Since `studio` Record, its Stop and a jump never wait for a compile either: the take's own thread writes it.

### Audio lanes

**RENDER-30.** Audio lanes are streamed from the disk. A clip with no warp (plain, pitched, offset, reversed, faded) is worked out from its WAV in `assets/` as it plays; a warped clip is stretched once by std's offline kernel `std:stretch` ([ABI-2](#kernel-abi-reservations)), in pieces, into the OS's cache folder ([FMT-16](#assets-and-hashes)), `<cache>/renders/<hash>.f32`, named by everything the kernel is given (the source's hash, std's module hash, the time map, the pitch, the rate), and streamed from there, reversed, faded and gained as it is read, as a plain clip is. A live session's cache never waits for a stretch: its one stretch thread makes each new one in the background, in the order asked, each from the clip's start, into a part file renamed to its name once whole, and the lane plays it as it is made. A chunk is filled only once every frame it reads is made; till then it plays silence and counts a miss, and the disk thread fills it once made (each render wakes it). A stretch no build asks for any more stops, and its part file goes. An offline render's cache (an export, a range render) makes each stretch whole before it plays, with the same maker, so its bits never depend on timing. A stretch the cache folder can't take (no folder, a full disk, no permission) is made in memory instead, from the start or from where the folder stopped taking it, with the same bits. Offline, a stretch that still fails fails the render, naming the clip, `stretch.failed` ([RENDER-1](#offline)); live, that clip is silent alone, and each lane that plays it says so, once per plan, `stretch.failed` ([RENDER-25](#plans-data-and-arrangement-swaps)). A chunk whose file can't be read is silence; live its lane says so, once per plan, `asset.unreadable`, and offline the render fails. Files are read with `m2_os::fs::read_at` (`pread`; `seek_read` on Windows), never mapped, by a player's disk thread or an offline render's own thread. Each lane plays from a ring of `min(16, chunks with audio)` slots, each holding one chunk: 0.25 s of the lane's summed samples, as atomic bits with an atomic tag. The disk thread keeps, around the playhead, the chunk before and 2 s ahead along the transport's path (through a loop's wrap), the loop start's first 0.5 s, and where a jump fades out from. The audio thread only copies from the rings and checks each tag after its copy: a chunk not there whole plays silence for that block and counts as a miss; it never waits, locks or reads a file. A seek waits up to 30 ms for its target, and an activation up to 500 ms for its new lanes' next half second; both send anyway after. Neither wait holds a lock that live input, the meters or Record take: the server makes a new renderer ready (`Player::ready`) before it takes its control plane's lock, so only the send is under it. Offline renders fill the same rings on their own thread before each block, with the same code, so a streamed lane has the bits a whole render had (RENDER-1, RENDER-2).
- *Why:* a stem-heavy song must not need all its audio in RAM. Caches are rebuildable and can be gigabytes, so they stay out of the project folder, where git and sync services would carry them; keyed by content, they are safely shared between projects. No memory map: a mapped file that another program cuts short kills the app (`SIGBUS`), and a thread that reads ahead needs no map.
- *Today:* matches since step 9's io turn: the clip sources, the chunk maths and the ring (`core/runtime/src/stream.rs`; `a_streamed_lane_is_the_rendered_lane`, `a_long_file_never_lives_in_memory`, `a_torn_chunk_is_never_heard`), the `m2-disk` thread, one per player, at normal priority (macOS's background class throttles disk reads) (`core/play/src/disk.rs`; `a_seek_lands_on_audio`, `a_loop_over_audio_never_misses`, `a_tempo_change_finds_its_audio_ready`, `a_slow_disk_misses_but_never_waits` in `core/play/tests/stream.rs`, and `the_sound_thread_never_reads_a_file` in `no_alloc.rs`). A player marks every renderer before it sends it (`Renderer::from_disk`), so no ring is filled on the audio thread; a driven player (tests) lets the disk thread catch up before each callback, as a device's real time does. A lane's misses reach the status ("The disk was too slow: Vox skipped a moment."). Imports check a file, and waveforms are made, in pieces; imports take files up to 3 hours. Since track sx a warped clip's stretch is made in pieces too: its source is read from the disk, and each block goes straight to its file (`a_warped_render_holds_neither_file_whole`, `an_export_with_an_empty_cache_folder_equals_one_with_a_full_one`). Since sx's slice 3 a live session makes it in the background (`crate::renders`). Since the sx review, one the folder can't take is made in memory, and an export whose stretch fails fails, naming the clip (`a_cache_folder_that_cannot_be_written_still_plays_warped_clips`, `a_render_whose_disk_fills_goes_on_in_memory`, `an_export_whose_stretch_fails_names_the_clip`).

**RENDER-30a.** A sound input MUST be read without the audio threads ever waiting for it. The input's callback takes the device's first two channels, converts them to the song's `sample_rate` at the device boundary ([RENDER-31](#sample-rate)), and copies them into two rings made when the input opens: one the engine hears on a lane ([MON-8](#monitoring-overlays-and-mute-in-song)), one a take writes from. It never allocates, locks or waits ([RENDER-28](#plans-data-and-arrangement-swaps)). Each buffer is stamped with the engine clock heard when its first frame was captured, so a take's first sample is the one heard at its start. What the input's device reports is counted apart from the output's: an input that goes away (unplugged) ends the take with what it has and is opened again at the next Record, and the output plays on; its overruns are never the output's dropouts. The input's problems take the fault ring's path ([RENDER-25](#plans-data-and-arrangement-swaps)), each a problem listed until the next take, or until another song opens: an input chosen that isn't there records from one that is and says so, naming both (`input.missing`), as outputs do; an input gone during a take is `input.lost`; and when the OS refuses the microphone during a take, the take is dropped, as it holds only silence, and said (`input.refused`). A take's file is durable (its bytes pushed, named create-only, its folder synced) before a Tx names it ([WRITE-1](#the-two-write-primitives), [FMT-14](#assets-and-hashes)). A write that fails (a full disk) ends the take with every frame written before it, kept as a whole file, and a take ends before its WAV's 32-bit sizes run out (about 3.1 hours of 48 kHz stereo); each is said in words.
- A ring never waits for its reader: a reader that falls behind finds the frames it lost gone, and plays or writes silence in their place, so everything after stays where it was heard. A take ring holds 4 seconds.
- *Why:* a voice recorded a few milliseconds late is out of time with the song, and a take named by the song before its file is whole can be lost to a crash. The input's callback runs on the device's own real-time thread, as the output's does.
- *Today:* built in step 9's io turn (slice 2): the rings (`core/play/src/ring.rs`: `AtomicU64` frames, a claim checked after each copy, `a_torn_input_frame_is_never_heard`); the input, its callback and its stamps (`core/play/src/inputs.rs`: a device's capture time from cpal's timestamps, placed on the output's clock as a key is; the stamp a take uses is the median of the last 32). Under `M2_AUDIO=none` the silent input hands over what `Player::inject_input` queued, one frame for each the engine renders, so a test knows where each sample lands. The take's writer and its Tx: `crates/server/src/graph/take_audio.rs` and `crates/editor/src/take.rs`. Tested by `the_input_callback_never_allocates` (`no_alloc.rs`), and in `crates/server/src/graph/record_tests.rs` by `an_injected_input_records_where_it_was_heard`, `a_take_is_durable_before_the_song_names_it`, `a_count_in_keeps_nothing_before_the_start`, `one_undo_takes_the_recording_back`, `a_take_ends_at_the_loop_end` and `a_full_take_ring_writes_silence_and_keeps_time`. Since slice 3 the app records: `GET /api/audio/inputs`; Settings keeps `input {device, name, monitor}` and the record route passes it to the take; `POST /api/graph/input/inject` feeds the silent input, only with `M2_AUDIO_INPUT=inject`; the meters carry the input's level while a take records (`an_audio_take_through_the_routes`, `e2e/io-record.mjs`). On macOS a microphone refused in Privacy & Security is said in words before an input opens (`m2_os::host::microphone_refused`), and the bundle's `Info.plist` carries `NSMicrophoneUsageDescription`. Since track ex's slice 3 the player holds the app's fault ring (`Player::report_to`): `open_input` reports `input.missing`, numbers only, about the input's opening (`Listen::open`, counted for the whole app), and opens again at each take while the one chosen is missing; the take thread asks `Player::input_refused` at each tick (the OS's answer, or the inject API's `refuse`) and reports `input.refused` and `input.lost`. The session names them with the opening's `InputNamer` (`crates/server/src/graph/faults.rs`). Tested by `a_chosen_input_that_isnt_there_is_told_naming_the_one_used`, `a_take_the_os_refuses_is_dropped_and_said` and `an_input_unplugged_mid_take_is_one_problem` (`record_tests.rs`), and `the_inject_api_unplugs_the_input` (`routes_record_tests.rs`). A real device's input is unverified: no device here, and the Mac's check by ear is owed, Don't Allow during a take among it.

### Sample rate

**RENDER-31.** The live engine MUST run at the `sample_rate` in [the manifest](#manifest-and-versions). It opens the device at that rate, or resamples at the device boundary. It is built in step 4, with `sample_rate`.
- *Why:* live and export must run the same DSP at the same rate.
- *Today:* matches since step 4: the player runs the engine at `sample_rate` and converts to the device's rate at the boundary when they differ (`core/play/src/resample.rs`), and Export Audio, `m2 render` and `m2 bounce` render at `sample_rate`.

**RENDER-31a.** Offline renders MUST run at `sample_rate`. An export at another rate goes through one fixed, deterministic sample-rate converter after the render. 16-bit output uses TPDF dither from a fixed seed; 24-bit output rounds; 32-bit float is written as rendered. RENDER-2's bits are the render at `sample_rate`, before conversion and dither, and the export's provenance records the rate, the converter and the dither.
- *Why:* re-running the DSP and plugins at another rate would sound different, and unseeded dither would make exports differ from run to run.
- *Today:* offline renders run at `sample_rate` (step 4); `m2 render` and `m2 bounce` refuse `--sr` rather than run the DSP at another rate. Exporting at another rate (the converter) isn't built. Exports are 24-bit, rounded, with no dither and no provenance (`crates/editor/src/render.rs`); bounces are 32-bit float.

### Engine shape (step 7)

```rust
Renderer { plan: Plan { graph, order_after, cells, out, entry, info, taps }, lanes: Lanes,
           tempo, arrangement, transport, clock, next_note }
DataSet  { arrangement, lanes: Map<LaneAddr, Arc<LaneData>> }
Engine   { current: Renderer, clock, … }

enum Msg { Activate(Box<Renderer>), SwapLane(generation, slot, LaneSwap), Transport(Play | Stop | Seek(at) | Loop(range)),
           Panic, Hold(bool),    // Hold: an export that borrows the live plugin instances (RENDER-1)
           Live(generation, slot, LiveEv) }   // LiveEv: On(source, handle, pitch, velocity) | Off(source, handle) | Release(source) | Midi(source, bytes) (MON-7)
// SetCell(CellId, f32) is a write to the cell's slot, never a queued message (RENDER-28).
```

The arena is the boxes in the playing plan's stateful nodes; they move at activation by a move list computed off the audio thread. No RuntimeId reaches the audio thread.

A live plan's `taps` are its meters (8b.8): the song's output, and each track where its audio enters another module, read at its overlay (after mute and solo, before what it feeds). After each block the plan raises each channel's largest `|x|` into an atomic slot (`fetch_max` on the bits of a non-negative `f32`), which the server reads and resets (`TapSlots` in `core/runtime/src/taps.rs`, `PlanInfo.taps`). They read buffers only, so they change no bit, and offline renders have none of them: an agent's listen copies its offline render's buffers instead, through a `Copying` that owns that renderer, so no live plan has them ([AGENT-7](#agents)) (`taps_change_no_bit`, `a_tap_reports_its_blocks_peak`, `a_muted_tracks_tap_reads_silence` in `core/runtime/src/tests/taps.rs`). The server reads and resets the taps of the plan that plays every 33 ms, and sends every window `{"t": "meters", "tracks": {k: [l, r]}, "out": [l, r], "dsp": {"load", "dropouts"}}` while the engine runs (one last as it starts to idle, none while it idles or a new plan waits to be taken), on a channel that keeps only the latest, never the event broadcast, so a slow window skips meters and never loses a `graph` event. `load` is the longest audio callback of the last second as a share of the device period, timed with the clock alone (`Player::load`, read only there); `dropouts` are the device's xruns and the late callbacks. `/api/graph/status` carries `load` and `late` (`crates/server/src/graph/meters.rs`; `meters_are_sent_while_playing_and_stop_when_idle`, `a_new_plan_moves_the_taps`, `muting_the_kick_floors_its_meter_and_the_bass_still_ducks`, `meters_reach_every_window_and_the_status_has_the_load`). Until 8b.3's channel gains, a track is metered before its fader: a duck or a gain written in the song acts after its meter.

**RENDER-32.** Sandbox layout: kernels run in sandboxes. A sandbox is one wasmtime `Store` with one instance of one package's module and its one memory. It holds the kernel states of exactly one box: a kernel outside an instrument, or a voice pool's kernels from one package. It is born off the audio thread and sealed before it plays: after that its memory and tables never grow, so it never moves. It moves whole between plans, is entered by one thread at a time, and is dropped whole off the audio thread, which frees its memory. Never a sandbox shared by two boxes or across plans. Memories take wasmtime's default reservation (4 GiB and its guards), so the hardware catches a stray access, no bounds check is compiled in, and Cranelift's Spectre guard is on. A renderer holds at most N sandboxes, so that two renderers stay within 25% of the address space; a build that would pass N fails and says so. G7f judges a layout against S, the commit before step 7 (one `Store` per renderer, no arena), measured fresh in every run: S plays in a process of its own, taking turns with the app over the same music, in 5 rounds; no side takes two turns in a row, and the first half second of each turn is not judged. By the mean, a p50 block MUST cost at most 80 µs more than S's (3% of a 2.67 ms block), and a 512-frame callback at most 320 µs more than S's (3% of a callback). Against S, p50's footprint MUST grow by at most 64 MB and its build by at most half, and two p50 renderers MUST build side by side within 25% of the process's address space. An activation's block MUST take under half the device period (5.3 ms at 512 frames). The bounds are the owner's (2026-10-15; design-7 §11.3). This layout replaced the owner's 32 MiB layout (O1, 2026-10-15) on track ab's measured rule (2026-10-18, below); the Mac's G7f run at `e7518f4` confirmed it ([PLAT-1](#platforms)).
- *Why:* a box is already what a live edit moves, what frees memory and what one thread enters (step 7), so a sandbox per box keeps live edits as they were. A few hundred sandboxes fit wasmtime's safe 4 GiB layout; one per kernel state (6,060 in p50) did not: two p50 renderers held 37.9% of the Mac's address space, past the 25% rule, which is why the 32 MiB layout was chosen. In the 4 GiB layout the bounds checks go, and the Spectre guard costs nothing, which other people's kernels need. Kernel ABI v1 names the state in every call, so many states share one sandbox (`docs/designs/kernel-layout/report.md`). The mean is judged, not the p99: even paired, the p99's difference moves by hundreds of µs a block from one pair to the next, which 5 pairs can't settle, while the mean's stays within about 12 µs.
- *Today:* matches since track ab's slice 1, commit B (`fff7e86`): `core/runtime/src/sandbox.rs`, and a voice pool's sandboxes in `VoicePool`. p50 builds 202 sandboxes: 152 kernels outside instruments and 50 voice pools (`p50_builds_202_sandboxes`). The seal is checked by `a_sealed_sandbox_never_grows`, and `every_std_kernel_resets_in_a_sealed_sandbox` checks that no std kernel takes memory at a reset. The Spectre guard is on, so its Deferred row is done. Since slice 2, N is 3,999 (`SANDBOX_LIMIT` in `core/runtime/src/lib.rs`): two renderers within 25% of the 128 TiB a process may address, at 4.097 GiB a memory as the Mac measured (4.0625 on Linux), the same number on every OS. A build past it fails before any sandbox is made, in plain words that say what to do (`TooManySounds`; `a_song_past_the_sandbox_limit_is_refused_in_plain_words`). A sandbox may take 256 MiB while it is born, its birth cap (`a_kernel_past_its_birth_cap_says_it_ran_out`). G7f judges the app against S only: its third side, P1 until then, is a second renderer of the app's layout, its A/A control, printed as what noise alone gives, and kept so that three sides take turns. The same bits: the 10 recorded renders match `e2e/renders.sha256`, every pure fixture and p16 play the same bits on 1, 2, 3, 4 and 7 threads, and a 4:02 p16 render is the same file as `main`'s (`258275b`).
  **The decision** (Linux, information only, PLAT-1). The rule was fixed before measuring (`docs/designs/step9/ab-plan.md`): go if the bits match, the layout is not slower than `main` by the mean of 3 pairs, for a block and for a callback, and the bounds against S hold. Each run is G7f's `store` (a block) and `arena` (a 512-frame callback) on p50, held on CPUs 4 and 20 (the 96 MB-cache die), each against S measured fresh. The runs went main, B, main, B, main, B.

  | Pair | Block: main − S | B − S | **B − main** | Callback: main − S | B − S | **B − main** |
  |---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
  | 1 | −181 µs | −378 | **−197** | −530 µs | −1,983 | **−1,453** |
  | 2 | −155 | −395 | **−240** | −748 | −1,518 | **−770** |
  | 3 | −194 | −389 | **−195** | −759 | −1,775 | **−1,016** |
  | Mean | | | **−211 µs** | | | **−1,080 µs** |

  | From the same runs | main | B | Bound |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | p50's footprint, one renderer | 204 MB | 74 to 75 MB | S + 64 MB |
  | p50's build | 218 to 224 ms | 61 to 63 ms | 1.5 × S's (S: 181 to 187 ms; 326 once) |
  | Address space, two renderers | 381 GiB, 0.29% | 1,641 GiB, 1.3% | 25% |
  | Slowest activation block | 2,366 to 2,467 µs | 1,835 and 1,882 µs; 5,548 once | 5,333 µs |

  **Go.** The bits match. Both means are far below 0, so no 3 more pairs were needed (the rule asks for them only within ±20 µs). The bounds hold. The one activation block over 5.3 ms came with the ordinary block just before it at 5,782 µs, so the machine was busy then, not the activation; a fourth run of B gave 1,841 µs. S's footprint is recorded on the Mac only; B's is 129 MB under `main`'s, which is within the bound there (571 MB against S's 554). Commit A (`a5ec7bd`: ABI v1, still one state per sandbox and 32 MiB), one pair against `main`, for information: a block −53 µs and a callback −780 µs, but a footprint of 839 MB and a build of 609 ms, because it touches every page of 6,060 sandboxes at their seal. The Mac's run is below.

  **On the Mac** (the owner's `ci/check.sh` at `e7518f4`, 2026-10-18, against S measured in the same run): **passed**, with the same music and within the 25% rule (the gate passes only with both; the pasted summary didn't carry the address space's number).

  | G7f on the Mac at `e7518f4` | Measured | Bound |
  |---|---|---|
  | A p50 block, over S (mean of 5 pairs) | −1,018 µs (−1,142 to −997) | +80 µs |
  | A 512-frame callback, over S | −2,748 µs | +320 µs |
  | p50's footprint, one renderer | 76 MB (S: 554 MB) | S + 64 MB |
  | p50's build | 28 ms (S: 205 ms) | 1.5 × S |
  | Slowest activation block | 2,225 µs | 5,333 µs |

- *Before* (the owner's 32 MiB layout, step 7 to 2026-10-18): matched since step 7. One `Store` per kernel instance (`KernelInst` in `core/runtime/src/lib.rs`, its memory capped at 32 MiB) since the first slice. Each instance keeps its memory's address from when it is built. So a kernel call doesn't ask its store for it: there are thousands of stores, and they are slow to reach. This needs a memory that never moves, which both layouts guarantee. The test `a_kernel_memory_never_moves` checks it; so does each kernel's first reset when a plan is built, and, in a debug build, every call. The owner's layout since the review's fixes, after the Linux study `docs/designs/step7/g7f-linux.md` (`Layout::P2` in `engine()`, `core/runtime/src/packages.rs`; P1 is a gate-only option). Bounds checks change no arithmetic: the 8 recorded renders still match `e2e/renders.sha256`, and a 4-minute p16 render is the same file as before. G7f measures the app's layout against S in every run: `m2-gates store --reference 50` for blocks (`crates/gates/src/store.rs`), and `m2-gates arena --reference 50 --at 180` for callbacks (`crates/gates/src/arena.rs`). S, HEAD `8aa301e`, plays in a runner built from a clean copy of it, outside the repo (`crates/gates/s/`; `ci/check.sh` builds it first). In each of 5 rounds, the app's layout, P1 and S take turns of about 2 s over the same music, 18 each. No side takes two turns in a row, and the first half second of each turn is not judged. All three must play the same bits, and not silence. Each round gives the difference of two mean times, the app's layout minus S. The median of the 5 differences is judged. Printed beside it: the p99's difference, P1 against S, and the app's layout against P1. From its record (`crates/gates/store-S.txt`) G7f still reads three things the runner can't measure: S's footprint (judged), the address space S held with two renderers (printed), and how much P1's drop raised another thread's p99 over S's drop (for the batched-drops line, printed). As for every timing, only the reference Mac judges the comparisons: the Mac the two records name. Other machines print them with what they would say (PLAT-1). On a Mac the records don't name, the gate also says to copy that Mac's machine line into both records if it is the reference Mac now, and `ci/check.sh` marks the gates row, and its first line, PART: not a pass. On Linux (information only, PLAT-1), held on the CPU's 96 MB-cache die, by hand at `33812c4` (the gates as at `d4ee726`): the app's layout against S, by the mean, +16 µs a block (5 pairs, +15 to +20) and +48 µs a callback (+45 to +72). P1 against S: −9 µs and −64 µs. The app's layout against P1 (the owner's layout alone): +26 µs and +117 µs. Not held, as `ci/check.sh` runs it, one run by hand at the same commit: +10 µs a block (+3 to +37) and +112 µs a callback (+12 to +151). Held on the other die, whose cache is 32 MB: −208 µs a callback. Not held, runs spread far more: this CPU's two dies have very different caches. The Mac can't hold a process on one group of cores the way `taskset` does here, so its runs may spread more than the held ones. And a control with the app's own engine on both sides shows how far one run can be off: about ±150 µs a callback and ±30 µs a block (`crates/gates/s/README.md`). In `ci/check.sh --gates` at `ffc36aa`, not held: +20 µs a block (+17 to +27) and +86 µs a callback (+46 to +147). With the `sounds` merge, at `e108e46`, S still plays its own std, 0.1 (its runner sets each project's std line to S's version): −25 µs a block (−51 to +9) and −166 µs a callback (−775 to +125), the same music, the slowest activation block 3.4 ms. Two p50 renderers hold 380 GiB, 0.29% of 131,072 GiB. The slowest activation block was 2.3 ms held and 3.1 ms in the check (bound 5.3). The Mac's numbers come with its next run. History: Before the review of the speed work's fixes (2 s turns, 15 a round), by hand at `8daea12`, held: +14 µs a block and +55 µs a callback; not held +43 and +78; in `ci/check.sh --gates` at `a894448` +23 and +97. At `8177eb2`, before each instance kept its memory's address, the fresh comparison gave +53 µs a block and +236 µs a callback on Linux. Until `ed7fc8e`, G7f added the app's layout's cost over P1 to P1's cost over S as recorded on the Mac at slice 2. On the reference Mac at `ed7fc8e` that gave +73 µs a block (passed) and +328 µs a callback (failed 320). The Linux study, before the fresh comparison: the app's layout against P1, by the mean, +12 to +33 µs a block and −60 to +118 µs a callback; with the Spectre guard on, +128 and +473 µs, which fail. What the Mac measured of P1 at slice 2, on p50's 6,058 kernel instances (an M1 Pro, 16 alternated release runs each), against one `Store` per renderer (S, HEAD `8aa301e`): the build took 108 ms against 103, and the footprint was 575 MB against 554. Block time over 30 s from 3:00: a mean of 2,227 µs against 2,195 (+32 µs), and a p99 of 3,197 against 3,083 by the medians (+35 µs paired; runs vary by ±200 µs). The drop took about 100 ms either way, and raised another thread's block time not at all. Two renderers held 49,637 GiB, 37.9% of the address space, as S did, so P1 failed the 25% rule. The pooling allocator reserves as much per slot, up front (74.8% with the gate's two engines). With the arena, P1's 512-frame callback against HEAD's, p50 from 3:00 for 30 s: a mean of 9,141 µs against 8,936 (+187 µs paired), a p99 of 13,817 against 12,910 by the medians (+232 µs paired; one run's p99 varies from 11.6 to 15.6 ms). Most of it was the per-instance store (about +32 µs a block, four blocks a callback); RENDER-25's non-finite check costs at most about 4 µs a block. At HEAD a plain p50 callback already takes 84% of the 10.7 ms period and its p99 passes it, so p50 isn't real time on one thread with or without the arena (8a's partitions are for that). The activation block, over ten edits in each of 8 runs on the Mac (P1), as its own one-block callback: the slowest 3.0 to 3.8 ms, under the rule's 5.3 ms (half the device period); on Linux with the owner's layout, before the kept address, 3.1 to 4.2 ms. On the reference Mac, the owner's `ci/check.sh` at `cbac57c` (2026-10-17) passed G7f against S measured in the same run, by the median of 5 rounds' mean differences: −19 µs a block (−89 to +13; bound 80) and −196 µs a 512-frame callback (−633 to +298; bound 320). Its starting line read −184 µs a callback (+11 a block), so S's starts lasted longer there and the callback's verdict leans about 90 µs the app's way. p50's footprint 571 MB against S's 554; its full build 104 ms against S's 124; the slowest activation block 3,992 µs (bound 5,333). S measured a mean of 10,169 µs a callback, 14% over its record of 8,936 (2026-10-14); why is not known. At `85b069a` (2026-10-17) the owner's `ci/check.sh` failed G7f part 1: +135 µs a block over S (5 pairs, −65 to +160; bound 80). P1 read +177 over S and the app's layout −46 over P1, so the cost since step 7 is in what the two share, not the layout; footprint 581 MB against S's 554, full build 126 ms against 122. Part 2 passed: −69 µs a 512-frame callback (−154 to +253; bound 320), the slowest activation block 3,999 µs. S measured 10,589 µs a callback, 18% over its record. Since macfix the app's `std:gain` skips its `powf` while its dB holds still, with the same bits; S's runner plays S's own std, whose gain doesn't. So G7f store's line favours the app's side (its layout and P1 alike) by about 250 µs a p50 block on Linux, p50's 50 channel gains, and would hide that much of any other cost. Until the Mac's shift since step 7 is explained, its runs also play G7f store with `85b069a`'s std (`M2_STD`), the old gain (`docs/designs/step8/build-notes-macfix.md`, "The Mac's next run").

### The bit-identity test

**RENDER-33.** Live playback from transport 0, on a freshly reset engine, with no edits, no jumps and no overlays, driven with arbitrary device buffer sizes, MUST produce the same `f32` bits as the offline render at the project sample rate (48 kHz until step 4 adds `sample_rate`), before any device resampling, once the live output is shifted by `L` ([RENDER-19](#latency)). CI runs this on every pure fixture from step 3 on.
- *Why:* it is the one guarantee that live and export run the same engine.
- *Today:* holds since step 3a: the live engine and an offline render run the same `Renderer::render_block` on the same block grid, and `L` is 0 for a song with no latency, which gets no delay ([RENDER-18](#latency)). CI checks it through the player on every pure fixture (the whole song) and on p16's first 30 s, in device buffers of 1 to 4,096 frames (`core/play/tests/bit_identity.rs`); recorded in release: all of p16 and all of p50 (`live_is_the_export_on_all_of_p16`, `live_is_the_export_on_p50`). Since step 7 (G7c), a plan of the same revision activated three times mid-note changes no bit either: on every pure fixture that makes sound, and on p16 (`a_no_op_activation_changes_no_bit`, and with every lane's data rebuilt, `an_activation_with_rebuilt_lane_data_changes_no_bit`), and recorded on p50 (`a_no_op_activation_on_p50`). Since the `sounds` merge the pure fixtures are five: `examples/first-song`, `examples/resample`, `examples/sounds` (every std synth and drum, and FM keys, bell and metal: all of std's new sounds but `fm.custom`), `templates/starter` and `templates/empty`. Since then "freshly reset" no longer matters to std's effects and drums ([RENDER-6](#live-engine-and-its-two-clocks)): a song opened and played after the engine sat stopped, 2 s or 5 blocks, is the export bit for bit, on every pure fixture, on a beat of std's drums, and on a held chord through each of chorus, phaser, tremolo and bitcrush alone (`std_plays_the_export_after_the_engine_sat_stopped`). Each effect failed it before (std 0.1). It still matters to free-running sources at the top level (std's oscillators and `noise`), and to a smoother that sets itself from its first input (`Gain`, `Duck`, `Glide` in `std/kernels-src`): stopped, it does that from the input it has then, which is the export's only when that input at frame 0 is the same. Every fixture meets this.

### Buses

**RENDER-34.** A bus is a track the song feeds. `bus(key, opts, fn)` in `@m2/core` makes one: a track whose `fn` reads `input`, what the song feeds it. The song feeds it with `feed(bus, x)`, from song code only, once per bus; a bus nobody feeds is a build error. A bus has no notes or audio lanes (a control lane, to automate it, is fine). In the IR a bus is a track with `bus: true` (written only when true, ID-11), and `input` is an import of the song's export `bus:<key>`. The song and its buses read each other both ways, so they split into partitions by crossings ([RENDER-2](#offline)); latency is made up as for any graph ([RENDER-18](#latency)). A loop through a bus is a build error that names it.
- std's mixer (0.4.0) does the routing in one place. A channel that reads a bus as it is (`verb: verb.out`) is that bus's channel, and the mixer feeds the bus: the channels that go to it (`to: "drums"`, instead of the master) and the sends to it (`sends: { verb: db(-12) }`), summed in key order, or silence. A send is taken after the channel's pan and fader, or before both with `pre: true`. Sends and `to` name a bus by its channel key. A loop among the channels is refused by the mixer, naming it by keys ("verb feeds itself: verb → delay → verb. A bus can't feed itself. Take away one of these sends or outputs."); one through code is the checker's loop error, which names the modules it goes through.
- *Why:* a shared reverb, a delay and a group are what a beginner reaches for first (gaps #27). A bus as a track gets keys, files, rename, delete, layout, mute in song, mute and solo cells and its own partition for free; one mixer holds every fader and send, as the Mix view shows them.
- *Today:* the song code half since track bus, slice 1 (`@m2/core` 1.5.0, std 0.4.0): `bus`, `feed` and `busOf` in `core/script/sdk/core.ts` (`core/script/src/bus_tests.rs`), `Module.bus` and the checker's bus rules and loop names (`core/ir/src/bus_tests.rs`), the mixer in `std/src/mix.ts` (`core/session/src/bus_tests.rs`: a send after the fader is the strip, before it the input, a group leaves the master, sample for sample). The fixture `core/session/tests/fixtures/buses` (a drum group, a reverb, a delay that sends on to the reverb) renders the same bits on 1 to 7 threads (`buses_give_the_same_bits_on_any_thread_count`), a latent plugin on a bus stays aligned (`a_latent_plugin_on_a_bus_stays_aligned`), and the song and bus partitions have no cycle (`a_song_and_its_buses_split_into_partitions_with_no_cycle`). One engine change: the partitioner's rule that runs a strip with its track (`labels` in `core/runtime/src/lib.rs`) moves nothing into the song, so a bus runs in a partition of its own rather than in the song's serial tail; a bus fed by one other bus alone runs with it. The server's solo follows the routes (MON-3). A song with no bus builds the same graph and the same bits. The edits since slice 2, in `crates/editor/src/bus.rs` (`crates/editor/src/bus_tests.rs`): add a bus (a Reverb, a Delay, or a Group, which is `gain(input)` so that mute and solo have a node to act on), add or take out a send, send before or after the fader, and send a channel to a bus or back to the master, each one undo step. A send or an output that would make a loop is refused before anything is written, naming the tracks ("Echo already feeds Reverb, so Reverb can't send to Echo."). A send's level is a knob like a fader's, a cell write; one a send leaves out is written into it (nested ids, PKG-6), and it is automated as "Hats send to Reverb". Deleting a bus takes the sends to it and the outputs to it out too, after it asks. `track()`'s builders find `bus()`, so a bus is renamed, muted in the song and duplicated as a track is. `mix::view` lists bus strips last, each strip's sends, output and the buses it may use without a loop; the server's `POST /api/graph/mix/bus`, `/mix/send` and `/mix/route` answer with it. Since slice 3 each strip also lists the buses it can't use, with why in the edits' words (`loops`, `the_mix_view_says_why_a_bus_would_make_a_loop`), and a timeline track says it is a bus. The Mix view (`web/src/components/Mix.tsx`, `e2e/bus-mix.mjs`) has Add bus (Reverb, Delay, Group), bus strips after the tracks with the meter tracks have (before the fader, MON-3; after it is gap #20), a send row per bus on every strip (a + or a knob, a dot when before the fader, its menu), and an output picker; a choice that would loop is greyed with why. The timeline shows a bus as a row with no lanes.

## Identity

| Kind | Examples | Saved? | Rule |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Saved** | track keys, lane keys, plugin keys, `key()` frames | yes, written in code | readable, validated, never normalised |
| **Derived** | SiteId, InstancePath, ControlId, RuntimeId, CellId | never | memory only, rebuilt from code |
| **Never identity** | labels, binding names, file paths, ordinals, array indexes, node numbers, build-local counters | — | may be shown; never used as an address |

### Keys

**ID-1.** Every key MUST match `^[a-z0-9]([a-z0-9-]{0,30}[a-z0-9])?$`: 1 to 32 characters, lowercase letters, digits and inner hyphens. A leading digit is allowed (`808`).
- *Why:* keys are readable in file names, and lowercase-only keys can't suffer a case-only rename on a case-insensitive disk.
- *Today:* matches since step 4: one key rule, in `m2_ir::key` for the build and the checker and in `m2_song::Key` for paths and data.

**ID-2.** Keys MUST be validated, never normalised. An invalid key is the compile error `key.invalid`, which suggests a valid spelling.
- A writer (the app's add track, an agent) MAY propose a key from a label once, when it creates the thing: fold to ASCII, lowercase, turn every run of other characters into one `-`, trim to 32 characters and the key rule, and fall back to `track-2`, `track-3`… when the result is empty or taken. It shows the key, writes it into the code, and never derives it again. One shared helper in `crates/editor` does this.
- *Why:* normalising is what lets two names share files. A key proposed once and then written down is just a default the person can see and change.
- *Today:* matches: keys are checked, never normalised, and `key.invalid` suggests a spelling (`m2_ir::key::suggest`). Since step 6 the one helper is `keys::propose` in `crates/editor`; automating a knob uses it once, for the new lane's key, which it shows and writes into the code.

**ID-3.** `song` is reserved for the root module.
- *Today:* matches (`declareTrack` in `core/script/sdk/core.ts`).

**ID-4.** These have keys: tracks (modules), lanes (unique within their module), plugins that have saved state, and `key()` frames.

```ts
track("lead-vox", { name: "Lead Vox" }, ({ lane }) => ...)   // label "Lead Vox"
track("kick", ({ lane }) => ...)                             // label "kick"
lane.notes("hook", { name: "Hook" })                         // .control(key, unit, fallback, opts?), .mask(key, opts?), .audio(key, channels?, opts?)
plugin("clap:org.surge-synth-team.surge-xt", { key: "lead", notes })   // replaces `state:`
key("chain-l", () => vocalChain(x))                          // a frame: composes state paths, and pins what it holds
```

**ID-5.** A key used twice where it must be unique MUST be the compile error `key.duplicate`: a track key anywhere in the project, a `key()` frame key within its parent frame, a plugin key within its frame. The message names both evaluations: the call site, plus the stack of the second, since a `track()` in a helper or a loop has one site that runs twice.
- *Why:* the error must say exactly what to rename.
- *Today:* matches since step 4: `key.duplicate` names the call site and the stack of both evaluations (`core/script/sdk/core.ts`).

**ID-6.** A lane declared again with the same kind and settings is the same lane. A redeclaration with another kind or other settings is the compile error `lane.conflict`. A lane key declared from two different `key()` frame chains is the compile error `lane.shared`, unless the declarations say `shared: true`. Lanes are addressed by module key and lane key only, never by frame.
- *Why:* helpers may read the same lane twice, but `key("chain-l", …)` and `key("chain-r", …)` must not share one automation lane by accident, the way [ID-8](#keys) forbids for state. Keeping lanes out of frames keeps them flat on the timeline and lets code be wrapped in `key()` without moving data.
- *Today:* matches since step 4 (`declareLane` in `core/script/sdk/core.ts`).

**ID-7.** `key(k, fn)` opens a frame. Keys MUST be unique within their parent frame ([ID-5](#keys)). A frame composes plugin state paths. A `key()` frame is named by its key in every identity below it; nothing positional above the innermost one is part of an InstancePath. It is also reserved as a later place for monitoring overlays inside a module ([MON-1](#monitoring-overlays-and-mute-in-song)).
- *Today:* matches: `key()` frames compose plugin state paths since step 4, and since step 7 a node's delta name keeps, above its innermost `key:` segment, only the other `key:` segments (`Side::chain` in `core/session/src/tx/delta.rs`), so moving the code around a `key()` keeps what it holds (`names_ignore_everything_above_a_key_frame`).

**ID-8.** A plugin needs a key only once it has saved state, and then it MUST have one. Until then it has an in-memory RuntimeId. The state path is the module key, then the `key()` frames, then the plugin key: `data/vox/chain-l/tune.state`. Two stateful instances that resolve to one path are the error `plugin.state-collision`, whose message says "wrap one in `key()`". The same error applies when a plugin without its own key takes its frame's key while that frame directly holds another stateful plugin (plugins in frames nested inside it don't count). `key` is a reserved plugin port name.
- The app's first state save is a Tx built in `crates/editor`. It inserts `key: "<k>"` at the plugin call when that call is in a project file and runs once in its frame. Otherwise it goes outward along the instance's frame chain to the first call that is in a project file and runs once in its frame, and wraps that call in `key("<k>", …)`; the plugin then takes the innermost frame's key as its state file name, which is allowed only while that frame holds exactly one stateful plugin. If no call on the chain is in a project file (a plugin built inside a package), the save is refused before anything is written, with a message saying where to add `key()`.
- *Why:* most plugins never need saved state, and two plugins must never share it by accident. A key inserted at a call shared by two instances would collide at once.
- *Today:* matches since step 4, except that every plugin with a state path counts as stateful, since the build never reads data: a plugin's `key`, or else its innermost `key()` frame's, files its state at `data/<module>/<frames>/<key>.state` (`Op::state_path` in `core/ir`), and two that resolve to one path are `plugin.state-collision`. `key` is consumed by the SDK and is never a port. The app's first state save, a Tx, comes in step 6.

**ID-9.** Windows device names (`aux`, `con`, `nul`, `prn`, `com1`–`com9`, `lpt1`–`lpt9`) are legal keys. `DataPath` MUST add a trailing `-` to every path segment made from such a key (`data/aux-/`, `data/kick/con-.json`). Nothing else may map keys to paths.
- *Why:* producer names stay legal, Windows stays possible, and the mapping is reversible because no key ends in `-`.
- *Today:* matches since step 4 (`DataPath` in `core/song/src/path.rs`).

### Labels

**ID-10.** A label MUST be display text only: never an address, a file name or an import target. It defaults to the key. Changing a label is an `Identical` Tx: labels are ignored when graphs are compared. Code MUST NOT be able to observe a label: the track function's context exposes `key`, never the label, and no handle returns it. The label lives only in the IR (`Module.label`, a lane's `name`).
- *Why:* renaming a track must never silence it or hand its data to another track, and a song that branched on its label would change its sound when renamed.
- *Today:* matches: the context exposes `key`, labels live only in the IR, and no handle returns one (tested in `core/script/src/tests.rs`). A label change changes no data file, graph or sound (`core/session/src/gate_tests.rs`). Since step 6 renaming a label in the app, `m2 label` or `POST /api/graph/label` is an `Identical` Tx on the `track()` call's name (`crates/editor/src/rename.rs`).

**ID-11.** The IR module is `Module { key, label, muted }` (`muted` is "mute in song", [MON-5](#monitoring-overlays-and-mute-in-song)). The key is used by the import op, flat node names, lane addresses, `m2 bounce --track` and `--place`, bounce provenance, `TimelineTrack.module` and UI selection. `slug()` is deleted.
- *Today:* since 8b.4 `Module.muted` is in the IR, written only when true, so an IR without it keeps its JSON and its hash (`a_song_without_muted_has_the_same_ir_hash`). The checker refuses it on `song` and on a module that isn't a track, and lists the muted tracks in `Flat.muted` (`core/ir/src/check.rs`).

### Derived identity

**ID-12.** A number local to one build (site ids, call ordinals, node numbers, `Origin`, control ids such as `song.ts#2/0.hats.pan`) MUST NOT be used with any revision but the one it came from. To use a target in a later revision, resolve it again from its source position.
- *Why:* one inserted call renumbers every later site in the project.
- *Today:* `Origin.site` counts across the whole project in load order (`core/script/src/lib.rs`). At `92a19a3`, `revert()` carried the byte range forward but passed the stale origin to `apply()` (`core/session/src/edit.rs:752-761`): after one soundless call was added to `song.ts`, undoing a knob in `tracks/bass.ts` failed on every retry while `can_undo` said true. Step 1 fixed this: a value's undo recorded no origin. It found the control written at the carried bytes in the accepted revision and used that control's current origin. Since step 6 an undo commits its change's stored inverse, which names no origin, and both undo and `can_undo` refuse when its bases no longer apply ([WRITE-21](#undo); `Session::undo` and `Session::can_undo` in `core/session/src/session.rs`).

**ID-13.** A site's identity across two revisions is its file and the point of its argument list's `(`, in the reference coordinates of the comparison ([WRITE-8](#expectations-and-the-delta)). Nothing numbers sites. A Tx is judged in its own edits' coordinates; every other comparison, the engine's included, uses `Edits::between`: a line diff whose hunks a token-level Myers diff refines. A `key()` frame is named by its key. An unmatched or ambiguous site is new, and its state resets.
- *Why:* the arena keeps state by identity, and guessing would hand one oscillator's state to another. A `(` survives a wrap, a changed callee and an edited argument, wherever a diff puts the closing brackets. A session counter would be history: two processes would number one revision's sites differently and hash one Tx's delta differently, and the engine only ever compares what plays with what is accepted.
- *Today:* matches since step 7: `Site.open` is each call's `(` (`core/script/src/sites.rs`), delta names spell a call by it (`Side::called` in `core/session/src/tx/delta.rs`), and `Edits::between` refines each run of changed lines by a Myers diff over identifiers and numbers, strings and single punctuation characters; when more than 500 lines differ (a whole file re-indented, say), the token diff takes every line between the common first and last lines. Each run of inserted or deleted tokens is slid to start at a word. When it repeats a call's `name(`, the old `(` goes to the call whose arguments match the old call's: the inner one for a wrap in the same callee, the outer one for a call of the same callee put inside it. When neither matches, both calls are new (`slide` and `pick` in `core/session/src/text.rs`). So an effect wrapped around an instrument keeps the instrument, through the app's Tx or a file save (G7b's wrapped instrument); Add Effect and Remove Effect keep the user's own effect beside another of the same kind (`crates/editor/src/effect_tests.rs`); and two literals edited on adjacent lines are two value changes. What still resets: a call cut and pasted, one whose own tokens a reformat reorders, and every call in a stretch of changed lines where more than 500 tokens differ. What can still keep the wrong call: a call of the same callee written on lines of its own, above another (`const pre = delay(hook, …)` above `delay(hook, …)`) or around it with the old call moved to the next line. There the line diff decides before the tokens are compared, and the new call takes the old one's instance. Sites are still numbered per build (ID-12), for the tables of one build only.

**ID-14.** For repeated unkeyed sites in one parent instance (loops, `map`), state is kept by index only while the occurrence count and the surrounding structure are unchanged. If the count changes, every occurrence resets. `key()` or a keyed iteration helper keeps state through reorders.
Applied when the engine matches instances; a `key()` frame has no occurrence; a plugin with a state path is matched by that path ([ID-15](#derived-identity)).
- *Why:* an index alone would hand state to a neighbour.
- *Today:* matches since step 7: each delta name carries how many times each positional segment's call ran in its parent and how many nodes share it but for its occurrence (`Facts.counts`), and `continues` keeps a pair only while those are the same (`core/session/src/tx/continues.rs`). std's mixer runs its strips in a `map`, so adding a channel resets every strip; that is inaudible today (`std:gain`'s smoother starts at its target, and pan holds no state). Keying each strip by its channel isn't done: `key()`'s keys are unique per key frame, not per component call, so two mixers in one module with a channel key in common would be `key.duplicate` (tried in 8b.3b). So a channel's first pan renames the panners after it, and its planner asserts by channel ([WRITE-10](#expectations-and-the-delta)). A plain function (not a `component()`) makes no frame, so when one parent calls it more than once, its calls are matched by call order: swapping two such calls swaps their state. Make it a `component()`, or wrap each call in `key()`, to keep them apart.

**ID-15.** Derived ids are built as follows:
- SDK frames record `{ site, componentId, occurrence, key? }`. The frame chain continues inside component bodies, packages included.
- InstancePath = module key / frame chain.
- ControlId = InstancePath + the slot the callee declares ([PKG-3](#component-descriptors)).
- RuntimeId: a plugin with a state path ([ID-8](#keys)) has its module key and that path as its RuntimeId, whatever its place. Any other node's RuntimeId is InstancePath / the site of its `kernel()`, `plugin()`, `poly()` or `feedback()` call, matched by the same rules as [ID-13](#derived-identity) and [ID-14](#derived-identity); segments are `key:<k>` or `<ComponentId>@<file>:<point>#<occurrence>`. A body that calls `kernel("std:svf")` twice has two sites, so two RuntimeIds. A voice's RuntimeId is its slot in its kept pool.
- CellId = a cell's index in the active plan.
- *Why:* the arena key must be defined by the same matching as every other site, with no separate notion of a kernel's "role".
- *Today:* since step 5 the IR's `Module.frames` lists every component call and `key()` frame a module's build evaluated, as `{ site, component, occurrence, key, parent }` (and, for a component call with options, which argument held them, `options`), and a node names its innermost frame (`Node.frame`); a declared control's const names its frame and slot in its origin (`Origin.control`), and its delta name, InstancePath / `@<slot>`, is its ControlId (`core/session/src/tx/delta.rs`). A call site is taken only by the call it was set for: the SDK compares the arguments it received with what the call's instrumentation recorded, so a function native code calls (`xs.map(lowpass)`) takes no site and makes no frame (`core/script/src/sites.rs`, `takeSite` in `core/script/sdk/core.ts`). Sites are still numbered per build (ID-12). The session groups the view and recognises effects by frame chains (`Module::chain` in `core/ir`, `calls_of` in `crates/editor/src/effect.rs`), and `Node.within` (the component sites of the chain) is deleted.

**ID-16.** A project file MUST NOT contain a derived id. The saved roots are module keys, lane keys, plugin keys and `key()` frames. An optional editor index may live in `.music2/`; it is rebuildable at any time and used only for layout continuity. Seeds and musical defaults MUST NOT be derived from derived ids. Every id-bearing field in the format v1 schemas is a key, a clip id or a CLAP parameter id, and strict parsing ([FMT-8](#strictness)) admits no other field.
- *Why:* derived ids change with every refactor; saved ones must not. A schema that has no field for a derived id is checkable; "no file contains one" by looking at the files is not.
- *Today:* matches: nothing derived is saved, and a test refuses a derived id in every schema (`core/song/src/data/tests.rs`).

### Renames
