**ID-17.** Renames work like this:
- **A label rename** in the UI is an `Identical` Tx.
- **A key rename** in the app is one Tx that edits the code and renames `data/<key>/`. It may also write `layout.json` ([WRITE-3](#the-two-write-primitives)).
- **A key rename by hand** leaves the data unclaimed: `data.unclaimed`, with a Relink fix ([DIAG-6](#fixes)): for a track key, of the whole `data/<key>/` folder; for a lane key, of one lane file.
- *Today:* matches for label renames and renames by hand: a label rename is an `Identical` Tx (step 6); a track key renamed in code leaves its folder `data.unclaimed`, offered whole as one `Rename` of the folder on its directory hash ("Move all to …"), and a lane key its lane file, in the app and in every CLI command that opens the project ([DIAG-4](#delivery), [DIAG-6](#fixes)). Restore isn't built.
- *Today, from polish (A5):* the app's key rename is built as **Change track ID…**, offered only in the Code view's file list (a track's file, right-click; `crates/editor/src/rekey.rs`, `POST /api/graph/track/rekey`, with `/api/graph/track/rekey/check` for the dialog's words as you type). One Tx renames the key in the `track()` call, the channel key when it equals the old track key (ID-18, said in its label), `data/<key>/` (a `Rename`), and the key in `layout.json` when it reads. The track's file and binding keep their names. Its expectation is in [WRITE-10](#expectations-and-the-delta). The server moves the track's `heights` and `hidden` in `.music2/view.json` on any delta with `Rekeyed` (the change, its undo, a rename by hand), and the page moves its selection and open clip. The track's instruments and plugins aren't carried over: a re-keyed track restarts its sounds (design-8b §11.3).

**ID-18.** Mixer channel keys are dictionary slots where the user's key is identity on purpose. The app writes the track key as the channel key of a new channel, so a new track's key is never one a channel already has, in any capitals (`a_new_key_skips_the_mixers_channel_keys`), but finds a track's channel by following the graph edge from the track's export into the mixer, never by comparing keys. A key rename Tx renames a channel key only when it equals the old track key, and says so in its label.
- Following the edge means: the mixer is the song's one frame whose ComponentId is `std:mixer`; its channels are the properties of the object its call is given in the project's code. From each audio export of a track that the song reads, the edges go downstream from audio outputs, through the song's nodes outside the mixer (a duck, a compress), to the first node inside it: the channel's panner or gain, which reads that channel's `pan` or `db` (`channels.kick.db`). An edge into a sidechain input only listens, so it isn't followed (PKG-13). A track in no channel, or in two, has no fader.
- *Why:* a channel whose key differs from its track's (written by hand, or after a rename) must still be found, and so must one written as a name bound elsewhere (`kick: k`).
- *Today:* matches since 8b.3 (`crates/editor/src/mix.rs`, `mix_tests.rs`), when every channel got a gain (PKG-5), so every strip has a node that reads its channel's controls. Only the song's own nodes are followed: a track that another track reads (a bus) reaches the mixer as that track. A panner whose `pan` is a signal passes the walk on to the gain after it; a strip whose `db` is a signal and that has no panner reads none of its channel's controls, so there the read's place in the channel's property says which. The track builders edit a channel's property, so they refuse one that doesn't name its track (`kick: k`), saying why. No mixer, two, or one whose channels aren't an object written in the project's code, and the track builders refuse to add a channel, saying so.

## Project format v1

### Layout

```
music2.toml                  [project] name, entry, format = 1, sample_rate = 48000
                             [dependencies] "@m2/core" = "1", std = "0.2", "@alice/felt-piano" = "1.2"
music2.lock                  the exact version and hash of each pack the song uses (PACK-8)
song.ts  tracks/*.ts  lib/   code
data/arrangement.json        {"tempo": [{"beat", "bpm"}], "meter": [{"beat", "beats", "unit"}]}   a beat is a quarter note
data/<module>/<lane>.json    one lane's clips
data/song/<lane>.json        lanes of the root module
data/<module>/<frames>/<plugin-key>.state   a CLAP state blob, with its .state.json sidecar
assets/<path>                audio files, never overwritten; <path>.json is its note: a bounce's provenance, or where a sample came from (LIB-7)
layout.json                  shared presentation, keyed by track key; never part of a revision
.music2/                     local and gitignored: overlays, view settings (view.json), lock, tx journal, editor index
AGENTS.md  CLAUDE.md         for agents working on the song (FMT-23); never read by the build, never part of a revision
<cache folder>/music2/       outside the project, in the OS's cache folder (FMT-16): decoded and warped renders, the hash cache
```

**FMT-1.** Projects MUST use this layout. This app has no reader for format 0, today's layout: the 5 fixtures are converted once by script ([FMT-21](#conversion)). Formats after 1 follow [FMT-3a](#manifest-and-versions).
- *Why:* every project's bytes on disk are cheapest to fix before projects exist in the wild.
- *Today:* matches since step 4: the 5 fixtures are converted, and a project without `format` is `format.missing`.

### Manifest and versions

```toml
[project]
name = "Starter beat"   # required: the project's label
entry = "song.ts"       # optional, default "song.ts"
format = 1              # required: the version of the file layout
sample_rate = 48000     # required: the rate the engine runs and exports at

[dependencies]
"@m2/core" = "1"        # required: the SDK version the code is written against
std = "0.2"             # every package the code imports from must be listed
"@alice/felt-piano" = "1.2"                   # a pack from the store (PACK-7)
"@bob/strings" = { path = "../strings" }      # a pack in a folder, for its author trying it
```

**FMT-2.** `format` versions only the file layout. `[dependencies] "@m2/core"` versions only the code API. A data addition bumps only `format`; an SDK break bumps only `@m2/core`. Each reader checks only its own field. A requirement follows Cargo's caret rule: `"0.1"` means at least 0.1.0 and below 0.2.0, and `"1"` means at least 1.0.0 and below 2.0.0. A package declares its own version in `m2pkg.toml`.
- *Why:* the two change for different reasons and at different times, and a requirement needs one meaning.
- *Today:* matches since step 4 (`core/song/src/manifest.rs`).

**FMT-3.** Opening MUST read `[project] format` first, on its own and leniently, before anything else in the manifest. A missing `format` is `format.missing` (a format-0 project: convert it with the script), an older one `format.older`, a newer one `format.newer`. In each case the project does not open, and the message says what to do. Then the build checks every `[dependencies]` entry: an unsatisfied `@m2/core` is `sdk.unsatisfied`, any other unsatisfied package `pkg.unsatisfied`, each naming the required and the installed version. An import from a package not listed is the compile error `pkg.undeclared`.
- The app bundles one std. A std release that changes the bits of any existing render MUST bump its version (the minor version while it is 0.x), and CI checks this against the fixtures' recorded renders. So an old project gets `pkg.unsatisfied`, never a silent change in its sound. For a song that asks for an older std than the bundled one, the message also says how to move: the requirement to write, and that the song may sound a little different. The first std release, after people have songs, that changes existing renders is the trigger for the lockfile and for shipping several std versions ([Deferred](#deferred-with-triggers)).
- *Why:* an old app must never rewrite a newer project, a strict parse must not hide a newer format behind `manifest.invalid`, and code must never run against an SDK or a std it wasn't written for.
- *Today:* matches since step 4: `format` is read first, `sdk.unsatisfied` and `pkg.unsatisfied` are checked at compile, and `pkg.undeclared` by the build. A test records each pure fixture's render under std's version and fails when one changes while the version stays (`fixture_renders_change_only_with_stds_version`, `core/session/tests/renders.json`); it first says which fixture doesn't build, and why. std 0.2 (the `sounds` merge, 2026-10-17) changed no existing render: it bumped because its new components and the effects' `playing` input are new API, which a song written for them must not meet in std 0.1 (the owner's choice). Every project the repo ships moved with it, and `every_shipped_manifest_is_met` (`core/session/src/lib.rs`) checks that the bundled std meets every `music2.toml` under `examples`, `templates`, `core/session/tests/fixtures` and `e2e/fixtures`. One std is bundled; no lockfile yet, as no one had songs. The hint in `pkg.unsatisfied` (`check_dependencies` in `core/song/src/manifest.rs`, with `Requirement::admitting`) comes only for std, and only when the bundled std is newer than the song admits, and first, since the app shows a message on one line cut at the window's edge: "This app has std 0.5. To use it, change the std line in music2.toml to std = "0.5". The song may sound a little different." (`unmet_dependencies`, `unmet_dependencies_block`).

**FMT-3a.** After format 1, a format or SDK break MUST ship with a migrator outside core (`m2 migrate`). Migrating is explicit, journaled like a Tx, and never applied on open. The app keeps the previous `@m2/core` major for at least one release after a break.
- *Why:* "no backwards compatibility" holds only while the fixtures are the only projects. Once people have songs, a break must not orphan them.

**FMT-4.** After `format`, the manifest MUST be read strictly: unknown keys are the error `manifest.invalid`. It blocks acceptance (the last accepted revision keeps playing, or on open nothing plays), but never `save()`, so the manifest can be fixed.
- *Why:* the lenient reader drops unknown fields when a file is rewritten.
- *Today:* matches since step 4 (`core/song/src/manifest.rs`).

### Paths

```rust
enum DataPath {
    Manifest, Lock, Code(PathBuf), Arrangement, Module { module: Key }, Lane { module: Key, lane: Key },
    PluginState { module: Key, path: Vec<Key> }, Asset(AssetName), Presentation, Local, Unknown,
}
impl DataPath { fn path(&self) -> PathBuf; fn parse(p: &Path) -> DataPath; fn class(&self) -> Class }
```

| Path | `DataPath` | Class | Plane |
|---|---|---|---|
| `music2.toml` | `Manifest` | Manifest | code |
| `music2.lock` | `Lock` | Manifest | code |
| `*.ts` outside `data/`, `assets/`, `.music2/` and `node_modules/` | `Code` | Code | code |
| `data/arrangement.json` | `Arrangement` | Arrangement | arrangement |
| `data/<module>/`, the folder itself | `Module` | Data | data (renamed only by a `Rename` op) |
| `data/<module>/<lane>.json` | `Lane` | Data | data; prepared data for an audio lane |
| `data/<module>/…/<plugin-key>.state` and its `.state.json` | `PluginState` | Data | prepared data |
| `assets/<path>` | `Asset` | Data | prepared data |
| `layout.json` | `Presentation` | Presentation | none |
| `.music2/**`, a commit's staging folder `.music2/tx/<id>/` included | `Local` | Ignored | none |
| anything else, `.js` and `.mjs` files included | `Unknown` | Ignored | none |

**FMT-5.** `core/song` MUST be the only place a project path is spelled or classified, through `DataPath`. The canonical lane writer moves there, beside the reader.
- *Why:* every copy of a path rule drifts.
- *Today:* matches since step 4: paths are spelled only by `DataPath` (`core/song/src/path.rs`), and lane files are written by `m2_song::canonical`.

**FMT-6.** A snapshot MUST read only Manifest (`music2.toml` and `music2.lock`), Code, Arrangement and Lane files and `.state.json` sidecars. Assets and state blobs are not part of a snapshot; revisions record their hashes.
- *Why:* only those files define the song, and binary files are too big to snapshot.
- *Today:* matches since step 4: revisions record the hashes of the assets their lanes name and of state blobs (`core/song/src/lib.rs`).

**FMT-6a.** The build MUST read project code only from the snapshot. A project import resolves only against the snapshot's Code paths, byte for byte (the path, then with `.ts`, then `/index.ts`), with no filesystem probe and no case folding. Any other import (a `.js` file, anything under `data/`, `assets/` or `.music2/`, a path outside the project, or a spelling that differs in case) is the compile error `import.outside-code`, naming the file. Package files are read from disk, and each revision records the content hash of every package file its build read ([RENDER-2](#offline)). A package import that leaves its package (the package it names, or the importing module's own for a relative import, never another one loaded beside it), passes through a symbolic link, or differs in case from the file on disk, is also `import.outside-code`; a package file that simply doesn't exist is `code.build`.
- *Why:* a revision must be a function of its snapshot. A file read from disk would change what compiles without making a revision, and a case-insensitive disk would compile `./Tracks/bass` on a Mac and fail it on Linux CI.
- *Today:* matches since step 4: project code comes only from the snapshot, and every revision records the package files its build read (`core/script/src/lib.rs`).

**FMT-7.** A file under `data/` that `DataPath` can't parse raises the Notice `file.unknown`. Other unknown files are ignored silently.
- *Why:* a typo in a data path should be visible; a README should not.
- *Today:* matches since step 4, except that the notice reaches a running session only with its next revision, since the unknown file alone makes none ([PLANE-3](#planes-of-change)).

### Strictness

**FMT-8.** The manifest, lane files, clips, notes, the arrangement and state sidecars MUST reject unknown fields.
- *Why:* a lenient reader silently drops what it doesn't know when the file is rewritten.
- *Today:* matches since step 4 (`core/song`).

**FMT-8a.** Data files MUST have one canonical form, written by the canonical writer in `core/song`: a fixed key order per schema; numbers in their shortest round-trip form, never `-0`; one clip per line and one note per line; clips sorted by `(start, id)` and notes by `(start, pitch)`. Readers put clips and notes in the same order before use, so the order of an array never changes the sound; overlapping control clips with the same start are decided by id, never by file order. Reading and then writing a canonical file gives the same bytes.
- *Why:* files must diff cleanly, two writers must agree byte for byte, and a sort, a merge or a hand reorder must never change the sound with no value changed.
- *Today:* matches since step 4 (`core/song/src/lane.rs`); a test checks that every fixture's data files are canonical (`core/song/tests/fixtures.rs`). The reader and writer were made about 3 and 6 times faster for G11 (no copies of each clip, a pitch read without an error made per name, the sort's lines made only for ties, one buffer); the old ones are kept as test oracles (`core/song/src/lane/oracle.rs`), and the fast ones give the same data, the same errors and the same bytes on every lane file in the repository and on generated ones (`the_fast_read_is_the_old_read`, `canonical_bytes_are_unchanged`).

**FMT-9.** Only manifest, code and arrangement errors block acceptance. The arrangement is a deliberate addition to the plan's "only code and manifest", because every clip's timing depends on it. A lane file that fails to parse, or doesn't match its declaration, MUST NOT stop anything else, and raises an Error (`lane.invalid` or `lane.unit-mismatch`) that names the file, the field and the fix:
- when the session has accepted data for that lane, the last accepted data keeps playing, and the message says so;
- when it has none (on open, or for a new lane), the lane plays empty.

A file or folder that can't be read at all (permissions, an I/O error) is reported with the code of its class, and the message carries the system's error: `lane.invalid` for a lane file or a folder under `data/` (including one no lane reads), `state.unverified` for a state blob, its sidecar or a folder holding them, `arrangement.invalid` when `data/` itself can't be listed, `code.build` for a code file or a folder that may hold code, and `manifest.invalid` when the project folder can't be read. No separate code is needed: the fix is the same as for a file that doesn't parse.

Export renders the accepted revision, as it does for code.
- *Why:* one bad hand edit must not silence the whole song, and a lane file caught half-written must not drop out of a running session.
- *Today:* matches since step 4 (`Project::data` in `core/song/src/data.rs`, given the last accepted data by the session).

**FMT-10.** Lane files carry no `kind`. Notes and audio clips fail as the wrong shape, and a file with no clips fits any kind. Control lanes record their `unit`; a unit that differs from the declaration raises `lane.unit-mismatch` with Retag and Convert fixes.
- *Why:* the kind is in the code; writing it twice invites disagreement.

### Lane files and clip ids

```json
{"clips": [{"id": "k3f9q2zt", "start": 0, "length": 16,
            "notes": [{"pitch": "A1", "start": 0, "length": 0.5, "velocity": 0.9}]}]}
```

```json
{"unit": "hz", "clips": [{"id": "q7m2c8xa", "start": 32, "length": 8, "points": [[0, 500], [8, 2500]]}]}
```

```json
{"clips": [{"id": "z1b4r9td", "start": 64, "length": 4, "asset": "vox/lead.wav", "hash": "9f2c3a1be07d44c1",
            "offset": 0, "gain_db": -3, "fade_in": 0.01, "fade_out": 0.05, "reverse": false, "pitch": 0, "warp": []}]}
```

Notes, control (masks are control lanes in `unit01`) and audio lanes, in that order. Fields are format 0's plus `id`, `unit` and `hash` (the first 16 hex digits of the asset's SHA-256). They are read and written in `core/song/src/lane.rs`.

**FMT-11.** A clip id is chosen by whoever builds the op that writes the clip, and travels in the Tx; core never mints one during `check` or `commit`. Ids are 8 characters from the lowercase Crockford base32 alphabet (`0-9a-z` without `i`, `l`, `o`, `u`). A builder outside a sandbox MAY draw them at random. A sandboxed package tool, the CLI and a fix derive them: the first 8 Crockford characters of SHA-256(`tx.id` ‖ lane address ‖ n), so `tx.id` is the only random input. Readers MUST accept any id matching `^[0-9a-z]{1,16}$`.
- *Why:* random ids survive merges, other processes and git. A sandbox has no randomness, `check` and `commit` must write the same bytes, and a scripted run given its `tx.id` must be reproducible.
- *Today:* matches since step 4. The app draws ids at random; the CLI and fixes derive them from SHA-256 of the Tx id, a NUL, the lane address `module/lane`, a NUL and `n` in decimal (`ClipId::derive` in `core/song/src/key.rs`).

**FMT-12.** A clip id is unique within its lane, and a clip's address is `(LaneAddr, ClipId)`. Moving a clip to another lane keeps its id. Copying or duplicating a clip always mints a new id, and a writer that finds its id taken in the target lane mints another.
- A clip without an id is anonymous: it can be addressed only within one revision, and raises the Notice `clip.anonymous` with an AssignClipIds fix.
- An id that breaks the rule, including one that isn't a string, is read as anonymous and kept as written, and raises the Notice `clip.invalid-id` with the same fix. "Kept as written" keeps the JSON value; the canonical writer may re-space it (`{"a": 1}` becomes `{"a":1}`).
- A duplicate id raises the Warning `clip.duplicate-id` with a RenumberClips fix, never a parse failure.
- Ids are never assigned on read. Note ids come later.
- A builder that writes a lane gives every clip of it without a usable id (none, one that breaks the rule, or one an earlier clip of the lane has) a fresh one, so `clip.anonymous` clears where the producer edits.
- *Why:* one hand edit must never make a lane unreadable.
- *Today:* matches since 8b.5 for clip gestures (`crates/editor/src/clips.rs`; `touching_a_lane_gives_its_anonymous_clips_ids`, `a_move_onto_a_taken_id_mints_one`, `every_copy_mints`). A gesture names an anonymous clip by where it starts and its rank there (`at`). The piano roll's whole-lane write (`notes.rs`) gives ids only to the clips it was sent without one.

### Arrangement

**FMT-13.** `data/arrangement.json` holds `tempo` (`[{beat, bpm}]`) and `meter` (`[{beat, beats, unit}]`, the time signature `beats`/`unit`). A beat is a quarter note.
- Tempo: the first entry is at beat 0, each is 20–999 bpm, and beats strictly increase.
- Meter: an empty list means 4/4 from beat 0; otherwise the first entry is at beat 0. `beats` is 1 to 64, and `unit` is 1, 2, 4, 8, 16 or 32. A bar lasts `beats × 4 / unit` quarter notes. Each change falls on a bar line of the meter before it, and beats strictly increase.
- Anything else raises `arrangement.invalid`, naming the entry, and blocks acceptance.
- *Why:* time signatures are needed for bars, plugins and the tempo bar, and every reader must count bars the same way. Two entries at one beat would be decided by file order.
- *Today:* matches since step 4 (`core/song/src/arrangement.rs`).

### Assets and hashes

**FMT-14.** An asset MUST never be overwritten: a new version gets a new name, and assets are created with create-only semantics (`O_EXCL`). An asset name is a relative path under `assets/`, with `/` between folders. It is NFC, and has no empty, `.` or `..` segment, no segment that starts with `.`, and no `\`, `:` or control character. A name with a `:` is a package's file instead ([PACK-5](#packs)), so the two never collide. An import whose name is taken gets a numeric suffix (`kick 2.wav`). An asset is always inside the project: importing copies the file. `m2 bounce --force` is removed.
- *Why:* an overwritten asset can't be undone and silently changes every clip that uses it. Sample-heavy projects need folders, and a reference outside the project breaks when the project moves.
- *Today:* matches for what writes assets today: `m2 bounce` creates its asset create-only and `--force` is gone, an exact render that `m2 render` or Export Audio writes into `assets/` is created, never written over (`Render::write_for` in `crates/editor/src/render.rs`, since step 6), a Tx's `Write` of an asset must have the base `Absent`, and names are checked as `AssetName`s (`core/song/src/path.rs`). Since `studio`, an import (File ▸ Import Audio…, a drop, `m2 import`) copies the file in create-only under the first free name, `kick 2.wav` when `kick.wav` is taken, and the same bytes already there are used, not copied again (`crates/editor/src/import.rs`: `an_import_copies_the_file_create_only`, `a_taken_name_gets_a_number`, `the_same_bytes_use_the_asset_there`). An audio take is written into `assets/` the same way ([RENDER-30a](#audio-lanes)).

**FMT-15.** A clip's asset reference carries a `hash` pin: the first 16 hex digits of the SHA-256 of the file's bytes. The app always writes it.
- A reference without a pin is read, and raises the Notice `asset.unpinned` with a RepinAsset fix.
- A pin that differs from the file raises the Warning `asset.changed` with a RepinAsset fix, and the clip plays the file as it is.
- A reference MUST equal an entry of `assets/` byte for byte, found by listing the folder, never by `open()`. A name that differs only in case is `asset.missing`, with a fix that rewrites the reference to the exact name. A file whose name on disk isn't NFC can't be referenced at all ([FMT-14](#assets-and-hashes)), so a name that differs only in Unicode normalisation is `asset.missing` whose message says to rename the file to its NFC form; no fix renames the file yet; the message says to.
- A missing or unreadable asset raises the Error `asset.missing`, and that clip is silent.
- *Why:* an asset changed behind the project's back must be visible, and a case-insensitive disk must not make a clip play on a Mac and go silent elsewhere.
- *Today:* matches since step 4 (`core/song/src/data.rs`).

**FMT-16.** A hash cache keyed by `(path, size, mtime, ctime, inode)`, in the OS's cache folder (`m2_os::dirs::cache`: `~/Library/Caches/music2/` on macOS, `~/.cache/music2/` on Linux), refreshed by the watcher, covers assets and state blobs. An entry whose mtime lies within the volume's timestamp resolution of the moment it was cached is unverified, and is hashed again on next use (git's racy-timestamp rule). Compile MUST NOT decode audio.
- *Why:* every compile decoding every WAV makes data edits slow. On exFAT, with 2-second timestamps, a same-length file rewritten in place would otherwise keep its old hash.
- *Today:* matches since step 4, with the racy window at 2 s on every volume, since a volume's resolution can't be read portably. The CLI and the app keep the cache in `hashes.json` there (`core/song/src/hash.rs`).

### Plugin state and parameters

**FMT-17.** A plugin's state is the CLAP blob in `data/<module>/<frames>/<plugin-key>.state`, with a required sidecar `<plugin-key>.state.json` that holds exactly `{plugin, plugin_version}`: no parameter values and no hash. A missing state file means the plugin's defaults. A blob whose sidecar names another plugin than the code does raises the Error `state.plugin-mismatch`; a blob without a sidecar raises the Error `state.unverified`. Either way the blob isn't loaded, the plugin plays its defaults, and the fixes are Relink (when another key now has that plugin) and DeleteData. A state write always writes both files in one Tx.
- *Why:* state belongs to its module's data, and parameter values belong to code or the blob, never both ([HOME-4](#homes-of-values)). One plugin's state loaded into another crashes many plugins, and a hash beside the blob is a second truth that a merge can split from it.
- *Today:* matches since step 6: `m2 plugin-state` writes the blob and its sidecar as one Tx, undone as one step (`plugin_state` in `crates/editor/src/data.rs`).

**FMT-18.** A plugin parameter is addressed in code as `<name>_<id>`: its display name, lowercased, with other characters turned to `_`, then its CLAP parameter id (`mix_12`). The build binds by the id alone, and the name part is a label: when it differs from the plugin's current display name, that is the Notice `plugin.param-renamed`, never a rebind. A name without an id (`mix`) is the compile error `plugin.param-unaddressed`, which suggests the full address. Editors and Tx builders always write the full form. The IR stores CLAP parameter ids, and the runtime binds by id.
- *Why:* a display name is not identity (P1). A plugin update that renames parameters, swaps two names or adds one with a clashing name must not rewire anything, and code that compiled must keep compiling.
- *Today:* matches since step 4. The IR keeps each address as written; `PackageSet::bind_plugins` binds it by its id before the check, so the runtime binds by id (`core/runtime/src/packages.rs`).

**FMT-18a.** A plugin that isn't installed MUST NOT stop the song. It raises the Error `plugin.missing`, which doesn't block acceptance. Its node is typed from the code as written: stereo audio in and out, and each `<name>_<id>` port a plain control. An instrument (no `in`) outputs silence; an effect passes its `in` through to its `out`. Its code and its state blob are never rewritten. An export made while a plugin is missing carries `plugin.missing`.
- *Why:* a project opened on a collaborator's Mac, in CI or in an agent's sandbox without one plugin must play everything else, as in every DAW.
- *Today:* matches since step 4 (`core/runtime/src/packages.rs`; the node in `core/runtime/src/lib.rs`).

### Presentation and local files

**FMT-19.** `layout.json` holds shared presentation keyed by track key: track order and colour, markers, later canvas positions. It is never part of a revision and never read by the build. Unknown keys in it are ignored.
- Its schema: `order`, the track keys top to bottom; `tracks.<key>.colour`, one of the palette's twelve names (a `#rrggbb` written by hand reads as the nearest name by hue, and is rewritten as a name when that track's colour is next written); `markers`, a list of `{"beat", "name"}`, sorted by beat, one a beat, each name one line of 1 to 40 characters; `key`, the song's musical key, `{"root": "A", "scale": "minor"}` (roots in ASCII: `C`, `F#`, `Bb`; `major` or `minor`), which nothing that plays reads.
- A key that names no track (one commented out of the song for a while) is ignored when read and kept when the file is written: in `order` after the key before it, in `tracks` with its colour. Only deleting the track removes its key. Unknown keys are written back as read, in the order read. The canonical form is two-space JSON with `order`, `tracks` and `markers` first. Markers are written by `POST /api/graph/markers` (the whole list) and the key by `POST /api/graph/song-key`, not by `/api/graph/layout`; each is a layout-only Tx, one undo step with no revision.
- The server reads it, watches it, and publishes an outside change with no revision. A Tx that writes it is based on what the disk says when it is planned, since no snapshot holds it, so an outside write in between is a conflict.
- A file that doesn't parse is `layout.invalid`: the view shows the defaults; the track builders leave it out of their Tx; reorder, recolour and markers refuse; Reset layout writes the defaults over it as one undo step.
- *Why:* presentation must never trigger a compile or change the sound. A git merge that leaves conflict markers in it must cost order or colour, never the gestures that make tracks.
- *Today:* matches since 8b.1 for order and colour (`crates/editor/src/layout.rs`; the watch is `Graph::watch`'s stat of the file, `crates/server/src/graph.rs`: with file-system events, when an event names it or a tick walks everything, and then every tick until it holds still, so an idle project is watched with no stat, as WRITE-19 says (`an_idle_project_is_watched_without_a_stat`); by walks alone, every tick. An outside edit shows within a second: s8-tracks measured 87 ms on Linux's inotify). The effective order is the stored order of the tracks there are, then the mixer's channel order, then the order the build made them, never alphabetical; with no colour stored, colours go by place in the palette's default order; a track without one gets the first colour no track uses, or the least used; and every layout write writes every track's colour, so the first one freezes them all (`layout_tests.rs`). Markers are read, shown on the ruler and written since polish (B5, `crates/editor/src/markers.rs`), and so is the key (D2, `crates/editor/src/songkey.rs`); the timeline view publishes both (`markers`, `key`).

**FMT-20.** `.music2/` is local to one machine. New projects MUST get a `.gitignore` line for it. Caches live outside the project ([RENDER-30](#audio-lanes), [FMT-16](#assets-and-hashes)). A project in a synced folder (iCloud Drive, Dropbox) works with these rules:
- The app marks `.music2/` for the sync services that honour a marker (Dropbox's ignore attribute), and raises the Notice `project.synced` when the project is under a known synced folder.
- The lock file and every journal record the host name. A journal from another host is reported as `tx.foreign`, with no fix ([WRITE-16](#commit-journal-and-recovery)).
- Another machine editing the project is an outside writer ([WRITE-19](#outside-writers)).
- *Why:* overlays, locks, journals and caches are not the song, and a lock or a journal synced from another machine means nothing on this one.
- `.music2/view.json` holds this machine's view settings of the song: `loop`, and the page's `zoom` (2 to 192 px a beat), `follow`, `heights` (by track key, and `track.lane` for an automation lane), `hidden` (lanes, as `track.lane`), `grid` (each view's grid and snap) and `audition`. The page writes them with `POST /api/graph/view` (`{session, set}`: a map merged in, `null` takes a key out; only those keys), one lock for every write of the file. A new song, or a copy made by Save As, starts with the defaults. Since `studio` it also holds `guide: {open, folded, done}` (Help ▸ Make Your First Song). The dock and the browser panel stay per window, in the browser's storage. `~/.music2/settings.json` is this computer's, for every song: the output and its buffer, where new songs go, `record` (the count-in, snap), `midi: {off: [names]}` (keyboards turned off, by the names `m2_os::midi` gives) and, since track io, `input` (the sound input and hearing yourself, [MON-8](#monitoring-overlays-and-mute-in-song)).
- *Today:* new projects, from the app and `m2 new`, get a `.gitignore` with `.music2/`, and `.music2/` is Local to `DataPath` (step 4). Since step 6 the first writer also puts a `.gitignore` of `*` in `.music2/`, and the lock file and every journal record the host name, with the machine id (`m2_os::host::machine_id`: the hardware UUID on macOS, `/etc/machine-id` on Linux) as what tells another host's journal (`core/session/src/lock.rs`; the step 6 design's C18). The sync marker and `project.synced` are not built. `~/.music2/session.json` is per user, not per project (`apps/music2-desktop/src/main.rs`), and so is `~/.music2/recent.json`, the recent songs (newest first, 20 kept), whether the newest was open when the app quit (`open`), and the guard that keeps a song that crashed the app at launch from opening again (`reopening`). Only the app and the headless server write it (`crates/server/src/recent.rs`). So is `~/.music2/settings.json` since `next` (gaps #31): the sound output (by its id, with its name), its buffer size and where new songs go, this machine's, never a song's, with no undo, applied at once (`crates/server/src/settings.rs`). The metronome is a view setting, per song, in `.music2/view.json` (`metronome`).

**FMT-23.** A new project made from a template or an example (New Song and Examples in the app, `m2 new`) MUST get two files for agents, unless what it is copied from has its own: `AGENTS.md`, the text of `templates/song-agents.md` with its one line that starts `m2 for this song: ` naming the exact path of the `m2` found, and `CLAUDE.md`, the line `@AGENTS.md`. The `m2` named is, in order: the running program when it is `m2`; `m2` beside the running program, unless the program runs from inside a mounted AppImage (under `$APPDIR`, a folder that goes away when the app quits); the source tree's `target/release/m2`, then `target/debug/m2`; the source tree's `target/release/m2` not built yet, with the command that builds it; else the line stays as the template has it. Save As copies a song as it is and adds nothing. The build never reads either file, and neither is part of a revision. `music2.app` MUST carry `m2` beside its own program (`Contents/MacOS/m2`), and the Linux packages beside theirs (`/usr/bin/m2` in the `.deb` and in the AppImage), built by the same `tauri build` that makes the app ([PLAT-8](#platforms)).
- *Why:* an agent opened in a song's folder reads `AGENTS.md` (Claude Code reads `CLAUDE.md`, which imports it) before anything else; it must learn there how to work on the song, and where its `m2` is, since `m2` is on no PATH.
- *Today:* built in the docs plan (`docsite`). `project::fill` writes both files after the copy (`crates/server/src/project.rs`, which embeds `templates/song-agents.md`); `resources::m2_program` finds the `m2` (`crates/server/src/resources.rs`). The app's `beforeBundleCommand` runs `apps/music2-desktop/bundle-m2.sh`, and `bundle.macOS.files` puts its `m2` at `Contents/MacOS/m2`; since the release track, `bundle.linux.deb.files` and `bundle.linux.appimage.files` put it at `/usr/bin/m2`, and a program in a mounted AppImage names none (`find_m2`'s `appdir`). A test pins the config to the finder. Tested in `project.rs`'s and `resources.rs`'s tests and `apps/m2/tests/docs.rs`.

### Conversion

**FMT-21.** A throwaway script converts the 5 fixtures from format 0 to format 1. Core MUST NOT contain migration code; later migrations live in `m2 migrate` ([FMT-3a](#manifest-and-versions)).
- *Why:* there are no users yet, so there is nothing to migrate but fixtures. Once there are, migrating is an explicit tool, never a side effect of opening.

MIDI files are converted at the edge, and never kept in the song. Since `studio`, `crates/editor/src/smf.rs` reads Standard MIDI Files of format 0 and 1 in ticks a beat, refuses the rest in words, and writes format 1 at 960 ticks a beat, outside the song only (Exports, or a file picked; a file picked inside the open song's folder is refused, whoever asks). A MIDI part is a track and a channel with notes: channel 10 plays the Drum kit, the others a std sound by General MIDI's families. An import into a lane keeps the song's tempo; one as new tracks (track pm: File ▸ Import MIDI…, a `.mid` drop) asks first when the tempos differ (`crates/editor/src/midifile.rs`).

### Presets

**FMT-22.** A preset is a sound's settings under a name: one JSON file, `<name>.json`, two-space, its keys in this order: `m2preset` (the format's version, `1`; a newer one is refused in words), `name`, `sound`, `values`, and for a plugin `plugin_version` and `state` (its CLAP state, base64).
- `name`: one line of 1 to 40 characters, none of `/ \ : * ? " < > |`, not starting with a dot, no space at either end and no dot at the end, so it is a file name on every OS. A name taken in other capitals is taken, as for songs ([PLAT-7](#platforms)).
- `sound`: what it is for, a ComponentId (`std:synth-pluck`) or a plugin's id (`clap:<id>`). A preset's identity is its `sound` and its `name`, never its folder (P1): a file whose `sound` isn't the call's is left out of that call's list.
- `values`: numbers by name: a std control by its slot, in base units (`{"brightness": 1800}`); a plugin's parameter by its CLAP id ([FMT-18](#plugin-state-and-parameters)).
- Where: yours in `m2_os::dirs::presets()` (`~/Music/music2/Presets`, under `$HOME`, [PLAT-7](#platforms)), in a folder per sound named from its id, `<package>/<name>` (`std/synth-pluck`; every character outside `A–Z a–z 0–9 . _ -` turned to `_`). std's own in `std/presets/<component>/`, shipped with the app, and a pack's own in its folder the same way, `presets/<component>/` ([PACK-11](#packs)); a scoped sound's folder of yours is `_alice_felt-piano/felt-piano`, never nested. A plugin's own come from the plugin, never from a file music2 writes.
- Saving writes that file and no song file: no Tx, no Undo. It is written to a temporary file, then renamed. It holds every declared control of the call that holds a number (a dictionary's are left out): the numbers written, and the defaults of the ones the call leaves out. A control a lane or other code drives is left out, and named. A plugin's holds the state its instance that plays saves now, and the parameters its code writes as numbers.
- Loading is one `Values` Tx ([WRITE-6](#expectations-and-the-delta)), one Undo step. A number written in the call is rewritten through its lens, keeping its spelling (`khz(0.9)`). A control the call leaves out is written in ([PKG-3](#component-descriptors)), several as one insertion. A control the preset doesn't name keeps its value, so a preset from an older std still loads. A control a lane or other code drives, a value other calls share, and one written in other units keep what they have, and are named. A preset for another sound is refused in words, and nothing is written.
- A plugin's loads as one `Confined` Tx: its state and sidecar ([FMT-17](#plugin-state-and-parameters)), and each parameter its code writes as a number rewritten. A parameter the code doesn't write is the state's. Then the usual state load reaches the instance that plays ([RENDER-26b](#plans-data-and-arrangement-swaps)). Its plugin needs a key (its state file, [HOME-6](#homes-of-values)), else it is refused in words. The plugin's own presets load the same way, made on an instance of their own ([HOST-8](#pluginhost)).
- *Why:* one file to copy or share; a folder that is easy to find and back up, not hidden like `~/.music2`. A load is a gesture like a knob's, so its Undo and its checks are a knob's.
- *Today:* matches for std's instruments and effects since track br (`crates/editor/src/presets.rs`; `GET /api/graph/presets`, `POST /api/graph/preset/save` and `/load` in `crates/server/src/routes_presets.rs`; the card's **Presets** in `web/src/components/PresetPopover.tsx`). std ships ten: two each for Synth pluck, Synth pad, Synth bass, Reverb and Delay (`every_factory_preset_loads`). A plugin's since track br's slice 3: `capture_plugin` and `load_plugin` (`saving_a_plugin_preset_keeps_its_state_and_code_values`, `loading_a_plugin_preset_is_one_undo_step`, `a_preset_for_another_plugin_is_refused_in_words` in `crates/server/src/graph/presets_tests.rs`); a load's state writes are `data::plugin_state`'s. A plugin that marks itself dirty after a load makes no second Undo step, as long as the state it saves then is the one loaded (the probe's is).

## Writes

### The two write primitives

**WRITE-1.** Every write to a project file through music2 MUST be a `save()` or a `Tx`, except creating a new asset with create-only semantics ([WRITE-18](#the-project-lock)). A file music2 creates outside a Tx (a new asset, a bounce's provenance, a new song's files) is made durable, its bytes pushed and its folder synced, before anything names it. These bypasses close: `write_whole` becomes private, `PUT /api/graph/file` becomes `save()`, `m2 bounce --place` becomes a data Tx, and `m2 plugin-state` becomes a Tx that writes the state and its sidecar. That Tx may replace existing state as one undoable step, so `m2 plugin-state --force` goes away.
- *Why:* each bypass skips the lock, the journal or the diagnostics.
- *Today:* matches since step 6: the only writer is the commit's staging (`core/session/src/tx/stage.rs`); `PUT /api/graph/file` is `Session::save` (`crates/server/src/files.rs`); `m2 bounce --place` and `m2 plugin-state` commit Tx, and `--force` is gone (`crates/editor/src/data.rs`, `apps/m2/src/main.rs`); an exact render into `assets/` is created, never written over. `the_write_bypasses_are_closed` (`crates/editor/tests/crate_graph.rs`) keeps it so.

**WRITE-2.** `save(file, base_sha, text)` is a draft write: compare-and-swap on `base_sha`, then an atomic write. It is never rejected for errors. The change is then taken in like an outside write ([WRITE-19](#outside-writers)), and recorded for undo only if it compiles. Code editor saves and agents' free-form edits use it. An agent's save is the one exception ([AGENT-1](#agents)): it is written only when it is taken in as written, so it is one undo step, else it is refused and nothing is written.
- *Why:* a half-typed file must be saveable, and agents keep writing files directly (owner decision 5).
- *Today:* matches since step 6: `Session::save` compares and swaps on the bytes' hash, writes through the commit's staging, takes in the settled draft with that one file (never a fresh read of the folder), and records an undo step only when it was accepted as written (`core/session/src/take.rs`). Since the review of `ag-fwd`, `Session::save_built` takes the draft with the file in before it writes, and writes only when that takes the file in as written: an agent's save (the app's save route with the tools key, and `m2 edit` and `m2 write` while the code doesn't build). A file that isn't UTF-8 is saved over from the lossy text the code editor shows (`crates/server/src/files.rs`).

**WRITE-3.** Every other write is a `Tx`:

```rust
Tx   { id, base: Map<Path, Base>, label: (before, after), ops: Vec<Op>, expect: Expect, hint: Option<CodeRev> }
Base = Sha | Absent | WithText { sha: Sha, text: String }                    // the base text itself (WRITE-12)

Op   = TextEdit { file, edits: Vec<{ start: Pos, end: Pos, expected, text }> }
     | Write { file, bytes }                                                  // JSON: "text" or "base64"
     | Remove { file }
     | Rename { from, to }                                                    // a file, or a folder data/<module>/
     | Clips { lane: LaneAddr, put: Vec<Clip>, delete: Vec<ClipId> }          // at most one per lane
Pos  = Byte(u32) | LineCol { line: u32, utf16_col: u32 }
```

- Bases live only in `Tx.base`: every file an op reads or writes has exactly one, and a `Rename`'s `to` must be `Absent`.
- A `TextEdit` position is a UTF-8 byte offset into the base text, or a line and a UTF-16 column, which core resolves to bytes against the base text. `expected` still guards each edit: a mismatch rejects the Tx.
- `Clips` puts whole clips, in the lane format's schema, and deletes clips by id. It applies to a lane file newer than its base when every clip it puts or deletes is the same in the base and the current file, byte for byte, or absent from both. It is the only data op, so a new data feature changes the lane format, never the op set. The arrangement, plugin state (blob and sidecar) and new lane files are written with `Write`.

A Tx may touch several files. It is journaled and is one undo step. A Tx MAY also write `layout.json`; that write is journaled with the Tx but is never part of a revision or a delta.

The same JSON serves the server, `m2 tx`, MCP and later TypeScript tools. This Tx automates the hats pan in `examples/first-song/song.ts`: it gives the song function its `lane`, replaces the literal, and creates the lane file.

```json
{"id": "tx-7k2m9q4c",
 "base": {"song.ts": "sha256:4be1…", "data/song/hats-pan.json": null},
 "label": ["hats pan 0.3", "hats pan automated"],
 "ops": [{"op": "text_edit", "file": "song.ts",
          "edits": [{"start": 327, "end": 329, "expected": "()", "text": "({ lane })"},
                    {"start": 422, "end": 425, "expected": "0.3", "text": "lane.control(\"hats-pan\", \"bipolar\", 0.3)"}]},
         {"op": "write", "file": "data/song/hats-pan.json", "text": "{\"unit\": \"bipolar\", \"clips\": []}\n"}],
 "expect": {"confined": {"ranges": {"song.ts": [[327, 329], [422, 425]]}, "lanes": [["song", "hats-pan"]], "modules": []}}}
```

- *Why:* the next gestures (add track, automate, relink, delete track) all touch several files. A closed op set keeps core from growing with every data feature, and one base per file leaves nothing to disagree. Agents and TypeScript count in UTF-16, so a byte offset alone would make every Tx on a file with `é` or `ベース` in it fail.
- *Today:* matches since step 6 (`m2_song::tx` in `core/song/src/tx.rs`; `Session::check` and `Session::commit` in `core/session/src/tx/`), with what the step 6 design's C1 and C3 add for the owner to confirm: `Payload::Local` (a staged file or a before-image, for recovery and the undo of a binary write), `"whole"` in `Confined` ranges, and `Expect::Journal`. Every gesture is a builder of Tx in `crates/editor`; agents send any Tx to `POST /api/tx/check` and `/api/tx/commit`, or `m2 tx`.

**WRITE-3a.** Every path in a Tx (the keys of `base`, `file`, `from`, `to`, and a `Clips` lane) MUST be relative and `/`-separated, with no empty, `.` or `..` segment, and MUST parse through `DataPath::parse` to Code, Manifest, Arrangement, Module, Lane, PluginState, Asset or Presentation. A Local or Unknown path is refused with `tx.path`, and so is a target that is a symlink or whose parent resolves outside the project. `Tx.id` is metadata matching `^[0-9a-z-]{1,32}$`; core names the journal file itself.
- *Why:* Tx JSON comes from agents, MCP and later sandboxed tools, and `../../song` must never name a file. Ops that carried unparsed paths would also break [FMT-5](#paths).
- *Today:* matches since step 6: `Tx::validate` and `TxPath` check every path and the shape before anything is read, and the commit checks on disk, under the lock, links (a target that is one, or a folder on its way that is one, inside the project or not), names in other capitals, text that isn't UTF-8, and that every path, whatever its base, is spelled as the disk spells it: on a disk that ignores capitals or Unicode forms, `Song.ts` for `song.ts` would change the real file while the delta saw another (`core/song/src/tx.rs`; `hostile_paths_and_ids_are_refused` in `core/session/src/tx/props.rs`, `hostile_targets_on_disk_are_refused` and `a_file_spelled_another_way_is_refused` in `core/session/src/tx/tests.rs`).

**WRITE-4.** `check(tx)` compiles the candidate in memory and writes nothing. A Tx whose files are all class Data or Arrangement skips QuickJS, as an outside write to such files does ([WRITE-19](#outside-writers)). It returns the `GraphDelta`, the delta's hash and the diagnostics.

**WRITE-5.** `commit(tx)` returns one of:

```rust
Committed { code_rev, data_rev, delta: GraphDelta, diagnostics, patches }
Conflict  { files: Vec<{ file, current: Sha }> }   // every base that no longer matches
Rejected  { diagnostics, unexpected: Option<GraphDelta> }
```

### Expectations and the delta

**WRITE-6.** Core MUST verify a Tx against a `GraphDelta` it computes itself, using this closed set of expectations:

```rust
Expect = Identical                                  // an empty delta: source positions, labels, node numbering, site numbers
                                                    // and whether a control was written or inherited are ignored
       | Values(Vec<(Anchor, f64)>)                 // only these constants change, to these values (the same things ignored)
       | Confined { ranges: Map<Path, Vec<Range>>, lanes: Vec<LaneAddr>, modules: Vec<ModuleChange> }
       | Seen(DeltaHash)                            // commit only if the delta equals the one check() returned
Anchor = Written { file, range }                    // a value written in the code
       | Slot { site: { file, range }, slot }       // an inherited (unwritten) control, named by its slot (PKG-3)
```

`Confined` holds when, after nodes are matched by their delta names ([WRITE-8](#expectations-and-the-delta)): every node that differs was made by a site written inside a declared range (by its origin, `within` or `src`), or differs only in edges to such nodes; lane data differs only for the declared lanes; modules are added, removed, re-keyed or muted only as declared; and every byte outside the declared ranges is unchanged. A new kind of expectation needs a design review that changes this page.
- *Why:* gesture-shaped expectations would make core grow with every feature, and checks supplied by tools would take the guarantee out of core. Inserting an omitted argument turns an inherited control into a written one, which must still count as `Values`.
- *Today:* matches since step 6: core judges every Tx by its expectation on the delta it computes (`core/session/src/tx/expect.rs`), with the step 6 design's C1 (`Confined`'s rules on bytes and on states, assets and the arrangement) for the owner to confirm; each planner's own assertion stays in `crates/editor`. A node inside a voice was made within its `poly`, so from 8b.1 it is also placed where that `poly` is: a note field (`lane:gate`) has no place of its own in the project, and before, no range could admit a new track's voices (`scope` in `core/session/src/tx/delta.rs`). From 8b.1 the write API is recorded: every op, expectation, base, declaration, module change, anchor and position, matched with no wildcard, and one Tx per op and expectation that serialises back byte for byte (`the_write_api_is_step_6s` in `core/song/tests/write_api.rs`), so a new one fails CI until this page changes. Until track bus, slice 2, a node outside the ranges could differ only in edges to nodes added or removed there; now, as this says, also to nodes made there in both graphs, which a node added before them renamed (std's mixer numbers its nodes in order, so a new send renames the sends after it, and a song export that feeds a bus then names the renamed node).

**WRITE-7.** Core MUST take structured anchors, never `file#ordinal` strings. Those become debug labels made by `crates/editor`.
- *Why:* ordinals shift with every inserted call ([ID-12](#derived-identity)).
- *Today:* matches since step 6: core takes `Anchor`s; `file#ordinal/arg` ids are the editor's handles within one code revision (`crates/editor/src/controls.rs`), carried from an older one by the call's range (`controls::carry`).

**WRITE-8.** The `GraphDelta` lists nodes added and removed, inputs rewired, constants changed, lanes changed, plugin states changed, assets changed, whether the arrangement changed, and modules changed (added, removed, re-keyed, `muted`). Nodes are named so that inserting one can't rename another:
- A node made at a site is named by its module key; then its frame chain, each frame its ComponentId and its call's point ([ID-13](#derived-identity)), with nothing positional above a `key()` frame ([ID-7](#keys)); then the call that made it; then its argument path; then its occurrence within that site. Points are in the comparison's reference coordinates: through the Tx's own `TextEdit`s, or for an outside write and between revisions through `Edits::between` ([WRITE-12](#bases-and-revisions)). Sites inside packages keep their package offsets, since packages don't change within a Tx.
- A lane, `time` or import node is named by what it reads. A `time` node made inside a component call (or a `key()` frame) is named under that call's frame chain: each call that reads song time has its own.

`DeltaHash` is the SHA-256 of the delta's JSON in RFC 8785 (JCS) form, with nodes sorted by name. The delta drives the engine: values → `SetCell`, or an activation when other things change; notes and control lanes → `SwapLane`; audio lanes, assets, plugin states and the arrangement → prepared renders and loads, then an `Activate` of the same structure; a module's `muted` → its mute-in-song cell; structure → `continues`, prepare and `Activate`.
- *Why:* the engine needs to know exactly what changed, not guess. Names by walk order would let one inserted node rename every node after it: `Confined` would fail, the engine would replace instances it should keep, and two equal changes would hash differently.
- *Today:* matches since step 6 (`core/session/src/tx/delta.rs`, `delta_between`), with calls named by their `(` since step 7, in the base text's coordinates (the step 6 design's C17, now in the rule's words). Since the `sounds` merge a `time` node made inside a component call or a `key()` frame is named under that frame chain (the `Op::Time` arm in `delta.rs`), so adding a call that reads song time before another of its kind, or removing it, renames neither's (`a_call_reading_time_added_or_removed_beside_another_renames_nothing` in `core/session/src/tx/delta_tests.rs`). Before, std/fx's chorus reading `time.playing` would have made Add Chorus before another chorus a refused edit. Of what the delta drives, the server writes the cells of changed constants and swaps changed lanes into what plays when that is all the delta from what plays holds; for anything else it prepares an activation against what plays, which keeps every instance that continues, loads each changed plugin state into the instance it keeps, renders what audio needs, and switches the arrangement with it (`crates/server/src/graph/actor.rs`). The guess is gone.

**WRITE-9.** What a gesture means ("the wrap wrapped", "the named outlet is wired") MUST be asserted by planners in `crates/editor` and their tests, never by core. Adding a gesture MUST NOT change core: no core crate may depend on `crates/editor` (the crate graph checks this), and a change that adds a gesture and touches `core/*` changes this page first.
- *Why:* core stays small, and its guarantee stays the same for every client.

**WRITE-10.** Gestures map onto expectations as follows. Step 6 ports the 7 gestures that exist today (set a value, connect, disconnect, add an effect, remove an effect, write a notes lane, a code editor save) and step 1's two fixes; automate a knob, add a track, rename a label and mute in song are new builders.

| Gesture | Expectation |
|---|---|
| set a value | `Values` |
| connect, disconnect, add or remove an effect | `Confined`; the planners keep excise, rewire and wrap as their own assertions |
| write a notes lane | `Confined { lanes }` |
| relink unused data | `Confined { lanes: the lane it moves to }` |
| delete unused data | `Identical` |
| automate a knob | `Confined { the literal's range, a new lane }` |
| add a track | `Confined { the new file, the import and the mixer call in song.ts, the new lane, a new module }` |
| duplicate a track | `Confined { the new file (or the copied statement), the import and the mixer call in song.ts, its lanes, a new module }` |
| delete a track | `Confined { its file (or its statement), its imports, the mixer call, every excised call and every import that leaves unread, its lanes, the module removed }` |
| move a mixer channel's fader that the channel doesn't write (its first move) | `Values`: the `db` written into the channel's entry |
| a mixer channel's first pan (an optional control written in) | `Confined { the mixer call }`, at the drag's threshold; the planner asserts by channel |
| reorder, recolour, Reset layout | writes only `layout.json`: no revision, so `Identical` |
| rename a label | `Identical` |
| mute in song | `Confined { the track call's options, modules: that module's muted }` |
| set the tempo or the time signature, add or remove a change | `Confined {}`: check 5 admits the arrangement it writes, and nothing else may change |
| rename the song | `Identical` |
| markers, the song's key | write only `layout.json`: no revision, so `Identical` |
| change a track's instrument | `Confined { the instrument call, and the imports it changes }` |
| add a bus | `Confined { the new file, the import and the mixer call in song.ts, a new module }` |
| add or take out a send, set it before or after the fader, send a channel to a bus or the master | `Confined { the mixer call }`; the planner asserts every channel's tracks and the routing it wrote |
| change a track's ID | `Confined { the track's file, each read of it in another file, lanes: its lanes }` when every reading file is edited; else checked first and committed `Seen` ([WRITE-11](#expectations-and-the-delta)). Either way the planner asserts the modules are exactly `[Rekeyed]`, no value changes, and every lane plays the same clips under the new key |
| repeat a clip | `Confined { the lane }`, as duplicate |
| note tools (transpose, quantize, legato, humanize, swing) | `Confined { lanes }`, as writing a notes lane |
| code editor save | `save()` |
| bring audio in onto a lane, a take (notes or audio), Follow tempo | a clip gesture: `Confined { lanes }`, one `Clips` Tx |
| bring audio or a MIDI file in as new tracks | one Tx, as add a track for each |
| set a Sampler's file | `Confined { the call's range }` |

- *Today (8b.1):* add, duplicate and delete a track, reorder and recolour are built (`crates/editor/src/tracks.rs`, `layout.rs`; routes `POST /api/graph/track/{add,duplicate,delete}` and `/api/graph/layout`; `m2 add-track`, `duplicate-track`, `delete-track`, `layout`). The whole mixer call is declared because the new strip's nodes are std's, made inside it; each planner's assertion takes up the rest: exactly one module added or removed, no value changed, nothing of the song removed but an empty mixer's silent stand-in, and every other channel as it was, by key. A delete that other code reads answers with the reads (409 at the route): one an effect that takes audio first can lose (a duck's sidechain) goes with the track only when asked again with `cascade`; any other blocks it.
- *Today (8b.3b):* a channel's fader and first pan are built (`Insertion` in `crates/editor/src/lens.rs`, `mix::insert` in `mix.rs`; route `POST /api/graph/mix/insert`, which answers with the new knob's id). A fader's first move writes `db` into the channel's entry as one `Values` step: a bare entry is wrapped, keeping its text (`kick: kick.out` → `kick: { in: kick.out, db: db(-3) }`), and an object entry gets a property ([PKG-3](#component-descriptors)). The first pan writes `pan: 0` the same way, with the whole mixer call declared, since the new panner is std's, made inside it. A panner is named by how many panned channels come before it ([ID-14](#derived-identity)), so a first pan renames the panners after it, and the planner's assertion is by channel, through `mix::mixer`: every channel keeps its tracks and its controls' values and readers, this one gains only the pan, read by its new panner, and the graph gains that constant and that panner, net, and changes no value (`the_first_pans_assertion_refuses_more_than_the_pan`). It is inaudible: live, the song plays within 1e-5 of the same song with no edit, through the activation, on first-song and in a mix that is mono all the way (`a_first_pan_while_playing_is_inaudible` and `a_first_pan_in_a_mono_mix_while_playing_is_inaudible` in `crates/server/src/graph/tests.rs`).
- *Today (8b.2):* the song bar's tempo, time signature and title are built (`crates/editor/src/song.rs`; routes `POST /api/graph/tempo`, `/meter`, `/title`; `m2 tempo`, `meter`, `title`). The tempo and the time signature write `data/arrangement.json` whole, in its canonical form, and assert on the checked candidate that it plays exactly the arrangement asked for; a new time signature change lands on the bar line at or before where it was asked, and later ones keep their bar: each goes to where its bar now starts, so an edit and its inverse give back the same file (FMT-13; `a_later_meter_change_keeps_its_bar`). A ruler mark with both a tempo and a time signature change is removed in one step (`remove_changes`, the tempo route with `meter`). The title is a `TextEdit` of `[project] name` in `music2.toml`, whose delta is empty (`a_title_edit_is_identical`). Each is one undo step, with no toast.
- From 8b.4: mute in song is built (`crates/editor/src/mute.rs`): one `Confined` Tx for one or several tracks, each declared by the range it edits in its `track()` call's options (or the point after the key, for a call with none) and its `ModuleChange::Muted`; the planner asserts that each named track is as asked in the candidate. Its history label is "Kick → Kick, muted in song". It is reached by `POST /api/graph/track/mute` and `m2 mute-in-song`.
- From 8b.5: clip gestures are built (`crates/editor/src/clips.rs`): create, move (to another lane, a copy), resize (either edge), split, join, duplicate, paste, delete, quantize, one clip's notes (the piano roll's edit), one clip's points (automation drawn in place), and the general put-and-delete for agents. Each is one `Confined { the lanes it touches }` Tx: a `Clips` op per lane, or the whole lane when its clips can't all be named by id (a move across lanes has two). The planner asserts each lane reads every clip written as written and none deleted. A gesture is pure first (`clips::apply`, what it makes of the lanes, from the accepted revision), so the lane lease can preview the commit exactly. Notes, automation and audio clips never cross, and automation crosses only within its unit. It is reached by `POST /api/graph/clips` (`{session, gesture}`) and `m2 clips`. Labels say what changed: "clip at bar 1 (8 bars) → clip at bar 9 (8 bars)".
- From polish: change instrument (`crates/editor/src/swap.rs`, `POST /api/graph/track/instrument`), markers (`POST /api/graph/markers`), the song's key (`POST /api/graph/song-key`), change a track's ID (`POST /api/graph/track/rekey`), repeat a clip (the clips route's `duplicate` gesture with `times`, 1 to 64) and the note tools (a notes lane write, `POST /api/graph/lane`). Change instrument drops the old instrument's settings and every import only it used; a setting code works out (a lane, a sum) refuses it in words. The project routes `POST /api/project/save-as` (a copy without `.music2/`, opened; refused inside the song's own folder; works read-only) and `POST /api/project/rename` (the title Tx, then, with `folder`, the folder renamed with the song closed and opened again, so Undo starts over; refused while the code editor has unsaved edits unless `anyway`) are not Tx: Save As only reads the song, and the folder rename moves the project.
- From `studio` and step 9: imports (`crates/editor/src/import.rs`, `midifile.rs`), takes (`take.rs`: the file is finished and synced before the Tx names it), a Sampler's file (`sampler.rs`, track ab) and Follow tempo (the `audio` clip gesture's `follow`, track sx).
- From 8b.5: Remove automation is `Confined { the lane read's range, and each place the code reads its binding by name, lanes: the lane }`: a plain number written in a read's place is made where a call reads that name (PKG-3). Its history label is "cutoff automated → cutoff hz(1250)".

Core keeps `save`, `Tx`, `GraphDelta` and its checkers, undo, the journal, the raw site, origin and control tables, and diagnostics. `crates/editor` gets `view.rs`, `timeline.rs`, `lens.rs` (with unit spelling generated from `core.ts`), the cable and effect planners, labels, the source helpers the planners use (once in `core/script/src/sites.rs`, now `crates/editor/src/sites.rs`), and `render.rs` (export at host level).

**WRITE-11.** Agents opt in to verification by calling `check`, showing the delta, and committing with `Seen(hash)`.

### Bases and revisions

**WRITE-12.** A Tx's base MUST be the content hash (SHA-256 of the bytes) of each file it touches, or `Absent` for a file it creates. Anchors are positions in those texts.
- A base equal to the file's current hash always applies.
- Otherwise core carries the ranges from the base text into the current one, failing when a change touched them. The base text is the one the Tx carries (`WithText`, whose hash core checks), or else the one in the session's store of recent texts keyed by hash, which is only a cache. With neither, the Tx is a `Conflict` naming the file.
- So a Tx that carries its base texts has the same outcome in every process, whatever versions a session happened to see. Agents and `m2 tx` SHOULD send them.
- A directory's hash (for a `Rename` of `data/<module>/`) is the SHA-256 of the `\n`-joined lines `<path>\0<sha256 hex>`, one for each file under it that `DataPath` parses as Lane or PluginState, with paths relative to the folder, `/`-separated and sorted bytewise. Dot files such as `.DS_Store` aren't hashed and move with the folder; any other file makes the `Rename` refuse, naming it.
- A revision number is only a hint.
- *Why:* a revision number means nothing outside the session that issued it, the 300 ms poll can miss versions, and the Rust and TypeScript tools must compute the same directory hash.
- *Today:* since step 6 bases are content hashes, `Absent` or `WithText`, found in a 32 MiB store of recent texts when a Tx doesn't carry them (`core/session/src/texts.rs`), and carried by `Edits::between` ([ID-13](#derived-identity)): a line diff whose runs of changed lines a token-level Myers diff refines, each hunk trimmed to bytes, so a change on another part of the same line doesn't stop a range (`core/session/src/text.rs`); the editor's control ids, leases and cables carry by it too (`crates/editor/src/controls.rs`, `cable.rs`); the directory hash is `m2_song::dir_hash`. Except: the editor's notes write, relink and delete-data still name the revision they were planned at and plan on the session's remembered snapshot of it (`Session::snapshot`, the last 64), so the piano roll sends no lane hash. Since 8b.5 clip gestures plan on the accepted revision and name each clip they touch with the hash the window drew it with: each clip in the timeline carries `sha`, the first 16 hex digits of the SHA-256 of its canonical line, and a clip changed since is refused as a conflict naming it (`a_gesture_on_a_changed_clip_is_changed`; `POST /api/graph/clips` answers 409). The roll's whole-lane write stays until the roll sends `notes` gestures; it takes clips as the timeline shows them, `sha` and all.

**WRITE-13.** A revision is a pair `(code_rev, data_rev)`. A change to Code or Manifest files bumps `code_rev`. A change to only Data or Arrangement files bumps only `data_rev` and stores lane diffs, not full snapshots. Control ids, gestures and carrying are scoped to `code_rev`, and a lease is re-resolved at every new `code_rev` ([WRITE-22](#gesture-leases)).
- *Why:* a lane edit must not invalidate a knob drag.
- *Today:* matches since step 6: a `Revision` is numbered `(code_rev, data_rev)` beside its sequence `number` (`core/session/src/session.rs`), and the server's leases and ids follow the session's `code_rev`. Except that the last 64 revisions each keep a full snapshot, not lane diffs.

**WRITE-14.** While the draft code is broken but the session has an accepted code revision, a data Tx MUST still work. It writes data files only and is checked against the last accepted lane declarations. Without an accepted code revision, a data Tx is rejected.
- *Why:* fixing code must not block editing notes.
- *Today:* matches since step 6 (`the_draft_rule`, `outside_data_is_taken_in_while_the_code_is_broken` in `core/session/src/tx/tests.rs`).

### Commit, journal and recovery

**WRITE-15.** A commit runs these steps, under the project lock:
1. compare-and-swap every target against its base;
2. write the journal `.music2/tx/<id>.json` and sync it. It holds the host name, the before and after hashes of every target, and the before bytes of every text target. A binary target's before bytes (a plugin blob) are stored once, by content, in `.music2/tx/blobs/<sha>`;
3. stage every write in `.music2/tx/<id>/`, which is on the same volume, then rename each into place;
4. accept the new revision;
5. remove the journal and the staging folder.

Between steps 1 and 2 the engine may hear the candidate as a preview ([PLANE-5](#planes-of-change)); nothing is accepted before step 4.
- *Why:* several files can't be replaced atomically, and the journal makes an interrupted commit recoverable. Staging beside each target would put temporary files under `data/`, where the watcher and `git status` see them. A 50 MB plugin blob must not be copied into the journal twice per save.
- *Today:* matches since step 6 (`core/session/src/tx/stage.rs`): the journal, then the staged files, pushed with `fsync`; one barrier (`m2_os::fs::barrier`: `F_FULLFSYNC` on macOS, `fsync` where a volume refuses it; `fsync` on Linux, where each push is already durable); renames checked at each step with an exchange and a create-only rename (`m2_os::fs::rename`: `renamex_np` on macOS, `renameat2` on Linux), rolled back in process when one finds an outside change. A volume without them (FAT, exFAT, SMB, NFS; NTFS always) reads and compares the target, then renames the staged file over it in one rename, so every target always holds its before or its after bytes; a rollback renames the before bytes back over it the same way. Step 5 comes later than this page says (the step 6 design's C9): a commit's journal and staging are removed after the next commit's barrier, or at close, and a completed journal found when the lock is taken only after its commit's folders are pushed and a barrier, so the removal is never durable before the renames are. A barrier that fails removes nothing: such a journal waits for the session's next barrier, as its own commits' do, and a close whose barrier fails leaves its journals to the next lock (`a_completed_journal_whose_barrier_failed_goes_after_the_next`, `a_close_whose_barrier_fails_keeps_its_journal_until_the_next_lock`). A folder sync that can't be done is skipped by every writer alike (`m2_os::fs::sync_dir`): on a volume with no folder sync (`vboxsf`), and for a folder that can be written but not listed, which can't be opened to sync it. Any other error (`EIO`) fails the write, except in step 8's push after the renames, which can't undo a commit that landed. A rollback pushes the folders it renamed into before its barrier; a staging folder is removed before its journal, and what a killed commit left half made (temporary files, a staging folder whose journal went) is swept when a writer next takes the lock. Killing the process at every disk call of a commit is tested (`core/session/src/tx/kill_tests.rs`). Since the G11 fix the pre-check (step 1) passes into a callback before the journal's first byte (`stage::commit`), where the session's host may send a data-only candidate to the engine; commits whose disk work fails there are swapped back ([PLANE-5](#planes-of-change)). A rollback whose barrier fails leaves its journal and staging to the next barrier, never tidied before the rollback is durable (`a_rollback_whose_barrier_fails_keeps_its_journal_until_the_next`).

**WRITE-16.** A journal left behind raises `tx.interrupted`, with FinishTx and DiscardTx fixes, when a process on the same host next takes the lock. Finish uses the staged files; Discard uses the recorded before bytes. Each fix is allowed only while every target still matches the hash the journal recorded before or after the Tx. If a target matches neither, the journal is set aside and reported, with no fix, and no project file is touched. A journal from another host (a synced folder) is `tx.foreign`, reported with no fix. Recovery never runs by itself.
- *Why:* recovery must never overwrite someone else's later edit, and a commit interrupted on another machine is that machine's to recover.
- *Today:* matches since step 6 (`core/session/src/tx/journal.rs`), with the step 6 design's C9 and C18 for the owner to confirm: a journal whose every target is at its after hash is a completed commit, tidied silently when the lock is taken next, a target at a newer journal's start or end (one that started where it ended, such as its undo) counting as at its after hash; another host is told by its machine id ([FMT-20](#presentation-and-local-files)), and its journals' before-images, and those of journals set aside, are kept. A Tx overlapping an interrupted commit's targets (a folder holding one included) waits for it. Finish and Discard apply while the interruption left code that doesn't compile (P3), and every CLI command that opens the project says them with their `m2 fix` commands, `m2 check` and `m2 render` included (`killed_at_every_commit_step_the_project_recovers`, `apps/m2/tests/recovery.rs`). The kill test runs every case twice: on a disk that swaps, and on one that can't (`Recorder { plain: true }`); one case writes `music2.toml`.

### The project lock

**WRITE-17.** A process that writes a project through music2 MUST hold an exclusive OS advisory lock on `.music2/lock` (the std file lock, `File::try_lock`, through `m2_os::fs::try_lock`; `core/session/src/lock.rs`) for as long as it may write: the app while the project is open, a CLI write command for its run. So one process at a time writes. The file holds `{pid, program, host, token, since}` only to make messages clear. The OS releases the lock when the process exits, so a crash leaves no stale lock.
- When the app opens a project another process holds (a long `m2 bounce --place`, a second app), it opens it read-only: it plays, says so naming the holder, and offers to retry. Read-only means no `save()` and no Tx.
- When the volume can't lock (`ENOTSUP` or `ENOLCK`, as on some SMB, cloud and FUSE volumes), the project opens read-only with the Error `lock.unsupported`. music2 never writes unlocked.
- *Why:* two writer processes with no lock race between compare and rename.
- *Today:* matches since step 6 (`core/session/src/lock.rs`). The app opens a held project read-only, with a Read only pill in its top bar whose popover names the holder and offers Retry (`POST /api/graph/lock/retry`), and its controls disabled, and answers every route the route table marks as a write 423 meanwhile ([AGENT-8](#agents), `crates/server/src/api.rs`). On a network volume (`m2_os::fs::is_local`: no `MNT_LOCAL` on macOS; on Linux an NFS, SMB, CIFS, 9p, Ceph, AFS, Coda or FUSE file system) it takes the lock and raises the Notice `lock.local`, as the step 6 design's C2 proposes. A lock whose file was removed while held (`git clean -fdx` takes `.music2/` away) excludes nobody, so every write checks it first: it is taken again, or, when another process took it meanwhile, the session turns read-only naming it (`a_lock_whose_folder_was_removed_is_taken_again`).

**WRITE-18.** A writer that can't take the lock MUST fail before changing anything, with a message that names the holder and what to do:

```
error: nothing was changed: this project is open for writing in music2 (pid 4121, since 14:02).
Make the edit there, or close the project in music2 and run this again.
```

The CLI's commands sort like this:
- `check` (and its alias `build`), `render`, `graph`, `controls`, `cables` and `effects` only read. They never take the lock, and open their own session.
- `kernels` and `plugins` open no project, and `new` creates a new folder. None needs the lock.
- `bounce` without `--place` only creates a new asset and its provenance, with create-only semantics ([FMT-14](#assets-and-hashes)). That can't race, so it needs no lock and works while the app is open.
- Every other command that changes a project takes the lock: `set`, `connect`, `disconnect`, `add-effect`, `remove-effect`, `bounce --place`, `plugin-state`, `relink`, `delete-data`, `tx`, `automate`, `label`, `add-track`, `duplicate-track`, `delete-track`, `layout`, `tempo`, `meter` and `title`. The message for `bounce --place` also names the lane file, and says the clip can be added by writing that file directly.
- From 8b.4: `mute-in-song` takes the lock too (`apps/m2/tests/mute.rs`).
- From 8b.5: `clips` and `unautomate` take the lock too (`apps/m2/tests/clips.rs`).
- Also: `import`, `fix`, `edit` and `write` take the lock too, and `listen` only reads. `undo`, `focus`, `watch`, `point` and `call` act only through the open app and take no lock; `routes` opens no project.

A writer that finds the app holding the lock forwards ([AGENT-1](#agents)); a lock held by any other program fails as above.
- *Why:* owner decision 10: the lock, then forwarding. While raw file writes are allowed (owner decision 5), the safest writer must not be the one blocked: it isn't, only asked to wait while the musician holds a file ([AGENT-3](#agents)).
- *Today:* matches since step 6 (`apps/m2/src/main.rs`; `apps/m2/tests/lock.rs`), with `automate` and `label` among the writers, from 8b.1 `add-track`, `duplicate-track`, `delete-track` and `layout`, and from 8b.2 `tempo`, `meter` and `title` (`a_write_while_another_process_holds_the_lock_fails_and_changes_nothing`), and readers taking the lock for a journal scan when it is free, never while they read (the step 6 design's C19). Since `ag-fwd`, a lock held by `music2` is forwarded to when `m2` can reach that app (AGENT-1); one it can't reach fails naming it, and so does a lock held by any other program.

### Outside writers

**WRITE-19.** Outside writers (text editors, agents writing files, git, another machine through a synced folder) are first class. The watcher picks up their changes, classifies the paths with `DataPath`, and takes the same plane a Tx would. It waits for changes to settle: it batches them until the folder has been quiet for 150 ms and every changed path's `(size, mtime, hash)` is the same on two polls in a row, and while `.git/index.lock` exists.
