It then reads one snapshot and accepts it as one revision, on the slowest plane of the batch ([PLANE-2](#planes-of-change)), and, in the app, one undo step unless git wrote it ([AGENT-2](#agents)). The lock can't stop an outside writer; compare-and-swap narrows the race between an outside write and a commit but can't close it.
- *Why:* files are the truth, whoever wrote them. A checkout, an editor's save-all or an agent writing several files arrives over several polls, and accepting a half-applied state plays wrong music and makes diagnostics flap.
- *Today:* matches since step 6: the settle rule (`core/session/src/watch.rs`) is fed by the server's watcher (`crates/server/src/graph/watcher.rs`); a batch that settled is read once, its paths alone (`Snapshot::read_paths`, `a_settled_batch_reads_only_its_paths_and_takes_in_the_same`), and taken in whole, each path only while it reads as it settled (`Session::take_in_only`); the session's own writes are told to the rule by their stamps; a path there for one poll only settles as gone. Since the G11 fix, on a local volume the OS's file-system events name what changed (`m2_os::watch`: FSEvents on macOS, in-tree over CoreServices for `FSEventStreamFlushSync`; inotify on Linux, one instance per process, read at most every 10 ms as FSEvents' latency groups events, and an event is queued before the writer's call returns, so a flush is a read that never waits), and a tick stats only the named paths, the open batch's and what the session just wrote: an idle tick stats nothing. The whole project is walked at open, every 5 s, when events were dropped or more than 4,096 paths came at once, and whenever a batch is due: only a poll that saw every path makes a batch ready, so a change whose other half an event names late is never taken in half (`a_late_event_never_readies_half_a_change`; `a_scoped_poll_sees_what_a_full_poll_sees` replays the full-poll tests' scenarios). The watcher ticks every 50 ms, at once when an event names a change while no batch is open, and when a batch's quiet ends. A tick holds the watch lock only to plan and to apply, never over a stat (`the_watch_lock_is_never_held_over_a_stat`); what the session wrote meanwhile is left out of that tick, and every file the session wrote is looked at again by the next, so an outside write right after it is taken in (`an_outside_replace_right_after_an_own_write_is_taken_in`). A path is stat'd only through names spelled as the disk spells them (`a_case_only_rename_settles_as_one_path`), and a folder moved or renamed in is walked (`a_folder_moved_into_the_project_is_taken_in`, `an_assets_folder_replaced_by_a_rename_is_taken_in`). On a network volume (its events name only this computer's writes), when events won't start (inotify's per-user limits included), with `M2_WATCH=walk`, or once events missed changes twice in 10 minutes (a change a walk found that no event named within 60 s; `events_that_miss_changes_give_way_to_walks`), a walk of everything is the only source: every tick while a batch is open, else every 50 ms to 1 s (20 times the last walk's time). A walk lists an asset no lane names from its folder entry, with no stat. A walk that lost events asked for (a Rescan) counts no missed events (`a_burst_past_the_hint_limit_is_walked_and_never_missed`); a folder made, filled or renamed after open is watched from its events (`a_folder_made_after_open_is_watched`, `a_folder_made_and_filled_at_once_is_watched`, `a_folder_renamed_in_the_project_is_watched_by_its_new_name`). On Linux a folder put where the project's folder was (a restore, or the folder removed and made again) is watched from the next walk, and a folder that could be read only after it was made is watched once its mode changes (`m2_os::watch`'s tests); a change of a file's change time alone (a hard link to it made elsewhere) is no miss (`a_change_of_the_change_time_alone_is_no_miss`). With a 5,000-file `assets/` on p16 (`m2-gates --server watcher`, G11's watcher; release, M1 Pro, five runs; before: the step 6 watcher, three runs):
  - Idle, its ticks use 0.18 to 0.27% of a core: two walks of everything in 10 s, 4.6 to 7.4 ms each, and no stat in between (before: a walk every tick, 32 to 33%). By walks alone, 3.8 to 4.3%.
  - A walk of everything takes 4.2 to 4.7 ms at p50, and 6.0 to 7.2 ms with 1,000 of the files named by a lane (before: 14.8 to 16.5 ms and 17.7 to 18.9 ms).
  - An outside write of a lane file, in place or as an atomic save, is played 172 to 181 ms after its last byte at p50 and 180 to 190 ms at p95, of which 150 ms is the quiet (before: 210 to 233 ms and 267 to 295 ms; 10 writes each). By walks alone, 205 to 254 ms at p50 and 247 to 326 ms at p95 (5 writes each).

### Opening a project

**WRITE-20.** `Session::open` MUST NOT fail because of code, manifest content, arrangement or data errors. A project whose code doesn't compile opens with diagnostics and no accepted code revision: the code editor works, and playback is silent until the code compiles. Opening fails only when the folder can't be read, has no `music2.toml`, or its `format` is missing, older or newer than this app's ([FMT-3](#manifest-and-versions)). A project whose lock is held elsewhere, or can't be taken, opens read-only ([WRITE-17](#the-project-lock)).
- *Why:* a project with a half-typed `song.ts` must be fixable in the app (owner decision 6).
- *Today:* matches since step 6: `Session::open` opens a broken project with the empty revision and `draft_errors`, and the app opens it silent, in its code, and plays it once a save compiles (`a_broken_project_opens_and_plays_once_saved_fixed` in `crates/server/src/graph/tests.rs`).

### Undo

**WRITE-21.** Undo applies the stored inverse Tx. One Tx is one undo step, and so is a watcher batch that builds ([AGENT-2](#agents)). History stays in memory. An inverse undoes only what its Tx did, or it is a `Conflict`:
- a `TextEdit` is located by the carried range plus the recorded value;
- a `Write`, `Remove` or `Rename` applies only while its target's hash is the one the Tx left (or still `Absent`);
- a `Clips` op applies only while every clip it touches has the content hash the Tx left (or is still absent); otherwise the `Conflict` names the lane and the clip id;
- the undo of a `save()` is a `Write` whose base is the hash the save wrote.
- *Why:* undo must restore the edit, not an old copy of the file over someone else's work.
- *Today:* matches since step 6: every step is a stored inverse Tx on one history, at most 500 steps and 64 MiB of retained text (`core/session/src/session.rs`, `TxStep` in `core/session/src/tx/mod.rs`); the inverse of a `Clips` op that meets an outside change to its clip is a `Conflict` naming the lane file and the clip (`undoing_a_clip_after_its_outside_change_conflicts`). Since `next` (gaps #29's names) each step keeps the revision it made and an optional name (`Session::name_step`, `step_names`; `Session::tops`), which follows it through undo and redo: the window that made an edit names it (`POST /api/graph/step`), and a step made elsewhere gets a plain name from what it changed ("Bass notes"). Undo's and Redo's tooltips and the app's Edit menu show them. Names live as long as the history; Tx, its ops and expectations are unchanged. Since `ag-steps`, each step also has an id and a source, and undo and redo name the step they mean by its id ([AGENT-2](#agents)).

### Gesture leases

**WRITE-22.** A drag uses a lease:
- `begin(anchor, code_rev)` resolves once through the session and returns the lease, its cells and the current value;
- `update(lease, seq, value)` goes straight to the engine as `SetCell`, never through the session, and out-of-order updates are dropped;
- `commit(lease, seq, value)` and `cancel(lease)` go to the session. A commit is one coalesced `Values` Tx, and the engine installs the committed value in the same step that ends the lease;
- at a `code_rev` change, the session carries the lease's anchor into the new revision ([WRITE-12](#bases-and-revisions)). If it carries and still reads the accepted value, the lease stays, with its cells mapped into the new plan. Otherwise the lease ends and the accepted value is put back.

The session runs as an actor on its own thread, so nothing that waits on a compile blocks the engine, the transport or a lease's updates.
- *Why:* previews must never wait on a compile, and an agent saving `tracks/drums.ts` must not snap back a knob being dragged in `tracks/bass.ts`.
- *Today:* matches since step 3b (`crates/server/src/graph.rs`, `graph/lease.rs`, `graph/actor.rs`), and its cells since step 5, with these differences until step 6:
  - `begin` resolves against what the session last published: a revision among the last 64, through its code revision, or an id of one of the two code revisions before the accepted one's, through aliases the session carries once per code change. Only a client further behind waits for the session, and so does one a code revision or two behind while a settle hasn't yet carried the aliases to the code revision it published (they are read only once they carry to the accepted one's). Its cells are a `CellTarget`, the `CellId`s of its value in the renderer that plays (`Begun.cells` counts them), and what can be leased is a literal written in code or a declared control a call leaves out, which plays its default until its commit writes it in (both `home: code`).
  - `update` is a cell write straight into the slots of the renderer the lease aims at (`Player::set_cells`, by its `CellTarget`), never through the queue, so it is never dropped or kept waiting (RENDER-28). It is written under the control plane's lock, as everything else for a lease is, and a renderer built while the lease lives gets its value before it is sent, so an activation loses no update. Its seq answers `Graph::applied` once a block has heard it: a block late at most, never early.
  - `commit` is one `Values` Tx since step 6 (`Gestures::set` in `crates/editor`), one undo step; for a control its call leaves out, it writes the argument in (the step 5 design's D5). An outside change in the knob's own file that compiles rides along in the commit's baseline, and doesn't end the drag (`a_knob_commit_after_an_outside_edit_in_its_file_keeps_both`). The committed value reaches the engine in the step that ends the lease: in a new plan prepared with it, else as a cell write.
  - At a code change, a lease is carried by its whole call (WRITE-12's carry, by `Edits::between`), which is stricter than its literal's range. A lease's value is written into every new plan before it is sent, so it plays from its first block, window included. When the new plan fails to prepare, the lease's control moves to the accepted revision while its updates go on to the plan that plays, by its own origins.
  - Pending the owner's decision on the step 3b design's clarifications: `cancel`, idle expiry and "the window closed" act on the control plane at once, never through the session (C2); a commit refused only because the draft doesn't compile is pending, the value plays on and the session writes it once the draft compiles, or cancels it after 30 s (C5); a lease ends when idle for its TTL (10 s, renewed by updates and the web's keepalive every 2 s while a knob is held; `ttl_ms` on `POST /api/graph/lease/begin`, at most 60 s), or when the WebSocket of the window that began it closes (`?client=`); the news and `GET /api/graph/leases` list every live lease, so an agent can cancel one (C6). A pending commit that ends unsaved (its 30 s, the project closing or another opening, an outside save that changes its control, a cancel) is said in a toast.
  - The knob routes (`/preview`, `/set`, `/preview/cancel`) find a lease by its gesture string and the control the request names, so a string holds a lease per control: a script that sends `"script"` for every knob reaches each one's own. A late request of a lease that ended says why, unless its own `/preview/cancel` ended it. `GET /api/graph` lists the live leases with their controls' ids as its view names them, so the page keeps a dragged knob mounted when a save renumbers it.
  - Since `ag-fwd`, an agent's change never ends a drag: it is refused while the drag lives; a drag begun while an agent's change lands takes the change back, and so does a change to any file that the drag couldn't carry to ([AGENT-3](#agents)).
  - Measured: `crates/server/src/graph/tests.rs` (an outside save to another file during a drag never plays the code's value, not for one frame of the crossfade; saves above and below the dragged call keep it; a begin between a settle's phases; one gesture string on two controls; a pending commit that ends unsaved; a broken save then a release; a renderer that fails to build; a cancel during a commit; expiry and a closed window), and `m2-gates --server drag` (G9, p16: a drag through 12 saves, each a new renderer).

**WRITE-22a.** A lane edit made while playing (dragging a note, drawing automation) uses a lane lease: `begin(lane, clip)`; `update` sends a preview `SwapLane` straight to the engine with no file write, under the sounding-note rules of [RENDER-26a](#plans-data-and-arrangement-swaps); `commit` is one `Clips` Tx; `cancel` swaps the accepted data back. A tempo drag is a lease too ([RENDER-27a](#plans-data-and-arrangement-swaps)).
- *Why:* a dragged note must be heard where it is during the drag, without a journaled commit, a revision and an undo entry for every step.
- *Today:* since `next` (8b.7; `crates/server/src/graph/lanes.rs`; routes `POST /api/graph/lane/lease/{begin,update,keepalive,commit,cancel}`). `begin` checks the gesture on the accepted revision the control plane last published, never waiting for the session (RENDER-29). Only drags of notes and automation (`move`, `resize`, `notes`, `points`, `create`) are previewed; a gesture on an audio lane needs renders, so it answers `previewable: false` and is heard on release, as before. `update` applies the newest gesture with the commit's own pure function and Tx id (`clips::apply_as`, Q21), and swaps each lane it edits into the renderer that plays (`SwapLane`, under RENDER-26a), timed in the tempo map that plays; a lane it no longer reaches gets its accepted data back. `commit` is one `Clips` Tx under the lease's Tx id, so its PLANE-5 preview is what the lease played and nothing heard changes; until it lands, a fresh engine plays what was let go (an activation keeps the swapped lane already). Each settle carries the live leases: a new plan, or new data for a lane a lease names, hears the preview again, applied on the new revision; a change to a dragged clip ends the lease ("Bass changed elsewhere: your drag was dropped."). Cancel, an idle TTL (10 s) and the window closing put the accepted data back. The page leases every drag while playing (clips, edges, automation points, notes in Notes); Esc cancels. Tested in `crates/server/src/graph/lanes_tests.rs`, `routes_leases_tests.rs` and `e2e/s8-leases.mjs`.

## Agents

How the musician and agents share one open song: one writer, one undo history, and every change shown as it lands. The rules come from the [agents design](designs/agents/design.md), whose owner decisions D1 to D6 were all answered as it recommends (2026-10-23). Each of its tracks adds the rules it builds: AGENT-2 and AGENT-5 came with track 1 (`ag-steps`); AGENT-1, AGENT-3, AGENT-4, AGENT-9 and AGENT-10 with track 2 (`ag-fwd`); AGENT-6 with track 3 (`ag-see`); AGENT-8 and AGENT-11 with track 4 (`ag-routes`); AGENT-7 with track 5 (`ag-listen`), the last. AGENT-12 came with the docs plan (`docsite`).

**AGENT-1.** While a music2 app holds a project's lock ([WRITE-17](#the-project-lock)), an `m2` command that writes the project MUST hand its Tx to that app instead of failing. It builds the Tx on a read-only session from the files on disk and checks it there, under its own expectation and its planner's assertion. It sends it to the app's `POST /api/tx/commit` with `seen`, the hash of that check, and the new assets it names, using the url and the tools key in `~/.music2/session.json` ([AGENT-10](#agents)). The app commits it only if its delta has that hash. `m2` forwards only when the lock's holder is the program `music2` with the pid that file names, and the app says this folder is the song it has open. Otherwise it fails before changing anything, naming the holder ([WRITE-18](#the-project-lock)). `m2 edit` and `m2 write` make a change to a song file a Tx with a base, so an agent changes files through the app while it is open. `m2 import` hands the files it brings in to the app's own import instead (`POST /api/graph/audio/import`), so they come into the song only inside the change that lands ([AGENT-3](#agents)). While the song's code on disk doesn't build, they save the file instead, as the code editor saves it ([WRITE-2](#the-two-write-primitives)), in the app while it is open: an agent's save lands only when the song takes it in as written, as one undo step, the agent's; otherwise nothing is written, and `m2` says why. A forwarded write prints what a local one prints, and says it is one undo step in the app. A forwarded request may be up to 144 MiB: room for a file's old text and its new one at what undo keeps (64 MiB each). `m2` says so when one is bigger, and sends nothing; an answer it can't read is said with its status, and never as a write that landed.
- *Why:* while the song is open there must be one writer and one history, and only a change that comes through the writer can be refused when the musician holds what it touches.
- *Today:* since `ag-fwd`. A read-only session can be given the lock's holder (`Session::forward_to`, the `Forward` trait in `core/session/src/forward.rs`): its `commit_with` checks, judges and asserts as a writer's does, then sends the Tx with its own expectation, the hash of that check, the files under `assets/` it took in, and what each target holds once it landed; once it landed, it takes the touched files in from disk. Its undo and redo are refused. The app's `POST /api/tx/commit` takes `seen`, `assets`, `as` and `why`: it takes the assets in, then commits under the Tx's own expectation, asserting the delta's hash is `seen` (422 otherwise), so the agent's step is judged when undone. Since the review of `ag-routes`, `m2 import` sends its files' paths, its target and its words to `POST /api/graph/audio/import` while the app has the song open: before, it copied them into `assets/` itself, then forwarded the Tx, so a busy import left them there and the watcher's batch closed the Compare pill (`m2_import_while_comparing_copies_nothing`). No `m2` command sends `assets` now (gaps). `apps/m2/src/app.rs` reaches the app (plain HTTP/1.1 to 127.0.0.1, waiting while its process lives) and says each case of the design's 5.2 in words; an app that went away mid-call is read from the disk (landed, a journal of this Tx, never started, or can't tell). `m2 fix` asks the app for the fix its problems offer (`POST /api/graph/fix`); `bounce --place` reads the song after its render; `m2 edit` and `m2 write` (`apps/m2/src/text.rs`) commit a lane file confined to its lane and anything else on its checked hash. While the code on disk doesn't build, they save the file (`Session::save_built`, which takes it in only as written, else writes nothing): forwarded to the app's `PUT /api/graph/file` (`Forward::save`), whose save for the tools key is that one, refused 422 with why; with the app closed, `m2` saves it itself. Other `m2` writes still need code that builds (gaps). `POST /api/tx/commit`, `/api/tx/check`, `/api/graph/fix` and `PUT /api/graph/file` take bodies up to 144 MiB (`TX_BODY` in `crates/server/src/lib.rs`), which `m2` checks before it sends. Tested in `core/session/src/tx/forward_tests.rs`, `apps/m2/tests/forward.rs` and `apps/m2/tests/edit.rs`.

**AGENT-2.** Every change the app accepts while it has the song open for writing (a Tx, a save, or a watcher batch that builds, [WRITE-19](#outside-writers)), other than an undo or a redo, MUST be exactly one undo step, with an id that is never reused in that history, and a source: the page, an agent by its name, or outside the app. An agent's step is named by the words it gives. Only the watcher's job makes outside steps: files the app takes in for its own Tx (an import's) make none. An outside step holds only what outside writers changed. Its inverse puts back each file they changed or removed as the app last knew it, a code-editor save that didn't build yet included, guarded by the hash the batch left, as a save's inverse is ([WRITE-21](#undo)), and removes each file they made, other than under `assets/`. A watcher batch makes no step when it only adds files under `assets/`; when git wrote it, which is when git's `index.lock` was seen while it settled and every file it changed is as git's index has it (it says so: git can undo it); and when its undo couldn't put every file back: it changed or removed a binary file (an asset or a plugin state), a file is a link or has another file's name in other capitals, or it is more than undo keeps (it says so, with how many other files it changed). A step undone and redone keeps its id and its source. Undo and redo name the step they mean by its id, and are refused when it isn't the newest (for redo, the newest undone). Undo's name says the source. A step whose undo is refused because a file it puts back changed since can be skipped: it leaves the history, nothing is written, and the steps before it can be undone.
- *Why:* whoever made a change, the musician can take it back with ⌘Z, sees who made it, and never takes back a step they didn't see named. Undo never takes back the musician's own work with someone else's change, and a step that can't be undone never blocks the ones before it.
- *Today:* since `ag-steps`. Each `TxStep` has an id from a counter that never goes back, and a source, `Source::Page` or `Source::Outside` (`core/session/src/session.rs`); undo and redo carry both to the step they push. The watcher's job takes a batch in with `Session::take_in_batch` (`core/session/src/take.rs`), which records the outside step. It keeps, for each path a batch changed while it waits, what the session knew there before (a save takes its file out), so a batch that makes a waiting save build leaves the save out of its step; and it checks each path of the inverse as a commit would. The watcher says when it saw git's `index.lock` while the batch settled; the session then asks git (`git status`, which takes none of git's locks then) whether each changed file is as git's index has it. A read-only window records no step. `POST /api/graph/undo` and `/redo` take `{session, step}`, and answer 409 naming the newest step when `step` isn't it (`crates/server/src/graph/steps.rs`); without `step` they take the newest, as before. An undo refused because a file changed since answers 409 with `stuck`, and `POST /api/graph/undo/skip` `{session, step}` takes that step out of the history; the state's `undo_stuck` says the newest step can only be skipped. The state's `undo_name` and `redo_name` carry `step` and `source`; every edit answers with the `step` it made (`POST /api/tx/commit` too), and an undo or a redo with the step it took back and the tops after it. The page's Undo, ⌘Z and a toast's Undo send the step they name, or 0 when the window names none. The page keeps the ids of the steps its own edits made and names each one as its answer comes. The server makes an edit's step before its answer reaches the window, so a ⌘Z pressed in between named the step below and was refused, which a slow machine showed (`e2e/notes.mjs`). Pressed while the window's edits are on their way (`editing` in `web/src/lib/history.ts`; a timeline drag let go counts from then, while it waits for the state), Undo chooses its step at the press: the newest step those edits make once they are answered, or with none the step shown at the press, never one that lands meanwhile, so an agent's step landing during the wait is named, refusing, not taken back (`undoTop` in `web/src/components/GraphCanvas.tsx`). It waits at most 2 s, then takes back nothing and says so; another ⌘Z pressed while it waits does nothing. A ⌘Z refused because the newest step is one of those, made a moment before the window showed it, is sent again for that step; any other newest step is named, never taken back. Since `ag-fwd`, a step made with the tools key has the source `agent`, with the name the agent gives (`Source::Agent`), and is named by its words, when it gives some (AGENT-10). Since the review of `ag-routes`, the routes the server names a step for (an audio import, a Sampler's file, an audio track, a MIDI file) name it in the job that makes it, by the agent's words when it gave some: before, a second job renamed it after its event was told, so `m2 call` and the Edit menu disagreed (`an_agents_step_is_named_by_its_words_in_the_same_job`, `m2_call_names_every_step_by_its_why`). A step of `layout.json` with no name is "the track order, colours, markers or the song's key". Tested in `core/session/src/tx/steps_tests.rs` and `crates/server/src/graph/steps_tests.rs`.

**AGENT-3.** Agents MUST NOT hold leases. The app MUST refuse an agent's write (a Tx, an undo, a fix, any write route) with status 423 and a `busy` answer that names what the musician holds, when the write touches: a file in which a knob lease lives; a lane file a lane lease holds; `data/arrangement.json` while a tempo lease lives; a plugin's state while its window has changes not yet saved; a file the code editor has unsaved text in; or anything, while a take records or the Compare pill (D2) is open. Compare MUST NOT change the song: V on the newest step, when an agent or another program made it, plays Before, the song with that step taken back, built in memory and never written; V again plays After, the song as it is. The song's files, undo and the view stay at After all the while, so the app quitting or crashing while Before plays leaves the song as it was. Each V names its step, and one that names a stale step is refused: one that isn't the newest when the pill opens, or isn't the one compared while it is open; V on the musician's own newest step is refused, naming it. Esc closes the pill and keeps what plays: on Before, the step is undone then, one undo step of the musician's that Redo brings back, and the app says so. Everything else that closes the pill closes it at After, writing nothing: any other action of the musician's but listening (play, stop, the loop, a seek), any other change to undo's history (a write with the page key, an undo, a redo), the socket of the window that opened it closing, the song closing or another opening, and a batch the watcher takes in, which says that a file changed outside the app. Every change of the pill is told to every window and agent: its step, Before or After, and why it closed. The app checks when the write arrives, and again at the start of the settle that follows, under the control plane's lock, before anything of the change is carried, sent to the engine or shown. If the second check finds something the musician began meanwhile, or finds that the change would end something they hold, whichever files it touched (a drag its revision can't carry, a plugin window not yet saved whose instance its plan doesn't keep), the app takes the agent's change back and answers busy: no step, and no notice of it. That check hears what the plugin windows did up to then. An agent's write is never previewed, so the musician never hears a refused change, and nothing they hold ends or waits. The tools key MUST NOT act on what the musician holds: a drag's routes, the code editor's list of unsaved files, undo's skip, a take, and Compare are the musician's ([AGENT-11](#agents)), and a knob's value an agent sets is a plain edit, which never commits the musician's drag. A WebSocket opened with the tools key names no window: its closing ends nothing the musician holds, which only the closing of their window's own socket ends. An agent's write refused on arrival leaves nothing behind: a file it brings in (an audio file, a Sampler's WAV) comes into the song's `assets/` only inside the change that lands, once that check passed. A file an agent writes itself is an outside write ([WRITE-19](#outside-writers)): this rule can't refuse it, and it can end a drag.
- *Why:* the musician wins every clash. Only a write that comes through the app can be refused, so agents are told to write through it.
- *Today:* since `ag-fwd`, and the Compare pill since `ag-see`. The holds and the check are in `crates/server/src/graph/busy.rs`; the actor runs it on arrival (`Actor::arrive`: a Tx's and an undo's files, a set's knob file; a gesture route's files aren't known before it is planned, so only a take is checked there, gaps) and at the start of each settle that follows an agent's change, under the control plane's lock, before any lease is carried or anything sent. `set_unsaved` changes its list under that lock too. A change met there is retracted (`Session::retract`: it commits the inverse of the last change and leaves undo and redo as they were before it), the settle plays what was there, and the job answers 423 with `taken_back`. An agent's job runs without the preview hook. The drag routes refuse the tools key. Since the review of `ag-fwd`: the settle's check asks what decides each hold's fate, the carry of every knob lease and lane lease to the new revision (`lanes::carried`) and the plugin instances the new plan keeps, so a change that would end one is taken back whatever file it touched; it first hears the plugin windows (their host events and changed flags, as the next job would); and the notices the settle found before it (a state that didn't load, a renderer that didn't build) are shown only once it passed. A plugin's state is named by `DataPath`, as the song spells it (a key `aux` is `aux-.state`). The caller reaches the actor as a value in its job, so a drag begun there is refused to an agent too. `retract` also puts back the oldest steps the change pushed out of a full history. The routes above answer the tools key 403 (since `ag-routes`, from the route table, AGENT-8). Since `ag-see` and its review, the server keeps what the pill compares (`crates/server/src/graph/compare.rs`). `POST /api/graph/compare` `{session, step, before, client}` (the page key only) opens it on Before, or flips it: Before is built by `Session::undone` (the newest step's undo, checked and built, nothing written; the undo Esc may send next reuses it), and each settle plays it in place of the accepted revision while Before is chosen and its step is still undo's newest, by the same delta from what plays ([RENDER-26](#plans-data-and-arrangement-swaps)). A V naming another step than the newest, or than the one compared, is 409 with `newest` or `comparing`; V on the musician's own newest step is 409 with `newest`. `{session, open: false, keep}` closes it: with `keep` on Before, it undoes the step as the page's Undo does. It ends at After when undo's newest step is no longer the one compared, at the closing of the socket whose `client` opened it, at the song closing or another opening, and when the watcher takes a batch in. Each change is the event `{"t": "compare", session, n, step, open, after, name, why}`: `n` counts up, so a window shows only the newest it heard; the state and the focus record carry `comparing` (`{step, after, name, n}`, or null). While it is open, it is a hold of everything, "you're comparing Claude: brighter hats", which `/api/graph/open`, `/api/graph/close` and `/api/project/new` met too when an agent sent them, until `ag-routes` made them the musician's (AGENT-11). Since `ag-routes`, `POST /api/graph/set` with the tools key never finds a drag's lease by its gesture: it is a plain value edit, busy while the drag lives; and an agent's change through any gesture route that its settle takes back answers busy, `taken_back` (before, it answered as if it had landed: `an_agents_gesture_taken_back_at_its_settle_answers_busy`). Since the review of `ag-routes`: a socket opened with the tools key names no window (`routes::ws` ignores its `client`), so its closing ends no drag and no audition (`a_tools_socket_naming_a_window_ends_none_of_its_drags`); window ids stay in what agents read, as the page needs them, and no route open to agents takes one. An agent's audio import and Sampler file are checked on arrival before anything is copied (`Graph::arriving`), then copied to a hidden part file, which no read of the song or the watcher sees (`m2_editor::import::stage`), and named into `assets/` in the job that lands the change, after that job's own check; refused, the part goes, and so does the folder when the import made it (`an_agents_import_while_comparing_copies_nothing`, `a_take_begun_while_an_agents_import_copies_leaves_nothing`). One taken back at its settle leaves its new file in `assets/` (gaps). Tested in `crates/server/src/graph/busy_tests.rs`, `lanes_tests.rs` (a clip drag), `record_tests.rs` (a take), `plugin_window_tests.rs` (a window not yet saved) and `compare_tests.rs` (the pill).

**AGENT-4.** An undo sent with the tools key (`m2 undo`) MUST take back the newest step only when its source is an agent of the same name, and otherwise refuse, naming the newest step and its source. The page's Undo takes the newest step, whatever its source (D1).
- *Why:* one history in one line; an agent may only take back its own work.
- *Today:* since `ag-fwd` (`crates/server/src/graph/steps.rs`; `m2_undo_takes_back_only_its_own_step`).

**AGENT-5.** Every step not made by the page MUST be shown as it lands: a notice that never takes the keyboard's focus and never blocks, with Undo while it is still the newest step, and a glow on the tracks, lanes and knobs its delta changed, until the musician's next action.
- *Why:* trust comes from seeing every change, without being interrupted.
- *Today:* since `ag-steps`. After each job that records a step, whatever its source, the server sends every window `{"t": "step", "session", "step", "made", "source", "name", "files", "undoable", "tracks", "lanes", "knobs"}`: `undoable` says whether undo can take the step back now; `tracks`, `lanes` and `knobs` are what the step's delta changed, by module key, lane and the view's knob ids (`crates/editor/src/changed.rs`), worked out off the session's thread from the revisions before and after it. The page shows a notice for each outside step ("Changed outside the app: …", with Undo, which sends that step's id), one at a time, a newer one replacing it. It offers Undo only while undo can take that step back; once a newer change came, the notice says so and offers no Undo. It outlines what changed until the next click or key press (`web/src/lib/notice.ts`, `web/src/components/Toasts.tsx`; `e2e/ag-notice.mjs`). Since `ag-fwd`, an agent's step is shown too, by its name: "Claude: brighter hats"; the event, `undo_name` and `redo_name` carry `by`. Steps in a row from one author are counted in one notice ("Claude: 3 changes · newest: …"); another author's starts its own.

**AGENT-6.** The focus record and pointers are attention, not song. The page posts the focus record; `m2 point` posts a pointer. The server keeps them in memory only and sends them as events. They MUST NOT be written to a file, a revision or undo. A pointer MUST NOT move the musician's view or change their selection, or with it the MIDI keyboard's target; its note takes no press but its own buttons', and OK on it ends nothing the musician does. The focus record says what the musician looks at: the dock's view, the selected track, the clips picked and a marquee's bars, the roll's selected notes by pitch, start and length, the knob last touched, the bars the timeline shows, and the code view's file and line; the server adds where playback is, the loop, the unsaved code, what Compare compares and the MIDI keyboard's target. A focus for another song than the open one is refused, the tools key can read it but not post it, and both go when the song closes or another opens. A pointer names a track, a clip, a lane, a knob or bars, with an agent's words (AGENT-10); there is one at a time, and a target the song hasn't got, bars past its end included, is refused. Off screen, it waits at the edge with Show: only that click moves the view, and it never selects. Agents follow the song through the events the page hears (`m2 watch <song>`): every event names the song it is about, by its session; a socket opened with the tools key MUST NOT take a toast from the page; a socket that falls behind is told how many events it missed, and the page then reads the song, the pointer and the Compare pill again. `m2 watch <song>` follows that song only: when the app opens another song or closes it, it says so and ends.
- *Why:* what someone looks at is not the song, and the agent must never take the musician's place.
- *Today:* since `ag-see`. `POST /api/focus` (the page key, 200 ms after the last change and when its socket connects) and `GET /api/focus`, kept beside the unsaved list (`crates/server/src/graph/focus.rs`); every window and agent hears `{"t": "focus", "focus": …}`. `POST /api/point` (`{session, target, words}`, or `{session, clear: true}`: the page's OK clears it) checks its target against the accepted revision and is heard as `{"t": "point", "point": …}`; the state's `point` holds it for a page that loads later. Neither is a write: both work in a read-only window. `m2 focus`, `m2 watch` and `m2 point` (`apps/m2/src/see.rs`). The server sends `undo` and `redo` events after the `step` events before them; a toast is kept until a page's socket hears it, and a socket that falls behind hears `{"t": "lagged", "missed": n}`. Since the review of `ag-see`: every event carries `session`, the open song's when it is made (null with none), stamped where events are made (`AppState::send`, `Inner::tell`), and `/api/graph/status` says it too; `m2 watch` prints `{"t": "watch", "session": …}` first, and ends with `{"t": "closed", "why": "song"}` when an event or the status names another song or none, `"why": "app"` when the app goes away, or `"why": "error"` (exit 1) when its socket says what it can't read; it ends quietly when what reads it goes away, and when its reader falls behind it drops what waits beyond 1,024 lines and says `{"t": "lagged", "missed": n, "by": "m2"}`. A `bars:` pointer past the song's end is refused, saying how many bars it has. Show on a knob of a track the dock doesn't show selects nothing: its note waits beside that track's header, naming the knob. The note takes presses only on Show and OK, and neither closes the Compare pill. Tested in `crates/server/src/graph/focus_tests.rs`, `crates/server/src/routes_see_tests.rs`, `crates/server/src/lib.rs` (the toasts), `apps/m2/tests/watch.rs` and `e2e/ag-focus.mjs`.

**AGENT-7.** Agent listening (`m2 listen`) MUST be an offline range render ([RENDER-4](#range-render)) in a process of its own, at background priority, its plugin host process included, and one at a time on a machine. Background priority is the lowest the OS gives a process beside playback: on macOS the background state, which runs it on the efficiency cores playback doesn't use; on Linux, where no core is playback's (its engine threads aren't pinned), `SCHED_IDLE`, which gives a core to any other thread at once, and the idle I/O class. It reads committed files, or a Tx it checked and never wrote. It MUST NOT touch the live engine, the app's plugin instances or the audio thread. It asks the open app only for what its range or `--as-heard` need, before it waits its turn, and never waits on the app for long: when the app doesn't answer, it reads the song's files instead, and says so. It measures what its own offline plan copies after each block, through copy taps: the song's output; each track where its audio enters the mixer's module, its own sound before its channel, as a stem is; and each mixer channel after its strip (its pan and fader), the track as it reaches the mix. A channel that goes to a bus channel reaches the mix inside that bus: it is read at its own fader, and the bus is measured as a strip of its own. Copy taps MUST change no bit of what plays or renders: they only read buffers once the graph ran, and only an offline render that owns its renderer and never gives it up has them (`Copying`); a renderer, which is what the live engine takes, holds none. Each meter MUST be warmed with what the render has before the range, up to a second, and measure the range alone, so a range that starts while a sound is loud reads it as it is. A sample that isn't a number is counted and read as 0. Masking and stereo are measured on a track as it reaches the mix; masking is among what the master sums (the strips that go to it), moment by moment: two tracks mask each other only in a band, in a moment, where both sound and each has sound of its own there, and the result says how much of the range they do. A track in no channel, in two, or in a channel with another track has no strip of its own, and the result says its masking isn't measured. The listening mute and solo reach it only when it asks (`--as-heard`, [MON-4](#monitoring-overlays-and-mute-in-song)), given to each revision by its tracks' keys, and then the result says so. `--with` measures the same bars before and after, each in its own revision's tempo and meter, and matches tracks by key. Gate G13 counts playback's dropouts while it runs, its plugin host included, and while agents' edits are forwarded.
- *Why:* real-time audio never glitches, whatever an agent does; and what an agent measures is what the song renders, never another sound.
- *Today:* since `ag-listen`. `m2 listen <song> [--bars 9-16] [--tracks kick,bass] [--as-heard] [--with tx.json] [--png out.png] [--json]` (`apps/m2/src/listen.rs`): bars count from 1, and stop at the song's last clip; without `--bars` it listens to the loop when one is on (the open app's, else `.music2/view.json`'s), else the song to the end of its last clip, with 2 bars of preroll, so it says it is approximate unless the preroll reaches the song's start ([RENDER-5](#range-render)). A song with no clips has no bars to listen to, and says so, the same with `--bars` as without. `Copying::new(renderer, taps, from)` (`core/runtime/src/taps.rs`) copies each tapped buffer from the block that holds frame `from` on, with the frames each tap's samples come late by ([RENDER-19](#latency)); it refuses a renderer the live engine plays, or one prepared for it (`copy_taps_copy_each_block_from_the_frame_asked`, `copy_taps_are_offline_only` in `core/runtime/src/tests/taps.rs`; `copy_taps_change_no_bit`, every pure fixture with the output, every node with an audio output and every overlay site tapped, in `core/play/tests/bit_identity.rs`). Which buffers (`crates/editor/src/listen/hear.rs`): each track's overlay site into the song's own module, where std's mixer is, else its first ([MON-1](#monitoring-overlays-and-mute-in-song)); a bus that passes its input on, what enters its strip (`mix::strip_input`); and the fader of its channel, when the channel is its own (`mix::fader`: the gain that reads `channels.<key>.db`, or for a fader a signal sets, the gain std's mixer made at that channel's place), with the bus channel it goes `to`. The listen copies from a second before the range (less when the render starts nearer), which warms each meter (`Meter::warm`). The measures (`crates/editor/src/listen.rs`): loudness, integrated and short-term max, per ITU-R BS.1770 with EBU R128's gates, as EBU Tech 3341's cases read (K-weighting made from its analogue shapes, BS.1770's table at 48 kHz); true peak 4× oversampled (BS.1770 Annex 2, a windowed sinc); sample peak, RMS and crest; ten octave bands from 31.5 Hz to 16 kHz, each an 8th-order Butterworth band-pass of four biquads (a tone at the centre of the band beside reads 26 dB down); stereo correlation, width (side against mid) and whether the lows under 120 Hz are mono; clipped samples, samples that weren't numbers (`non_finite`, each meter's), and silent tracks. Masking (`masking`), each pair a band, from each strip that goes to the master, in 50 ms moments: in a moment, a track counts in a band when it sounds in the moment (louder than −60 dBFS), is louder than −60 dBFS in the band, and isn't 16 dB or more under a band beside it (what a band-pass lets through from its neighbour, not sound of its own); any two that count within 6 dB of the loudest that counts there mask each other in that moment; each pair says its band, the share of the range's moments it masks in (`share`), and their levels in them. `--with` checks the Tx on the read-only session that read the song (`Session::check`), turns the same bars (or the loop's or the song's beats) into each revision's own seconds, says when they moved (`moved`), and renders the song and the candidate, with both results and the difference: what changed, by track key, a measure one side has and the other hasn't as `{"was", "now"}` (a track gone silent), a track only one side has as `added` or `removed`, and masking that began, ended or changed its share. `--as-heard` gives each revision's overlays the values the mute and solo give them by its own tracks' keys (`Overlaid`), from the open app, else `.music2/overlays.json` ([MON-3](#monitoring-overlays-and-mute-in-song)), and says `"as_heard": true` with what is muted and soloed. The open app is asked (`GET /api/graph`, waiting 5 s at most, before the listen's lock) only for the loop, without `--bars`, and the mute and solo, with `--as-heard`; `app` says what was read from it, or that it didn't answer and the files were read instead; `unsaved_plugins` names the plugins whose window changed their sound, not saved yet (the graph state's `plugins[node].unsaved`), whose saved state the listen hears. Flags it doesn't know, a flag where a value should be, and a `--png` path in the song or that can't be written are refused before anything renders. `--png` draws third-octave rows in 50 ms columns, a block for the mix and one for each track `--tracks` names, as heard (written with the `png` crate, compressed: `crates/editor/src/listen/png.rs`). The process runs at background priority, every thread of it (`m2_os::process::background`: on Linux `SCHED_IDLE`, the idle I/O class and a nice of 10), and so does its plugin host, started with `--m2-background` (`Remote::background`, `m2_plugins::background_host`); its render on `Threads::Beside`; a second `m2 listen` waits for the first on `~/.music2/listen.lock`; the JSON's `priority` says what the OS says of both (`m2_os::process::in_background`; on macOS `proc_pidinfo`'s flags, which hold the background state a process gave itself and one another process gave it: `getpriority` asked of another process reads only the second, so the Mac's first full check read the host as normal). Not yet as the rule says: a host started with `--m2-background` still asks for real time for each plugin's audio thread (`start_audio` in `core/plugins/src/remote/child.rs`), so where the OS grants it (Linux with real-time rights, `RLIMIT_RTPRIO`) that thread leaves `SCHED_IDLE` and runs above playback's other work ([gaps.md](gaps.md#scripts-agents-and-the-command-line), "A background plugin host's audio threads still ask for real time"). Gate G13 is `m2-gates realtime --beside agents` (`crates/gates/src/realtime/beside.rs`), its listens 2 bars of p50 each, so several land in its minute on a Mac's efficiency cores (one of 8 bars didn't, at `a1e3c77`); every other listen there is of `examples/with-plugins` when its plugins are installed. On Linux, a listen beside G12 costs its callbacks about as much as any one busy core does (p50 +14 to +22%; the review of `ag-listen`, F6): recorded, as Linux timings are ([PLAT-1](#platforms)). Tested in `crates/editor/src/listen_tests.rs`, `apps/m2/tests/listen.rs`, `a_host_asked_to_runs_at_background_priority` (`core/plugins/src/remote/mod.rs`) and `the_state_says_a_plugin_window_has_changes_not_saved_yet` (`crates/server/src/graph/plugin_window_tests.rs`).

**AGENT-8.** Every HTTP route MUST be declared once, with its method, its path, the largest body it takes, whether it writes, whether agents may call it, plain words and an example body. The router, the read-only window's 423 ([WRITE-17](#the-project-lock)), AGENT-11's 403 and `m2 routes --json` are all made from that one table, and an `/api` path or method it hasn't got answers 404 or 405, whoever asks: no route outside the table answers. `m2 call <song> <path> [json]` sends a route's request to the app that has the song open, with the tools key, the method the table gives it (`--method` picks one where a path has two), and the song's `session` when the body leaves it out; a GET's keys go in the query. It refuses a route not for agents, and a body bigger than the route takes, before sending anything, saying that nothing was sent (for the first, with the words the app would answer), and waits while busy, as every `m2` write does ([AGENT-3](#agents)). It says the undo step a write made, and what an undo took back.
- *Why:* a list kept by hand drifts. Agents learn the API from it, not from the source.
- *Today:* since `ag-routes`. Each `crates/server/src/routes*.rs` declares its routes with `api::routes!`, which makes that file's part (`PART`: its router and its part of the table) from the same lines; `api.rs` names each file once, `api::all()` joins the parts, and one middleware (`api::gate`) answers from it: 403 for the tools key on a route not for agents, then 423 for a write in a window open read-only. `m2 routes` prints the table, the routes for agents first, and `m2 routes --json` as `[{method, path, limit, writes, agents, words, example}]`, with no app running. Since the review of `ag-routes`: each route carries `limit`, the largest body it takes (`api::BODY`, 2 MiB, unless its line says `up to`), which its router keeps and `m2 call` checks before it sends; every `/api` path reaches the table's routes only through `api::gate` (`routes::served`), which answers a path or method the table hasn't got 404 or 405 whoever asks, so a route added outside the table answers no one (`a_route_outside_the_table_answers_no_one`, in place of the scan of the source for route spellings; the scan for hand lists stays); and `m2 call` says the step a write made only when the answer's step is undo's newest, and what an undo took back (`m2_call_undo_says_what_it_took_back`). Tested in `crates/server/src/api_tests.rs` (every route declared once and no hand list left; every example body reads; each body up to its limit; every write 423 read-only; every route not for agents 403 to the tools key; no route outside the table) and `apps/m2/tests/call.rs`.

**AGENT-9.** The app MUST contain no model and call none. It only shares focus, shows what changed and takes actions. There is no "agents may change the song" switch (D3); when an agent changes things is decided by what the musician asks it.
- *Why:* owner rule: no AI in the app, simple yet elegant.
- *Today:* matches: the app has no model, no switch, and calls none; `m2` is the agents' one door.

**AGENT-10.** The app MUST give its page a key of its own, handed only to its window, and publish a different key, the tools key, in `~/.music2/session.json`. A request with the tools key is an agent's. Its name is the one it gives (`as` in the body, or the `x-m2-as` header), else `agent`; a header carries its words as UTF-8, percent-encoded (`%C3%A9` for é), so any name fits in one. A step's source, AGENT-3, AGENT-4 and AGENT-11 go by the key, never by what a request says about itself. What an agent gives to be shown (its name, why it made a change, a pointer's note) is plain text, at most 80 characters, with no control or invisible characters (bidi controls, zero-width marks): a line break or a tab reads as a space. An agent's name can't read as the app's own words for a change made outside it ("Changed outside the app", "outside change"): it is shown as `agent`. Keys are compared in constant time.
- *Why:* with one key, a script that leaves out its name passes as the musician and skips every refusal.
- *Today:* since `ag-fwd`. The shell makes both keys at each launch, puts only the page key in the window's start script and only the tools key in `session.json`, which it writes whole: to a new file with mode 0600, then renamed over the old one. The server's `auth` tells the two apart and puts the caller on the request (`crates/server/src/caller.rs`), which every job on the session's actor carries; the words an agent gives lose their control characters and are cut to 80 characters with "…". Since the review of `ag-fwd`, they lose the invisible characters a Tx's path may not hold too (`m2_song::tx::hidden`), a name that reads as the app's words for an outside change is `agent`, and `auth` compares keys in constant time. The headless server writes both keys into the session file it is told to write, `page_token` for the browser suites.

**AGENT-11.** (D6.) The transport (play, stop, seek, the loop, Stop All Sound), recording, listening mute and solo, the view, plugin windows and their latches, looking again for installed plugins, auditions and the MIDI keyboard's target, what a MIDI keyboard or the sound input would send, drags ([AGENT-3](#agents)), redo and skipping a step, naming steps, the code editor's list of unsaved files, the focus record and Compare, Export Audio and stems, saving a preset into the musician's own presets, settings, and opening, closing, making, saving as or renaming songs, the recent songs list and the read-only window's Retry are the musician's. The app MUST answer them 403 with plain words when the tools key asks, and MUST ignore what a WebSocket opened with the tools key sends. Every read stays open to agents, and so does every song change the page can make, the code editor's save included, as [AGENT-1](#agents) says: an agent's save lands only when the song takes it in as written.
- *Why:* none of these is a song change an agent should make, so none needs a step, a notice or an undo of its own; a jump ends a take (`crates/server/src/graph/record.rs`); an export may pause playback to borrow the plugins' instances ([RENDER-1](#offline)), and agents render in their own process (`m2 render`, `m2 bounce`); a scan for plugins loads every installed plugin's library into the host where the song's plugins play; and a preset saved over another can't be undone.
- *Today:* since `ag-routes`: the route table marks each route (AGENT-8), and `api::gate` answers the tools key 403 on every route not for agents, with the route's words, one sentence for each: "POST /api/graph/transport is the musician's: play, stop, toggle or restart. Agents can't call it (AGENT-11); ask the musician, or point at it with m2 point." (`Route::refusal`, which `m2 call` says too; `the_refusals_read_as_the_contract_quotes_them` checks this quote). A WebSocket opened with the tools key hears every event, and what it sends (an audition, the MIDI keyboard's target) is ignored. Since the review of `ag-routes`, Look again (`POST /api/plugins/scan`) is the musician's; `GET /api/plugins`, the list kept, stays open.

**AGENT-12.** `m2 docs` MUST print the guide built into that `m2`: the pages `docs/guide/SUMMARY.md` names, in its order, each as its Markdown with its includes filled in, and needs no song and no app. Pages made from code (every `m2` command, every route) MUST be printed from the code, and a test MUST fail when the committed page differs. Every `m2` command MUST be declared once, in one table that `m2`'s usage and `m2 docs commands` are made from; a command `m2` doesn't know is refused, exit status 2. `m2 docs agents` prints the text a new song's `AGENTS.md` gets from that `m2` ([FMT-23](#presentation-and-local-files)).
- *Why:* agents learn music2 from what `m2` says; a guide kept apart from the binary, or a list of commands written twice, drifts from what that `m2` does.
- *Today:* built in the docs plan (`docsite`). `apps/m2/build.rs` reads `SUMMARY.md` and embeds each page; `apps/m2/src/commands.rs` is the command table; `m2 docs commands` and `m2 docs routes` make `docs/guide/commands.md` and `routes.md` (`ci/guide.sh gen` writes them). Tested in `apps/m2/tests/docs.rs`.

## Diagnostics

### Model

**DIAG-1.** Every problem MUST be reported as:

```rust
Diagnostic { severity: Error | Warning | Notice, code: &'static str, message: String,
             src: Option<Src>, module: Option<Key>, fixes: Vec<Fix> }        // fixes: the likeliest first
Fix        { code: &'static str, title: String, likely: bool, tx: Tx }       // a ready-to-commit Tx, bases included
```

The message is plain words, and names the file, the field and the fix where they apply.
- A fix is built by whoever raised the diagnostic: `core/song` for data fixes, the journal code for FinishTx and DiscardTx, `crates/editor` for Restore. Applying it is `commit(fix.tx)`, and a stale base gives `Conflict`, as for any Tx.
- `likely` marks the one fix the problem plainly calls for, which the app offers in one click. Others are a choice.
- Fix codes are a closed vocabulary, the kebab-case of the names this page uses: `relink`, `restore`, `delete-data`, `repin-asset`, `fix-asset-name`, `retag`, `convert`, `assign-clip-ids`, `renumber-clips`, `finish-tx`, `discard-tx`. A new fix adds a code here, never a type to core.
- *Why:* agents and the app need to act on a problem, not parse prose. A fix that is a Tx is checked, journaled and undone like any edit, and a new data feature never grows core's types.
- *Today:* matches since step 6: a fix is `{ code, title, likely, tx }`, its Tx's id derived from the problem and the hashes of the files it was raised on, and applying it is committing its Tx (`core/song/src/diag.rs`). Build and check errors are `m2_ir::Diagnostic`, with a severity and a code but no fixes. The codes the step 6 design's C2 adds (`tx.invalid`, `tx.expected`, `tx.unexpected`, `lock.held`, `lock.local`, `git.locked`) are raised but not yet in the table below, pending the owner.

### Codes

| Code | Severity | Raised when | Fixes | Blocks acceptance |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `code.build` | Error | the TypeScript doesn't parse or throws | — | yes |
| `code.check` | Error | the graph checker rejects the IR | — | yes |
| `kernel.offline` | Error | song code calls an offline kernel, which only music2 runs ([ABI-2](#kernel-abi-reservations)) | — | yes |
| `import.outside-code` | Error | an import resolves to anything but a Code file of the snapshot ([FMT-6a](#paths)) | — | yes |
| `key.invalid` | Error | a key breaks [ID-1](#keys) | — | yes |
| `key.duplicate` | Error | a key is used twice where it must be unique: two tracks, two `key()` frames in one parent, two plugin keys in one frame ([ID-5](#keys)) | — | yes |
| `lane.conflict` | Error | a lane is declared again with another kind or other settings ([ID-6](#keys)) | — | yes |
| `lane.shared` | Error | a lane key is declared from two `key()` frame chains without `shared: true` ([ID-6](#keys)) | — | yes |
| `manifest.invalid` | Error | `music2.toml` or `music2.lock` doesn't parse or has unknown keys | — | yes, but never `save()` |
| `format.missing` | Error | `music2.toml` has no `format`: a format-0 project | — | the project doesn't open |
| `format.older` | Error | the project's `format` is older than this app reads | — | the project doesn't open |
| `format.newer` | Error | the project's `format` is newer than this app | — | the project doesn't open |
| `sdk.unsatisfied` | Error | the `@m2/core` requirement isn't met; names both versions | — | yes |
| `pkg.unsatisfied` | Error | another package's requirement isn't met; names both versions. For an older std than the bundled one, says how to move ([FMT-3](#manifest-and-versions)). A pack's: no installed version is admitted, the version `music2.lock` names isn't, or there is no store ([PACK-9](#packs)) | — | yes |
| `pkg.undeclared` | Error | code imports a package `[dependencies]` doesn't list | — | yes |
| `pkg.missing` | Error | a pack the song uses isn't there: the version `music2.lock` names isn't installed, or a path dependency's folder doesn't hold that pack; says what to install and how, and `m2 pack lock` when another admitted version is installed ([PACK-9](#packs)) | — | yes |
| `pkg.changed` | Error | the installed version isn't the one `music2.lock` names (another hash), or one of its files no longer matches its `m2pack.json` (changed, missing, added, or a link), naming the file ([PACK-6](#packs), [PACK-9](#packs)); says how to put it back | — | yes |
| `pkg.broken` | Error | a pack the song uses doesn't load: its `m2pkg.toml`, its kernels, or what it needs of the SDK and std ([PACK-2](#packs)); names why | — | yes |
| `pkg.invalid` | Error | a package module throws while it loads, a descriptor is invalid, or two components in a package share an id ([PKG-9](#component-descriptors)); its components are left out of the catalog. On the session, not a revision, and only for std and the packs the accepted revision uses ([PACK-11](#packs)) | — | no |
| `arrangement.invalid` | Error | `data/arrangement.json` breaks [FMT-13](#arrangement); names the entry | — | yes |
| `plugin.state-collision` | Error | two stateful plugins resolve to one state path | — | yes |
| `plugin.param-unaddressed` | Error | a plugin parameter is written without its id (`mix`, not `mix_12`) | — | yes |
| `plugin.missing` | Error | a plugin isn't installed; its node is silent, or passes its input through ([FMT-18a](#plugin-state-and-parameters)) | — | no |
| `plugin.param-renamed` | Notice | a parameter address's name part differs from the plugin's current name | — | no |
| `plugin.param-reverted` | Notice | the plugin changed parameters code owns outside a gesture, and they were set back ([HOME-5a](#homes-of-values)); on the session, not a revision | — | no |
| `plugin.not-borrowed` | Warning | an export couldn't take a live plugin instance offline, so a new one played it from its committed state ([RENDER-1](#offline)); on the export, not a revision | — | no |
| `state.plugin-mismatch` | Error | a state blob's sidecar names another plugin than the code; the blob isn't loaded | Relink, DeleteData | no |
| `state.unverified` | Error | a state blob has no sidecar; the blob isn't loaded | Relink, DeleteData | no |
| `lane.invalid` | Error | a lane file doesn't parse or doesn't match its lane; it plays its last accepted data, or empty ([FMT-9](#strictness)) | — | no |
| `lane.unit-mismatch` | Error | a control lane's `unit` differs from its declaration; it plays its last accepted data, or empty | Retag, Convert | no |
| `asset.missing` | Error | a clip's asset is missing, unreadable, or named with other case or normalisation, or a package file a kernel reads isn't there ([PACK-5](#packs)); that clip or that sound is silent | FixAssetName, when only case or normalisation differs | no |
| `asset.changed` | Warning | an asset's hash differs from its pin | RepinAsset | no |
| `asset.unpinned` | Notice | a clip's asset reference has no `hash` | RepinAsset | no |
| `data.unclaimed` | Warning | data that no declared lane or plugin reads | Relink, Restore, DeleteData | no |
| `data.claimed` | Notice | a Tx makes a module claim data that was unclaimed before it ([DIAG-3](#codes)) | — | no |
| `clip.anonymous` | Notice | clips without ids | AssignClipIds | no |
| `clip.invalid-id` | Notice | a clip id breaks [FMT-11](#lane-files-and-clip-ids); the clip is read as anonymous | AssignClipIds | no |
| `clip.duplicate-id` | Warning | two clips in one lane share an id | RenumberClips | no |
| `file.unknown` | Notice | a file under `data/` that `DataPath` can't parse | — | no |
| `latency.in-feedback` | Warning | latency inside a feedback loop ([RENDER-20](#latency)); a notice on the session, said when the nodes change | — | no |
| `latency.changed` | Warning | a plugin's latency changed during an offline render, which kept the old value ([RENDER-17](#latency)); on the render: an export's problem, `m2 render`'s warning | — | no |
| `tx.interrupted` | Error | a commit on this host was interrupted | FinishTx, DiscardTx | no |
| `tx.foreign` | Warning | a journal from another host was found, as in a synced folder | — | no |
| `tx.path` | Error | a Tx names a path [WRITE-3a](#the-two-write-primitives) refuses; on the Tx | — | the Tx is rejected |
| `lock.unsupported` | Error | the volume can't take the project lock; the project is read-only | — | no |
| `project.synced` | Notice | the project is in a synced folder ([FMT-20](#presentation-and-local-files)) | — | no |
| `layout.invalid` | Notice | `layout.json` doesn't parse; the view uses the defaults, the track builders leave the file out, and reorder, recolour and markers refuse ([FMT-19](#presentation-and-local-files)); on the session, not a revision | ResetLayout | no |
| `overlays.active` | Warning | an export while monitoring overlays are active (on the export, not a revision) | — | no |
| `plugin.crashed` | Error | the plugin host process crashed, or stopped answering and was ended; its instances are silent until the next activation makes them again; one problem naming each of them; on the session, not a revision, through the fault ring ([HOST-3](#pluginhost), [RENDER-25](#plans-data-and-arrangement-swaps)) | — | no |
| `sound.stopped` | Error | a sound failed while the song played (a kernel's or a voice's trap, or a kernel over its memory) and is silent alone until the next activation or Stop All Sound; on the session, not a revision, through the fault ring ([RENDER-25](#plans-data-and-arrangement-swaps)) | — | no |
| `plugin.failed` | Error | a plugin reported an error while the song played, or didn't come back after an export, and is silent alone until it is made again from its saved state ([HOST-5](#pluginhost)); on the session, not a revision, through the fault ring ([RENDER-25](#plans-data-and-arrangement-swaps)); offline, the render fails ([RENDER-1](#offline)) | — | no |
| `asset.unreadable` | Error | a file in `assets/` that is there can't be read or decoded while it plays (damaged, cut short, on a drive unplugged): a clip's or one a kernel reads; it plays silent. On the session, not a revision, through the fault ring ([RENDER-25](#plans-data-and-arrangement-swaps)); offline, the render fails ([RENDER-1](#offline)). `asset.missing` stays what validation finds | — | no |
| `stretch.failed` | Error | a warped clip's stretch failed ([RENDER-30](#audio-lanes)); the clip plays silent. On the session, not a revision, through the fault ring; offline, the render fails ([RENDER-1](#offline)) | — | no |
| `input.missing` | Warning | the sound input chosen in Settings isn't there, so the take records from one that is, naming both ([RENDER-30a](#audio-lanes)); listed until the next take, or until another song opens. On the session, not a revision, through the fault ring | — | no |
| `input.lost` | Warning | the sound input went away during a take (unplugged): the take ended there, kept up to then ([RENDER-30a](#audio-lanes)); listed until the next take, or until another song opens. On the session, through the fault ring | — | no |
| `input.refused` | Error | the OS refuses music2 the microphone during a take (macOS's Don't Allow): the take is only silence, so it isn't kept ([RENDER-30a](#audio-lanes)); listed until the next take, or until another song opens. On the session, through the fault ring | — | no |

**DIAG-2.** Codes are stable. A new code is added to this table in the change that raises it.

**DIAG-3.** `data.claimed` is raised by `check(tx)` and `commit(tx)`: they compare which data each module claims before and after that Tx, so the notice is deterministic. The watcher MAY raise the same Notice for an outside write by comparing with the previous accepted revision. That form is session-only: project reads and `m2 check` never return it, and tests never treat it as project state. A swap within one change (two tracks trading keys in one save) isn't detected: no site has a number to compare. Comparing the `(` point of each module's `track()` call ([ID-13](#derived-identity)) would detect it; that is deferred until a swap is seen to mislead.
- *Why:* a claim that changed hands should be visible, but only what can be detected is promised, and what a project read says must not depend on how long a session has run.

### Delivery

**DIAG-4.** Diagnostics MUST be sent with every revision over the WebSocket, returned to agents by project reads, and printed by every CLI command that opens a project.
- *Why:* a problem nobody sees is the silent data loss this contract exists to end.
- *Today:* matches for what exists. The session's problems are in the project state the app fetches, and in every `{"t":"graph"}` event over the WebSocket, together with the accepted revision and the draft's errors (`Graph::news` in `crates/server/src/graph.rs`; `graph_changed` in `crates/server/src/lib.rs`). Since step 6 the session's own (`tx.interrupted`, `tx.foreign`, `lock.*`) come first, and an outside write's `data.claimed` is a toast. The app counts them in the top bar's status pill and lists them, with their fixes, in its popover (`web/src/components/Status.tsx`, `Attention.tsx`). Every CLI command that opens a project prints them, each with the commands that apply its fixes (`m2 relink`, `m2 delete-data`, `m2 fix`). Since step 5 a package module that doesn't load (`pkg.invalid`, the session's) comes first among them, in the state, the news and every CLI command's output (`Session::problems`; `package_problems` in `apps/m2/src/main.rs` for the commands that only compile). Since track ex's slice 1 the faults found while sound is made (`sound.stopped`, `plugin.failed`, `plugin.crashed`; since slice 2 also `asset.unreadable` and `stretch.failed`) come after the revision's own in the state and the news, each until its part plays again, and a new one is toasted once ([RENDER-25](#plans-data-and-arrangement-swaps)). The draft's errors are still a separate list, `diagnostics`, and there is no MCP yet.

### Fixes

**DIAG-5.** A fix MUST NOT be applied automatically: not on open, read, watch or commit. It runs only when a person or an agent asks (a button, `m2 relink`, committing the fix's Tx). Applying a fix is a Tx: journaled and undoable.
- *Why:* only the files on disk decide what is accepted (P3).

**DIAG-6.** Reconciliation proposes and never applies. It works from the files of one revision, never from history:
- **A lane key changed:** a lane file no declared lane reads is offered to every declared lane that can read it (a file with no clips fits any kind) and has no data yet. Likeliest first: the same lane key on another track (its track was renamed), then another lane of its own track, then the rest.
- **A track key changed:** when exactly one `data/<k>/` folder is claimed by no module and exactly one declared module has no folder, `data.unclaimed` on that folder offers Relink of the whole folder first (`DataPath::Module(k)` to `Module(new)`: lanes and state together, one `Rename`). It is likely when the folder's lane files match the new module's declared lanes. The per-lane offers follow.
- A fix is `likely` only when it is alone in first place and on the data's own track or lane.

The app shows these in its status popover ("Needs attention"), with Relink, Restore (rename the key back, a Tx built by `crates/editor`) and Delete.
- *Why:* a rename by hand must be one click from sounding again, and the offer must be the same in the app, the CLI and an agent's read.
- *Today:* matches since step 4, except Restore (`core/session/src/unclaimed.rs`). A renamed track's folder is offered whole, then lane by lane (lane by lane only while it holds a file that is neither a lane nor a state, which keeps it from moving whole, and the problem names that file); plugin state no plugin reads is offered to a plugin of its kind. Since step 6 each fix is a Tx built where its problem is raised (`core/session/src/unclaimed.rs`, `core/song/src/data.rs`), and applying it is committing it: `m2 fix` and `POST /api/graph/fix`; `m2 relink`, `m2 delete-data`, `POST /api/graph/relink` and `/api/graph/data/delete` commit the offered fix, or build the same Tx (`crates/editor/src/data.rs`). A fix whose base went stale is a `Conflict`. Restore isn't built.

## Component descriptors

The package API is the component descriptor, written in TypeScript. `m2pkg.toml` keeps only the ABI side: kernels, their ports and the wasm file.

```ts
export const delay = component({
  id: "delay", label: "Delay",
  inputs: { in: port.audio() },
  controls: { time: control.secs(0.25).range(0.001, 2).log(), feedback: control.frac(0.3), mix: control.frac(0.3).smooth() },
  settings: {},                  // build-time values: changing one rebuilds
  output: port.audio(),
  positional: [],                // sugar, e.g. ["cutoff", "q"], so lowpass(x, 1000) keeps working
  editor: { category: "Delay & Reverb", insert: { time: 0.25 } },
}, (x, { time, feedback, mix }) => kernel("std:delay", { in: x, time, feedback, mix }));
```

**PKG-1.** A package's components MUST be declared with `component(descriptor, body)`. Plain functions keep working, with fewer editor features.
- *Why:* every package and song call is written against this, and it defines the ControlId slots.
- *Today:* since step 5 `@m2/core` 1.1.0 has `component(descriptor, body)` with `port`, `control`, `setting`, `each` and `signature`, and derives each component's call type from its descriptor, so the editor checks calls against it (`core/script/sdk/core.ts`; `core/script/sdk/check/calls.ts`), and its signature help and hover list a call's options. The build takes every form the type does: a helper may forward options it wasn't given, since `undefined` where the options go is no options (`options_left_out_may_be_forwarded` in `core/script/src/tests.rs`). Every std component is declared this way (`std/src/*.ts`; `std_is_declared_with_descriptors` in `core/script/tests/std.rs`), and `core/script/sdk/check/std.ts` checks std's calls as the editor does. The old form, `component({ controls: ["1.cutoff"] }, fn)`, still works; std no longer uses it.

**PKG-2.** `m2pkg.toml` MUST NOT describe TypeScript. `[[effect]]` entries and their `args` text are deleted; the descriptor's `editor` field replaces them.
- *Why:* TypeScript written as text in TOML can't be checked.
- *Today:* since step 5 std's `m2pkg.toml` describes its kernels only. Each effect's category, description and `insert` (the old `args`, value for value) are in its descriptor's `editor` (`std/src/filter.ts`, `std/src/fx.ts`, `std/src/dsp.ts`; `std_effects_are_the_manifests_old_effects` in `core/script/tests/std.rs`). The runtime keeps no effects of its own: a manifest that still has `[[effect]]` fails to load, naming PKG-2 (`core/runtime/src/packages.rs`), and editors read effects from the descriptors (`Session::effects`).

**PKG-3.** Every declared control that isn't optional ([PKG-5](#component-descriptors)) and isn't connected to a signal MUST become exactly one const node whose origin is `(instance, slot, written | inherited)`, and that const is a cell. The body always receives a signal, whether the caller wrote a value, connected a signal or left it out.
- *Why:* one cell per control makes inherited controls addressable (`Anchor::Slot`) and keeps every use of a control live.
- *Today:* matches for components declared with descriptors since step 5 (`normalise` in `core/script/sdk/core.ts`): a written number's origin is its argument path, a quantity's is its own call, and a left-out control's is its instance's call with no path, marked inherited. A call made by native code has no site, so its controls are consts with no origin, which the checker folds. Every std component is declared with a descriptor, so every control a std call leaves out is a const too. The session shows each as a knob at its default (`Control.written` false, its id `file#call/@slot`), whose first edit writes it into the call as a plain number, positionally when it is the next argument, else in the options object (inside the braces of one with no properties, keeping what is in them), verified against the graph, and undone by its Tx's stored inverse ([WRITE-21](#undo); `Insertion` in `crates/editor/src/lens.rs`). Since 8b.3b a dictionary entry's control (`channels.kick.db`) is written into its entry: an object entry gets one more property, and a bare signal is wrapped as `{ in: <signal>, db: … }` with its own text kept, so no call in it moves (`a_bare_entry_is_wrapped_on_its_first_move` in `crates/editor/src/mix_tests.rs`). It follows the dictionary's style: `db(-3)` when another entry writes that control with a unit call in base units that the file imports from `@m2/core`, else a plain number (`an_insertion_follows_the_objects_style`). Which argument holds the options is the one the call's run read them from (`Frame.options`), since only the run can tell `lowpass(x, opts)`'s options from a cutoff. A call that can't take it (its place holds `undefined`, its options aren't an object in the call) shows it read-only (`every_declared_control_is_a_knob` in `crates/editor/src/gate5_tests.rs`, `options_the_call_reads_are_where_controls_go` in `crates/editor/src/insert_tests.rs`). The edit's message shows the call before and after, with each long argument it doesn't touch cut to `…` when the call doesn't fit on a line (`master(…)` → `master(…, { ceiling: 0 })`), and so does a cable written into a call.

- Since `next` (gaps #21): any audio-to-audio component call takes `bypass: true` in its options. Its audio input then goes on as its output, and the engine never runs it; its frame records `bypass` (written only when true). The Device view's ⋯ menu writes and removes it as one Tx (`POST /api/graph/effect/bypass`), and moves an effect before or after the one written next to it (`/effect/move`: one Tx swapping two nested calls, each keeping its text); a Duck's sidechain is picked from the song's tracks (`/effect/sidechain`).

**PKG-4.** The descriptor's default is what songs see. A kernel port default in `m2pkg.toml` applies only when a kernel is called directly with that input unset. When std is rewritten on descriptors, each descriptor default MUST equal today's effective default, so every fixture renders the same bits.
- *Why:* one place states what a song gets when it says nothing.
- *Today:* matches since step 5. Each std default is in its descriptor, made by the expression that gave it before (`control.ms(45)` is `ms(45)`; `control.scalar(0.707)` reads the same decimal as the port default did), and `m2pkg.toml`'s port defaults reach only direct `kernel()` calls. `std_defaults_are_todays` (`core/script/tests/std.rs`) compares each with the kernel port default or `??` fallback it replaced, and every pure fixture renders the same bits.

**PKG-5.** A control whose presence changes the graph (the mixer's `pan`, which adds a panner only when written) MUST be declared optional. Absent means no node. Adding or removing it is structural; changing its value is not.
- *Why:* normalising every optional control to a signal would change the sound of existing songs.
- *Today:* matches since step 5. `control.<unit>(…).optional()` declares such a control: left out, it makes no node and the body gets `undefined`. The mixer declares its `pan` so, and adds a panner only for a channel that writes one (`std/src/mix.ts`): a mono sound through a centred panner isn't bit for bit the same.
