Since 8b.3 its `db` isn't optional: every channel has a gain, its fader, and the master has one before its limiter (`master(x, { db })`), as a gain at 0 dB is `x × 1.0`, which changes no bit (`the_mixer_and_master_gains_change_no_bit` in `core/session/src/gate_tests.rs`: each pure fixture through a copy of the mixer and master from before, against today's). −96 dB or lower is silence (`db_to_gain`), so a fader's foot writes −96. The master's input goes through `std:stereo` first, which copies a mono signal into both channels, so what follows is stereo from the start: when a mix that is mono all the way gets its first pan, the master's gain and limiter keep their instances, and the limiter its envelope (`a_first_pan_in_a_mono_mix_keeps_the_masters_gain_and_limiter` in `crates/server/src/graph/tests.rs`).

**PKG-6.** Dictionary slots, such as the mixer's `channels: each({ in, db, pan })`, make the user's key the identity on purpose ([ID-18](#renames)).
- An entry may hold a dictionary of its own and settings: the mixer's `sends: each({ db, pre })` and `to: setting.key()`. A nested control is named by its whole path, `channels.vox.sends.verb.db`. A nested dictionary left out is empty.
- The `key` setting kind is a string that follows the key rule ([ID-1](#keys)).
- An entry may be a bare signal when it declares one input (`kick: kick.out`), or a bare value when it declares no input and one control (`verb: db(-12)` is `{ db: db(-12) }`).
- *Today:* `each()` exists since step 5: an entry's controls are named `<slot>.<key>.<control>` (`channels.hats.db`), so the keys follow the key rule (ID-1), and an entry may be a bare signal when it declares one input. Nested dictionaries, settings in an entry, `setting.key()` and the bare one-control entry since track bus (`@m2/core` 1.5.0; `a_nested_dictionary_control_is_found` in `core/script/src/tests.rs`). `Descriptor::control` follows a path of any depth; an entry's settings are in its `EachDesc`. The editor's knob writes and automation on a nested id come with the bus edits (track bus, slice 2).

**PKG-7.** `editor.category` is a free string that the UI groups. Core MUST NOT hold a list of categories.
- *Why:* core holds no UI vocabulary (P10).
- *Today:* matches since step 5: a category is what a descriptor's `editor.category` says, and core lists none (`the_deleted_items_are_gone` in `crates/editor/src/gate5_tests.rs`). The web's effect browser keeps an order and an icon for the categories it knows, and lists any other as a group of its own after them (`web/src/components/EffectBrowser.tsx`). From 8b.1 a category on a component that takes notes first lists it as an instrument a new track can play, which must take only its notes, make audio, and build from its notes alone (`core/script/sdk/core.ts`, `Descriptor::is_instrument`); one that takes audio first is an effect, as before. Only a category lists one: a component a track could play that has none is left out of the + Track list, and code still plays it. std's sounds say Drums (the kit, each drum, FM metal), Instruments (the synths' and FM's named sounds), Sampler (`sampler`, since std 0.2.1) and Sound design (`synth.custom` and `fm.custom`, with every knob); std's first five instruments (`std/instruments`) say none, as std/drums and std/synth have fuller ones of each. The + Track list puts Drums first, then Instruments, then the other categories by name, each in the order its package declares them, and plays drums from a `hits` lane. A member of an exported object is written as such: `import { synth } from "std/synth"` and `synth.pad(lane.notes("notes"))` (`crates/editor/src/tracks.rs`).

**PKG-8.** A control's `.range()` is a display hint. It is a validation constraint only when declared as one, and a value is never clamped silently.
- *Why:* a knob's preferred range must not rewrite what the code says.
- *Today:* matches since step 5. A descriptor declares `.range()` and `.strict()`, and at build the SDK refuses a value outside the strict range declared for its unit (the control's own, or the one `accepts` gives that unit), never clamping it (`a_strict_range_holds_for_the_values_unit` in `core/script/src/tests.rs`). A knob's range and log scale are the ones its control declares for the knob's unit, which is its constant's (`accepts` gives another unit's: `osc.saw(semis(60))` shows 0..127); a raw `kernel()` input shows its port's `in lo..hi`; otherwise, or when one literal feeds controls that declare different ranges, the knob has none (`Knob.range` null), and the web picks a span for its unit and widens any span to show the value written (`web/src/lib/graph.ts`). Core guesses no range from a unit.

**PKG-9.** Frames MUST record the ComponentId: the package and the descriptor's `id`, never the module file it is in. An `id` is unique within its package; a duplicate is a package load error. So `import { delay as echo }` is recognised, and moving `delay` from `std/src/fx.ts` to `std/src/delay.ts` changes nothing.
- *Why:* recognising a component by how it is spelled, or by the file it lives in, breaks on any alias or move (P1).
- *Today:* since step 5 frames record the ComponentId, `<package>:<id>` or a project component's bare id, from the package the defining file belongs to (`__m2_home` in `core/script/src/lib.rs`), so an alias or a move changes nothing in the IR; two definition sites with one id are a build error, and `m2_script::describe` reports them as `pkg.invalid`. The session recognises an effect by its call's frames, never by the name it is called by, so `echo(x)` after `import { delay as echo }` is the Delay effect with delay's knobs (`effect_at` in `crates/editor/src/effect.rs`; `an_import_alias_is_the_component`, `moving_a_component_keeps_its_id` in `effect_tests.rs`).

**PKG-10.** Units are a closed core enum. Packages MAY add spellings (unit constructors) for existing units, never units.
- *Why:* units are part of the IR type system that the checker enforces.
- *Today:* matches (`core/ir/src/kind.rs:8-58`). The SDK's spellings are one table, `UNITS` in `core/script/sdk/core.ts`, with exactly one canonical spelling per unit.

**PKG-11.** The build MUST emit the descriptors and a table of unit constructors generated from `core.ts`. Editors and gestures use that table; no other copy exists.
- *Why:* a hand-kept copy drifts from the SDK.
- *Today:* since step 5 the unit constructors are made from one table in `core/script/sdk/core.ts`, and every build returns it (`Build.units`) with the descriptor of every component its modules declare (`Build.components`), numbers in base units with their spelling (`core/script/src/component.rs`); `m2_script::describe` catalogues every package's components module by module. The session keeps both with each revision (`Compiled.components`, `Compiled.units`) and the catalog with the session (`Session::catalog`): the cable planner writes a default in its descriptor's spelling from the build's table, `ms(45)` with `ms` imported when the file lacks it, and no other copy of the table exists; the effect browser and planner read the catalog. Every call form the planners write is listed in `crates/editor/tests/forms/forms.ts` (`planned_call_forms_are_current` in `crates/editor/src/gate5_tests.rs`), which `tsc -p crates/editor/tests/forms` checks as the editor does, as `tsc -p core/script/sdk/check` checks the SDK's and std's own call forms; web's `npm run typecheck`, and so `npm run build`, runs both.

**PKG-12.** Descriptors and cells land in step 5, before Tx in step 6.
- *Why:* Tx anchors must be able to name inherited controls, or step 6's anchors and ported gestures would be redone.

**PKG-13.** An audio input that only listens (a detector or key input, such as `follow`'s `in` or a compressor's side input) MUST be declared `port.audio({ sidechain: true })`. A hosted plugin's audio inputs other than its main one are sidechain ports. Monitoring mute and solo never act on a sidechain port ([MON-2](#monitoring-overlays-and-mute-in-song)).
- *Why:* muting the kick must not un-duck a bass whose compressor is keyed from the kick's audio.
- *Today:* since step 5 a frame records the signal each of its sidechain inputs received (`Frame.sidechain`), a dictionary entry's by its path (`others.hats.side`, `an_entry_sidechain_input_is_recorded`), whatever order the calls ran in, and the checker carries each as a port into `Flat.sidechain`, or a voice's `Voice.sidechain` when a voice made it (`core/ir/src/check.rs`). Finding a track's channel reads them, so that an edge into one isn't followed ([ID-18](#renames), `crates/editor/src/mix.rs`). std declares `follow`'s `in` and `duck`'s `side` as sidechain inputs (`std/src/dsp.ts`, `std/src/fx.ts`). Since 8b.4 the checker also lists where overlays go (`Flat.overlays`, [MON-1](#monitoring-overlays-and-mute-in-song)) and leaves out every sidechain input: an input made inside a frame that recorded a sidechain, which reads the signal that frame received; a plugin's audio inputs other than `in`; and a voice's capture that such an input reads (`core/ir/src/overlay_tests.rs`).

**PKG-14.** A control MAY be declared `.smooth()`: a gain, a mix or anything else that clicks when it steps. Its cell then ramps over one block on every change ([RENDER-22a](#cells)).
- *Why:* the descriptor knows which controls click; the engine doesn't.
- *Today:* since step 5 std marks smooth the controls its kernels read every sample without smoothing, where a step jumps the output: every filter's `cutoff` and `q`, `delay`'s `time`, `feedback` and `mix`, `drive`'s `amount`, `pan`, `vca`, `scale`, `add`, `blend`, `when` and `choose`, `range`'s and `xrange`'s `lo` and `hi`, `lfo`'s `phase`, the square's `width`, the mixer's `pan`, and the instrument controls that reach them (`bass`'s `cutoff` and `drive`, `pluck`'s `brightness`, `pad`'s `cutoff`). `std_smooth_controls_are_the_per_sample_ones` (`core/script/tests/std.rs`) holds the list, and `a_range_bound_ramps` (`core/play/tests/knobs.rs`) hears a range's bound ramp where it sets a gain. A control whose step only changes a rate steps, as the phase runs on: an oscillator's `freq`, `mtof`'s `pitch`, the envelopes' times (and `adsr` approaches a new `sustain` at its decay rate). The effects' kernels, `gain` and `duck` glide their own levels (`compress` reaches its through its envelope). `.smooth()` helps only a kernel that reads the control every sample, and only where a ramp is a glide: `limit` reads `ceiling` once per block, where a one-block ramp arrives as one value, and `lfo` divides the song's beats by `period`, so a ramp of it would sweep the LFO through many cycles in one block; neither is marked, and gliding either would be a kernel change. A one-block ramp of `delay`'s `time` (about 3 ms) still moves the read head, so it removes the hard step but not the pitch chirp.

**PKG-15.** `setting.regions()` declares a list of **regions**: which recording plays for which notes, how hard, in which turn and when. It is the one shape music2 compiles sample maps into (a pack's `instruments/*.toml`, an SFZ file), so its fields, their defaults and their checks are fixed here:

| Field | Meaning | Left out |
|---|---|---|
| `file` | the recording: an asset name the build accepts (`"piano/C4.wav"`) | required |
| `root` | the MIDI note it was recorded at, 0 to 127 | 60 |
| `keys` | `[lowest, highest]`: the notes it plays, 0 to 127 | `[root, root]` when `root` is written, else `[0, 127]` |
| `velocity` | `[lowest, highest]`: how hard, 1 to 127 | `[1, 127]` |
| `round_robin` | `[position, of]`: take `position` of `of` takes, played in turn; 1 ≤ position ≤ of ≤ 128 | `[1, 1]` |
| `trigger` | `"attack"`: when the note starts; `"release"`: when it ends | `"attack"` |
| `loop` | `[start, end]`: frames of the file, both played (SFZ's `loop_start` and `loop_end`); start < end | none |
| `tune` | cents, −2400 to 2400 | 0 |
| `volume` | dB, −120 to 24 | 0 |
| `pan` | −1 (left) to 1 (right) | 0 |
| `offset` | frames into the file it starts from, 0 or more | 0 |

The build MUST refuse a list that isn't one, a region that isn't an object, any other field, a field of the wrong kind, a number outside its range or not whole where a note, a velocity, a turn or a frame goes, and a pair whose first is above its second, in words that name the region by its place from 1: "regions: region 3: keys 70 to 60: the lowest is above the highest". It MUST also refuse turns that would play nothing: wherever a note and velocity are played by regions of one trigger whose `round_robin` is of `n` takes (`n` > 1), there must be a region for each take from 1 to `n`, else that turn is silent there ("regions: region 3: round_robin [1, 3]: no region is take 3 of 3 on note 60 at velocity 1, so that turn plays nothing there"). This is how `[1, 2]` beside `[2, 3]` on one note is refused. Groups of different `n` on one note, each whole, are layers that take turns apart, and play on every turn. A pair is two numbers: TypeScript types it as a list of numbers, so a region made by a helper or kept in a `const` type-checks, and the build refuses any length but 2. Left out, the setting is `[]`, which plays nothing.
- *Why:* readers outside the code (a pack's sample map, an SFZ import) compile to this shape, so it must be one shape, said once; a field misspelt, or a range upside down, would otherwise play silence that no one hears.
- *Today:* since track sampler2 (`@m2/core` 1.6.0): `setting.regions()` and `checkRegions` in `core/script/sdk/core.ts`, the kind `regions` in the descriptor (`SettingKind::Regions`, `core/script/src/component.rs`). Each refusal is checked by `regions_settings_are_checked` (`core/script/src/kernel_settings_tests.rs`), the turns by `round_robin_groups_must_have_every_take`; the types by `core/script/sdk/check/std.ts`, which `tsc` checks as the code editor does.

**PKG-16.** std's Sampler (`std/sampler`) plays `regions` ([PKG-15](#component-descriptors)) by these rules, the same live and in an export ([RENDER-33](#the-bit-identity-test)):
1. A note's velocity, 0 to 1, becomes a MIDI velocity once, by one rule: `clamp(round(velocity × 127), 1, 127)`, halves rounded up. Its pitch, rounded, is its key.
2. When a note starts, every attack region whose `keys` hold its key, whose `velocity` holds its velocity, and whose `round_robin` position is its turn plays, from `offset`. Several play at once, summed (layers), up to 32 in one voice, the first in the list first; none is silence.
3. A note's turn is counted outside the voices, from the notes alone: one voice of `std:drumhit` marks each sample on which notes start, so notes that start together count once, and `std:hitcount` counts the marks. The nth start's turn, in a region of `of` takes, is ((n − 1) mod `of`) + 1: regions with the same `of` take their turns together. A count never follows engine time, so a song opened and played live from 0 takes the export's turns, however long the engine ran before. A jump resets no kernel ([RENDER-6](#live-engine-and-its-two-clocks)), so after one (back to bar 1 and Play again) the turns carry on from where they were, and notes auditioned or played on the lane count as any others. The count is the lane's, not a key's: notes that alternate on one lane (a kick and a snare, a trill) keep their takes, so a drum whose takes must alternate goes on a lane of its own.
4. When the note ends, the release regions that match it (its key, and the velocity and turn it started with) start, and play to their end, wherever the note ends on the block grid: the voice says it is active while such a note is held ([RENDER-16](#voices)); the attack regions follow `mode` and `release` as before.
5. A region with a `loop` loops while its note is held: after `end` comes `start` (an `end` past the file is its last frame, and a loop to the last frame loops like any other), crossfaded in a straight line over the last min(5 ms of the file, `start`, half the loop) frames before it. A crossfade started while the note is held finishes; after that, once the note has ended, it plays on past `end` to the file's end (fading over `release` in gate mode).
6. `tune` adds to the pitch; `volume` scales; `pan` balances (the far side × sin((1 − |pan|) × π/2), the near side as it is); `offset` starts the file later. The component's `velocity` control, 0 to 1 (SFZ's `amp_veltrack`), sets how much velocity changes the level: level × (1 − v + v × velocity). Its default, 1, is the level before it: velocity × level.
7. `sample` and `zones` play as they did, bit for bit, through `std:sampler`; regions play through `std:regions`. A call gives one of `sample`, `zones` and `regions`; `sample` with `zones` still plays the zones, as before.
- *Why:* one engine for real recorded instruments (a piano's velocity layers, a drum's takes, a release noise, a held string), from SFZ's ideas; and a turn that a voice took from engine time, or from the voices that happened to be free, would make live playback differ from the export.
- *Today:* since track sampler2 (std 0.5.0): `std/kernels-src/src/sampler.rs` (both kernels, the values layout in its header and in `std/m2pkg.toml`; tested in `sampler/tests.rs`: keys and velocity, layers, turns from the count, release regions (and a held note one matches is active), loops held then played out with a bounded step (and a loop to the file's last frame, past it, or within a step of it above the root), tune, volume, pan and offset, any block split, and the zones' bits as before), `std/src/sampler.ts`, which checks the one-of rule and counts the note starts for every call with regions (`std:regions`' `count` is an audio input, which must be connected). `core/session/tests/fixtures/sampler-regions` (two velocity layers, a round robin of 2, a release sound and a loop) is one of the pure fixtures: it renders the same bits on 1 to 7 threads, live, and live after the engine sat stopped (`std_plays_the_export_after_the_engine_sat_stopped`), and is recorded in `renders.json` and, as `m2 render` writes it (whole, and 6 s from 4 s in, where the replay must bring the count), in `e2e/renders.sha256` ([PLAT-4](#platforms)); four C4s on two takes play take 1, 2, 1, 2, live after idling as in the export (`round_robin_takes_turns_by_the_notes_live_as_in_the_export`, `core/play/tests/bit_identity.rs`). The component's regions reach the kernel in its layout (`the_sampler_gives_its_regions_their_kernel`). The Device card says what a Sampler with regions plays in words ("5 recordings, 2 layers, round robin of 2, release sounds, loops") and refuses a dropped WAV in words, as for zones (`crates/editor/src/sampler.rs`, `a_sampler_with_regions_says_what_it_plays_and_refuses_a_drop`). Not yet (gap #22): a sustain pedal, choke groups, crossfades between velocity layers, band-limited playback and streaming; a turn counted for each key or region alone, as SFZ does, which needs a count that sees each note's pitch: one signal outside the voices can't say which keys of a chord started, and kernels get no events yet ([ABI-2](#kernel-abi-reservations) reserves them).

## Packs

A pack is a package other than std: instruments, effects, sounds and recordings anyone can make ([design](designs/packs/design.md)). To the build a pack is a package like std ([PKG-1](#component-descriptors) to [PKG-14](#component-descriptors)); these rules say how it is named, found, pinned and added.

**PACK-1.** A package is named `std` or `@owner/name`. `owner` and `name` are 1 to 39 characters of `a-z`, `0-9` and `-`, neither starting nor ending with `-`. `@m2/` is music2's own scope, and `@m2/core` is the SDK, never a package. A name never holds `:`. One type checks it, `m2_song::PackName`, wherever a name comes in: `m2pkg.toml`, `[dependencies]`, `music2.lock` and the store.
- *Why:* the owner is the GitHub account that publishes (design D2), and one rule written once can't drift.
- *Today:* since packs (`core/song/src/pack.rs`; `pack_names` in its tests).

**PACK-2.** `m2pkg.toml` MUST be read strictly: an unknown key anywhere is refused, as [FMT-4](#manifest-and-versions) refuses one in `music2.toml`. `[package]` holds `name`, `version`, and optionally `license`, `description`, `authors = [..]`, `repository`, `tags = [..]` and `wasm`. `[dependencies]` may name only `"@m2/core"` and `std` (packs that need packs come later, design D4), each a version ([FMT-2](#manifest-and-versions)) checked against the SDK and the std this app has when the pack loads: `@m2/core` with the caret rule, std as a lower bound, so `std = "0.4"` is std 0.4.0 or any later one. A pack the song uses that doesn't load, or needs more than this app has, is the build error `pkg.broken`, naming why.
- *Why:* std is one version, bundled with the app, and its minor moves with every addition (the sounds design, §4). A pack asking for the std it was made with must keep working when the app's std grows; a song's own std line, which the caret rule checks, is what keeps its sound ([FMT-3](#manifest-and-versions)).
- *Today:* since packs (`PkgManifest::read` and `PkgManifest::needs` in `core/song/src/pack.rs`, used by `build_code` in `core/session/src/lib.rs`; `a_package_manifest_is_read_strictly`, `a_pack_made_for_an_older_std_still_builds`; `every_shipped_manifest_is_met` checks every `m2pkg.toml` the repo ships too).

**PACK-3.** An import's package is its first segment, or its first two when it starts with `@`: `"@alice/felt-piano"` is that pack's `src/index.ts`, `"@alice/felt-piano/keys"` its `src/keys.ts`, as `"std/dsp"` is std's `src/dsp.ts`. `pkg.undeclared` and the rest of [FMT-6a](#paths) hold for scoped names as for std. An import stays inside one package's folder: a bare one inside the package it names, a relative one from a package's module inside that module's own package. One that reaches another package's folder, even one loaded beside it, is `import.outside-code`, so a pack never uses another pack (D4).
- *Today:* since packs (`package_of` and `State::package_file` in `core/script/src/lib.rs`; `a_scoped_package_is_imported_by_its_two_segments`, `an_import_never_reaches_another_package`, `a_package_import_never_passes_through_a_link`). `m2 explain` names a scoped package whole (`a_missing_scoped_module_names_its_whole_package`).

**PACK-4.** A ComponentId or a kernel id is `<package>:<id>`: `@alice/felt-piano:felt-piano`, `@alice/felt-piano:grain`. A package name holds no `:`, so an id splits at its first `:`, and so does a plugin's `clap:<id>`. One function splits them, `m2_song::pack::split_id`, in Rust; the web splits the same way, at the first `:`.
- *Today:* since packs. `PackageSet::module_for`, the presets' folders and the bounce's provenance split at the first `:` or by the package's name (`ids_split_at_the_first_colon`).

**PACK-5.** An asset reference with a `:` names a file in a package: `"@alice/felt-piano:samples/C4.wav"`, `"std:kits/…"`. One without is the song's `assets/` ([FMT-14](#assets-and-hashes)). A package file resolves inside that package's folder only: its path is relative, `/`-separated, with no empty, `.` or `..` segment and no `\`, found by listing its folders byte for byte, with no case folding and no part that is a symbolic link ([FMT-6a](#paths)'s rules for package files), and only for std or a package the song's `[dependencies]` lists. It is resolved by its name, never by who passes it: a pack's code passes it to std's Sampler as a song would. Each revision records the hash of every package file its kernels read, beside its assets' ([RENDER-2](#offline)). One that isn't there, or leaves its package, is `asset.missing`, and that sound is silent. `@m2/core`'s `file(import.meta, "samples/C4.wav")` spells a module's own package's file, so pack code needn't spell its own name.
- *Today:* since packs (`AssetRef` and `package_file` in `core/song/src/pack.rs`, read by `Project::data` and `DataSet::asset_path`; `@m2/core` 1.7.0's `file`; `package_assets_resolve_inside_their_package`, `a_package_file_is_never_reached_through_a_link`). A bounce's provenance names each package file its kernels read, with its hash, and each pack's version hash (`m2 bounce`; `a_bounce_names_the_pack_files_it_played`).

**PACK-6.** The store is `m2_os::dirs::packs()`, `~/Music/music2/Packs` on every OS ([PLAT-7](#platforms)), one folder per version: `Packs/@alice/felt-piano/1.2.0/`. Installed files and folders are read-only. Every installed version holds `m2pack.json`: every other file of the pack, by sorted path, with its size and SHA-256, made by `m2 pack build`, never by hand. The version's hash is the SHA-256 of `m2pack.json`'s bytes, written `sha256:<hex>`. A version is never changed: before a build uses an installed version, every file in its folder is checked against its listing, its size and SHA-256 through the hash cache ([FMT-16](#assets-and-hashes)), so an unchanged file costs one stat; a file changed, missing, not listed, or a link is `pkg.changed`, naming it. A pack holds no symbolic link: `m2 pack check` names one, and build and install refuse it, never following one. Installing a version that is there with another hash is refused (design P5); with the same hash it does nothing when the copy still matches its listing, and replaces the copy when it doesn't, which is how a changed one is put back.
- *Why:* a song keeps the sound of the version it was made with (design M5), so what plays must be what was installed. A synced `~/Music` or an editor saving in place can change a file without anyone meaning to.
- *Today:* since packs (`Store`, `Store::check` and `Listing` in `core/song/src/pack.rs`; `m2 pack install` and `m2 pack remove`, `crates/editor/src/packs.rs`; `installing_twice_is_a_no_op_and_a_new_hash_is_refused`, `a_changed_installed_pack_is_caught_and_reinstalling_repairs_it`, `a_store_version_is_checked_file_by_file`, `links_are_refused_in_a_pack`).

**PACK-7.** A `[dependencies]` entry other than `@m2/core` and `std` names a pack, and is a requirement ([FMT-2](#manifest-and-versions)), `"1.2"`, or a folder, `{ path = "../felt-piano" }`, relative to the project: a pack its author is trying. A key that isn't a package name ([PACK-1](#packs)), a requirement that isn't one, or a table with any key but `path`, is `manifest.invalid`, naming the entry and what to write.
- *Today:* since packs (`Dependency` in `core/song/src/manifest.rs`; `a_bad_dependency_says_which_and_why`).

**PACK-8.** `music2.lock` is a project file, read strictly and written canonically by `core/song` ([FMT-5](#paths)), classed with the manifest ([FMT-6](#paths)): a change rebuilds the song. It holds one `[[pack]]` per pack the song depends on from the store, sorted by name: `name`, `version`, `hash`. Path packs and std are not locked. A lock that doesn't parse is `manifest.invalid`, naming it. The app never writes it on its own (P2, P3): only the edit that adds a pack's item ([PACK-10](#packs)), `m2 pack lock` and `m2 pack update`. Both write it from the manifest, the lock it replaces and the store, never from a build: `lock` keeps each entry the manifest's requirement admits whose version is installed, and puts the highest installed version the requirement admits in place of one it doesn't admit, one whose version isn't installed, and a pack with no entry; `update` puts the highest installed admitted version in place of every entry, or of the pack it names. A locked version kept whose installed copy isn't the one locked (another hash, or files that no longer match its listing) is refused, saying how to put it back or move. An entry for a pack the manifest no longer lists goes. Neither needs the song to build first: while it doesn't, the lock is written only when the song then builds, as one undo step, as `m2 edit` saves ([AGENT-1](#agents)).
- *Why:* moving a song to another version is an edit its person makes (M5), and it must work from a song the move itself stopped building (a new requirement the old lock doesn't meet).
- *Today:* since packs (`Lock` and `relock` in `core/song/src/pack.rs`; `DataPath::Lock`; `packs::write_lock` in `crates/editor`; `the_lock_has_one_canonical_form`, `relocking_moves_only_what_must_move`, and the guide's recipe end to end, `moving_a_song_to_another_version`).

**PACK-9.** A song builds with std and its resolved packs, resolved each build from the manifest, the lock and the store:
- a path dependency is that folder, which must hold that package (else `pkg.missing`);
- else the lock's version, when installed and its hash is the lock's (another hash is `pkg.changed`); a locked version not installed is `pkg.missing`, which says what to install and how (`m2 pack install <folder>`); a locked version the requirement doesn't admit is `pkg.unsatisfied`;
- else, with no lock entry, the highest installed version the requirement admits (none is `pkg.unsatisfied`).

Each blocks acceptance, as [FMT-3](#manifest-and-versions)'s do. With no store (no home folder), a pack from the store is `pkg.unsatisfied`, saying so; nothing is looked for elsewhere. The packs a build resolved are part of its code, so a change to the manifest or the lock makes a new revision that resolves them again.

Each build gets its own view of the packages: std and one loaded version of each pack it resolved, nothing else. A loaded version is known by its folder and the hashes of its `m2pkg.toml` and wasm (a store version's files checked against its listing first, [PACK-6](#packs)), so a path pack whose `m2pkg.toml` or wasm changed is loaded again at the next build, and several versions of one pack can be loaded at once. A revision keeps the view it was built with: its plans and exports run that view's kernels, whatever was built since. The session's lists, presets and package lookups follow the accepted revision's view only. So a check, a refused Tx or a planner's probe that builds with other packs never changes what the accepted revision lists or plays. A loaded version is let go when no view holds it any more (no revision, candidate or plan); a plan holds its own modules.
- *Why:* a song keeps the version it was made with (design M5), nothing changes it but an edit the person makes, and the bits of a revision's export never depend on what was checked before (RENDER-2).
- *Today:* since packs (`resolve` in `core/song/src/pack.rs`; `PackageSet::view_of`, `View` and `PackageSet::show` in `core/runtime/src/packages.rs`; `build_code` in `core/session/src/lib.rs`, and `Session::accept`, which shows the accepted revision's view; `every_resolution_case_says_what_it_is`, `a_view_holds_std_and_exactly_the_songs_packs`, `a_dry_run_never_moves_what_the_accepted_revision_plays`, `a_path_packs_new_wasm_reaches_the_next_build`, `a_pack_that_fails_to_load_never_panics_the_build`, and `core/session/src/pack_tests.rs`). A plan of the app's engine and an export or a render of a revision see its own view (`PackageSet::viewing`). Not yet: a path pack's files aren't watched, so an edit to one reaches the song with the song's next build (gaps #46).

**PACK-10.** Adding a pack's item (a track that plays its instrument, its effect, a preset of its sound) writes its import by the scoped name, its `[dependencies]` line (`"@alice/felt-piano" = "1.2"`, the major and minor of the version used) when the song doesn't list it, and its `music2.lock` entry when the lock lacks one or the song didn't list the pack (an entry left from before is replaced), as one Tx, one Undo step. The one helper is `packs::needs` in `crates/editor`, which every adder calls.
- *Today:* since packs, for + Track (and the browser's double-click and drag, which call it), changing a track's instrument (`swap`), and Add Effect (the browser, the Device view's +): `packs::needs_all` gives one `[dependencies]` edit and one lock for every pack a Tx adds (`adding_a_pack_instrument_writes_its_dependency_and_lock_in_one_undo`, `adding_a_pack_effect_writes_its_dependency_and_lock`, `changing_an_instrument_to_a_packs_writes_its_dependency`, `a_lock_left_from_before_gets_its_line_again`). A preset loads onto a call already in the song, whose pack the song lists already.

**PACK-11.** The lists of instruments, effects and presets offer std's items and those of the accepted revision's packs, and of every other pack in the store (its newest version this app can run: one whose `[dependencies]` this app meets, [PACK-2](#packs)), each knowing its package. A pack's own presets are in its folder, `presets/<component>/*.json`, listed with std's ([FMT-22](#presets)). A module of a store pack the song doesn't use that doesn't load leaves its components out of the lists, and puts no problem on the song.
- *Today:* since packs: the session's catalog is made as the song opens, after its first build, from std and `PackageSet::offered` (`catalog` in `core/session/src/lib.rs`; `a_pack_this_app_cant_run_isnt_offered`, `a_store_pack_the_song_doesnt_use_puts_no_problem_on_it`), so a pack installed while a song is open shows when it opens again (gaps #46). Each list item names its pack and version (`packs::from` in `crates/editor`; `a_pack_in_the_store_is_offered`); `GET /api/packs` lists the store for the browser's Packs list (`crates/server/src/routes_packs.rs`; e2e `pk-packs`).

**PACK-12.** `m2 pack check` checks what the registry's robot will (design P1 to P4, and P6 where it can): a strict `m2pkg.toml`; only `.ts`, `.wasm`, `.toml`, `.json`, `.md`, `.txt` and `.wav` files (in any capitals: `.WAV` is a WAV), and `LICENSE` with no extension, and no symbolic link; every component builds and has a category and a shape; every kernel loads sealed; every preset loads on its sound; every package file its code names resolves; a licence is named; and its samples ([LIB-4](#library)): each WAV under `samples/` decodes, and `samples.toml` reads strictly and names only files and folders that are there. A pack of samples alone needs no component. It prints each problem in words and exits 1 on any.
- *Today:* since packs (`check` in `crates/editor/src/packs.rs`; `apps/m2/tests/pack.rs`: `check_says_what_is_wrong`, `a_capital_extension_is_its_type`, `a_preset_outside_a_strict_range_is_named`, `links_are_refused_in_a_pack`). A preset is checked as a preset of a component of the pack, in its component's folder, naming only that component's controls, each value inside its range where that range is strict ([PKG-8](#component-descriptors)); it isn't loaded onto a call in a song (gaps #46). Kernels are checked as a song loads them (the ABI section, the exports, an instance), and every instrument and effect is built in a scratch song, not each kernel prewarmed in a sealed sandbox on its own; a licence is any one named, not checked against the design's list (D6); tags aren't checked against a word list (P7) (gaps #46).

## Homes of values

| Home | Holds | Example |
|---|---|---|
| **Code literals** at declared slots | std knobs, mixer faders and pan, effect settings, "mute in song": the code is the preset (a preset file is loaded into the code as one edit, [FMT-22](#presets)) | `reverb(x, { mix: 0.25 })` |
| **Lanes** | values that change over time | `data/bass/cutoff.json` |
| **Plugin state blob** | a plugin's internals | `data/lead/lead.state` |
| **`layout.json`** | shared presentation: track order and colour (a palette name), keyed by track key; markers; the song's key | `{"tracks": {"kick": {"colour": "rose"}}, "key": {"root": "A", "scale": "minor"}}` |
| **`data/arrangement.json`** | the tempo and the time signature, each from a beat ([FMT-13](#arrangement)) | `{"tempo": [{"beat": 0, "bpm": 120}], "meter": []}` |
| **`.music2/`** | local state: mute and solo, override latches, the loop and the view settings (zoom, follow, heights, hidden lanes, grids, audition) | `.music2/overlays.json`, `.music2/view.json` |

**HOME-1.** Every value MUST have exactly one home, and the UI MUST show which. Every value that `controls()`, the view and leases return carries `home: code | lane | state | layout | arrangement | local`, and a test checks it for every control of every fixture.
- *Why:* each new feature (mixer, reorder and colour, automation, plugins) would otherwise pick its own convention.
- *Today:* matches since step 5: `m2_session::Home` is on every `Control`, knob, read-only value, lease (`LeaseInfo`) and lease begin (`Begun`), and is `code` for each (a literal, or a declared default whose edit writes the code) until lanes, latches and plugin state have knobs. `every_value_names_its_home` (`crates/editor/src/gate5_tests.rs`) checks the view as it is sent, for the five fixtures and a song that calls every std component. Since 8b.2 the timeline carries `home: arrangement` for its tempo and time signatures, as the server publishes it (`m2_editor::TimelineView`; `the_published_timeline_is_the_sessions_and_shares_what_didnt_change`), and the song bar's tooltips say "Saved in data/arrangement.json".

**HOME-2.** "Automate this knob" MUST be a `Confined` Tx that replaces the literal with `lane.control(key, unit, <the old literal>)` and creates an empty lane file. Dragging a knob that a lane or a cable drives sets an override latch ([MON-6](#monitoring-overlays-and-mute-in-song)); it never edits the fallback, the lane or the cable.
- *Why:* the fallback keeps the song sounding the same where no clip is, and a drag must not quietly rewrite automation.
- *Today:* matches for "Automate" since step 6: one `Confined` Tx on the value's expression and the track function's parameters, with the empty lane file (`crates/editor/src/automate.rs`; renders before and after are the same bits). Override latches aren't built ([MON-6](#monitoring-overlays-and-mute-in-song)).
- *Today, from 8b.5:* the lane's key is the control's name (`decay`; a channel of the song's mixer by its track and the control's word, as the Mix view says it: `hats-pan` for Hats pan, `hats-volume` for Hats volume, never "hats db"), with the call's binding in front only when the track has that key already (`pad-decay`), and `lane.control` gets `{ name }` only when the key, read as words, isn't the control's label (`{ name: "Detune (cents)" }`). **Remove automation** is built, the inverse (`automate::remove`, `POST /api/graph/automate/remove`, `m2 unautomate`): one `Confined` Tx that writes what the lane plays at the playhead (its fallback, as written, with none) where the lane was read, spelled as the fallback is (`hz(1250)`), and removes the lane file; the track function keeps its `lane`, and undo puts both back. In the view, an input a control lane drives names it (`InputPort.lane`), so a knob can say "Follows the Cutoff lane" and offer Show lane and Remove automation. A drag on such an input has no knob to move until latches exist.

**HOME-3.** Presentation MUST NOT go in files that define behaviour: no colours in `track()` options, no canvas positions in code.

**HOME-4.** For a plugin parameter, precedence is: a signal connected in code, then a literal in code, then the state blob. The state's value for a parameter owned by code is ignored.
- *Why:* what saved state means must be decided once.
- *Today:* matches since step 7. A literal or a port is sent whenever it differs from the value last sent, and every parameter code owns is sent again at the first call after a reset, and at the first two after a state load or an export (`plugin_events` and `PluginNode::process` in `core/runtime/src/lib.rs`): a plugin may apply a loaded state at its next call, over what that call sends (Surge XT does). So the blob's value for one plays for at most two blocks (`a_state_load_resends_code_owned_parameters`). A plugin that applies a loaded state later than that, and says so in its output events, is set back by [HOME-5a](#homes-of-values)'s revert (since ph-2), silently for the three calls after a load.

**HOME-5.** A gesture in a plugin window on a parameter that is a code literal MUST write the literal through a coalesced Tx. On a parameter driven by a signal, it sets an override latch ([MON-6](#monitoring-overlays-and-mute-in-song)).
- *Why:* a plugin's GUI can't refuse a gesture, and a cable must not be overridden silently.
- *Today:* matches since ph-2 (step 9). A number written in a `plugin()` call is a control since ph-2 (`@m2/core` gives it an origin, `{site, arg: ["1", <port>]}`, so it is a cell, a knob and a lease target like any value in code; no new API, so the SDK stays 1.2.0). The plugin node reads its call's output events (HOST-4): a gesture's begin on a parameter set by a value in code holds it in the instance's override table (`HELD` in `core/runtime/src/plugins.rs`), so the node sends the window's value in its place. The session, at its next tick (every job, and the watcher's catch-up every 300 ms), begins a gesture lease (WRITE-22) on that control, follows the window's value, and at the gesture's end commits the lease: one value edit, one Undo step. Then it lets the hold go, and the node sends the code's value, now the window's (`a_gesture_on_a_literal_commits_one_tx` in `crates/server/src/graph/plugin_window_tests.rs`).

**HOME-5a.** The host MUST treat parameters that code owns like this:
- Between the plugin's gesture begin and end on such a parameter, the host stops sending it the code value, until the coalesced Tx commits. If the Tx is rejected, the host sends the code value again.
- A change the plugin reports outside a gesture (a preset loaded in its own browser) is set back to the code value at the next block, and the session raises the Notice `plugin.param-reverted`, listing the parameters.
- When code stops owning a parameter (its literal or cable is deleted), the plugin keeps its current value, and the next state save records it.
- *Why:* during the 100 ms or more before the Tx is accepted the host would fight the user's hand, a preset load must not silently overrule the code, and a released parameter must not jump.
- *Today:* matches since ph-2 (step 9). The first point: a held parameter isn't sent while the gesture lasts; a commit that is refused, or a value that isn't a written literal (a computed one), lets the hold go at the gesture's end, and the node sends the code's value again. The second: a parameter value the plugin outputs outside a gesture, on a parameter its code sets, makes the node send the code's value at its next call, and the session says `plugin.param-reverted` once per tick, naming the parameters (`a_preset_change_outside_a_gesture_is_set_back`); in the three calls after a state load it is set back without a word, as a plugin may say what a loaded state set. The third since step 7: a kept instance whose code stops setting a parameter keeps the value it was last sent, as nothing sends it another. It is flagged (`PluginInstance::released` in `core/runtime/src/plugins.rs`) until a state load lands, so an export plays its node with a new instance from the committed state, as `m2 render` does ([RENDER-1](#offline); `a_released_parameter_keeps_its_value_and_its_instance_isnt_lent`).

**HOME-6.** State autosave, once plugin windows ship, is a coalesced data-plane write: one Tx after a gesture ends, or after about 1 s of quiet following the plugin's `mark_dirty`, which is how CLAP reports many changes (a wavetable or a modulation edit) that have no gesture. It takes the prepared-data plane, outside the 20 ms budget, and the journal stores its before bytes once ([WRITE-15](#commit-journal-and-recovery)). A saved state is never pushed back into the instance that produced it: a load is skipped when its blob's hash equals the state the instance holds, the one it last saved or last loaded, at creation included.
- *Why:* state blobs are often 1–50 MB, and a write per parameter change would flood the disk and the undo history. A load skipped against the last save alone would skip a redo: save S, load C (an undo), load S.
- *Today:* matches since ph-2 (step 9). `HostEvent::Dirty`, or a value or a gesture's end on a parameter code doesn't set (the node marks its instance changed), starts a quiet second for that instance; each new change starts it again. Then the session asks the host for `save_state` (off the session's thread) and writes blob and sidecar as one `plugin_state` Tx (FMT-17), one Undo step; a save equal to the file writes nothing (`a_dirty_plugin_autosaves_its_state_once`: a storm of `mark_dirty` makes one Tx; the save isn't loaded back; an undo loads the old blob). A plugin with no state path (no key) can't be saved, and the session says so once. Until it is saved, its instance isn't lent to an export, which saves first ([RENDER-1](#offline)). The host keeps the hash of the state each instance holds, set by every load, at creation too, and by every save, and cleared by a load that fails, which may have landed in part (`MainSide.holds` in `core/plugins/src/inprocess.rs`); tested by `a_load_of_the_held_state_is_skipped`, `a_reload_after_another_load_is_never_skipped` and `after_a_refused_load_the_old_blob_loads_again` (`core/plugins/src/tests.rs`).

## Monitoring overlays and mute in song

**MON-1.** Mute and solo MUST be engine overlays: gain cells keyed by module key, on track modules. They work for every song, whatever shape its code has inside its tracks. The planner puts one overlay gain cell on each consumer port that a track's audio export feeds. The root module (`song`) can't be muted or soloed, and neither can parts within one track; `key()` frames are reserved as a later place for that.
- The consumer ports are the checker's sites (`Flat.overlays`): each input of a node, each capture of a voice and each feedback that reads the audio of a track other than its own module, except sidechain inputs ([MON-2](#monitoring-overlays-and-mute-in-song)); and the song's output when it reads a track directly. The same code gives the same sites.
- The overlay is a built-in op, in every plan, offline renders included. At 1 it copies its input, so it changes no bit; at 0 it writes +0.0. One node and one cell per site.
- In an activation's window every audio edge that crosses tracks reads through an overlay: a fade on a site's input has the site's overlay on its new side; a fade's old side and a ghost's input that the plan before read through one get one of their own for the window, at the new plan's value for the same track and consumer, or, where the new plan has none (the track deleted, or read by another module now), at the gain the plan before had there.
- *Why:* a song written by hand or by an agent, with no mixer, still needs mute and solo.
- *Today:* the engine half is built in 8b.4 (`core/ir/src/check.rs` `overlays`, `core/runtime/src/overlay.rs`; `core/runtime/src/tests/overlay.rs`, including `an_edit_while_muted_keeps_the_track_silent`). Each overlay runs right after its source, so a feedback consumer reads this block's. The server's mute and solo come next.

**MON-2.** Mute MUST silence its module's audio export at every consumer port except sidechain ports ([PKG-13](#component-descriptors)), which always get the unmuted signal. Control exports such as `kick.env` keep flowing. So muting the kick still ducks the bass, whether the ducking reads `kick.env`, `follow(kick.out)` written in `song.ts`, or a compressor keyed from `kick.out`. The mixer shows this.
- *Why:* muting the kick must not suddenly un-duck the bass, and a refactor that keeps the sound must not change what mute does.
- *Today:* the engine half is built in 8b.4: sidechain inputs and control exports are never overlay sites ([MON-1](#monitoring-overlays-and-mute-in-song)). What the mixer shows comes from `feeds` (`crates/editor/src/mute.rs`), in the graph state's `overlays.feeds`: for each track, each module and component that reads it through a sidechain input or a control export, such as first-song's kick, read by `song`'s Duck (`feeds_name_the_duck`). A node written in `song.ts` is named as `song` until the mixer's channel lookup can name its channel's track.

**MON-3.** Solo MUST keep every audio export on a path through a soloed module, and mute the others, with the same exception for sidechain ports.
- The paths are over the sites: a site `m → c` (track `m`'s audio read in module `c`) is heard when no track is soloed, or when some soloed `s` is `m` or feeds `m`, or is `c` or is fed by `c`. Sidechain inputs aren't sites, so they are no edges here. Each site has its own cell, so soloing a bus that reads a vocal itself keeps the vocal's way into it and mutes the same vocal's direct way to the song.
- A bus the song feeds ([RENDER-34](#buses)) is read from the song, which is no track, so the feed is no site. The checker's routes (`Flat.routes`, `m → b`: track `m`'s way into the song reaches bus `b`'s feed) join the "who feeds whom" graph, and a site `m → song` counts as feeding each bus `m` routes to:

  | You do | You hear |
  |---|---|
  | Mute a track | nothing from it: dry, sends and group, since all start after its one cell |
  | Solo a track | it, and every bus it feeds (through sends or `to`), and the buses those feed |
  | Solo a bus | it, and every track that feeds it, their dry sound too: one cell serves both ways |
  | Mute a group | its tracks go silent with it |
- Mute wins over solo on the same track, mute in song included: a track muted in the song stays silent when soloed.
- *Today:* built in 8b.4 (`values` in `crates/server/src/graph/overlay.rs`; `solo_keeps_a_bus_fed_and_its_sources`, `mute_wins_over_solo`, `a_track_muted_in_song_stays_silent_when_soloed`). Routes since track bus, slice 1 (`solo_keeps_the_buses_a_track_feeds`, `soloing_a_bus_keeps_what_feeds_it`, `muting_a_track_silences_its_sends`). Soloing a bus keeps its sources' dry sound too: a wet-only solo needs a cell per path (gaps).

**MON-4.** Mute and solo persist in `.music2/`. Overlays MUST NOT change files, revisions, exports, or an agent render that didn't ask for them (`m2 listen --as-heard` asks, and says so, [AGENT-7](#agents)). An export made while overlays are active carries the `overlays.active` Warning.
- The file is `.music2/overlays.json`, `{"mute": [keys], "solo": [keys]}`, sorted, written whole (a temp file, then a rename). Entries of a track that is gone stay, so it comes back as it was; meanwhile they are ignored, so a solo of a track that is gone silences nothing. A track renamed in code (`Rekeyed`) keeps its entries. A window open read-only keeps its mutes and solos in memory only.
- *Why:* owner decision 4: mute and solo are for listening only.
- *Today:* built in 8b.4. `POST /api/graph/overlay` (`{session, mute?: {key: bool}, solo?: {key: bool}, exclusive?: key, clear?: true}`, not a write, so it works read-only) writes the cells of what plays under the control plane's lock and never waits for the session; the answer, and `overlays` in the graph state, say what is muted and soloed, which tracks are muted in the song, how each track is heard (`heard`, `muted`, `muted-in-song` or `silenced`) and whether this window saves them (`saved`). The file is read when the project opens, so the first renderer plays them from its first block; the server's `music2-tell` thread writes it after a change, off the lock, and a change not yet written is written before the project closes, opens again or the app quits. Every new plan gets the values before it is sent. An export carries `overlays.active` when any of them changes what is heard, and its file is byte-identical either way (`crates/server/src/graph/overlay_tests.rs`). Since `ag-listen` (owner decision D4 of the agents design), `m2 listen --as-heard` reads the open app's mute and solo, else the file, gives its render's overlays the values they give, and says `"as_heard": true`; without it, an agent's listen hears none (`m2_listen_as_heard_applies_the_listening_mutes_and_says_so`).

**MON-5.** "Mute in song" is saved in code and applies to every render, export included. It is a track option in `@m2/core`, so every song has it: `track("kick", { muted: true }, fn)`, carried in the IR as `Module.muted`. The planner applies it as it applies a monitoring mute ([MON-2](#monitoring-overlays-and-mute-in-song)), with the same cells and the same sidechain exception, in every render. Toggling it is a `Confined` Tx on the track call's options; the delta's `muted` change sets the cell, with a ramp and no rebuild.
- *Why:* owner decision 4 asks for a saved mute in every song. A mute that lived only in std's mixer would privilege one package (song-as-program principle 2) and leave a song without a mixer with none.
- *Today:* since 8b.4 the option is in `@m2/core` 1.2.0 (`core/script/src/mute_tests.rs`), the IR carries it, and every render starts each site of a muted track at 0 (`muted_in_song_silences_an_offline_render`). The delta reports the change as `ModuleChange::Muted` (`core/session/src/tx/muted_tests.rs`). The server takes a delta whose only module changes are `Muted` as overlay cell writes, which glide, with no new plan (`a_mute_in_song_toggle_is_a_cell_write`).

**MON-6.** A gesture, in the UI or a plugin window, on a control that a signal drives (a lane or a cable) sets an override latch. The control follows the gesture until "Re-enable" or "Write to code", and the UI marks it as latched. A latch is keyed by the ControlId of a descriptor control, or by the plugin instance and its CLAP parameter id. It persists in `.music2/` only when its whole key is saved keys (a keyed plugin's parameter: module key, frame keys, plugin key, parameter id); otherwise it lasts for the session. A latch never reaches an export.
- *Why:* a lane or a cable must not be overridden silently, and a ControlId, derived from where its calls are ([ID-13](#derived-identity)), doesn't survive a restart.
- *Today:* for plugin windows since ph-2 (step 9): a gesture's begin on a parameter a lane or a cable drives latches it in the instance's override table (`LATCHED`), keyed by the instance and the CLAP id, and the node sends the window's value in place of the driven one from then on. The graph state's `plugins` lists each node's latches; its box says "<name> latched" with Re-enable (`POST /api/graph/plugin/latch {session, node, id?}`), after which the lane plays again. For the session only (no `.music2/` file yet), and never in an export: a latched instance isn't lent (`a_gesture_on_a_lane_driven_parameter_latches_it`). Latches on descriptor controls, "Write to code", and keeping a keyed plugin's latches in `.music2/` are gaps.

**MON-7.** Audition is not an overlay. It is a `Live(Event)` source merged into a lane's event buffer ([RENDER-14](#events)).
- A live note is played on a notes lane, not written in its data. It belongs to a source, which numbers its own notes by handle: a window today, a MIDI keyboard later, on the same path. A message names the plan it is for (its generation) and the lane's slot in it; one for another plan is refused and counted.
- Each lane's scheduler holds up to 16 live notes; one more On is refused and counted with the dropped Ons. Their ids are the engine's, so a live note's id never meets a scheduled note's in any consumer.
- A live note ends only by its Off, its source's release (all its notes, on every lane), a panic, or an activation that replaces its consumer or removes its lane. Never by a stop, a seek or a loop wrap: a held key is a hand, not the song. Offline renders never get one.
- *Why:* you can't write a melody you can't hear. A key held while the song plays, loops or is edited must keep sounding, as a held MIDI key does in other DAWs.
- *Today:* the engine half is built in 8b.6 (`LiveEv` and `NoteScheduler::live` in `core/runtime/src/sched.rs`, `Renderer::live`, `Msg::Live`; `core/runtime/src/tests/audition.rs`). The table moves with its lane's scheduler at an activation, into a grown one too (`a_live_note_moves_with_its_lane_across_an_activation`). A replaced consumer is a ghost released at entry, so its live notes end there; the Off later reaches the new consumer, which never had the note and ignores it (`a_replaced_consumer_ends_its_live_notes`). A pitch outside 0 to 127, a velocity outside 0 to 1 or a slot the plan doesn't have is refused, never a panic on the audio thread. Held for an export, an On is refused (it would sound only when the export ends) and an Off waits for the resume. The player sends one with `Player::live`, and publishes how many are held after each callback (`Player::live_notes`). The server half is built too (`crates/server/src/graph/audition.rs`): a window sends `{"t": "audition", "module", "lane", "on": [{"h", "pitch", "velocity"}], "off": [h], "keep": [h]}` on its WebSocket, and an agent the same with its `client` to `POST /api/graph/audition` (not a write, so read-only windows play too). Each window is a source. The server finds the lane in the plan it last sent and sends the note under the control plane's lock, so it queues behind that plan's activation; it remembers each held note's lane, not its slot, so an Off after an edit finds it (`an_off_after_an_edit_finds_its_lane`). Since `next` two more sources play the selected track the same way: the keyboard piano (a window's audition, from the computer's keys) and each MIDI keyboard (`midi:<name>`, `crates/server/src/graph/midi.rs`, through `m2_os::midi`), with velocity and the sustain pedal. A keyboard is never let go by audition's watchdog: its notes end when it is unplugged, the song closes, or ⌘. stops all sound. `M2_MIDI=none` opens no MIDI device (tests, the check), and `inject` takes what a keyboard would send at `POST /api/graph/midi/inject` (the suites). A lane nothing plays answers that window only, `{"t": "audition", "silent": "Nothing plays this lane"}`. A window's notes are let go when its WebSocket closes, or when it said nothing for 10 s (the window sends a keepalive every 2 s while a key is held; the `music2-tell` thread checks). `/api/graph/status` and the graph state carry `live`, the live notes sounding (`crates/server/src/graph/audition_tests.rs`, `audition_over_http_and_the_websocket` in `crates/server/src/routes_8b_tests.rs`). Since track pm's slice 2 a keyboard's pitch wheel and mod wheel reach its target lane live too ([RENDER-14](#events)'s `Midi`, `Graph::wheel`), and go back to rest on the lane they moved when it lets go: unplugged, All Notes Off, turned off in Settings, another target (its held keys sound on where they are), ⌘. (in the engine); a song closed takes its lanes with it. Held for an export, a wheel isn't refused, as an On is: the latest waits in its slot for the resume, as an Off does, since one let go must never stay bent (`held_for_an_export_a_wheel_waits_for_the_resume`). An export puts a borrowed plugin's wheels at rest before it renders. The status carries `wheels`, the lanes whose wheels are away from rest (`a_keyboards_bend_reaches_its_lane`, `a_keyboard_let_go_centres_its_bend` in `crates/server/src/graph/midi_tests.rs`). The sustain pedal stays in the server. Since `studio` a take reads live notes on the control plane as they are played, never through the session. Recording the wheels isn't built.

**MON-8.** Hearing yourself while recording MUST be a live source on the take's audio lane, like audition ([MON-7](#monitoring-overlays-and-mute-in-song)): the sound input is added to that lane's block before the graph runs, so the track's effects, fader, mute and solo play it. It never reaches an export, a bounce or an agent render ([RENDER-3](#offline), [MON-4](#monitoring-overlays-and-mute-in-song)). It is off unless Settings turns it on, and lasts from Record (the count-in included) to the take's end. One lane hears at a time.
- A message names the plan it is for (its generation) and the lane's slot, as a live note does; one for another plan is refused and counted. Stopping names no lane: it stops hearing on every lane. While a lane hears, the engine never idles.
- *Why:* a singer needs to hear their voice with the song, through the effects it will have. Speakers feed back into the microphone, so it is off by default and Settings says to use headphones.
- *Today:* the engine half is built in step 9's io turn (slice 2): `Msg::Hear` and `Renderer::hear`, the input read through `m2_runtime::LiveInput`; the hearing moves with the lane's audio player across an activation (`core/runtime/src/audio.rs`). `Player::hear` sends it; the server sends it at Record when the take's options ask (`crates/server/src/graph/record.rs`). Tested by `hearing_yourself_plays_through_the_track_and_never_in_an_export` (`core/play/tests/live.rs`: the input comes out at the track's fader, an offline render has none of it) and `hearing_yourself_never_reaches_an_export` (`crates/server/src/graph/record_tests.rs`: an export made during a take is silent). The Settings toggle comes with slice 3.

## PluginHost

```rust
trait PluginHost {
    // the host's main thread
    fn create(&self, id: &PluginId, state: Option<Blob>) -> Handle;
    fn activate(&self, h: Handle, sample_rate: u32, max_frames: u32);
    fn deactivate(&self, h: Handle);
    fn load_state(&self, h: Handle, blob: Arc<Blob>) -> bool;  // false: skipped, its hash equals the state the instance holds (HOME-6)
    fn set_render_mode(&self, h: Handle, mode: RenderMode);  // Realtime | Offline
    fn main_call(&self, h: Handle, call: MainCall);
    fn destroy(&self, h: Handle);
    // queued to the host's main thread; each result comes back as a message
    fn latency(&self, h: Handle);                            // -> Latency(h, frames)
    fn save_state(&self, h: Handle);                         // -> Saved(h, Blob)
    // the audio thread
    fn process(&self, h: Handle, io: &mut SharedBuffers, ev_in: &Events, ev_out: &mut Events, t: &Transport);
    fn reset(&self, h: Handle);                              // panic, and before an offline render: voices and tails cleared
    // any thread but the audio thread
    fn presets(&self, id: &PluginId) -> Vec<Offered>;        // the plugin's own presets (HOST-8)
}
```

As built in step 3b (`core/plugins/src/host.rs`), pending the owner's decision on the step 3b design's clarification C3, the signatures differ from the sketch:

```rust
trait PluginHost: Send + Sync {
    // the host's main thread; the caller waits (inline on the main thread itself)
    fn create(&self, plugin: &Found, state: Option<Arc<Blob>>, owner: &Owner) -> Result<(Handle, Info)>;  // init, state load, ports and params
    fn activate(&self, h: Handle, sample_rate: u32, max_frames: u32) -> Result<u32>;                  // -> latency, after the state load
    fn deactivate(&self, h: Handle);                                 // only while no renderer calls it, or every one that does is held (RENDER-1)
    fn load_state(&self, h: Handle, blob: Arc<Blob>) -> Result<bool>;  // skipped (false) when its hash equals the state the instance holds:
                                                                     // last saved or last loaded, creation included; none after a failed load (HOME-6)
    fn set_render_mode(&self, h: Handle, mode: RenderMode) -> Result<bool>;  // before activating; offline for every render that isn't live (RENDER-1)
    fn main_call(&self, h: Handle, call: MainCall) -> Result<Reply>;  // a parameter's value or text, its info, its log, its window,
                                                                     // one of its own presets loaded (LoadPreset, HOST-8)
    fn destroy(&self, h: Handle);                                    // queued: never waits
    // queued to the host's main thread; the one result comes back on the reply each returns
    fn latency(&self, h: Handle) -> Pending<Result<u32, String>>;
    fn save_state(&self, h: Handle) -> Pending<Result<Blob, String>>;
    // the audio thread (or an offline render's): never allocates, locks or waits
    fn process(&self, h: Handle, io: &mut SharedBuffers, ev_in: &Events, ev_out: &mut Events, t: &Transport) -> Status;
    fn reset(&self, h: Handle);
    fn take_process_request(&self, h: Handle) -> bool;               // CLAP's request_process: wakes an idle engine
    // any thread but the audio thread
    fn scan(&self) -> (Vec<Found>, Vec<String>);                     // every plugin installed: the host loads them, never the app (HOST-3)
    fn presets(&self, plugin: &Found) -> Result<Vec<Offered>>;       // its own presets, found where it runs (HOST-8)
    // any thread
    fn live(&self) -> u64;
    fn counts(&self) -> (u64, u64);                                  // created and destroyed so far (gates, tests)
}
```

- `create` returns the `Info` the checker types a plugin from, so describing a plugin is `create`, `activate`, `destroy`, with no second path around the host. It takes an `Owner`: where the instance's unsolicited events (`Restart`, `Dirty`, `Latency`, and `Crashed` or `Hung` when its host process ended) go, and the count of its live instances, so two sessions in one process never see each other's events.
- `latency` and `save_state` answer as one message on their own reply, which no other waiter can take.
- `Events` is the host's CLAP-shaped list, sized when built (`m2-plugins` can't depend on the runtime's `Event`); the runtime translates into it without allocating.

**HOST-1.** The engine MUST reach plugins only through `PluginHost`, and call each method only on the thread named beside it. Nothing may assume a plugin shares the app's process or threads.
- *Why:* plugins will move into a host process, and the threading model must not be built twice. CLAP needs `activate` with the rate and block size to apply a latency change, and calls `latency` and `save_state` on the main thread only.
- *Today:* matches since step 3b. The runtime's plugin nodes hold an `Arc<PluginInstance>` (a handle and its host) and call `process` and `reset` through the host; renderers create instances, `PackageSet::describe_plugins` finds (`scan`) and describes them and `m2 plugin-state` saves them through `PackageSet::host()`, which is the process's host unless set (`m2_plugins::host()`): since ph-1 the host process (`m2_plugins::Remote`, [HOST-3](#pluginhost)) in every program, the in-process host only where none was installed (tests, and inside the host process). `m2_plugins::Instance` is gone.

**HOST-2.** The in-process implementation (step 3) MUST make every main-thread call on the plugin main thread below, and process plugins on the audio thread. That plugin main thread runs the OS's main loop (`m2_os::main_loop`). On macOS, where plugins post work to the process's main run loop, it is the process's main thread: in the app AppKit's, reached through the main dispatch queue; in the CLI, the headless server and the gates, the main thread runs a `CFRunLoop`. On Linux, where plugins get main-thread time only from the host (CLAP's fd and timer extensions), it is the process's main thread in the CLI, the headless server and the gates, and in the app a loop thread of its own, since GTK owns the app's main thread. The host's thread checks answer truthfully, and `on_main_thread` runs on that thread, never inside `process`. The host process ([HOST-3](#pluginhost)) keeps the same rule inside itself.
- *Since ph-1:* plugins run in the host process, which is the program run again: there the in-process host makes every main-thread call on that process's own main loop, under `run_main`, as below. The app's own process runs no plugin code: its main loop (`use_app_main_thread`) still starts, and serves nothing (a gap: fold it away). What follows is what runs inside the host process.
- *Why:* plugins kept alive across rebuilds turn today's threading violations into crashes. Many commercial plugins (JUCE-based ones among them) post timers and work to the process's main run loop, so a separate "plugin main thread" would give them two main threads that race. The cost on macOS: until the host process ships, a slow plugin load blocks the app's main thread. Linux has no process-wide run loop for plugins to post to, so its app keeps plugins off GTK's thread.
- *Today:* matches since step 3b (`core/plugins/src/main_thread.rs`, `inprocess.rs`, `audio.rs`):
  - every main-thread call (create, init, state, activate, parameters, `on_main_thread`, deactivate, destroy) runs on the plugin main thread; callers on other threads wait for it, and `destroy` is queued. Libraries load on the calling thread, once per bundle, never on the main thread;
  - the app calls `m2_plugins::use_app_main_thread()` first thing in `main` (`plugin_host::install`, `apps/music2-desktop/src/main.rs`): AppKit's main thread through the main dispatch queue on macOS, a loop thread of its own on Linux (`tests/app_main_thread.rs` runs this hook on both). The app opens its launch project off the main thread, which must never wait on a thread that creates plugins. Its quit closes the project first (`Graph::close`), waiting up to 2 s while the main thread destroys the instances, and leaves without exit handlers when some are still alive (`apps/music2-desktop/src/actions.rs`). On Linux the check's app row runs the open inside the app, with the test plugins; on macOS neither has run inside the app, and quit's close has run in neither;
  - `m2`, the headless server example and the gates binary run under `m2_plugins::run_main`: the program on a worker with an 8 MiB stack, the main thread running the OS's main loop (a `CFRunLoop` serving the main queue on macOS, a job queue on Linux); on return it serves until every instance is destroyed (2 s at most), then refuses further work. Tests, where libtest owns the main thread, opt in to one dedicated thread instead (`m2_plugins::test_main_thread()`); nothing falls back silently: with none installed, creating a plugin is an error naming what to call. Using a dedicated thread in tests is the step 3b design's clarification C1, awaiting the owner's decision; `core/plugins/tests/main_thread.rs` (no libtest harness) runs the real one, with Surge XT when `CLAP_PATH` has it;
  - `is_main_thread` asks the executor (`m2_os::main_loop::Loop::is_current`: `pthread_main_np` for macOS's main thread, else the loop thread's token); `is_audio_thread` holds when the caller holds the instance's audio role, which `process` and `reset` take and the main thread takes only to `stop_processing` before deactivating. No thread-local is touched, so the audio thread never allocates for one;
  - a plugin's requests (`request_callback`, `request_restart`, `latency.changed`, `mark_dirty`, `params.rescan`) only raise flags; every 5 ms a tick thread has the main thread sweep them, so `on_main_thread` never runs inside `process`, and the rest goes to the instance's owner.
  - since 8a's live half a song with plugins renders all its partitions on the device's thread: workers never call a plugin (`render_block_on`; `a_song_with_a_plugin_runs_no_task_on_a_worker`).

**HOST-3.** The host process, before plugin windows ship, uses shared-memory audio and event rings, message-based main-thread calls and its own main loop for windows (`m2_os::main_loop`: AppKit's on macOS; on Linux a loop over the X connection, the plugins' fds and timers, serving CLAP's `posix-fd-support` and `timer-support`). When it crashes, the affected instances become silence with `plugin.crashed`, and the app keeps running.
- *Why:* one plugin crash must not take down the session.
- *Today:* matches since ph-1 (step 9); windows, fds and timers since ph-2: the main loop (`m2_os::main_loop::Loop::watch` and `timer`; Linux a `poll` loop, macOS `CFRunLoopTimer` and `CFFileDescriptor`, Windows designed) serves CLAP's `timer-support` and `posix-fd-support` to every plugin, and the host process opens plugin windows on it ([HOST-7](#pluginhost)). In short:

  | Part | How |
  |---|---|
  | The process | The program run again with `--m2-plugin-host` (`run_main` and `use_app_main_thread` serve when they see it; tests run `m2-plugin-host`). One per app process, all plugins in it. It starts at the first call that needs it (a song with no plugins starts none), and again after it ended. Inside it, the in-process host runs under `run_main` (HOST-2). It writes no core dump, so a crash ends it at once |
  | Main-thread calls | Framed binary messages on its standard input and output, with request ids (`core/plugins/src/remote/wire.rs`); a plugin that prints goes to standard error. One reader thread in the app routes each reply, and each plugin's own events to its `Owner`. Both sides must be one build (a stamp, checked at the start) |
  | Audio and events | One shared-memory slot per active instance (`m2_os::ipc::Shm`; `remote/slot.rs`): the call's audio, transport and events in CLAP's own layout, at most `MAX_CALL_EVENTS` (16,384) in (a node that could need more is refused at build, by name) and 256 out. A call is synchronous: copy in, ring, spin 20 µs, then sleep on the done word (`m2_os::ipc::wait`: a futex, `__ulock` shared on macOS). The host process has one audio thread per instance, asleep on its slot, asking for real time, and puts the one float mode back after each call ([RENDER-2a](#offline)). Nothing on the app's side allocates, locks or waits on anything but the call (RENDER-28) |
  | A crash | The pipe closes: every instance of that process is marked, its calls fail at once (`process` returns `Status::Error`, so the fail-live path of HOST-5 runs) and its owner hears `HostEvent::Crashed`. The actor reports each of its instances into the fault ring, `plugin.crashed`; the session tells them as one problem naming the plugins ([RENDER-25](#plans-data-and-arrangement-swaps)), and the next activation makes them again from their saved states, in a new process. The audio thread tells a crash from the plugin's own error by the host (`PluginHost::gone`), so a crash is never also `plugin.failed`. The app plays on |
  | A hang | Every wait has a limit. A live call that waits 500 ms (RENDER-28's stall time) makes a watchdog end the process; the same path follows, `HostEvent::Hung`, said as "stopped answering". So does an offline call, a main-thread call or an answer awaited (a latency, a save) that waits 30 s (`remote::LIMIT`; a scan, which loads every library, 2 minutes): a plugin stuck on the host's main loop holds up the session's actor, or an export, for that long at most, and the caller hears why (`a_main_thread_call_that_hangs_ends_its_process`, `an_offline_call_that_hangs_ends_its_process`). Quitting ends the process, so it ends any wait at once |
  | Finding plugins | `scan` is on the trait: `m2_plugins::scan` and `find`, `describe_plugin` and `m2 plugins` ask the host. The app never loads plugin code. Since track br the app's lists come from the same scan: the side browser's Plugins groups, the Device view's + and + Track ask `GET /api/plugins`, which scans on the first ask, off the async threads, and keeps the list for the app run until Look again (`POST /api/plugins/scan`; `crates/server/src/routes_plugins.rs`). Each plugin is an instrument, an effect, or both, by its CLAP features (`Found::is_instrument`, `is_effect`). Adding one is one Tx built in `crates/editor/src/plugins.rs` (the add routes take `clap:<id>`): the plugin is described through the host first, so one that isn't installed, or is of the wrong kind, is refused in words with nothing written |

  Tested by `the_plugin_list_comes_from_the_host_scan` (`crates/server/tests/plugin_list.rs`, a process of its own), `the_host_suite_passes_on_both_hosts`, `a_crash_marks_every_instance_of_its_process`, `a_hung_call_ends_its_process` (`core/plugins/tests/remote.rs`), `the_app_never_loads_plugin_code` (`no_plugin_code.rs`: no `.clap` mapped after Surge XT and Airwindows played), `a_host_crash_is_one_plugin_crashed_problem`, `a_hung_host_process_is_ended_and_said`, `an_export_borrows_instances_in_the_host_process` (`crates/server/src/graph/tests.rs`), `renders_through_the_host_process_are_the_same_bits` (`core/play/tests/bit_identity.rs`), the allocation trap on the host process (`no_alloc.rs`), and the app's hook test (`apps/music2-desktop/tests/app_main_thread.rs`). On Linux a call's round trip adds about 11 µs at the median and 35 µs at the 99th percentile (information, [PLAT-1](#platforms)). Tests that read the probe's records keep the in-process host (`test_main_thread`). macOS's shared memory and wake are lint-checked only.

**HOST-4.** Plugin output events (parameter gestures) MUST come back through `ev_out`, to an override latch, a literal Tx or automation recording.
- *Today:* to a latch and a literal Tx since ph-2 (step 9); recording is a gap. `Events` reads CLAP's gesture begin and end beside parameter values. After each live call the plugin node reads what it output (`heard` in `core/runtime/src/lib.rs`, atomics only, no allocation): a gesture on a parameter its code sets holds or latches it in the instance's fixed override table (32 parameters); a value outside a gesture on one is set back and its id put on a fixed ring of 64 for the session; one on a parameter code doesn't set marks the instance changed. The session reads them at its tick (`crates/server/src/graph/plugin_window.rs`). An export's nodes read none.

**HOST-5.** Plugin instances persist across plans like every instance ([RENDER-25](#plans-data-and-arrangement-swaps)), by RuntimeId ([ID-15](#derived-identity)) and shape (id, version, channels). A state change is loaded into the instance ([RENDER-26b](#plans-data-and-arrangement-swaps)). An instance that asked for a restart is new at the next activation, created from the committed blob. So is one that failed live, which plays silence until then. Offline renders in the app share the live instances ([RENDER-1](#offline)); the CLI creates its own.
- *Today:* matches since step 7 (`continues` in `core/session/src/tx/continues.rs`, `Shape::Plugin` in `core/runtime/src/plan.rs`); since ph-1 a crash of the host process takes the same path for each of its instances, told as one `plugin.crashed` problem ([HOST-3](#pluginhost)): a keyed plugin follows its key, wherever its call is; an unkeyed one keeps its instance by its name, which a wrap keeps ([ID-13](#derived-identity)), and one given a key continues by its name too. The shape doesn't hold the state: a changed blob is loaded into the kept instance ([RENDER-26b](#plans-data-and-arrangement-swaps)), and a blob taken away makes it new. A restart request (`HostEvent::Restart`) puts the instance in the actor's `Open.restarts`, and the catch-up activates at once with it new. A plugin that fails live (a processing error, or no activate after an export) is marked failed on the audio thread (`PluginInstance::kill`): its node plays silence and never calls it, the renderer plays on, the node reports `plugin.failed` into the fault ring ([RENDER-25](#plans-data-and-arrangement-swaps); the export's give-back reports one that won't come back), and the session puts it in `Open.restarts` (`a_plugin_that_fails_live_goes_silent_alone` in `core/runtime/src/tests/live/keep/plugins.rs`, `a_plugin_that_fails_live_is_one_problem` in `crates/server/src/graph/tests.rs`). One whose new instance fails within 10 s too is left silent, listed while it is, until the song is opened again, so a plugin that fails at every call isn't made again every 300 ms (`Open.revived`, `a_plugin_that_keeps_failing_is_started_again_once` in `crates/server/src/graph/tests.rs`). Offline, a plugin's error, or an instance that died while the render played it, fails the render, strict or not: `plugin.failed`, or `plugin.crashed` when its host process ended ([RENDER-1](#offline)). Only an activation in the same engine keeps instances: a new player or a rate change creates every one. The app's export borrows the live instances while playback is held ([RENDER-1](#offline)); `m2 render`, `m2 bounce` and range renders create their own. The last plan holding an instance drops it off the audio thread, and its destruction is queued to the plugin main thread.
