**HOST-6.** Plugins get the transport from the engine: the playing and loop flags, tempo, the meter from the arrangement, a transport event at the frame of every jump, and `steady_time` from the engine clock.
- *Today:* matches since step 3b. A plugin is called once per run of a block, so a call never spans a jump, and each call carries its run's transport: playing or not, the loop, the tempo, the meter and bar from the arrangement, and its song position; `steady_time` is the engine clock (`PluginNode::process` in `core/runtime/src/lib.rs`, `transport_event` in `core/plugins/src/audio.rs`). Tested by `plugins_are_told_the_transport` (`core/runtime/src/tests/live/keep/plugins.rs`), with the probe recording what each call was told: playing and stopped, the loop across a mid-block wrap (two calls, each told its own run), the tempo, a 7/8 meter and its bar, and `steady_time` against the engine clock. Since ph-3 (step 9) a plugin whose inputs arrive late ([RENDER-18](#latency)) is told the transport its offset earlier: its calls split where that earlier transport's runs do, each told its run's position, play state and the arrangement there (`Past::told` in `core/runtime/src/delay.rs`); before the engine started it stood at the first position, stopped (`lanes_notes_and_time_arrive_late_by_the_offset`).

**HOST-7.** Plugin windows, VST3 and AU MUST be built only behind `PluginHost`.
- *Today:* plugin windows since ph-2 (step 9): `MainCall::Window(WindowCall::Open { title } | Close)` answers `Reply::Window(open)`, and the person's close comes as `HostEvent::WindowClosed`. Inside the host, CLAP's `gui` is embedded (`is_api_supported`, `create`, `get_size`, `set_parent`, `show`; `hide`, `destroy`) in a window of `m2_os::window`: X11 through `libX11.so.6` opened at run time on Linux (a run without a screen never needs it; Wayland through XWayland only), an `NSWindow` through the Objective-C runtime on macOS (the host process an accessory app, its main loop pumping AppKit's events; lint-checked only), an `HWND` designed for Windows. A plugin's `request_resize` resizes it. `Info.gui` says whether a plugin has a window this OS can show. The app opens one from the plugin's box (`POST /api/graph/plugin/window {session, node, open}`; the graph state's `plugins` says `open`, `closed` or `none` for each node, by its name in the checked graph, which its view node gives as `plugin`); the windows float on their own (D12). A window is asked about only by the process that owns it: a `Native` handle (on macOS an `NSView` pointer into the host process) is never used in another process, so tests ask the host process, through the debug builds' `WindowCall::Shown` (`Reply::Shown`: the handle, to compare, and whether it is shown) and `WindowCall::AskToClose` (the person's close, made there); `m2_os::window::Window::shown` and `ask_to_close` answer for the caller's own window only, on every OS. On macOS a window's `closed` runs next on the main queue, outside AppKit's `windowShouldClose:`. Tested by `a_plugin_window_opens_on_the_host_process_main_loop` (`core/plugins/tests/window.rs`, on Xvfb; without a screen it says why) and `e2e/ph-window.mjs` (Surge XT's window from the lead's box). VST3 and AU are not built.

**HOST-8.** A plugin's presets MUST reach it only through `PluginHost`. Its own presets are found, and loaded, where the plugin runs: in the host process, never in the app. One of them is loaded on an instance made for it, never on the instance that plays: that one changes only when the load's Tx commits, through the usual state load ([RENDER-26b](#plans-data-and-arrangement-swaps)).
- *Why:* a preset load runs plugin code, which may be slow or crash ([HOST-3](#pluginhost)). Loading into the instance that plays would change the sound before the Tx is checked, so a refused Tx or an Undo would no longer be exact.
- *Today:* matches since track br. In short:

  | Part | How |
  |---|---|
  | Finding them | `PluginHost::presets(&Found)` answers `Offered { name, location, key }`, where `location` is `PresetAt::Plugin` (inside it) or `PresetAt::File(path)`. CLAP's preset-discovery factory, on a thread of its own in the host process, never the main thread: the factory is thread-safe, and a long search must not hold up the main thread, which serves every playing plugin's calls and windows. Each provider's locations, a file location's folder walked for the file types it declared (2,000 files at most, `MAX_PRESET_FILES`). A preset that names other plugins is left out. Over the wire as `Req::Presets` (`core/plugins/src/presets.rs`) |
  | Loading one | An instance made with no state, then `MainCall::LoadPreset { location, key }` (CLAP's `preset-load`, answers `Reply::Loaded`), `save_state`, the values of the parameters its code writes (`MainCall::Value`), and destroy. The state and those values then load as a preset of yours does: one Tx ([FMT-22](#presets)) |
  | The app | The card's **Presets** has the plugin's own as a group named for it ("Surge XT's"), shown when it offers some. The list is kept per plugin for the app run (`crates/server/src/graph/presets.rs`) |

  Tested by `the_probe_offers_its_presets_on_both_hosts`, `a_preset_search_never_runs_on_the_main_thread` and `a_plugin_preset_loads_on_a_scratch_instance` (`core/plugins/tests/remote.rs`; the probe offers Bright and Dark), `the_plugins_own_preset_loads_on_a_scratch_instance` (`crates/server/src/graph/presets_tests.rs`) and `e2e/br-presets.mjs`. Surge XT on Linux, by hand: 1,974 presets listed in about 0.5 s, and one loads in about 0.1 s (information, [PLAT-1](#platforms)). A plugin with no discovery factory has no group; yours still work.

## Kernel ABI reservations

The detail of kernel ABI v1, byte by byte, is in `docs/kernel-abi.md`. These rules are what it must keep.

**ABI-1.** Kernel input memory is host-owned and read-only to kernels: `k_process` MUST NOT write it, and `k_reset` MUST NOT change it. The host places every port buffer, so no two overlap. Debug builds check input memory after each call and fail on a change.
- *Why:* cells are written once ([RENDER-22](#cells)).
- *Today:* matches since step 5. The Rust kit keeps it by construction (`std/kernels-src/kernel/src/lib.rs`). Since track ab the host places each state's port buffers with the module's own allocator (`m2_alloc`), so the overlap check at build went. Debug builds compare each kernel's input memory before and after every `k_process` and `k_reset` call (a copy and a compare rather than a hash: cheaper, and exact), and a change fails the render naming the kernel and the input (`KernelNode` in `core/runtime/src/lib.rs`); a test plays every std kernel through it (`every_std_kernel_keeps_its_inputs`).

**ABI-2.** Kernel ABI v1 is the contract with kernel code, frozen in step 9:
- a module says its version in its custom section `m2_abi` (4 bytes, a little-endian u32), and exports `memory` (defined, 32-bit, 64 KiB pages, not shared) and `m2_alloc(size, align)`; each kernel `k` exports `k_ports`, `k_new(cfg) -> state`, `k_reset(state)`, `k_process(state, io, from, to)` and `k_active(state)`. Every call names its state;
- the host writes a settings block before `k_new`: the rate, `max_block`, flags, the channel count per port, the kernel's values (numbers) and its assets (a handle, frames, channels and rate each). It starts with its own length, every list in it is an address and a count, and fields are only ever added at the end;
- the port table holds one buffer address per port channel, inputs first;
- the one import is `m2.asset_read(handle, channel, frame, dst, frames)`: an asset's decoded audio copied into the kernel's memory, from where the host holds it, its choice per sandbox: decoded whole and shared outside every sandbox (a live kernel's: no allocation, no wait), or read from its file (an offline kernel's, off the audio thread). Live, a frame the host has not loaded yet reads as silence and counts as a miss, with the same returned count, so live kernels may stream later within v1; never in an export or an offline render;
- offline kernels (`offline = true` in the manifest, `flags` bit 0): run by the host off the audio thread to make something from a file, never by song code (`kernel.offline`). No inputs; the source is asset 0, read from its file; a time map of (source seconds, output seconds) pairs; the output in `max_block` chunks, each to a sink as it is made;
- reserved, shapes fixed and not built: event ports (`{count, cap, events}`, [RENDER-14](#events)'s `Event` in 24 bytes), `k_save`/`k_load` (off the audio thread) and `k_latency`/`k_tail` (fixed by the settings).
- *Why:* the sampler, arpeggiators and third-party kernels all need these, and their shape must not change once kernels are written against it.
- *Today:* built since track ab: the kit and std (`std/kernels-src`), the sandbox and the import (`core/runtime/src/sandbox.rs`), settings in the graph (`assets` and `values` on a kernel node, `core/ir/src/lib.rs`), and `kernel(id, inputs, { assets, values })`, `setting.asset()` and `setting.keymap()` in `@m2/core` 1.3.0 (and `setting.regions()` in 1.6.0, [PKG-15](#component-descriptors)). Tested by `a_kernel_reads_its_asset_through_the_import`, `an_asset_read_allocates_nothing`, `a_kernel_takes_its_settings` and `asset_and_keymap_settings_are_checked`. The reserved shapes are in the Deferred table. The Rust kit reads them through `Cfg::values`, `Cfg::assets` and `Asset::read` (off wasm32, a stand-in keeps files for tests). Its first user is `std:sampler` (std 0.2.1, `std/kernels-src/src/sampler.rs`; `std:regions` beside it since std 0.5.0, [PKG-16](#component-descriptors)), and `examples/sampler` renders the same bits on 1, 2, 3, 4 and 7 threads (`the_sampler_example_gives_the_same_bits_on_1_to_7_threads`). The files live kernels read are decoded whole, once, into the audio cache's own map by content, kept while builds read them, so a live edit shares them with the renderer it replaces (`a_kernels_file_is_decoded_once_across_edits`); clips stream from disk ([RENDER-30](#plans-data-and-arrangement-swaps)), live kernels don't yet (gaps). Since track sx a sandbox may hold an asset as its file instead (`Held::Disk` in `sandbox.rs`), read through the lanes' WAV reader (`stream.rs`) with `m2_os::fs::read_at`, for offline kernels; it reads the same bits as a whole decode (`an_asset_read_from_disk_is_the_decoded_asset`). Offline kernels are built since track sx (`core/runtime/src/offline.rs`; `Cfg::offline` and `Cfg::time_map` in the kit), kept out of the kernels songs call (`song_code_cannot_call_an_offline_kernel`). The first is `std:stretch` (std 0.2.2; std 0.3.0 since the pm merge, `std/kernels-src/src/stretch.rs`), which stretches every warped clip ([RENDER-30](#plans-data-and-arrangement-swaps), `a_warped_clip_is_stretched_by_std_stretch`): in a live session on the session's stretch thread, in the background, while the clip plays what is made (`core/runtime/src/renders.rs`, `a_tempo_change_never_waits_for_a_stretch`); in an export before it plays (`an_export_never_plays_a_render_half_made`). The app's Follow tempo gives a clip its warp markers (the clip menu; `Gesture::Audio`'s `follow`).

**ABI-3.** A module's `m2_abi` section says 1. The runtime reads it from the module's bytes before it compiles the module, and refuses one built for another version, naming it, whatever else the module holds; a module with no section is refused as v0. Every later ABI keeps this section. Its only import may be `m2.asset_read`. A shape that changes, or a rule, makes v2; an optional export or a field at the end of the settings block keeps v1.
- *Today:* matches since track sx (`abi_version`, a hand parser, and `check_abi` in `core/runtime/src/packages.rs`, before `Module::new`): a v2 module that also imports `m2.other` and has a 64-bit memory is refused by its version (`a_later_abi_module_is_refused_by_its_version_first`); a module with no section is refused with "built for kernel ABI v0, or isn't a music2 kernel", even one that exports an `m2_abi()` (`a_module_with_no_abi_section_is_refused_as_v0`); std.wasm says v1 there and exports no `m2_abi()` (`std_wasm_says_v1_in_its_section`). Any other import is refused, naming it (`only_the_asset_import_is_allowed`).

**ABI-4.** The IR reserves a `const-events` op for events generated by code (`notes([...])`, `euclid()`, a seeded rng). The rule that build code never reads lane data stays.
- *Why:* owner decision 7: room now, built later.

**ABI-5.** Every kernel MUST keep five rules: (1) take memory only in `k_new`, since a sealed sandbox can't grow; (2) `k_reset` takes no memory and leaves the state as `k_new` (and later `k_load`) left it; (3) never write an input, and read the port addresses from `io` at each call without keeping them; (4) write every frame of `from..to` on every output channel, and touch only the channels a port has; (5) keep no per-state data in statics, let no static point at memory a state took, and keep all one `k_new` takes in one run of bytes.
- *Why:* many states share a sandbox, the audio thread never waits for memory ([RENDER-28](#plans-data-and-arrangement-swaps)), and a later layout may move a state's bytes without calling the kernel.
- *Today:* the host enforces (1) and (2) by the seal and the prewarm (`a_sealed_sandbox_never_grows`, `every_std_kernel_resets_in_a_sealed_sandbox`), and debug builds check (3) ([ABI-1](#kernel-abi-reservations)). The kit keeps (4) and (5) for Rust kernels: its allocator moves only forward and works only in `k_new`, and a mono port's channel 1 is mapped for the kernel.

## Library

What you can add to a song, found in one place: ⌘F's panel, the side browser's search and `m2 search` ask the same list with the same search. Built by track `spotlight` (2026-10-30), slice 3 of the packs design (`docs/designs/packs/design.md`, its §9.2 and §10).

**LIB-1.** The library MUST be one list of **items**, gathered in one place from its sources, and searched by one function in Rust that every search in music2 calls. An item has a kind (`instrument`, `effect`, `sound`, `sample` or `plugin`), an id, a label, a category, a description, tags and a package (`std`, `song` for the song's own components, `yours`, or `plugin`); a sample also has its type (`loop` or `one-shot`), its tempo, its key and its length.
- Sources: the components a build offers with a category ([PKG-7](#component-descriptors)): std's, the packs the lists offer ([PACK-11](#packs): the open song's, and the newest runnable version of every other pack in the store, with or without a song open) and the song's own code; every preset, std's, each such pack's and yours, as a **sound** each, with its sound's category ([FMT-22](#presets)); the **samples** of std, of each such pack and of your sample folders ([LIB-4](#library)); and the installed CLAP plugins the app has listed already. A search never scans for plugins, and never waits for a scan: a scan loads every plugin's library ([AGENT-11](#agents)) and can take minutes, so a search meanwhile uses the list from before it, or none. A new kind of item is one more source in that one place.
- What is typed is split into words as an item's text is: at every character that is not a letter or a digit, so `hi-hat` is `hi hat` and `synth.pluck` is `synth pluck`. Every word must match, case-insensitively: the start of a word of the label, the label itself (inside it), the start of a tag, or the start of a word of the description. An item's whole id typed (`std:reverb`) finds it first.
- Some words are filters. A kind (`instrument`, `effects`, `fx`, `presets`…), `loop` and `one-shot` find the items of that kind or type, and also the items whose label has that word, so a name with one in it (`Surge XT Effects`, a `Loop…` instrument) is still found by it. A plugin is an instrument or an effect too, by what it plays: `effect` finds the effect plugins. A tempo (`90 bpm`, `90bpm`) and a key written with a capital (`Am`, `F#`, `Bbm`, `C major`) are filters only a sample can meet: a tempo finds the samples within 2 bpm of it, and a key the samples in that key or its relative (`Am` finds `C` too, and `C` finds `Am`). **Fits my song** is clicked, not typed: LIB-2's route with `fits=<beat>` (the playhead's) keeps only loops within 8% of the open song's tempo at that beat and, when the song has a key (`layout.json`'s `key`), in that key or its relative; a loop with no key (drums) fits any key.
- Ranked: the whole query is the label (word for word, its punctuation aside), then the label starts with it, then by each item's weakest word: a word of the label, inside the label, a tag, the description. Then std's and music2's own (`@m2/`) before yours and the song's, and those before plugins; then by kind (instruments, sounds, effects, samples, plugins); then by label, then id. The same list and query give the same order every time.
- A page shows what it found for the words in its field now: an answer to older words is dropped, and Enter waits for the answer to the words typed, never acting on rows of an older search. The side browser filters its own rows as you type until that answer comes.
- *Why:* two searches drift apart, and a musician learns one. Searching on this computer, over what it has, is instant and sends nowhere what is typed (the packs design §9.2).
- *Today:* since track `spotlight`: `crates/editor/src/library.rs` (`items`, `search`, `Query::parse`), tested in `library_tests.rs` (`what_is_typed_is_split_into_words_as_an_items_text_is`, `a_kind_word_finds_that_kind_and_names_with_it`, and one test for each rule above). The plugin list is kept apart from the scan's lock (`crates/server/src/lib.rs`, `a_search_never_waits_for_a_plugin_scan`). ⌘F's panel (`web/src/components/Spotlight.tsx`) and the side browser's field ask it through LIB-2's route; `e2e/spotlight.mjs` checks that Enter right after typing adds the newest words' best hit, in both. The + Track list and the Device view's + still search in the window (gaps). Since track `samples` the packs of the store are sources with no song open too, and samples are (LIB-4 to LIB-9); the filters' tolerances are tested in `library_tests.rs` (`a_tempo_finds_within_two_bpm_and_a_key_its_relative`, `fits_my_song_keeps_loops_near_the_songs_tempo_in_its_key`) and through the route (`crates/server/src/routes_library_tests.rs`, `the_samples_of_std_and_a_pack_are_found_and_fit_the_song`).

**LIB-2.** `GET /api/library?q=<words>` MUST answer the items the search finds, ranked, to the page and to agents ([AGENT-8](#agents)). `m2 search <words…> [--kind <kind>] [--json]` asks it when the app runs; with the app closed it gathers the same list itself from std and your presets (no plugins), and searches it with the same function.
- *Why:* an agent finds a sound the way the musician does, with or without the app.
- *Today:* since track `spotlight`: `crates/server/src/routes_library.rs` (`the_library_route_answers_the_one_search`); `m2 search` in `apps/m2/src/search.rs`, through the app with the tools key or by itself (`apps/m2/tests/search.rs`). The server reads your presets and keeps previews in the folders `AppState::keep_library` gives it: the app and the headless server give the user's, tests scratch ones.

**LIB-3.** A preview (`GET /api/library/preview?id=<id>`) MUST be an offline render by music2's own engine ([RENDER-1](#offline)) of a small song written for it in the cache folder (`m2_os::dirs::cache`, [PLAT-7](#platforms)), never in a project and never through the live engine. It plays a phrase chosen by the item's category: a beat for drums (on the notes `editor.notes` names, else 36, 38 and 42), a bass line for a bass, a line for a lead, four chords for the rest; a sound plays its instrument's phrase with its preset's values; an effect plays a fixed loop of a pluck and a drum kit, the first bar without the effect and the second through it.
- It loads std and the item's own package, as the open song's build found it, so a pack's instrument plays from its pack.
- About 4.5 seconds, a 16-bit stereo WAV at 48 kHz, its last quarter second faded out. Every preview is brought to one loudness, measured as ears hear it: −23 LUFS (ITU-R BS.1770's integrated loudness: K-weighted, gated), or less where that would put a peak above −1 dBFS. A few sounds of short, sharp hits can't reach it: std's Hats and Rim play up to 2 dB quieter.
- A preview that makes no sound is none, said in words, and is never kept. An instrument that plays a file you give it (one with an asset setting, as the Sampler) has no preview, said in words.
- Kept in the cache folder by the item's id and the hash of what it renders (the song's files, every file of the packages it loads, and music2's version and its SDK's), so a changed preset, package or music2 renders anew, and a second ask renders nothing. Only one render of an item runs at a time: a second ask waits for it, and an answer is read before the next render of that item can replace it. A part file of a render that failed is removed. A pure render gives the same bits every time ([RENDER-2](#offline)).
- A render runs on a thread of its own at background priority, as an export's workers do beside playback ([AGENT-7](#agents)): off the audio thread and the session's.
- A plugin, a plugin's preset and the song's own components have no preview, said in words.
- *Why:* a preview must be the item playing, so it can't oversell (the packs design §10), and it must never cost the song that plays a glitch.
- *Today:* since track `spotlight`: `crates/editor/src/library/preview.rs` (the song, the render and the loudness; `a_preview_is_the_same_bits_twice_at_one_loudness`, `every_std_item_with_a_preview_renders_one`, `loudness_is_measured_as_bs_1770_does`, `a_preview_renders_on_a_thread_of_its_own`), the cache in `crates/server/src/library.rs` (`a_preview_is_kept_until_what_it_renders_changes`, `a_second_ask_waits_for_the_first_render`, `a_packs_instrument_plays_from_its_pack`). No check yet measures dropouts while previews render beside playback, as G13 does for `m2 listen` (gaps).

**LIB-4.** A **sample** is a WAV file the library lists as an item of kind `sample`: a file under a package's `samples/` folder (std's, and each pack's the lists offer, [PACK-11](#packs)), or under one of your sample folders ([LIB-5](#library)). Its id is `sample:<package>:<path in the package>` (`sample:std:samples/kit/kick.wav`), or `sample:folder:<the file's path>` for yours; its package is the package's name, or `yours`. A sample has:
- what is **measured** from the file, never typed: its length, channels, rate, peak and SHA-256;
- what is **said** about it: its type (`loop` or `one-shot`), its instrument (one word of a short list kept in one place, `m2_editor::library::samples::INSTRUMENTS`: kick, snare, clap, hat, cymbal, tom, perc, bass, keys, guitar, synth, pad, lead, vocal, strings, brass, fx, texture, foley), a loop's tempo (20 to 300 bpm), its key, and tags. A package says them in `samples.toml` at its root, with paths inside `samples/`: a `[folder."<path>"]` table for every file under that folder (the deepest folder wins), and a `[file."<path>"]` table for one file (which wins over its folders), each with `type`, `instrument`, `bpm`, `key` (`Am`, `F#`, `Bb minor`, `C`) and `tags = [..]`. It is read strictly: an unknown key, a word not on the list, a tempo out of range or a key that doesn't read is refused, naming it, and that package's samples are then guessed.
- What isn't said, and all of your folders', is **guessed** from the file's path, by one function (`library::samples::guess`): a tempo (`90 bpm`, `120bpm`, `bpm 95`; a bare 60 to 200 only in a name with a key or a loop word); a key (`Am`, `C#m`, `Ebmin`, `F# minor`, `Bb maj`; a bare letter isn't one, and a lowercase root needs a sign or a mode written out); the type (a tempo, or `loop` or `loops` in the path, makes a loop, unless the path says one-shot); and the instrument (the first word of the list, in the list's order, that a word of the file's name names, so `808 kick` is a kick; else its folders', deepest first).
- Only WAV files are samples for now: another audio file (FLAC, MP3, AIFF, OGG, M4A) is skipped and counted, and the library says "WAV only for now" with the count.
- *Why:* a musician finds a loop by its tempo, key and sound, and a pack author shouldn't have to type what the file can say (the packs design P7). A guess that a pack can correct is right most of the time for no work.
- *Today:* since track `samples`: `crates/editor/src/library/samples.rs` (`guess`, `Said::read`, `measure`, `index`), tested in `samples_tests.rs` (`a_name_says_its_tempo_key_type_and_instrument`, over many real-world names, `samples_toml_is_read_strictly`, `samples_toml_wins_over_the_guess`, `only_wav_files_are_samples_and_the_rest_are_counted`).

**LIB-5.** **Your sample folders** are kept in this computer's settings (`samples`, a list of folders, in `~/.music2/settings.json` beside where new songs go), added and removed in the library (Add a Folder…, and Remove beside each). Nothing in them is copied until a sample is added to a song. The server indexes a folder off the session's and the request's threads, in the background, when it is added and when a search finds it not indexed or indexed over a minute ago; a search meanwhile lists what was indexed before, or none, and never waits. What is measured is kept in the cache folder (`samples/` in `m2_os::dirs::cache`, [PLAT-7](#platforms)), by the file's path, size and modified time, so a folder indexed before is indexed again with no file read; a package's samples are measured and kept the same way. `m2 search` with the app closed indexes your folders itself, through the same cache.
- *Why:* a folder of 10,000 samples must not hold up a search, and must open fast the second time.
- *Today:* since track `samples`: `index` in `crates/editor/src/library/samples.rs` (`a_folder_indexed_again_reads_no_file`), the background index in `crates/server/src/library.rs` (`a_folder_is_indexed_in_the_background`), the settings and routes in `crates/server/src/routes_library.rs` (`GET`/`POST /api/library/folders`).

**LIB-6.** A sample's preview is its own file, served whole by LIB-3's route (`GET /api/library/preview?id=<id>`), and only for a sample the library lists at that moment: the route takes an id, never a path.
- *Why:* hearing the sample itself is the preview that can't oversell; a route that served any path would read any file on the computer.
- *Today:* since track `samples` (`a_samples_preview_is_its_file_and_only_a_listed_one`).

**LIB-7.** **Adding a sample** to a song (Enter or a drop in the app, `POST /api/library/sample`, `m2 add-sample`) copies its file into `assets/` as an import does ([FMT-14](#assets-and-hashes): create-only, under the first free name whose note's name is free too; the same bytes already there are that file), and writes its **note** beside it, `assets/<file>.json`, create-only (a note already there is kept), before any change names the file ([WRITE-1](#the-two-write-primitives)): `{"from": {"pack": "<package>", "version": "<version>", "file": "samples/…", "author": "…", "license": "…"}}` for a package's (std's too), `{"from": {"folder": true}}` for one of yours, with no path, which would show your disk's layout to whoever gets the song. Then one Tx, one undo step: a clip at the beat given, on the audio lane given or on a new audio track. A loop whose tempo is known and differs from the song's at that beat is added with Follow tempo on: warp markers made from its own tempo, `[[0, 0], [<its seconds>, <its beats>]]`, its beats rounded to a whole beat when within 5% of one, so it plays in time at once. Dropped on a Sampler's card (one with no `zones` or `regions`), the sample becomes its `sample`, copied and noted the same way, one undo step.
- *Why:* a song keeps its files, so it plays anywhere; the note keeps the credit a licence asks for (the packs design §11), without a path.
- *Today:* since track `samples`: `crates/editor/src/library/samples.rs` (`note`, `write_note`), `import.rs` (`Staged::name_noted`, `place_with`), the server's job in `crates/server/src/graph/import.rs` (`add_sample`, `sample_to_sampler`), tested by `adding_a_sample_copies_it_notes_it_and_places_one_clip`, `a_loop_at_another_tempo_follows_the_songs` and `a_sample_dropped_on_a_sampler_is_its_file` (`crates/server/src/routes_library_tests.rs`), and `e2e/spotlight.mjs`.

**LIB-8.** **std's kit** is std's own small drum kit (the packs design D8): one-shots in `std/samples/kit/`, each one note of std's drum machine (`std/drums`' kit) rendered by music2 from beat 0 until it falls silent, peak-normalised to −1 dBFS, written as 16-bit WAV at 48 kHz, mono as the drum is. `std/samples/make.sh` writes them; the test it runs checks, whenever it runs without being asked to write, that they are made again to the same bytes. They are listed in `std/samples.toml`, are under std's licence, ship with std ([PLAT-8](#platforms)), and a song names one as any package file, `"std:samples/kit/kick.wav"` ([PACK-5](#packs)). They change no render and no API, so std's version stays.
- *Why:* a beginner needs a few sounds to drop in before getting any pack, and music2 can make them itself, so they carry no one else's licence.
- *Today:* since track `samples`: `crates/editor/src/library/kit.rs` (`std_kit_is_made_again_to_the_same_bytes`).

## Platforms

**PLAT-1.** music2 MUST build and pass every check on macOS on Apple silicon (the reference platform: judged timings, the look, native menus) and on Linux x86_64 with the glibc it was built against (the development platform). Windows is designed for in every OS piece and built in its own step. Timings are judged on the [reference Mac](#acceptance-gates) only; other machines run the same gates and record their timings with the machine named.
- *Why:* the work moved to a Linux server; the Mac stays where music2 is used and timed.
- *Today:* since the platform step: Linux on the development server, macOS by the owner's run of `ci/check.sh`. Linux passes every check from that step's slice S8 ([PLAT-6](#platforms)).

**PLAT-2.** Rust code that differs between operating systems MUST live in the `m2-os` crate (`core/os`). Outside it, Rust code other than tests MUST NOT name an OS: no `cfg` on the OS, no `libc`, no `std::os`, no `extern "C"` block, no `$HOME`. The desktop shell (`apps/music2-desktop/src`) and the gates' fault injection are the exceptions. Tests may use OS-only fixtures under `cfg`. The web UI differs only in its keys and labels (⌘ or Ctrl, "Show in Finder" or "Show in Folder"), each spelled by `web/src/lib/native.ts` (`chord`, `opt`, `shifted`, `keyName`, `mod`, `revealLabel`) from `isMac`; in what Ctrl+Backspace does in a text field (the word before, where ⌘⌫ on a Mac goes to the line's start); in its font (Inter off the Mac, the Mac's own font on it, by `data-os`, which `main.tsx` sets before the first render); and, in the Mac app only (`data-app`), in the top bar's 80 px inset for the window's buttons, the time shown from 1312 px rather than 1212 (since `studio`'s Record button), and the song name's wider clearance. The shell's menu bar is one per OS (`apps/music2-desktop/src/menu.rs`): the Mac's with its app menu and Window menu; elsewhere Quit ends File and About ends Help.
- *Why:* one place per OS to read, port and check; later steps write each OS piece once.
- *Today:* since the platform step's slices S5, S6 and S8: `m2-os` holds thread identity, the plugin main loop, the disk calls, the lock, the machine's identity, the folders and file-system events, and `core/os/tests/one_place.rs` checks the rule, with an empty allow-list. Since `next` it holds MIDI input too (`m2_os::midi`, through `midir`, behind the server's `midi` feature): one file, the OS's differences inside `midir`.

**PLAT-3.** `m2-os` MUST do what the OS does, with the strongest call the volume accepts (`F_FULLFSYNC`, else `fsync`), or return `Unsupported` naming what. It never emulates: no copy for a clone, no compare-then-rename for a swap, no walk for events. Those fallbacks and their rules stay with the caller.
- *Why:* an emulation hidden in the OS layer would change a rule's meaning on one OS without the rule saying so.
- *Today:* since the platform step's slices S5, S6 and S8, for what `m2-os` holds (thread identity, the plugin main loop, the folders, the disk calls, the lock, the machine's identity, file-system events). The watcher's walks and its limit on names stay with it (`crates/server/src/graph/events.rs`, `watcher.rs`).

**PLAT-4.** A pure offline render MUST give the same bits on every supported OS and CPU. Host code that makes audio for a render, and song code run by QuickJS, MUST compute every transcendental function with the `libm` crate, the same code as `std.wasm`'s math. One record of render hashes serves every OS (`core/session/tests/renders.json`, `e2e/renders.sha256`).
- *Why:* an export must not depend on whose computer made it. Each OS's math library rounds some results differently in the last bit (glibc against `libm`: 0.04% to 9.7% of inputs, measured).
- *Today:* since the platform step ([RENDER-2](#offline)).

**PLAT-5.** `std/kernels/std.wasm` MUST rebuild to the same bytes in any folder on any supported OS with the toolchain `rust-toolchain.toml` pins, and hold no machine path. A commit that changes it says that the kernel hashes changed.
- *Why:* a kernel's hash is part of its identity ([RENDER-25](#plans-data-and-arrangement-swaps)); a rebuild must never change it by accident.
- *Today:* since the platform step: `m2-kernel` is a member of the kernels' workspace, and `std/build.sh --check` rebuilds it in another folder and compares.

**PLAT-6.** `ci/check.sh` is the full check, the same script on Linux and macOS: the build, every Rust test, clippy with warnings denied (on Linux also for the macOS target), the desktop app's tests, the web build, every browser suite, std's tests and rebuild, and the offline renders against `e2e/renders.sha256`; on macOS also the timing gates `ci/gates.txt` names. A check passes only with the test plugins present ("PASS without plugins" is not a pass). Every slice passes it on Linux. The owner runs it on a Mac at the end of each step, and before a branch that changes macOS-only code is merged. Until it passes there, the step's commit message and `HANDOFF.md` say "macOS: unverified since <commit>".
- *Why:* a hosted Mac can't judge real-time timings and has no copy of the test plugins; the owner's Mac is checked by hand, as rarely as is safe.
- *Today:* since the platform step. Linux passes it since that step's slice S8, first at `9f0b19a` (twice, with no retry); before S8 it failed only where `docs/designs/platform/design.md` §13.1 said a later slice fixes it. On Linux it also opens the desktop app on an invisible Xvfb screen. At the step's end Linux passed it at `63c45da`, with no retry, and both passed at `1750322`: the owner's Mac (MacBookPro18,1, macOS 26.6.2) on its second run, the gates included; its first run, at `b90bacf`, failed two tests that assumed Linux. Step 7 passed it on Linux at `ffc36aa`, and the `sounds` merge at `e108e46`, each with the gates and with no retry. On the owner's Mac step 7 passed it at `cbac57c` (2026-10-17), the gates included, on its first run. At `85b069a` (2026-10-17) the Mac passed 11 of 13 rows: G7f store and one songs-folder test failed, and `macfix` fixed both. Since then every track of steps 8 and 9 passed it on Linux with the gates and no retry, the last at `6d212a8` (track ex). The owner's Mac ran all of them at `e7518f4` (2026-10-18) and passed 11 of 13 rows: two tests failed (a take's steps looked up by its folder's spelled path, and the plugin window test asking about a window another process owns), fixed on branch `mf2`, merged since; and G12 failed only its set-up, the output at 44.1 kHz. The Mac ran it again at `a1e3c77` (2026-10-25) and passed 13 of 16 rows: the Rust tests (the plugin host's background state read wrongly on macOS, ten listens at once at background priority, a fix's path through `/var`, a test that waited a fixed time), the browser suites (`bus-mix`, a known flake; `sx-follow` passed on its retry) and the gates (G12's set-up, again at 44.1 kHz; G13 landed no listen in its minute) failed; branch `mf3` fixes them ([build-notes-mac.md](designs/agents/build-notes-mac.md)). One more Mac run, at 48 kHz, is owed. The last Linux run with the gates is at `c928f5d` (2026-10-24, no retry). Since `152c39d` a pull request runs the PR tier on GitHub (`ci/check.sh --pr`, a job per step of `ci/pr.sh`): no timing gates, and none of the tests `ci/nightly-tests.txt` and `e2e/run.mjs` tag nightly. The nightly tier (`ci/check.sh --nightly`, `.github/workflows/nightly.yml`) runs every test on GitHub's Linux each night, and on a hosted Mac without the test plugins once the repo is public. The timing gates run only on a quiet machine (`ci/nightly.sh`) and in the owner's Mac run.

**PLAT-7.** Every folder music2 writes outside a project (songs, your presets, caches, the shell's `~/.music2`) MUST be under `$HOME` when `HOME` is set, so a test or a suite isolates itself by setting `HOME`. Outside the desktop shell those folders come from `m2_os::dirs`. Folders music2 only reads (the system's CLAP folders, files bundled with the app) are exempt. A new song's folder name MUST count as taken when its folder holds the same name in other capitals, on every OS, as on macOS: New Song numbers past it (a song called "Night drive" beside a folder `NIGHT DRIVE` goes into `Night drive 2`), and `m2 new` refuses it ("already exists").
- *Why:* a test must never write to the user's real folders. Song folders stay safe to copy between computers (the owner's answer to the platform design's Q1).
- *Today:* every such folder is under `$HOME`; they come from `m2_os::dirs` since the platform step's slice S6, which also moved Linux's cache folder from `~/Library/Caches/music2` to `~/.cache/music2`. Your presets are in `~/Music/music2/Presets` on every OS since track br (`dirs::presets`, [FMT-22](#presets)), and installed packs in `~/Music/music2/Packs` since packs (`dirs::packs`, [PACK-6](#packs)). `XDG_*` folders are not read yet. A name in other capitals is taken by New Song since slice S2 and by `m2 new` since S6 (`crates/server/src/project.rs`: `a_name_taken_in_other_capitals_is_taken`, `create_at_refuses_a_name_taken_in_other_capitals`). In a folder that can be written but not listed only the exact name can be checked (`a_song_is_made_in_a_folder_that_cant_be_listed`).

**PLAT-8.** A release MUST be built by `.github/workflows/release.yml` from a pushed tag `v<version>` that matches the app's version, with no hand step but publishing its draft: `music2.app` for Apple silicon Macs (zipped), and for Linux on x86_64 an AppImage, a `.deb` and a `.tar.gz` of `m2` with what it needs but the app (for the AppImage's users, and anyone who wants the command line only), with a `SHA256SUMS` file and the notes in `docs/releases/<version>.md`. Each MUST run with no source tree: it carries std (with `std.wasm`), the templates, the examples, the UI and `m2`, and the plugin host is the program itself run again ([HOST-1](#pluginhost)). Each MUST also carry music2's licences and the notices of the code inside it that others wrote, made as it is built from its own lockfiles; and the `.deb` MUST ask for the glibc its programs need, the newest that isn't a weak need, computed from the built programs. The programs find what they carry from where they are, in order: the override (`$M2_STD`, `$M2_TEMPLATES`, `$M2_EXAMPLES`); beside the program in a bundle, `<program's folder>/../Resources/<name>` (`music2.app/Contents/MacOS/music2`) or `<program's folder>/../lib/music2/<name>` (`/usr/bin/music2` and `/usr/lib/music2/` in the `.deb`, the same inside the AppImage, `bin/m2` and `lib/music2/` in the `.tar.gz`), where Tauri puts its resources; else the source tree it was built from. Until signing is set up the Mac app is signed ad hoc and not notarized; the workflow signs and notarizes when the repo's Apple secrets are there, and only on a tag.
- *Why:* people who don't build from source need a download, and a downloaded app has no source tree to fall back on. One workflow from a tag makes every release the same way, and a person still decides when it goes out.
- *Today:* since the release track (0.1.0): `ci/release.sh` builds and checks each platform's files, as the workflow runs it; a pull request that changes the workflow, the bundle's config or how resources are found builds both platforms, without a release. `crates/server/src/resources.rs`'s tests fake each layout (`bundled`); `apps/music2-desktop/tests/bundle.rs` pins the config. Built and run on Linux under Xvfb from the `.deb`'s files and the AppImage; the Mac's build is owed to the workflow's first run (`docs/designs/release/build-notes.md`).

## Deferred, with triggers

Each item below is decided where this page says so, and built when its trigger arrives.

| Deferred | Trigger |
|---|---|
| Moving view, timeline view, lens, cable and effect planners, labels and `render.rs` into `crates/editor` | Built at the end of step 6: they are in `crates/editor`. |
| Gestures and tools (quantize, transpose, humanize, generators, track templates) as sandboxed TypeScript package tools that emit Tx JSON | The Tx JSON has stayed stable through one feature cycle, and the first tool-shaped feature arrives (std/gen). First check that QuickJS per gesture fits the code-plane budget. |
| Keyed iteration helpers and better structural matching (`key()` ships in step 4): ID-13's former rule 4, a unique structural match on callee, scope and argument shape, ignoring bindings and literal values, for a call cut and pasted, and for a call of the same callee written on lines of its own beside another, which can take that call's instance today ([ID-13](#derived-identity)) | Users hear state reset after refactors or loop reorders, a wrong keep is heard (an effect's state jumping), or generated banks need stable instances. |
| A saved editor index, and canvas positions in `layout.json` | Manual canvas layout. Track order and colour use track keys as soon as that feature lands. |
| Undo that survives a restart | Crash recovery is required, or users ask for undo after reopening. The journal and inverse-Tx format already allow it. |
| Playing a last-good copy (in `.music2/`) of a project whose code doesn't compile | Projects are often left broken, and "opens silent until fixed" becomes a complaint. |
| Package fetching (the registry, design packs §8), several bundled std versions, kernel ids relative to their package. `music2.lock` with content hashes and the store are built since packs ([PACK-6](#packs) to [PACK-9](#packs)) | Fetching: the owner's choice of where the bytes are kept (design packs D1, D10). Several std versions: the first std release, after people have songs, that changes existing renders ([FMT-3](#manifest-and-versions)). |
| `m2 migrate` ([FMT-3a](#manifest-and-versions)) | The first format or SDK break after format 1. |
| Kernel ABI v1's reserved shapes: event ports, `k_save`/`k_load`, `k_latency`/`k_tail` ([ABI-2](#kernel-abi-reservations), `docs/kernel-abi.md`) | Each with the first kernel that needs it: an arpeggiator, a kernel with state worth saving, a kernel with latency (a plugin's latency is compensated since ph-3, [RENDER-17](#latency)). |
| rtkit, and `RLIMIT_RTTIME` with `SIGXCPU` ignored, for Linux engine threads ([RENDER-28a](#plans-data-and-arrangement-swaps)): today they ask for `SCHED_FIFO` within `RLIMIT_RTPRIO` only | Someone plays music2 live on a Linux desktop. |
| Plugins on engine threads, with CLAP's `is_audio_thread` true on every engine thread and the probe's tests ([HOST-2](#pluginhost)) | A song with plugins measured over its budget on one thread. |
| `os_workgroup_testcancel` at each fill ([RENDER-28a](#plans-data-and-arrangement-swaps)): cpal's `DeviceChanged` already replaces the player | A dropout traced to a cancelled workgroup. |
| Clip stretching as a package kernel | Built in track sx: `std:stretch`, an offline kernel ([ABI-2](#kernel-abi-reservations)), run in the background in a live session ([RENDER-30](#audio-lanes)), and Follow tempo in the clip menu. |
| Disk streaming and the render cache ([RENDER-30](#audio-lanes)) | Built in step 9's io turn, with `read_at` and no memory map. |
| Exact range renders through kernel-only checkpoints (wasm memory snapshots cached by plan hash) | Agents need range renders exact in their tails. |
| Plugin output events as automation (recording a gesture into a lane), VST3 and AU. Plugin windows and state autosave are built (ph-2) | Automation recording from plugins, or a plugin only as VST3 or AU. |
| The `poly` op's `mode`, `retrigger`, `steal` and `glide` options: mono, legato and glide on any voice graph ([RENDER-16](#voices)) | Built in step 9's synth turns (track pm), on the arena's voice pools. std's `mono()` is one legato poly since std 0.3.0. Per-note slides written in the notes (`from_pitch`, [RENDER-14](#events)) and glide in `"poly"` mode stay deferred. |
| MIDI and computer-keyboard input, audio input with monitoring, recording takes as data Tx | Built: MIDI and the computer's keys in step 8 (`next`, `studio`); the sound input, hearing yourself ([MON-8](#monitoring-overlays-and-mute-in-song)) and audio takes in step 9's io turn ([RENDER-30a](#audio-lanes)). |
| Note expression, controller, parameter and raw MIDI events ([RENDER-14](#events)) beyond note on and off | MPE editing, recording controllers into clips, or sample-accurate plugin automation. Built in track pm: a MIDI keyboard's pitch wheel and mod wheel, live (`Midi` from live input, `Note.modWheel`). Left: recording them (note expression data, a format bump), other controllers, `NoteExpression` and `Param`. |
| Chasing notes already sounding at a seek, and preroll | The first producer complaint about missing held notes after a seek or loop. |
| Incremental compile (one cache per module, by hash) | The compile goes over 100 ms p95 at 50 tracks ([PLANE-4](#planes-of-change)). |
| Note ids, linked clips and a pattern pool, MPE per note, exact rational times | Per-note expression editing, a linked-clip or pattern workflow, triplet quantize, or merging note by note. Each is a `format` bump. |
| Events generated by code ([ABI-4](#kernel-abi-reservations)) | std/gen or generative patterns. |
| Mute and solo inside a track (in `key()` frames) and on the root module ([MON-1](#monitoring-overlays-and-mute-in-song)) | A producer needs to mute one part of a kit, or a sound written in `song.ts`. |
| Override latches on descriptor controls that survive a restart ([MON-6](#monitoring-overlays-and-mute-in-song)) | Users ask for latches to survive reopening. Needs ControlIds that outlive a session. |
| Sending deltas to clients instead of refetching the whole state | When UI update latency is measured over budget. |
| Track freezing, and bounce as a Tx (render, then an asset, then a clip). Render taps and meters are built (8b.8, [Engine shape](#engine-shape-step-7)) | The resample loop in the UI. |
| Content-addressed asset storage, deduplication, git LFS guidance. An import of bytes already in `assets/` uses that file (`studio`, [FMT-14](#assets-and-hashes)) | Sample packs, or projects with large stem sets. |
| `[locks]` in the manifest for agent variants | Agent variants ship. Globs over keys (`data/vox/**`) are already stable. |
| Units defined by packages | Only if a real need appears. |
| Windows: `core/os/src/windows/`, the Windows twins of every piece in `m2-os`, the tests' `cfg(unix)` fixes, and the build (MSVC, QuickJS under `cl.exe`, `llvm-rc` for the desktop app) ([PLAT-1](#platforms)) | The owner asks for Windows users. |
| The `XDG_*` folders (music, cache) ([PLAT-7](#platforms)). Linux packaging (deb, AppImage) and a Linux bundle in `bundled()` are built since the release track ([PLAT-8](#platforms)) | A Linux user asks for them, or someone runs the app on a Linux desktop that moves them. |
| Stored spelling on case-folding Linux volumes (vfat, exFAT, casefold folders): `m2_os::fs::stored_name` answers nothing on Linux today | A project on such a volume, or a volume to test on. |
| `FICLONE` clones on btrfs and XFS: Linux has no clone today, so a commit copies each file's before bytes | A Linux user on such a volume with large plugin states. |
| An event source that tells the watcher "I can't cover this project" at once (inotify out of watches after start, the root unmounted). Today each asks for one walk (a Rescan); a change the events then miss is found by the 5 s walks, and two such misses move the project to walks ([WRITE-19](#outside-writers)) | A user report of a machine out of inotify watches. |
| An exFAT disk image on the Mac, to run the macOS fallbacks there (the save without a swap, the barrier's `fsync`, the folder syncs a volume refuses, the clone, the lock) | A Mac user with a project on an exFAT or network volume reports a problem. |
| An allocator for song code that counts the bytes asked for, so QuickJS's 256 MB limit falls at the same place on every OS ([RENDER-2](#offline)) | A song near the limit, or one that catches running out of memory, builds differently on two computers. |

## Order of work

Steps 3 and 4 may run in parallel; everything else is in order.

1. **Close today's silent paths** (2 days). **Built.**
   - The session's warnings reach every client: the app's state and every graph event over the WebSocket carry them, the app shows them in its status pill's popover, and every CLI command that opens a project prints them with the commands that fix them ([DIAG-4](#delivery)).
   - Lane data no lane reads, left behind when a track or a lane is renamed in code, is the Warning `data.unclaimed`, with Relink and Delete fixes: `m2 relink`, `m2 delete-data`, and the strip's Move and Delete. Each fix is a checked, undoable session edit, applied only when asked ([DIAG-6](#fixes)).
   - `revert()` uses the current origin of the control at the carried position, with the regression test ([ID-12](#derived-identity)).
2. **Write the contract** (this page) and have it reviewed.
3. **Live step A** (2–3 weeks). **Built.** Land 3a before 3b, and keep the offline path untouched.
   - **3a, runtime (built):** the engine and transport clocks, with the engine running while stopped; an integer transport and loop ends computed from beats; lane schedulers that fill event buffers and own their sounding notes; a jump that never resets kernels; a new renderer activated at the audio thread's own position, with a 5 ms crossfade; `SwapLane` for notes and control lanes when the old and new `Flat` graphs are equal; the range render for agents.
   - **3b, around the runtime (built):** gesture leases that survive saves which don't touch them; the session as an actor; `PluginHost` with its whole trait and the in-process executor on the real main thread; plugin instances carried across rebuilds.
4. **Format v1 and SDK keys, in one change** (1–2 weeks). **Built.**
   - Track, lane and plugin keys, and `key()` frames; `Module { key, label }`; a context that exposes the key, never the label.
   - `data/<module>/` with clip ids and lane units; the manifest with `format` read first, `sample_rate`, and caret requirements for every dependency; the live engine at `sample_rate`.
   - `DataPath` and its classes; snapshots that read the explicit set, and a build that imports only from them; the canonical form of data files, written in `core/song`; the arrangement and meter rules.
   - Per-lane data errors that keep the last accepted data; the asset hash cache and pins, so compile no longer decodes audio; asset names; plugin parameters addressed `<name>_<id>` and bound by id; `plugin.missing`; required state sidecars; the diagnostic model, with fixes still named session edits.
   - Convert the 5 fixtures by script, then delete `slug()`.
5. **Descriptors and cells** (about 2 weeks). **Built.**
   - `component()` with declared inputs, controls, settings, defaults and editor metadata, sidechain ports and smooth controls; std rewritten on it.
   - One const per declared control with an `(instance, slot)` origin; ComponentIds in frames; the unit table generated from `core.ts`; runtime cells, written through per-cell slots, ramped where declared.
   - Delete `[[effect]] args`, `EFFECT_CATEGORIES`, the `cable.rs` unit table and the `view.rs` range guesses.
6. **Tx and the write path** (about 3 weeks, then 1 week of mechanical moves). **Built.**
   - The `save`/`Tx` split, with validated paths, the `Clips` op and base texts; the project lock (no forwarding), its read-only open and `lock.unsupported`; content-hash bases.
   - `GraphDelta` with node names an insertion can't shift, and `Identical`, `Values`, `Confined` and `Seen`; the journal, with host names, staging in `.music2/tx/`, and recovery as a diagnostic.
   - `(code_rev, data_rev)`, and a data-only path that skips QuickJS, chosen by path class; the watcher's settle rule; `data.claimed` from Tx.
   - `Session::open` on a broken draft.
   - Port the 7 gestures, step 1's two fixes and the bypasses; fixes become Tx; delete the guess at `graph.rs:341-348`; make `write_whole` private.
   - First new builders: Relink of a whole module folder, rename label, automate a knob. Then the move into `crates/editor`.
6b. **The platform layer** (about 2 weeks). Development moved to a Linux server, so Linux is made green there first, natively, and the Mac stays green. **Built** when its gates pass.
   - `m2-os` (`core/os`), one folder per OS ([PLAT-2](#platforms)): renames and the durable sync, the lock and machine checks, file-system events (FSEvents, inotify), the plugin main loop, the folders.
   - The same render bits on every OS through `libm` ([PLAT-4](#platforms)); a reproducible `std.wasm` and a pinned toolchain ([PLAT-5](#platforms)); saves that a crash can't break on volumes without an atomic exchange, and new audio made durable before a song names it.
   - One check script for both OSes, `ci/check.sh`, run by hand on the owner's Mac, timing gates included ([PLAT-6](#platforms)).
   - Steps 8a and 9 add their OS pieces to `m2-os`; Windows is designed for in each piece and built in its own step.
7. **The engine** (4–6 weeks). **Built** on Linux; done once the owner's Mac run passes.
   - `Plan` split from `DataSet`; SiteId matching, InstancePath and RuntimeId.
   - The arena, with prepare and activate, keeping instances whose RuntimeId and shape (wasm hash included) match.
   - Arrangement switches prepared off the audio thread; persistent CLAP instances behind `PluginHost`, with state loads into them as a plan is prepared, and offline renders that reuse them.
8. **Partitions, then producer features.** **Built** on Linux; done once the owner's Mac run passes (G12's Mac sweep, `ci/sweep.sh`, included).
   - **8a (built):** parallel partitions on a thread pool, right after the arena, with renders that don't depend on the thread count. Its offline half (exports on every core) and its live half (playback on real-time engine threads, a sound that breaks goes silent alone, G12).
   - **8b (built):** each a Tx builder or an engine feature on this base: add, duplicate, delete and rename track; the mixer with monitoring mute and solo plus the `muted` track option; automate a knob; the tempo bar, with its lease; clip editing by id, with lane leases; audition. With them (tracks `taste-next`, `8b-ui`, `polish`, `next`, `studio`): the look, meters, the metronome, the keyboard piano and MIDI keyboards, Settings, audio import, recording notes, MIDI files and stems, and help in plain words.
9. **After the core,** synth and plugin turns alternate with audio and sample turns. **Built** on Linux (2026-10-18, every track at `main` `3c26fcd`); done once the owner's Mac run passes.
   - synth and plugin: the plugin host process (built in ph-1), then plugin windows (with state autosave and output events, built in ph-2), then latency compensation (built in ph-3), then the `poly` op's mono, legato, glide and steal options (built in track pm), then MIDI input (notes and the sustain pedal built in step 8's `next`; the pitch and mod wheels built in track pm);
   - audio and sample: disk streaming (built in track io), then kernel ABI v1 and the sampler (built in track ab, with one sandbox a box), then stretching as an offline kernel (built in track sx, with Follow tempo), then recording (notes built in step 8's `studio`; audio built in track io).
   - between them, one path for faults (built in track ex): every fault found while sound is made goes through the fault ring and is one problem, live ([RENDER-25](#plans-data-and-arrangement-swaps), the sound input's too, [RENDER-30a](#audio-lanes)); offline, any fault that changed the sound fails the render ([RENDER-1](#offline)).
   - Then agents beside the musician, the [agents design](designs/agents/design.md)'s five tracks (`ag-steps`, `ag-fwd`, `ag-see`, `ag-routes`, `ag-listen`): **built** on Linux (2026-10-24), with the [Agents](#agents) rules and gate G13.
   - Next: Windows, as its own step ([PLAT-1](#platforms), the Deferred row).

   TypeScript package tools and the other deferred items come in when their triggers arrive.

## Acceptance gates

A step is done when its gates pass in `ci/check.sh` on Linux and on the owner's Mac ([PLAT-6](#platforms)). Timed gates are judged in the Mac's run, which runs the gates `ci/gates.txt` names, in release, on the reference projects. **p16** and **p50** are the 16- and 50-track reference projects: tracks cycle through std `bass` (with `delay`), `pluck` and `pad` (with `reverb`), playing `examples/first-song`'s notes repeated to several minutes. The script that generates them is checked in with step 3. Timings are taken on the **reference Mac**, the oldest supported Apple-silicon Mac (an M1), at 48 kHz. Other machines run the same gates and record their timings with the machine named; only the reference Mac's are judged ([PLAT-1](#platforms)).

| Step | Gates |
|---|---|
| 1 | **Passed.** Lane data left behind by a rename shows in the app with its fixes, and in the output of every CLI command that opens the project. Relink makes it play, and undo moves it back; Delete removes it, and undo restores its bytes. A test inserts a soundless call at the top of `song.ts` after a knob edit in `tracks/bass.ts`: undo succeeds and restores the bytes, and `can_undo` agrees with what undo does. |
| 2 | The owner has reviewed this page. Later changes cite rule ids. |
| 3 | On p16 at 1:00 and on `examples/with-plugins`: a seek is audible within 50 ms; a 4-bar loop at 127 bpm runs 10 minutes with no tail cut and no drift against the grid; a note edit is audible within compile time + 20 ms with no tails cut; deleting or shortening a sounding note while playing leaves no note stuck; an added effect is audible within compile time + 100 ms with no jump back; adding or removing an effect while a sine plays makes no sample-to-sample step larger than twice the sine's own largest; after stop, a reverb tail decays instead of freezing; previews never wait on a compile; an outside save to another file during a drag doesn't interrupt the drag. A range render from bar 64 takes no longer than one of the same length from bar 1 (within 10%), and says it is approximate. The bit-identity test ([RENDER-33](#the-bit-identity-test)) passes on every pure fixture. Every pure fixture's offline render is byte-identical to before step 3. |
| 4 | Each pure fixture, converted by the script, renders byte-identical to its render before conversion. Changing a track's label changes no file outside its `track()` call and no audio, and no property of the track's context or any handle returns the label. Renaming a track key by hand raises `data.unclaimed` on its folder, and its Relink restores the sound; the same holds, separately, for a lane key. A lane file with an unknown field plays its last accepted data in a running session, and silences only that lane on open, with an Error naming the file and the field. A manifest with `format = 2` and an unknown key gives `format.newer`, and the project doesn't open. Changing an imported `.js` file, or an import spelled with other capitals, fails to compile on every OS. Reading and writing each canonical data file gives the same bytes, and permuting any array in a fixture renders the same bits. Removing a plugin from the search path silences only that node, and every other track renders the same bits as before. A plugin build that renames and swaps parameter names leaves every connection on the same CLAP id. Compile time doesn't depend on asset size. Editing `layout.json` creates no revision. The live engine runs at the project's `sample_rate`. |
| 5 | std is rewritten on descriptors, and every pure fixture renders byte-identical to before. Every declared control, written or inherited, appears as a knob with its declared range. `import { delay as echo }` is recognised as `delay`. A `SetCell` changes a value with no rebuild and no allocation on the audio thread. The debug input-memory check passes for every std kernel. The four deleted items are gone. |
| 6 | The 7 gestures and step 1's fixes are ported, and their existing tests pass with the same meaning. Property tests over Tx hold: bytes outside the declared ranges are unchanged, the inverse restores the original, and hostile paths and ids are refused. `check(tx)` then `commit(tx)` write the same bytes, with equal delta hashes. Inserting one unconnected call at the top of `song.ts` gives a delta of exactly that call's nodes, with the same hash in two processes. A lane file changed by an outside writer is accepted without running QuickJS, and a lane file written in two halves never produces a revision in which that lane is empty. Undoing a clip edit after an outside change to that clip is a `Conflict` naming the lane and the clip. A CLI write while the app holds the lock fails, changes nothing and names the holder (since `ag-fwd` it is handed to the app instead, [AGENT-1](#agents)); `m2 bounce` without `--place` succeeds. Killing the process at every commit step and reopening offers `tx.interrupted`, and both Finish and Discard leave a project that opens. A project with broken code opens, the code editor saves, and playback starts when the code compiles. `write_whole` is private, and the guess at `graph.rs:341-348` is gone. |
| 6b | **Passed** on Linux and on the owner's Mac at `1750322` (the Mac's G11: `tx-data` p95 8.6 ms idle, 9.2 ms beside a syncing writer, 8.9 ms with 5,000 assets; `watcher` 0.40% of a core). `ci/check.sh` says `music2 check: PASS` on Linux, with the test plugins: every Rust test (the desktop app's, the kill test on disks with and without an atomic exchange, and the watcher's folder tests included), clippy `-D warnings` on the whole workspace and for `aarch64-apple-darwin`, the desktop app opened on an invisible screen (Xvfb) with `examples/first-song` and with `examples/with-plugins`, its UI connected and its plugins hosted, the web build, the kernels' tests and clippy, `std.wasm` rebuilt to its bytes in another folder, all 29 browser-suite runs in full, the 8 offline renders equal to `e2e/renders.sha256`, and the repo unchanged by the check. The same script says `PASS` on the owner's Mac at the step's last commit, G11 `tx-data` and `watcher` included. `core/os/tests/one_place.rs` passes with no file excepted (G11's fault injection is the one function PLAT-2 allows). |
| 7 | **Passed on Linux** at `ffc36aa`, after the speed work and a review of its fixes (step 7's build notes, "Speed"), and again at `e108e46` with the `sounds` merge. **Passed on macOS** at `cbac57c`, the owner's run of 2026-10-17, every gate (G7f's numbers in [RENDER-32](#engine-shape-step-7)); the run before, at `ed7fc8e`, failed only G7f part 2, by the old sum (+328 µs against 320). At `85b069a` (2026-10-17) G7f part 1 failed on the Mac, +135 µs a block against 80 (RENDER-32); at `e7518f4` (2026-10-18), with one sandbox a box, G7f passed on the Mac (RENDER-32). In p50, a structural edit at 3:00 is audible within 200 ms after the compile, and the tails of untouched tracks are intact. Live playback with no edits is bit-identical to offline. A kernel module rebuilt with the same kernel id but a new hash starts from init. An export of `examples/with-plugins` pauses playback, reuses its instances, and playback resumes with their state intact. The per-instance `Store` cost at p50's 6,058 kernel instances (as G7f counts them) is measured, and the store layout is chosen from it. As built and measured on Linux (information only, [PLAT-1](#platforms)): **G7a**, through the server: heard 106 to 116 ms after the request at p95, the compile included, with one kernel or none made new an edit, and never a voice pool; its engine-rig twin heard 26.7 ms after the compile, with no jump back, and no step in the edit's own sound over twice its largest in the 200 ms on each side. **G7b**: 49 untouched tracks bit-identical over 5,625 blocks, all 200 stateful nodes kept and none reset, and a pluck wrapped in an effect bit-identical both ways, as a Tx and as a file save; with `--no-arena` it fails, as it must. **G7c**: live with no edits equals the export on every pure fixture and p16 (recorded: all of p16 and of p50), and activations of the same revision change no bit. **G7d**: a rebuilt kernel module starts from init, and a move of the wrong shape is refused. **G7e**: the export pauses playback for about 255 ms, borrows its 2 plugin instances and makes none, and resumes where it held within 21.3 ms of their return, with no step over twice the music's own; the chord held across the pause sounds again, and the file matches `m2 render`'s within 1 dB in each second. **G7f**, the owner's layout ([RENDER-32](#engine-shape-step-7)): two p50 renderers hold 0.29% of the address space (P1 held 37.9% on the Mac); against S, measured fresh in the same run, by the mean of 5 rounds: +16 µs a block and +48 µs a 512-frame callback held on one CPU die, +20 and +86 µs in `ci/check.sh --gates` (the Mac judges them against 80 and 320 µs); the slowest activation block 2.3 to 3.1 ms (under 5.3). **The churn test**: nothing leaks over 55 activations, and p16's 200 edits stay within +20 MB (recorded: −4.0 MB). |
| 8 | p50 plays without dropouts at 128 frames and 48 kHz on the reference Mac, with the audio callback at no more than 70% of its block budget at p99 over 10 minutes; a 64-frame run is recorded (8a). Dropouts are counted from the first callback after Play: the device's overloads, callbacks longer than their period, and gaps of more than 1.5 periods between callbacks. Every engine thread runs as real time in the output's audio workgroup. On a Mac other than a base M1 the verdict is provisional. Each pure fixture renders the same bits on 1 thread and on N. Each producer feature lands without changing core's write API. Muting the kick in a fixture still ducks the bass, with the ducking read from `kick.env`, from `follow(kick.out)` in `song.ts`, and through a compressor keyed from the kick's audio. Toggling mute while a sine plays makes no sample-to-sample step larger than twice the sine's own largest. An export is byte-identical with and without overlays active. *8a's offline half:* each pure fixture renders the same bits on 1, 2, 3, 4 and 7 threads, on Linux. *8a's live half:* live playback renders on engine threads with the same bits on 1 and N threads, on Linux. G12's live run is built (`m2-gates realtime`; one minute of it in `ci/gates.txt`): on Linux it plays the silent clock, as information. On the owner's Mac (`ci/sweep.sh` at `e7518f4`, 2026-10-18), 10 minutes of p50 at 128 frames on 4 parked threads: p99 about 1,310 µs (1,308; 45% of the period), the longest 1,601 µs, 0 dropouts, no crash. **Provisional:** the output was at 44.1 kHz, not 48, so G12 said "not the designed set-up"; one more run at 48 kHz is owed. The sweep confirmed `live_count` 4 and `BETWEEN = Park` (spin was far worse) and exports on the 8 fast cores (6.4× one thread); the numbers are in [RENDER-28a](#plans-data-and-arrangement-swaps). Not built: G12 through the app's server while it is edited (`--server`), beside an export (`--export`), and `--like-m1`. *8b's clauses:* the ducking through `kick.env` and through `follow(kick.out)`, the click test and the byte-identical export pass on Linux (`core/play/tests/mute.rs`, `crates/server/src/graph/overlay_tests.rs`); the keyed compressor waits for a keyed compressor in std (std 0.3 has none). |
| 9 | Plugins (ph): a crash of the plugin host process silences its plugins alone, says so once, and they come back (G9a, `a_host_crash_is_one_plugin_crashed_problem`); a render through the host process is the in-process render, bit for bit (G9b, `renders_through_the_host_process_are_the_same_bits`); a latent plugin stays aligned, live and offline, and no fixture gets a delay (G9c, `core/runtime/src/tests/live/keep/latency.rs`, `no_fixture_gets_a_delay`). The other tracks add no timed gate: each kept every render's bits, or changed them on purpose with std's version ([FMT-3](#manifest-and-versions): `examples/resample` in sx, `examples/sounds` in pm), and G7f judges ab's layout on the Mac ([RENDER-32](#engine-shape-step-7)). **Passed on Linux** at each track's last commit (`ci/check.sh --gates`, nothing passed only on a retry; the last at `6d212a8`, track ex). On the owner's Mac at `e7518f4` (2026-10-18) G7f passed ab's layout; two tests failed, fixed on branch `mf2`, merged since (PLAT-6). One more Mac run is owed. |
| agents | Each track of the [agents design](designs/agents/design.md) adds its gate here. Track 1 (`ag-steps`): an outside save of a text file that builds is one undo step, which ⌘Z and the notice's Undo take back; an import makes one step and no outside step; an undo for a step that is no longer the newest changes nothing; the notice and the glow show an outside step (`e2e/ag-notice.mjs`); the offline renders still equal `e2e/renders.sha256`. Track 2 (`ag-fwd`): every `m2` write command works with the app open and lands as one undo step in the app, named by `--as` and `--why` (`apps/m2/tests/forward.rs`, `apps/m2/tests/edit.rs`); no lease, take or unsaved text of the musician's ends because of an agent's write, which is refused on arrival or taken back at its settle, never heard (`crates/server/src/graph/busy_tests.rs`); `m2 undo` takes back only its own step. Track 3 (`ag-see`): `m2 focus` says what the page selected, by key and by note, and focus and pointers change no file; `m2 watch` prints one JSON line an event and ends when the app quits; a pointer never moves the view or the selection; V plays the newest outside step's Before from memory while the loop plays, writing nothing, and an agent's write is busy while the pill is open; `m2 watch` follows one song (`e2e/ag-focus.mjs`, `apps/m2/tests/watch.rs`, `crates/server/src/graph/compare_tests.rs`). Track 4 (`ag-routes`): every route is declared once, in the one table the router, the 423, the 403 and `m2 routes` are made from, and every example body reads; the tools key gets 403 on every route not for agents; `m2 call` reaches an action with no command of its own, names its step, and refuses the musician's controls before sending (`crates/server/src/api_tests.rs`, `apps/m2/tests/call.rs`). Track 5 (`ag-listen`): copy taps change no bit of any pure fixture; the measures read known signals as EBU Tech 3341 and BS.1770 say (a 1 kHz sine at −23 dBFS reads −23 LUFS, a peak between samples is found, pink noise has flat octave bands, mono correlates 1 and inverted −1), a meter warmed before its range reads a loud sound at its start as it is, and a sample that isn't a number is counted and read as 0; masking names two tracks in one band but none turned down in the mix, none that take turns, a ducked bass only where it meets the kick, and a clash beside a loud track's band (`crates/editor/src/listen_tests.rs`); `m2 listen` reports every track and the mix, writes nothing with `--with`, hears the listening mutes only with `--as-heard`, by key in the change too, and says so, compares the same bars across a change of tempo, measures a group bus as it reaches the mix, draws its picture, runs at background priority with its plugin host, one at a time, refuses what it doesn't know, and never waits on a busy app (`apps/m2/tests/listen.rs`). G13 (`m2-gates realtime --beside agents`): p50 plays live while `m2 listen` runs back to back on it (every other listen on `examples/with-plugins` when its plugins are there) and forwarded `m2 set` edits land in an app beside it; judged everywhere, no crash or non-finite sample, that listens and edits ran, and every listen and its plugin host at background priority; its dropouts and callback times are recorded, on the Mac too until its first runs there are recorded, then judged as G12's (no dropout, p99 at most 70% of the period). **Passed on Linux** at `c928f5d` (2026-10-24, `ci/check.sh --gates=auto`, the 8 gate runs, nothing passed only on a retry). The Mac run is owed. |

These gates keep running in `ci/check.sh` once their step lands:
- offline determinism: two renders of each pure fixture are byte-identical ([RENDER-2](#offline));
- the bit-identity test, from step 3 ([RENDER-33](#the-bit-identity-test));
- every id-bearing field in the format v1 schemas is a key, a clip id or a CLAP parameter id, from step 4 ([ID-16](#derived-identity));
- the audio thread runs under the allocation and lock trap, from step 5 ([RENDER-28](#plans-data-and-arrangement-swaps));
- every value the view returns names its home, from step 5 ([HOME-1](#homes-of-values));
- every OS renders the bits `e2e/renders.sha256` records, and `std.wasm` rebuilds to its bytes in any folder, from the platform step ([PLAT-4](#platforms), [PLAT-5](#platforms));
- Rust code outside `m2-os` and the desktop shell names no OS (`core/os/tests/one_place.rs`), from the platform step ([PLAT-2](#platforms));
- renders don't depend on the thread count, from step 8a ([RENDER-2](#offline));
- overlays never change an export, from step 8 ([MON-4](#monitoring-overlays-and-mute-in-song)).
